Delivery (native flag, Windows temp file via `--append-system-prompt-file`, embed for providers without the flag, skip the embed on resume, system prompt as sole prompt) is DECIDED by the pure `resolveSystemPromptDelivery()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/launch/prompt-delivery.ts` and REALIZED in main by `applySystemPromptDelivery`, which `process:spawn`, `buildSpawnSpec` (Cue), and `spawnGroupChatAgent` all call; apply it BEFORE the launch plan (`buildAgentLaunchPlan` takes the prompt already final), SSH wrapping, and the maestro-p rewrite so all three see the final args and prompt. Do NOT append a section by string concatenation at a call site, and do NOT add another inline copy of the delivery branches. The CLI spawner realizes the same decision itself (it cannot import main, and it removes its temp file when the child exits rather than on a timer), so the rule tree exists once: change a delivery rule in the resolver, never in either realization.
- **Expanding `{{INCLUDE:name}}` in a prompt:** `resolvePromptIncludes(content, { load, rootId?, onMissing?, onCycle? })`, `findPromptIncludeNames()`, `MAX_PROMPT_INCLUDE_DEPTH` in `src/shared/promptIncludes.ts`. Two loaders build prompts - the desktop's `getPrompt()` (`src/main/prompt-manager.ts`, sync, from its cache) and the CLI's `getCliPrompt()` (`src/cli/services/prompt-loader.ts`, async, from customizations then the bundled file) - and they differ only in where text comes from, so the expansion rules live here and the loader is an argument. The CLI used to expand `{{REF:}}` but not `{{INCLUDE:}}`, which was invisible only because no prompt used an include yet: the first one would have reached every CLI-spawned agent as the literal directive. The CLI pre-loads what a prompt reaches (`loadIncludeSources`) and then resolves synchronously. A missing or circular include leaves its directive in place rather than an empty gap. Do NOT hand-roll another `{{INCLUDE:` regex.
- **The system-prompt envelope for a provider with no `--append-system-prompt`:** `EMBEDDED_SYSTEM_PROMPT_SEPARATOR`, `embedSystemPromptInPrompt(systemPrompt, userPrompt)`, `stripEmbeddedSystemPrompt(text)`, `hasEmbeddedSystemPrompt(text)` in `src/shared/embeddedSystemPrompt.ts`. Only `claude-code` declares `supportsAppendSystemPrompt`; for every other provider the spawn path has nowhere to put system content except the FIRST user turn, so it sends the whole Maestro system prompt, a `---` rule, and a `# User Request` heading ahead of what the human typed. The provider writes that turn to its session file verbatim, so the envelope is a WIRE FORMAT: changing the separator orphans every transcript already on disk, and a test pins the exact string. Both producers (`src/main/ipc/handlers/process.ts` for the desktop spawn, `src/cli/services/agent-spawner.ts` for the CLI) and the reader (`transcriptMessagesToLogEntries`) go through this module, because a third hand-built copy on the reading side is free to drift from the two on the writing side - which is exactly how a hydrated tab came to render the system prompt, the conductor profile, the cwd and the agent's own id as if the user had typed them (issue #1533). The strip is deliberately conservative: it honours only the FIRST separator (so a user request that quotes the marker survives), it requires something to precede it (a message that OPENS with `# User Request` was never wrapped, and stripping there would delete the user's first line), and only USER turns are unwrapped (an agent explaining the format keeps its answer). Do NOT template the envelope inline at a new spawn site, and do NOT strip it at a call site above `transcriptMessagesToLogEntries` - resume and backfill have to produce byte-identical entries for the same message or the backfill cannot find its splice point. Known gap: the Agent Sessions browser (`SessionMessagesView`) renders the raw messages and still shows the envelope.
- **Who asked for this turn (interactive vs automation):** `QUERY_SOURCE_ENV_VAR`, `QuerySource`, `DEFAULT_QUERY_SOURCE` in `src/shared/querySource.ts`. Every agent turn Maestro runs - one the user typed, an Auto Run task, a Cue subscription firing - is the same thing on the machine: an ordinary agent process, same binary, same args, same cwd, writing to the same provider transcript. Nothing outside Maestro can tell them apart, so tooling that hangs off the agent (Claude Code hooks, wrapper scripts, telemetry sidecars) is left guessing from prompt text - which is not an answer, because Cue prompts are the user's own words out of `cue.yaml` and read exactly like something a human typed. So Maestro states it: every spawn stamps `MAESTRO_QUERY_SOURCE` into the child env. Set it by CLAIMING the turn at the dispatch site (`querySource: 'auto'` on the Auto Run spawn, `'cue'` in `cue-spawn-builder`), never by inferring it downstream. It is stamped AFTER the user-editable env layers in `buildChildProcessEnv()` on purpose: a stray global var of the same name would otherwise silently relabel every turn on the machine, and the marker is only worth having if it is trusted. **SSH needs it re-stated**: only `customEnvVars` crosses to the remote host, so the local process env never gets there - `wrapSpawnWithSsh` takes `querySource` and exports it, and any new SSH spawn site must pass it or every remote turn arrives looking hand-typed. Terminal PTYs deliberately do NOT carry the marker (a shell is not an agent turn), which is why `collectMaestroEnvVars()` only reports it when the caller resolved one - that list mirrors what the process actually received and must not advertise a variable the user would then fail to find. The value describes ORIGIN, not autonomy: a `user` turn whose prompt says "go fix everything" is still `user`, and ties break toward the human because over-reporting delegation would flatter the numbers.
- **Prefixing an agent's New Session Message:** `prependNewSessionMessageOnFirstTurn(prompt, message, agentSessionId)` and `prependNewSessionMessage(prompt, message)` in `src/shared/newSessionMessage.ts`. The New Session Message is the user's standing instructions for an agent, and the contract is "the first turn of a new provider session carries it, however that turn starts". The guard used to be an inline `if (!tabAgentSessionId)` in the composer (`useInputProcessing`) and nowhere else, so the same message reached the agent or did not depending on the ROUTE: typed into an idle agent it was prefixed, while a message queued into a fresh tab, a slash command, a `maestro-cli dispatch`, a snooze wake prompt, a `maestro-cli send`, and every Cue run all spawned a first turn without it. Nothing fails visibly when it is missing; the agent just never sees the instructions. Two functions because there are two kinds of spawn. A turn that may be RESUMING (the composer, both branches of `agentStore.processQueuedItem`, `useRemoteHandlers`, `maestro-cli send`, which resumes only with `--session`) calls `prependNewSessionMessageOnFirstTurn`, which prefixes only when the tab has no provider session id yet - a resumed conversation already carries the message from its first turn. A spawn that is ALWAYS a new provider session (an Auto Run task, a goal-runner iteration, a Cue run, desktop and CLI) calls `prependNewSessionMessage` unconditionally. Three rules. The prefix is agent-only: build it into the prompt handed to the spawn and keep the plain text for the user bubble, the queued card, and the CLI run ledger. In Cue it is applied AFTER template substitution and after the empty-prompt check, because it is the user's own text rather than a Cue prompt (a literal `{{CUE_SOURCE_OUTPUT}}` in it must stay literal) and a standing prefix must not mask the warning about an empty subscription prompt. And the main-process Cue path only sees what `src/main/index.ts` copies off the stored session into `executeCuePrompt`'s `session`, so a new spawn site built from a hand-picked subset of the stored record must copy `newSessionMessage` too. Group Chat and `@mention` consults deliberately get nothing: those prompts are written by a moderator or another agent, not sent by the user to this agent. The Nudge Message is a separate rule (appended only to a message typed straight to the agent) and does not ride this module. User-facing table: `docs/general-usage.md` -> When Each Message Is Sent.
- **Who SENT this turn (Web Login attribution):** `getActingUser()` in `src/main/web-server/auth/acting-user.ts`, and `noteTurnActor()` / `resolveTurnActor()` / `forgetAgentActors()` in `src/main/web-server/auth/turn-attribution.ts`. With Web Login on, every turn a browser sends is attributed to the account that sent it - `HistoryEntry.userName` / `userDisplayName` drive the History row's sender pill and the `UserSourceFilter` beside the host picker, and `query_events.user_name` carries the same answer into the stats database. The thing that makes this non-obvious: **the account is in scope at SPAWN and nowhere else.** A bridge call from a logged-in browser runs inside an AsyncLocalStorage context, so a handler can ask `getActingUser()` - but the one-shot effects of a turn are not written by that handler. The `history:add` entry and the `stats:record-query` row are written LATER, by the DESKTOP renderer's exit listener, which is the single owner of one-shot turn effects across every client (see `useOwnedSideEffectGate`). By then no acting user exists anywhere, so reading `getActingUser()` at write time always answers `undefined` and a turn sent from a phone is recorded as if it had been typed at the keyboard. So `process:spawn` calls `noteTurnActor(agentId, tabId, getActingUser())` and the two write handlers resolve it back with `resolveTurnActor(agentId, tabId)`, preferring a live acting user when there happens to be one and an explicit `userName` from the caller over both. One entry per tab, overwritten by the next spawn, because a tab runs one turn at a time - an entry left behind by a finished turn is harmless. A DESKTOP spawn passes `undefined`, which CLEARS the entry rather than leaving it: without that, a phone's earlier turn on the same tab is credited to the next one typed at the keyboard, which is worse than no attribution. `forgetAgentActors(agentId)` runs where `sessions:setMany` tombstones a closed agent, since a closed agent can never produce another turn. The spawn also stamps `QUERY_USER_ENV_VAR` (`MAESTRO_QUERY_USER`) into `effectiveCustomEnvVars` at the same injection point as the caller-identity vars, for the same reason: it is the only place that reaches BOTH the local and the SSH env merges. Terminal tabs are excluded from both the note and the stamp - a shell the user drives is not an agent turn. NULL / absent means "nobody was signed in", which is a different fact from an account named the empty string: every surface renders the bare row (no pill, the `Desktop` bucket in the filter) rather than inventing a placeholder account.
- **An agent's effective environment:** `resolveAgentEnvironment()`, `isSecretEnvKey()`, `maskEnvValue()`, `envSourceLabel()` in `src/shared/agentEnvironment.ts`, rendered by `<EnvVarList>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/EnvVarList.tsx`. Two layers decide what an agent runs as, edited in different panes, so no single settings screen answers "which profile is this actually running as?": global (Settings -> Environment) underneath, and on top exactly ONE user-configured set - the agent's own vars, or the per-provider set (Settings -> Agents) when the agent has no vars record at all. The agent's record REPLACES the provider set rather than merging over it, even when it is empty, because that is what `applyAgentConfigOverrides()` hands the process; a merged view shows a provider `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` beside an agent's `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` that the agent never receives. The module does the SAME resolution as the spawner and reports which layer won plus which it shadowed. Do NOT re-derive the precedence inline: a merge that disagrees with the spawner's describes a process nobody is running. Distinct from `Settings/EnvVarsEditor`, which EDITS one layer - pick by question ("change a value" vs "which profile am I on?") and do not add an edit mode to `<EnvVarList>` to cover both. **A variable switched off with the editor's eye button lives in a SECOND record** (`shellEnvVarsDisabled`, same shape as `shellEnvVars`), and nothing but the editor may read it: parking is what keeps the value recoverable without it reaching a process, so being present in `shellEnvVars` is exactly the same statement as being live, and every consumer stays filter-free. Do NOT merge the disabled record into a spawn env or teach a spawner to skip entries - a single `enabled` flag inside one record would put that filter in every one of the dozen-odd read sites, and the first one that forgets it silently ships the API key the user thought they had switched off. Secret-looking keys are masked behind a per-row reveal, matched loosely on purpose: these panels get opened while screen-sharing for help, so a false positive costs one click and a false negative leaks a live key. A fourth source, `gateway` ("Local Models gateway"), is the base-URL variable an agent's gateway sets; pass `gateway: gatewayEnvVarsFor(toolType, url)` and the module applies it only to keys the agent's own layer leaves unset (see the Local Models gateway entry). Do NOT pass it for a view that describes a terminal or the ReauthModal login shell: those never receive the variable.
- **Whether a configured env value means "unset":** `isBlankEnvValue(value)`, `isBlankEnvKey(key)` and `stripBlankEnvVars(vars)` in `src/shared/agentEnvironment.ts`. A blank field in an env editor is an ABSENCE, not a value, and the two are not interchangeable to the child: exporting `FOO=` hands over a set-but-empty variable, so code that reads `process.env.FOO` as a path gets `''` instead of falling back to its own default. That is how a blank `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` made Claude Code call `mkdir('')` and die before it reached the provider. Every spawn path therefore drops blanks: `collectMaestroEnvVars()` and `buildChildProcessEnv()` in `src/main/process-manager/utils/envBuilder.ts`, and `withQuerySource()` in `src/main/utils/ssh-spawn-wrapper.ts`. Two rules when you touch a spawn env. **Merge the layers first and strip second** - dropping a blank before the merge leaves the lower layer's value in place, so a blank session override could not cancel a global one, which is the main reason a user blanks a field at all. And **`delete env[key]`, do not merely skip the assignment** - the child inherits `process.env`, so skipping leaves the inherited value live and the "unset" wins nothing. Deliberately DIFFERENT from `resolveAgentEnvironment()` above, which KEEPS blanks because it reports what the user CONFIGURED and the env viewer has to show the blank row that is doing the cancelling. Pick by question: "what did they type?" versus "what does the process actually get?".
- **Suggesting an env-var NAME:** `suggestEnvVarKeys()`, `PROVIDER_ENV_VAR_SUGGESTIONS`, `COMMON_ENV_VAR_SUGGESTIONS`, `rememberableEnvVarKeys()` in `src/shared/envVarCatalog.ts`, rendered by `<EnvVarKeyInput>` in `src/renderer/components/shared/EnvVarKeyInput.tsx` and fed by `useKnownEnvVarKeys()` (`agents:getKnownEnvVarKeys`). A mistyped name fails SILENTLY - `CLAUDE_HOME` is set, the CLI ignores it, and the agent runs as if nothing was configured - so the name field offers the provider's own vars plus every name the user has already set anywhere in Maestro. Two rules if you extend it. **Add a provider by adding one entry to `PROVIDER_ENV_VAR_SUGGESTIONS`**, not by special-casing an editor: both the per-agent panel (`AgentConfigPanel`) and Settings -> Environment (`EnvVarsEditor`) read the same function, and the global editor deliberately offers EVERY provider's catalog because it has no provider of its own. And **remembered names are derived, never stored** - the handler reads the keys already on disk in the agent configs, the sessions, and `shellEnvVars`, skipping blank-valued rows so a half-finished `VAR` row from the Add Variable button is never suggested back. Nothing new is persisted, and no VALUE ever crosses the IPC boundary, which is what keeps a remembered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from becoming a second copy of a credential. A new row is added with NO name at all (`withBlankEnvVarRow()` / `BLANK_ENV_VAR_KEY`, the one place that rule lives, replacing six copies of a `NEW_VAR_n` placeholder loop): a placeholder name is something the user has to select and delete, and it also filters the suggestion list down to nothing, so the field would open on an empty list. The editor autofocuses that row and opens the list, keyed on an explicit "user pressed Add" flag rather than on "this row is blank", so a blank row restored from disk never steals the caret on modal open. The unnamed row lives in the same record as the real ones, so every spawn path drops it via `isBlankEnvKey()` in `agentEnvironment.ts` - `env[''] = 'x'` is a variable no child can read, and Windows rejects the empty name outright.
- **`.env` text for an env-var editor (Advanced mode):** `parseDotEnv(text, { parked?, validateValue? })` / `serializeDotEnv(vars, disabledVars?)` / `envVarRecordsFingerprint()` in `src/shared/dotEnv.ts`, with the one validator both editing modes share beside them (`validateEnvVarEntry()`, `validateEnvVarName()`, `validateEnvVarValue()`, `ENV_VAR_NAME_PATTERN`), drawn by `<EnvVarsDotEnvInput>` + `<EnvVarsModeToggle>` in `src/renderer/components/shared/EnvVarsDotEnvInput.tsx`. Both env-var editors (`Settings/EnvVarsEditor` and the per-agent rows in `AgentConfigPanel`) have a Basic mode (a row per variable) and an Advanced mode (one textarea of `KEY=value` lines) over the SAME two records, so what a line means is decided once, in the parser. **Quotes are kept verbatim**: the row editor stores `"a b"` with its quotes and its special-character rule passes a value BECAUSE it starts with one, so a parser that unquoted the way real dotenv does would make the two modes disagree about the value of one variable and then fail that rule on the way back in. Do NOT "fix" it into a standards-compliant dotenv parser. `# KEY=value` is a PARKED variable only when the caller passes `parked` (it has somewhere to keep one); every other comment and blank line is dropped, because the records have nowhere to store it. A quote left open that closes on a LATER line is reported once and the lines it swallowed are skipped, since the `=` padding at the end of a PEM body would otherwise be committed as a variable named after a base64 chunk. `validateValue` is whatever the host's ROW mode enforces, not a fixed rule: the Settings editor validates values and the per-agent rows do not, and a text mode stricter than its own rows silently drops a variable the rows already hold. The textarea owns its text while the user types and rebuilds it only when `envVarRecordsFingerprint()` says the records changed somewhere ELSE, which is what keeps comments and a half-typed line on screen while the user's own edit echoes back. `AgentConfigPanel` takes the text mode through `onEnvVarsReplace` (both records, replaced whole) rather than its per-row callbacks: several of those fired in one tick each rebuild the record from the same stale copy, so only the last would survive a paste. Render the gutter AFTER the textarea in JSX (see `TextareaLineNumbers` below). The mode is one remembered preference for every editor (`ENV_VARS_ADVANCED_MODE_STORAGE_KEY`, via `usePersistedToggle`). Do NOT add a second `KEY=value` line parser for a new editor.
- **Assembling the prompt, arguments and launch request of a chat turn:** `assembleTurn(agent, tab, message, context)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/assemble.ts`, over `buildMessagePrompt()` / `appendNudgeMessage()` / `prependMergedContext()` / `expandCommandArguments()` / `resolveSlashCommand()` / `buildAgentSystemPrompt()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/prompt.ts`, `buildTurnArgs()` / `applySystemPromptDelivery()` / `applyCopilotPreamble()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/args.ts`, `loadTurnContext()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/context.ts` (the reads), `toTurnAgent()` / `toTurnTab()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/records.ts`, and the prompt loader in `src/shared/maestro-lib/prompts/load.ts` (`resolvePromptDirectives()`, `createPromptLoader()`, `bundledPromptCandidates()`). A desktop turn is shaped by about thirty inputs in three processes (the renderer builds the user and system prompts, main builds arguments and environment, the process manager places the prompt), and a headless client needs the same text, so the pure rules live here and the desktop calls them: `useInputProcessing`, `agentStore.processQueuedItem`, `prepareMaestroSystemPrompt`, `handleProcessSpawn`, `prompt-manager` and the CLI prompt loader are all callers now. Each function reproduces the desktop BYTE FOR BYTE, quirks included (F5 image-only test after the nudge, F6 the deprecated agent-level session id, F10 read-only not handed to the overrides, a `$&` in command arguments), and `src/__tests__/renderer/turns/assemble-parity.test.ts` proves it by driving the real renderer path into the real `handleProcessSpawn` and comparing system prompt, user prompt, final prompt, argument list and environment with `assembleTurn`. A rule that looks wrong is wrong in both places: fix it once for both surfaces, never in one. `assembleTurn` is PURE (no I/O, no clock beyond `context.now`, no `process.env`); everything it reads is gathered by `loadTurnContext` once per turn, so a prompt or setting edited in the desktop applies to the next headless client turn. It applies the composer's rules to every message (the desktop applies the nudge, new-session message and read-only instruction only on a composer direct send, F1), launches with `surface: 'desktop'`, and leaves everything with a side effect to the caller: the Windows temp file for the `file` delivery, SSH wrapping, the Claude token source, image placement. The prompt directives are one implementation (`{{REF:}}` is the absolute path of the BUNDLED file, resolved on the top-level text only; `{{INCLUDE:}}` is customization-aware, depth 3, with a cycle check); a headless client is an ES module with no `__dirname`, so the bundled directory is probed from `moduleDirectory` (the entry script's directory by default). Do NOT hand-build another system prompt, nudge layering, or `--append-system-prompt` placement at a new spawn site. The system prompt itself is `assembleMaestroSystemPrompt()` in `src/shared/maestroSystemPrompt.ts` on every surface; `buildAgentSystemPrompt()` only gathers the library's inputs for it, and delivery goes through `deliverSystemPrompt()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/args.ts`, which main's `applySystemPromptDelivery()` wraps.
- **Running a chat turn with no desktop (send, queue, stop, per-tab events):** `createRuntimeTurns()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/turns.ts`, which the in-process `MaestroClient` (`createMaestroRuntime`) answers `turns.*` through, over `createTurnEventMapper()` (`turns/turn-events.ts`) and the repository commands `beginTurn()` / `recordTabSession()`. It joins the queue, `loadTurnContext`, `assembleTurn`, `runAgentTurn`, and the recorder in one order, and holds three rules no caller should re-derive: a message that cannot start stays in the queue held (never dropped, never in the transcript, `send` reports why); the message is written to the transcript only once the process exists (`beginTurn`, one write that also names the owning provider and spends a merged context), so the recorder is told `userEntryWritten`; and the session id is written to the slot of the provider the turn was SENT under (`recordTabSession` over `updateProviderSlot`), which is what makes swap-and-back resume the right conversation. `outcome` is the last event of a turn and follows the records. Answer text is said once (streamed partials win; the result that repeats them is dropped). Shutdown stops the turns and lets them finish recording BEFORE the repository drains and the lock is released. A host hands the runtime its SQLite loader, CLI script and `maestro-p` through `resolveRuntimeTurnOptions()`; `loadBetterSqlite3()` now lives in `src/shared/maestro-lib/store/native-sqlite.ts` (re-exported at `src/cli/utils/native-sqlite.ts`). Do NOT add another spawn path or a second queue for a library client. See `Plans/maestro-tui-prompt-assembly.md` section 13.
- **Recording a finished turn (transcript, History, usage):** `createTurnRecorder()` / `buildTurnTranscript()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/turns/record-turn.ts`, over `createHistoryWriter()` (`turns/history.ts`), `createStatsRecorder()` (`turns/stats.ts`) and `AgentRepository.appendTranscript()`. The row is the desktop's own (`INSERT_QUERY_EVENT_SQL`, `bindQueryEvent()`, `generateId()` and `normalizePath()` now live in `src/shared/maestro-lib/stats/`, with shims at the old `src/main/stats/` paths), and the answer summary is `summarizeAnswer()` in `turns/summary.ts`, shared with the exit toast. The stats recorder only INSERTS into a `stats.db` the desktop created and migrated, and the History writer refuses a legacy `.json` file; both skip with a logged reason rather than fail a turn. Do NOT hand-roll another `query_events` insert or `history/<id>.jsonl` append. See `Plans/maestro-tui-prompt-assembly.md` section 12.
- **Serving a runtime to other processes (`maestro-cli host`):** `startRuntimeServer()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/server.ts`, over `createRequestHandler()` (`server-requests.ts`) and `framesForEvent()` / `progressToWire()` (`server-frames.ts`), run by `startRuntimeHost()` in `src/cli/services/runtime-host.ts` and driven by `src/cli/commands/host.ts` (`start` detaches the bundle with `--foreground`, `status` and `stop` ride `requestHostStatus()` / `requestHostStop()` in `client/host-control.ts`). The server speaks the DESKTOP bridge's messages, not a protocol of its own: `createWsMaestroClient` attaches to a headless host exactly as to a desktop, and `src/cli/commands/status.ts` and the rest of the CLI reach it unchanged. The two directions are written against the table in `Plans/maestro-tui-client-api.md` section 5, so a new `MaestroClient` method needs its message added to BOTH the client (`ws-client.ts`) and the handler. Rules the module holds: loopback only; the token is the URL path and `x-maestro-cli-secret` must also match (a detached host has no session cookie to fall back on); any request carrying an `Origin` is refused; nothing resumes (`connected` says `resumed: false`, so a client re-reads the snapshot); a message it does not know is `echo`ed, which a client reads as unsupported; `start_group_chat`, `get_group_chats`, `send_group_chat_message`, `stop_group_chat`, `cross_agent_ask` and the `groupChat:rename` / `groupChat:delete` invoke channels are answered by the library's group chat and consult services (`runtime/group-chats.ts`, `runtime/consults.ts`), and `host status` / `host stop` count group chat rounds and consults beside turns and runs (`HostWork.rounds`, `HostWork.consults`). `cli-server.json` is published LAST and unpublished FIRST, written owner-only by `writeCliServerInfoTo()` with `hostKind: 'headless'` (the label `headless pid N`), and removed only while it still names this pid (`deleteCliServerInfoFrom(dir, pid)`). `stop` refuses while `turnsInFlight()` or `runs.activeRuns()` is non-empty unless forced. Cue inside the host is `startHostCue()` (`host-cue.ts`) over the standalone engine and the existing Cue lock, never a second engine. Do NOT add a second socket server for a library client, and do NOT give `status` a signal-based path: a signal cannot say what is in flight.

## Providers, accounts, and auth

Agent-wide translation uses the same transaction over open `aiTabs` only. `translationSessionTabs()` still enumerates the full inventory for ownership and parking, not eager extraction. Closed, unified-closed, snoozed/grouped, orphaned and History-only identities go into `TranslationPlan.deferredTabs`; commit parks their source IDs and retains `pendingTranslation` for the next send. Reopening, waking or resolving History must not translate. See the [translation contract](../../PROVIDER-SUPPORT.md#tab-scope-and-translate-at-send) for the shared send path.

- **Provider translation filesystems:** `TranslationFs` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/fs-port.ts` and `localTranslationFs` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/local-fs.ts` own native file access for service planning, adapters, memory/resource discovery, projection guards, readback and rollback. Pass the same `nativeFs` through the service host, transaction host and stage context; omitted ports retain local behavior. `TranslationArchive.archiveFs` is fixed to the local implementation, even when native files use another transport; its process lock also stays local. Reuse the canonical atomic writer through `atomicWrite`. Exclusive creation reports `EEXIST` without touching an existing path, including a dangling symlink. Its `onCreated` callback journals proven ownership before the local payload write; a failed or unacknowledged remote creation must never manufacture ownership or be retried blindly. Bytes remain buffers, recursive listings do not follow directory links, and errors retain errno codes. `assertTranslationTarget()` and the asynchronous overload of `assertTranslationKnowledgeDirectory()` must inspect the selected native port, never local paths on behalf of a remote. The synchronous local launch binding retains the read-only `localTranslationFsSync` facade; its asynchronous overload reads the selected native port. `setMaestroLibSshTranslationFs()` / `resolveSshTranslationFs()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/host.ts` carry the host adapter into the shared SSH wrapper. The desktop/CLI shim registers a lazy `createSshTranslationFs()` factory; standalone hosts must register a factory or remote knowledge launches fail before spawning. No shared code imports main and no second SSH filesystem is introduced. Direct Node filesystem imports are limited to the local port and archive by a source-boundary regression test. The optional `paths` API follows the native host: SSH uses POSIX rules even on Windows, including literal backslashes in names. Native guards, discovery, staging and readback use those rules; archive paths always stay local.

- **SSH provider translation:** `createSshTranslationFs()` in `src/main/provider-translation-fs.ts` adapts only the canonical remote-fs primitives. It preserves binary bytes and errno, follows links for stat but not lstat/listing, and acknowledges exclusive ownership only after a successful transport result. No retry or deletion follows an uncertain create. `resolveSshTranslationHost()` in `src/main/provider-translation-ssh.ts` resolves the launch target before any probe and throws `sshUnresolvedRemoteMessage()` for missing or disabled targets. The desktop uses static provider definitions, never local detection, for remote binary names. Version probes honor custom paths/args and the remote launch environment; only remote HOME, CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR and CODEX_HOME are read. Account selection reuses `resolveAgentAccountKey`, memory discovery reuses the native port, and git queries use `execGitReadOnly` with the resolved remote. Each request gets an isolated native host so concurrent agents cannot cross filesystems. Identity includes the remote config fingerprint and home. Status reads the local archive even when SSH is unavailable. Planning fails with named missing-tool errors. Its `openCodexConnection` uses the same provider invocation settings as the identity probe, pins the destination CODEX_HOME, verifies the remote config identity and spawns through `wrapSpawnWithSsh`. Only the local SSH client inherits the local environment; provider configuration crosses through the wrapper. An unresolved wrapper or stdin bootstrap is refused before spawning. Apply, recovery and rollback validate the journal destination against the resolved SSH config and current remote home, then check the knowledge root for symlink escapes. Managed knowledge uses the remote native port; archives and journals remain local. This removes the temporary managed-knowledge gate, not the requirement for native acceptance evidence. Remote prerequisites are supported provider binaries, POSIX `sh`, `base64 -d`, `realpath` or working `readlink -f`, Git and the GNU/BSD filesystem tools listed in the [SSH translation contract](../../PROVIDER-SUPPORT.md#ssh-translation-boundaries-and-prerequisites). The 2026-10-17 SSH live check was explicitly skipped; both directions remain unverified and native SSH certification remains deferred without live evidence.

- **Codex translation import connection:** `openCodexTranslationConnection()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/codex/connection.ts` owns the existing `CodexAppServerRpc`, line framing, no-approval server responses, account-root handshake and child cleanup. A host may supply a pipe-backed child through the `spawn` option, while `env.CODEX_HOME` still names the expected provider account. Desktop SSH translation supplies that child through `TranslationNativeHost.openCodexConnection`; the service refuses remote apply without this channel instead of selecting a local binary. EOF, child exit/close and pipe errors reject pending RPCs without replay. Staging reads durable rollout bytes through the same native filesystem port, and the local archive records mutation intent before thread creation and injection. Rollback preserves Codex-owned rollout/index pairs after uncertain imports. Normal SSH turns keep their existing `codex exec` gate.

- **Single-tab provider translation:** `planTabTranslation()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/registry.ts` and `applyTabTranslation()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/transaction.ts` parameterize the existing planner and transaction through `TranslationPlan.tabId`. Pass the same ID to `commitTranslationSession()` and `translationSessionTabs()` in `translation/session.ts`; no separate transaction or parking implementation. Only that conversation is extracted, staged, read back and committed. Other tabs, provider defaults and queued snapshots stay intact. A destination that is not yet active stays parked; a pending tab with one parked source fills its live destination slot. Ambiguous parked sources require selection. Checkpoints merge the selected conversation and retain all native lineage keys. The checkpoint operation ID rejects stale plans; its content-addressed resource import reuses the prior operation's managed knowledge after readback, while native projections already matching their hashes remain untouched. Recovery and rollback use the same scoped journal and never delete reused knowledge. `TranslationRequest.tabId` travels over the existing IPC/preload/web/client path and survives deferred reloads.
- **Showing a committed provider translation:** `ContextTranslationRecord` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/types.ts`; `<ContextTranslationCard>` in `src/renderer/components/ContextTranslationCard.tsx` . `commitTranslationSession()` records one marker per operation and tab from a matching ready plan. Preserve it as a standalone transcript entry, including accepted limitations and verification status.

- **Provider translation knowledge scope:** `translationKnowledgeRoot()`, `resolveTranslationKnowledgeBinding()`, `assertTranslationKnowledgeDirectory()` and `bindTranslationKnowledge()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/knowledge-binding.ts` validate exactly `<host-root>/<64-hex-agent-hash>/<uuid>` and reject symlinked agent/operation directories and index files before embedding content. The host supplies local `<userData>/provider-knowledge` or remote `<HOME>/.maestro/provider-knowledge`, selected by `translationKnowledgeRoot()` from the probed native identity. Local launches keep the `translationKnowledgeRoot` override for library hosts. `MAESTRO_TRANSLATION_KNOWLEDGE_REMOTE` binds a remote directory to the saved SSH config fingerprint and home; local launches reject it, and SSH launches reject a missing or mismatched binding. The SSH wrapper checks that home against the remote login home and validates the root before reading the index through the port. It embeds the same prompt envelope on every continuation and sends knowledge content through the existing stdin script transport, including small indexes. Claude receives native POSIX `--add-dir` and memory settings on SSH. Index rendering also uses native path rules on Windows. Never derive the trusted root from the knowledge-directory environment variable. `translationWriteRoot()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/transaction.ts` selects the root for staged writes, readback and rollback, tying knowledge paths to the journal's agent and operation. Only recovery/rollback permits the exact legacy `<project>/.maestro/provider-knowledge/<agent-hash>/<operation-id>` directory; new writes and launch bindings never use that fallback. Reuse `assertTranslationTarget()` for resource-file containment and the existing provider additional-directory argument builder for Claude access. Native project files require `TranslationScope.projectResourcesIntoRepo`; `planResourceProjections()` reports omitted paths as info by default. The transaction host resolves git metadata with `execGitReadOnly()` and supplies `resolveProjectGit()`. `translationWriteRoot()` validates the exact common-directory `info/exclude` path as well as ordinary resource roots. Exclude additions use an operation-marked block in the same resource-write journal; rollback removes only that block and preserves other edits. Git metadata locks serialize worktrees sharing the common directory.

- **Provider translation context window:** `activeContextRecords(records)` and `formatTranslationRecordCounts(tab)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/context-window.ts` share compaction-window selection and plan copy across native projection, capacity checks, planning, Edit Agent and CLI. Only `window: 'archived'` is excluded; legacy records without a window remain active. Per-tab `records` stays the total retained evidence count, while `activeRecords` and `archivedRecords` split it. Saved plans without these counts display every record as carried. The module is renderer-safe and uses the canonical `formatCount()`. Never filter the archive or checkpoint down to this window: reconciliation needs all origins to prevent return-hop duplication.

- **Translating context before a send:** `prepareTabForSend(tabId, provider, host)` and `pendingTranslationSource()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/send.ts` are the desktop/runtime gate before consuming a prompt or constructing resume arguments. `pendingTranslation` names the source whose latest context must be imported even when an older destination slot exists. Without that marker, an existing destination slot or a fresh tab passes without extraction; one parked source is translated through the single-tab transaction. Ambiguous sources, new warnings and blocks raise `TranslationSendHeldError`; a live source defers without interruption. Warning acceptance matches the direction, both CLI versions and complete warning entries through `translationWarningsAccepted()`; info needs no acceptance. Callers retain the original message and provider/model/effort snapshot, then reload the committed native ID for resume. `prepareProviderTranslationForSend()` in `src/renderer/services/providerTranslation.ts` wraps the gate for direct desktop sends and queue processing, adds the existing translation IPC and progress text, and sends a `review-translation` toast click action for that tab. Remote pending sends use `enqueuePromptForTab()` to reach the same gate. `NativeTranslationService` in `translation/service.ts` owns native planning, archives, journals, readback, acceptance and recovery; the desktop service injects Electron dependencies, and `createRuntimeTranslationService()` in `runtime/translation.ts` supplies repository/process-registry dependencies. Runtime writes use the repository's serialized, fenced `updateTranslation()`. Never copy the native coordinator or consume a queued prompt before this gate succeeds.

- **Finding a Codex native transcript:** `findCodexSessionFile(sessionId, accountRoot)`, `listCodexSessionFiles(sessionsDir)` and `extractSessionIdFromFilename()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/codex/session-files.ts` own Codex's dated native transcript lookup. `CodexSessionStorage` delegates to them, and the headless translation host uses the same finder. They preserve UUID parsing and the existing year/month/day walk; unreadable roots and individual entries retain the existing storage skip behavior. Pass the selected provider account root rather than assuming the default home. Do not introduce a second recursive transcript scanner.

- **Provider translation acceptance:** `limitationsNeedingAcceptance(limitations)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/limitations.ts` (also exported from `translation/index.ts`) selects only `warning` entries. Main, renderer, CLI and transaction gates share this rule: `info` entries need no consent; `block` entries determine plan status and cannot be accepted. Deferred acceptance stores these warnings with `canonicalJson()` and compares their full values, so added or changed notes never force re-review but a new or changed warning does. `translationWarningsAccepted(plan, state)` reads `ProviderMigrationState.acceptedDirections`, recorded after the transaction's consent gate, and requires matching source/destination providers, CLI versions and complete warning values. Consent survives subsequent migrations; it never carries across versions or directions. Renderer consumers import this module directly, since the translation barrel includes Node code. Do not gate on all limitations or compare only warning codes.

- **Provider translation version compatibility:** `translationVersionLimitation(provider, version, support)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/support.ts`, over `TranslationVersionSupport` (`minVersion`, `testedVersions`) and the existing `semver` dependency. The translation registry, adapter preflight and isolated acceptance driver share this rule. Invalid semantic versions and versions below the minimum return an `unknown-version` block; supported versions outside the exact tested list return an `untested-version` warning naming installed and tested versions. Tested versions return no limitation. Directional native certification applies only to its tested version pair; compatibility does not certify native resume. Do not coerce malformed versions or strip prerelease/build tags to make an installed version appear tested. Existing record extraction blocks remain the schema-drift guard.

- **Claude Code's memory folder for a project:** `resolveClaudeMemoryPath(projectPath, claudeConfigDir)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/memory.ts`, with the config dir from `resolveConfigDirKeyFromEnv(env)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/launch/interactive-mode.ts`. Claude keeps auto memory OUTSIDE the project, at `<config dir>/projects/<encoded path>/memory`, keyed by `encodeClaudeProjectPath()` of the resolved path it was started in. The config dir is `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when set (multi-account setups point it at `~/.claude-work` and so on; a blank value means unset and a leading `~/` is expanded), else `~/.claude`. The Memory Viewer (`getMemoryDirectoryPath`), provider translation and Cue bundle export/import all build the path through `resolveClaudeMemoryPath`; do NOT join 'projects', encoded, 'memory' by hand again.
- **Discovering installed Codex skills:** `discoverCodexSkills(command, cwd, env)` in `src/main/agents/codex-skills.ts` asks Codex's `skills/list` for enabled skills, including plugins and system skills that legacy directory scans missed. Resolve the session's binary and effective environment before calling it so discovery matches the account used by agent turns. The helper starts only a metadata process, bounds its requests, and terminates the process before settling. Returned prompts preserve native `$name` invocation and the skill's resource path. Preserve an authoritative empty result instead of reintroducing disabled skills from disk; callers may fall back for unsupported CLIs and transport failures, while reporting unexpected errors.
- **Agent display name:** `getAgentDisplayName()` in `src/shared/agentMetadata.ts`
- **Two-letter labels for a set of agents:** `buildAgentInitials(agents)` and `agentInitialCandidates(name)` in `src/shared/agentInitials.ts`. The collapsed Left Bar (`SkinnySidebar`, behind the `showLeftPanelCollapsedInitials` setting) draws each agent as a two-letter badge, and the label is the ONLY thing telling two agents apart there, so it is chosen for the SET rather than one name at a time: every agent gets a ranked candidate list (a bracketed tag the user wrote, then word initials, then the first word's own letters, then a digit) and collisions are resolved across the whole list. Two passes on purpose - an agent whose first choice nobody else wants keeps it outright, so a collision between two other agents can never rename a bystander - and within a collision the agent listed first wins. Labels compare case-insensitively (`Ma` and `MA` are one label to a reader), and casing carries meaning: `MR` is two words, `Ma` is one. Feed it the WHOLE agent list, never a filtered view, or toggling a filter renames agents the user has already learned. Distinct from `abbreviateGroupName()` in `formatters.ts`, which shortens ONE name to a variable-length badge and knows nothing about its neighbors - do NOT take `name.slice(0, 2)` at a new call site, which is exactly the version that labels `Maestro` and `Marketing` identically.
- **Which providers a user can pick:** `AGENT_PICKER_META` / `PICKABLE_AGENT_IDS` / `getAgentPickerMeta()` in `src/shared/agentMetadata.ts`. ONE registry feeds all three provider pickers: the New Agent modal (`SUPPORTED_AGENTS` re-exports it), the New Agent Wizard's tile strip (`AGENT_TILES` is derived from it), and the Group Chat moderator dropdown (the same tiles, filtered by what detection found). Do NOT hand-write a fourth list of agent ids for a new picker: the three used to be written by hand, and Grok and Qwen3 Coder shipped selectable in the New Agent modal while being absent from the wizard and un-pickable as a moderator. The record is keyed by `AgentId`, so a new id fails to compile until it is either given metadata or explicitly set to `null` (internal or unshipped). **Order is derived, not authored:** `PICKABLE_AGENT_IDS` sorts by display name, so every picker shows one predictable alphabetical list and a new entry can go anywhere in the record. Do NOT re-sort the record by hand to move a provider, and do NOT default a picker to the first entry - that hands a fresh Group Chat to whichever beta provider sorts first. Auto-selection reads `AGENT_AUTOSELECT_ORDER` (a preference list) and picks the first entry the user actually has installed. Adding a provider also means a `case` in `AgentLogo` and a glyph in `AGENT_ICONS`, or the tile draws a blank fallback ring. What a picker SHOWS is narrowed again at render time by detection - see `filterToAvailableProviders` below - so a provider missing from a picker may simply not be installed on that machine. Full checklist: [PROVIDER-SUPPORT.md → Step 2.6](../../PROVIDER-SUPPORT.md#step-26-register-the-provider-in-the-pickers).
- **"Show only providers this machine can run":** `filterToAvailableProviders()` / `providerLocationLabel()` in `src/renderer/utils/providerAvailability.ts`, rendered by `<ProviderAvailabilityBar>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/ProviderAvailabilityBar.tsx`. Both provider pickers (the wizard's tile strip, the New Agent modal's list) offer one toggle between "everything supported" and "everything this machine can run", and the rules live in `shared` code so the two cannot drift on what "available" means. **Which way the toggle STARTS is per-picker and deliberately differs:** the New Agent modal opens filtered (the everyday path, whose user knows what they installed), the wizard opens on the full list (a first-run screen, where someone whose provider is installed but undetected would otherwise conclude Maestro does not support it). Three invariants: the count describes ALL supported providers, never the filtered rows (a count that shrank with the list reads "4 of 4" and answers nothing); filtering down to nothing falls back to the FULL list (an empty picker has no row to reach per-provider settings through, so a binary in a non-standard place becomes unreachable); and the SELECTED provider survives the filter (duplicating an agent whose provider is missing would otherwise hide the row showing what is selected). Say `providerLocationLabel(remoteHost)` rather than hard-coding "locally" - both pickers can point at an SSH remote, and the counts describe whichever host was probed. Flipping the filter renumbers the list, so carry the focus ring by PROVIDER, not by index. It also changes the tile COUNT, and in the wizard that changes the layout: `resolveAgentGridLayout()` (`Wizard/screens/AgentSelectionScreen/utils/agentGridLayout.ts`) draws a centered wrapping block whenever the tiles fit in two rows and only falls back to the scrolling strip past that, with rows BALANCED (five tiles are 3 + 2, never 4 + 1) and the column count reported upward so arrow-key movement steps by the layout actually drawn. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Two Shapes for One Tile Set](UI-PATTERNS.md#two-shapes-for-one-tile-set-agentgridlayout).
- **Which provider account an agent runs as:** `resolveAgentAccountKey(toolType, env, homeDir)`, `providerProfileKey()` / `parseProviderProfileKey()`, and `providerProfileLabel()` / `providerProfileShortLabel()` in `src/shared/providerProfiles.ts`, with the renderer-side per-agent index in `useProviderProfiles(sessions)` (`src/renderer/hooks/stats/useProviderProfiles.ts`). `toolType` alone stops being the identity the moment a second account is in play: three Claude agents can be three plans with three quota buckets, and only `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (or `CODEX_HOME`) separates them. `PROVIDER_PROFILE_CONFIGS` is the ONE place that says which providers split by account and what env var does it - the quota panels used to carry their own copy of the pair, which is how a badge's count and a filter's count get to disagree. The effective env is `effectiveAgentCustomEnvVars(sessionEnv, providerEnv)`: the session's own `customEnvVars` REPLACE the agent-level set, they do not layer over it, matching `applyAgentConfigOverrides()` and the CLI's `resolveAgentOverrides()` (a layered merge filed an agent that set only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` under a provider-level config dir its process never sees). An API key, gateway, or cloud provider outranks the config dir's login: `resolveAgentBillingCredential(toolType, env)` names it (an API key by its last 4 characters, never the secret), `resolveAgentProfile(toolType, env, homeDir)` files that agent under its own credential profile, and the quota panels do not count it against any plan. An SSH-remote agent's config dir is a path on THAT host holding THAT host's login, so `resolveAgentProfile()` takes the remote and files it under `account @ host` - never the local account of the same directory name, which one real setup had logged into a different account. The quota panels drop remote agents from their counts for the same reason, so a chip's "N agents" and the grid it opens are still the same number. A credential is the same account wherever it is presented, so credential profiles are NOT split by host. `useProviderProfiles().ready` is false until the agent-level env fetch settles; do not clear a profile filter whose key is missing before then, because it may be late rather than gone. Global env vars are deliberately not consulted, since a machine-wide config dir moves every agent at once and is not a per-agent attribution. Resolution returns `null`, and the agent is left unattributed, when $HOME has not resolved and the agent named no dir - guessing the default would file it under an account it may not belong to, and it self-corrects on the next render. `makeAccountKeyHelpers(prefix)` (the `.claude-smash` -> `smash` naming) lives here too and is re-exported from `quota/quotaFormatting.ts` for the panels. Do NOT re-derive an account from an env var at a new call site: the Agents grid's provider filter and the quota tabs' "N agents" chips are the same number by construction, and a click-through from one to the other lands on an empty grid the moment they drift. The index is what the Usage Dashboard's provider filter, the per-card account badge, and the `Provider` sort all read. A provider that keeps one credential store collapses to a single profile named after the provider, and an agent whose account has not resolved is ABSENT from `profileKeyBySessionId` rather than being given a placeholder key, so it drops out of an explicit account filter instead of being lumped into whichever one was picked. The startup usage sampler once probed a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the agent never receives, which is the layered-merge bug above in a second place.
- **Which account dirs exist for a provider (main):** `getProviderAccountDirs(toolType, options?)` in `src/main/agents/provider-account-dirs.ts`. The companion to `resolveAgentAccountKey()`: that one answers "which account is THIS agent on", this one answers "which accounts exist at all", which is what anything reading transcripts off disk needs. It is driven entirely by `PROVIDER_PROFILE_CONFIGS`, so a provider gains multi-account reads by gaining an entry there, not by gaining a discovery function here - there were already two (`discoverClaudeConfigDirs()`, `discoverCodexHomes()`), and both exist only because they gate on a provider-specific credential file (`.claude.json`, `auth.json`) before spawning a quota sample. Four sources in order: every live agent's effective env (authoritative, and the ONLY source that finds a dir outside `$HOME`), Maestro's own `process.env`, `~/<defaultSubdir>`, and a sibling scan of `$HOME` that requires the provider's sessions subdir to exist so an unrelated `.codex-notes` folder is not reported as an account. SSH-remote agents are skipped: their env var names a path on THAT host. The dedupe on `realpath(<dir>/<sessionsSubdir>)` is load-bearing, not defensive - a multi-account user who symlinks `~/.claude-work/projects` back at `~/.claude/projects` shares ONE transcript pool, and reading each dir blindly multiplies their reported tokens. Returns `[]` for a provider with no account concept, which callers read as "one pass against the default root"; pass the result to `listSessions(projectPath, sshConfig, accountDir)`, whose third argument is the account HOME (`~/.codex-work`), not the sessions subdir under it. Options exist to inject `homeDir` / `env` / `sessions` / `agentLevelEnvVars` for tests; production callers pass nothing and the stores are read lazily (cold stores yield no configured dirs rather than throwing).
- **Which account dir an agent runs under:** `resolveAgentAccountDir(agent, layers)` in `src/shared/agentAccountDir.ts`, wrapped for stored agents by `resolveAgentAccountDir(maestroSessionId)` in `src/main/agents/provider-account-dirs.ts`; the CLI's `list-sessions` calls the shared one with its own stores. Same precedence as `buildChildProcessEnv`: the agent's env (or the provider-level set, which replaces rather than layers), Settings -> Environment, the host env, then the default; an SSH agent uses only its custom value, unexpanded, since it names a remote path. The session browser passes `ownerAgentId` so `agentSessions:listPaginated` and `claude:getProjectStats` read that account's pool: claude resumes only from the config dir it started with, so a hardcoded `~/.claude` listed sessions the agent could not open. The per-project stats cache is keyed by `statsCacheScope()`, which stays unscoped when the account's `projects/` resolves to the default pool (a symlinked shared pool), so moving an agent onto an explicit config dir over the same pool never orphans its archived sessions. Reads by session id need no owner: `locateLocalSessionFile` in `claude-session-storage.ts` tries the default account, then every account dir.
- **Naming the account a login acts on:** `AccountPill` / `ghAccountLabel()` in `src/renderer/components/ui/AccountPill.tsx`, fed for gh by `parseGhActiveAccount()` in `src/main/utils/ghErrors.ts`. The provider re-auth dialog, the Feedback gh check, and the GitHub login dialog all act on ONE account out of possibly several, and the wrong one costs a round trip to discover, so each states it in the same accent pill. `parseGhActiveAccount` reads the `gh auth status` output the caller already ran (no extra call), picks the entry marked `Active account: true`, and names NOBODY when the active credential is an env token (`GH_TOKEN`): falling back to the inactive keyring account would put the wrong name on the pill. Do NOT hand-roll another `rounded-full` account span.
- **Whether a login flow can fix an auth failure:** `classifyCredentialKind()` and `credentialKindBlocksLogin()` in `src/shared/providerAuthIdentity.ts`, which also owns `providerAuthKey()` (the credential's shared scope: agent binary + host). NOT every credential is an OAuth login. An agent authenticating with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, one pointed at a gateway via `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, and one running on Bedrock/Vertex all fail with the SAME `auth_expired` output an expired login produces, and running the provider's login command for them produces a successful-looking flow that changes nothing the agent presents - the user believes it is fixed and the next prompt burns on the same rejection. So any surface that offers to sign the user in gates on this first (`ReauthModal` does), and reports the real remedy instead. Feed it the effective env from `resolveAgentEnvironment()`, never a partial one: a classification built from a different merge describes a process nobody is running. Related: an error `message` in `src/shared/agentErrorPatterns.ts` must name WHAT failed and never a terminal command, because the bank does not know which of these kinds produced the line.
- **Typing a login command into a shell:** `formatAgentLoginCommand(login, syntax?)` and `loginShellSyntaxFor(shellId, isWindows)` in `src/shared/agentMetadata.ts`. Rendering the line is not string concatenation, because the shell it lands in changes what runs. PowerShell parses a line that STARTS with a quoted string as an expression and echoes it, so `"C:\Program Files\Claude\claude.exe" /login` prints a path and logs nobody in; the call operator `&` is what makes it a command, and only PowerShell wants it - cmd.exe runs the quoted path directly. Windows agents installing under `C:\Program Files\...` makes this the common case there, not an edge case. So ask `loginShellSyntaxFor` for the dialect rather than sniffing the shell id at the call site: off Windows everything is `posix`, and ON Windows Git Bash and WSL are still `posix` while an unset or unknown shell falls back to `powershell`. Two related rules live with the caller (`ReauthModal`): the line is submitted with **CR, not LF** (ConPTY delivers LF as Ctrl+J, which PSReadLine ignores), and a Windows `wsl` default shell is replaced with `powershell`, because agents spawn as native processes and a login inside WSL writes credentials the agent never reads. An SSH remote is exempt from both - its shell is the remote host's.
- **Reading a Codex account's credentials off disk:** `readCodexAuth(codexHomeKey)` / `codexAuthHeaders(auth, extra?)` / `extractEmailFromJwt()` in `src/main/agents/codex-auth.ts`. `CODEX_HOME/auth.json` is the one place a Codex account's identity lives and two main-process callers need it - the quota sampler and the reset-credit primitives - so the read is shared rather than written twice. A second copy drifts first on the HEADER: a request without `ChatGPT-Account-Id` answers for the WRONG account when the login holds more than one workspace, and an EMPTY one is rejected outright rather than falling back, which is why the header is omitted rather than sent blank. It also keeps the two failure kinds apart, because their remedies differ - `missing_auth` means this CODEX_HOME was never logged in, `unauthenticated` means the file is there with no usable token. The JWT email is a LABEL only (signature deliberately unverified): the quota endpoint reports the email itself, but only while the network is up and the token is good, which is exactly when a fallback is not needed - decoding it is what lets an offline or rejected account still render under a name the user recognizes instead of a bare directory path.
- **Codex rate-limit reset credits:** types and spend rules in `src/shared/codexResetCredits.ts` (`describeCreditSpend`, `shouldAutoSpendCredit`, `nextCreditToSpend`, `spendableCredits`, `describeCreditExpiry`), the two HTTP primitives in `src/main/agents/codex-reset-credits.ts` (`fetchCodexResetCredits` READs `/wham/rate-limit-reset-credits`, `consumeCodexResetCredit` WRITEs `/wham/rate-limit-reset-credits/consume`), renderer state in `src/renderer/stores/codexResetCreditsStore.ts`, the automation in `src/renderer/services/codexAutoReset.ts`. OpenAI grants Codex accounts credits that reopen a consumed usage window on demand; Codex only exposes this in its TUI, so Maestro wraps it the way it wraps login. **The count is free** - `rate_limit_reset_credits: {available_count, applicable_available_count}` already rides the `/wham/usage` payload the sampler reads, so `CodexUsageSnapshot.resetCredits` costs no extra request and only a surface rendering the LIST pays for the detail read. **THE trap is that the two counts are different questions**: `available` is inventory, `applicable` is how many would take effect RIGHT NOW, and the API returns 0 there whenever no window is consumed enough for a reset to change anything - a real account read `available_count: 2, applicable_available_count: 0`. A credit spent then is simply gone, having reset nothing, which is why every surface goes through `describeCreditSpend()` rather than pairing `available` with an unconditional button, and why an ABSENT `applicable` must stay `undefined` rather than becoming 0 (unknown and zero produce different verdicts; coercing tells a user every credit they own is useless). **Three wire-format traps in `consume`**: `redeem_request_id` is REQUIRED (the endpoint 400s without it) and it is the idempotency key, so it belongs to one logical SPEND and must never be minted per HTTP attempt inside a retry loop; failure comes back as **HTTP 200** (`{"code":"no_credit","windows_reset":0}`), so `response.ok` alone reports every refusal as a success; and `windows_reset > 0` is the only proof anything happened, because the credit is gone either way and telling the user it worked is the one outcome they cannot check themselves. The AUTOMATIC path (`shouldAutoSpendCredit`) is strictly narrower than the manual one - it refuses `wasteful` AND `unknown`, where the button still offers a confirmed wasteful spend, because they are the user's credits - and it additionally refuses SSH-backed agents (the credits read here belong to THIS machine's account, not the remote's) and fires at most once per outage (keyed on `${sessionId}:${error.timestamp}` at MODULE scope, since the listener that calls it is re-registered whenever its effect re-runs and a ref rebuilt alongside would let one wall walk the whole balance one error at a time). Do NOT reach for `consumeCodexResetCredit` from a refresh, sweep, or retry default: every path to it is an explicit click or an explicitly enabled per-agent toggle.
- **Keeping a plan account on the Usage Dashboard after its agents leave:** `rememberQuotaAccounts(providerId, keys)`, `getRememberedQuotaAccountKeys()`, `pruneMissingQuotaAccounts()` in `src/main/stores/quotaAccountsStore.ts`, paired with `partitionSnapshotsByAge()` / `SNAPSHOT_RETENTION_MS` in `src/main/stores/usageSnapshotRetention.ts`. The dashboard's account list used to be derived entirely from live state: agents pointing at the account, an on-disk `~/.claude-*` / `~/.codex-*` sweep, and unexpired snapshots. The moment an account hits its limit the user moves every agent off it, so the first source is gone by definition; the sweep misses a symlinked dir, one outside $HOME, and one whose name trips the backup/scratch filter; and the snapshot expires 24h later. The account the user most needs to watch - the one they are waiting on a reset for - was the one that disappeared. Remembering is written by the SAMPLERS (`claude-usage-startup`, `codex-usage-startup`, and the interactive-replay write in `index.ts`), never by a filesystem guess, so a remembered key always means "an agent was configured against this account". The only thing that forgets one is its directory going missing: `pruneMissingQuotaAccounts` forgets on ENOENT/ENOTDIR and KEEPS an unreadable or unmounted dir, since a permissions blip must not erase the row. The retention helper is the other half: a snapshot stops being returned by `getSnapshot()` / `getAllSnapshots()` at the 24h TTL (nothing may DECIDE on a day-old quota reading) but stays on disk for 30 days and is served by `getRetainedSnapshots()` / `getRetainedCodexUsageSnapshots()`, which is what the dashboard IPC mirrors read - so the kept row still shows its last known bars, badged stale by `isSampleExpired()` in `quota/quotaFormatting.ts`. Do NOT widen `getAllSnapshots()` to cover the display case or re-add TTL deletion: the two clocks answer different questions, and collapsing them either lets a stale reading pick a spawn mode or deletes the row again.
- **Which provider generates a synopsis:** `AUTO_SYNOPSIS_PROVIDER` / `synopsisProviderChoice()` / `pickFirstAvailableProvider()` / `SYNOPSIS_PROVIDER_PREFERENCE` in `src/shared/directorNotesProvider.ts`, resolved to a concrete agent by `resolveSynopsisProvider()` in `src/main/utils/director-notes-provider.ts`. THREE surfaces start a synopsis - the desktop IPC handler, the web-server callback that the CLI drives, and (through it) `maestro-cli director-notes synopsis` - and before this they each hand-rolled `getAgent(provider)` plus their own "not available" copy, so a fourth caller would have written a fourth. `'auto'` is a SENTINEL ON THE WIRE, not a stored provider id: the renderer, CLI, and web client send the string and the MAIN PROCESS decides, because only it can see which agents are actually installed at the moment the run starts. That is the whole point - a conductor whose Claude OAuth expired still gets a synopsis from Codex without opening Settings. `DirectorNotesSettings` keeps the mode (`autoSelectProvider`) and the manual pick (`provider`) in SEPARATE fields, so turning auto off restores what the user last chose instead of snapping back to the first entry in the list; `undefined` counts as auto so installs predating the setting get the new behavior. An EXPLICIT provider that is unavailable is an error, never a silent fallback - the user named that agent, and quietly running somewhere else hides a broken install. Per-provider overrides (`customPath` / `customArgs` / `customEnvVars`) belong to the manual pick and are WITHHELD under auto: applying Claude Code's binary path to whichever agent auto lands on is worse than sending nothing. The preference order mirrors the wizard's `AGENT_TILES` but is redeclared in `shared/` because the main process cannot import renderer modules; `terminal` and the unsupported agents are absent on purpose, since a synopsis is a one-shot batch prompt. Do NOT add a second "first available" scan - the Feedback modal has its own older copy in `FeedbackChatView.tsx` and that one is not the canonical answer.
- **Parsing a Codex assistant directive:** `parseCodexDirective()`, `findCodexDirectives()`, `KNOWN_CODEX_DIRECTIVES` in `src/shared/codexDirectives.ts`. Codex writes structured annotations straight into its assistant markdown, so what arrives in a transcript is prose with machine-readable spans embedded in it - `:codex-followup[Label]{prompt="..."}`, `::code-comment{...}`, etc. The grammar is a wire format decided by OpenAI's bundled Codex plugin skills, and Maestro only reads it. **The allowlist is a hard requirement**: the grammar is permissive enough that ordinary prose parses as a directive - `a::before{...}` is a valid name, a flush brace, and attributes - so a scanner without an allowlist would find directives in text nobody wrote, and a caller that replaces `raw` would silently delete it. `parseCodexDirective(source, options?)` parses ONE directive from the start of a string and returns `null` on failure - the parser never returns a partial match, so a caller can replace exactly `raw` and leave the rest of the line intact. `findCodexDirectives(source, options?)` finds every KNOWN directive in a string with its offsets, in order, skipping unknown names entirely. Both accept an options object with `escaping` (how backslashes behave inside quoted values: `'backslash'` the default decodes `\"` and `\\`, `'literal'` ignores backslashes) and `budget` (char limit before giving up on a candidate, default 8192). **Two invariants that stop the parser from eating prose:** a failed parse returns `null` never a partial directive, and a directive is single-line - a newline inside a label, inside a quoted value, or where a separator is expected fails the parse. The `raw` field is the exact matched source from the first colon through the closing brace and no further, which is what lets a caller replace it blind without editing other text. A line scanned without an allowlist is vulnerable to `::git-create-pr{action=evil}` in prose appearing as live, so a safe integration always calls through `findCodexDirectives` and never through the lower-level parser alone. Do NOT hand-roll another directive parser - duplicates have already drifted on escaping, on single-line enforcement, and on the newline check. **Newer Codex builds use a SECOND grammar**, the content reference `U+E200 name U+E202 payload U+E201` (private-use delimiters, so an unaware reader draws boxes around raw JSON). `findCodexContentReferences()` parses it against its own allowlist, `KNOWN_CODEX_CONTENT_REFERENCES` (`visualize`, `cite`), and `findCodexDirectives()` merges both into one ordered list, dropping any colon directive that falls inside a reference's JSON payload. A JSON object payload yields its string fields as attributes; any other payload is kept whole as `ref` (the `cite` shape). The same two invariants hold: single-line, and a malformed reference returns nothing.
- **Rendering a Codex `visualize` reference inline:** `loadCodexVisualization()` in `src/main/codex-visualization.ts`, over `findVisualizeAssetsDir()` / `readVisualizeAssets()` / `buildCodexVisualizationDocument()`, reached through `window.maestro.codexVisualization.load` and drawn by `CodexVisualizationFrame` (`src/renderer/components/Markdown/components/CodexVisualizationFrame.tsx`). The document is wrapped the way the skill's own `render.py` does it, from the stylesheet, kit and host bridge in the AGENT'S OWN `CODEX_HOME` install: nothing of OpenAI's is shipped, and the assets track the plugin version the model was prompted against. It is registered on the Concerto `visualization` surface (`ConcertoHtmlSurface` in `src/shared/concerto-html.ts`), served under Codex's frame CSP (CDN allowlist, `connect-src` closed, sandbox `allow-scripts`). The frame is the host shell the bridge expects: it honors height up to `MAX_CODEX_VISUALIZATION_HEIGHT`, refuses state writes, opens https links only on a real click, and turns a follow-up into a composer PREFILL, never a send, because the text is agent-authored. Every failure (`remote`, `no-runtime`, `not-found`, `invalid`) falls back to the file chip; SSH agents always do, since the fragment and the Codex install live on the remote host.
- **Acting on a clicked Codex followup chip:** `requestCodexFollowup()`, `resolveCodexFollowup()`, `codexDirectivesLiveForSource()` and `CODEX_FOLLOWUP_EVENT` in `src/renderer/services/codexFollowup.ts`, drawn by `FollowupChip` (`src/renderer/components/Markdown/components/FollowupChip.tsx`) and answered by one `useEventListener` in `App.tsx`. A Codex agent ends a turn by OFFERING its next moves as directives inside its own markdown, and `LogItem.tsx` renders `<MarkdownRenderer>` at SIX call sites - so an `onFollowup` callback would be threaded through `MainPanel`, `TerminalOutput`, `LogItem`, `MarkdownRenderer`, `Markdown` and the component map purely as a conduit, and the next surface that renders assistant markdown would silently drop its chips. One app-level `CustomEvent` instead, the same shape as `requestHeadingPalette` and `requestOpenStagedImagesOrganizer`. Do NOT prop-drill it. Three rules ride it, and each guards a failure the user cannot undo. **The request's session and tab are CHECKED, never trusted:** a chip is only drawn in the tab on screen, so a mismatch means the store moved between the click and the handler, and the prompt is AGENT-authored - landing one in a conversation that never offered it is not recoverable, so a moved conversation degrades to a prefill plus a `notifyCenterFlash` saying why. That decision is `resolveCodexFollowup()`, a pure function rather than a branch inside the listener, because it is the consequential half and `App.tsx` is not a surface a test can reach - the listener is left as the event binding it should be. **The prefill goes through `loadAiDraft`, not `setInputValue`:** text, command mode and owning tab are one triple, so writing the text alone drops an agent prompt into a bang composer where Enter runs it as a shell command. **Both halves of the chip are agent-authored and nothing checks that they agree**, so the full prompt is always on the hover overlay AND on the `aria-label` (the overlay for a mouse, the label for a keyboard user and a screen reader, where `HoverTooltip` never fires) - a friendly label over a hostile prompt must not be able to hide it. An Alt-click prefills rather than sends, and an empty prompt renders the label as plain text, because a button that sends nothing reads as broken. Which BODIES get chips is `codexDirectivesLiveForSource()` (`stdout` / `ai` only, the pair `useAgentExitListener` treats as the agent's own output), applied by `LogItem`: a directive in a user message is text the user typed - handing a live push button to somebody who merely QUOTED the syntax is the worst failure in the feature - and one in the AI-command body, an error body, a thinking block or tool output describes the format rather than offering an action. It is separate from the provider check (`session.toolType === 'codex'`, applied in `TerminalOutput`), and both have to hold: the provider decides whether the syntax is a convention here at all, this decides whose words these are.
- **Selecting one tab's provider:** `resolveTabProvider(tab, agent)` and `switchTabProviderFor(tab, agent, providerOrInherit)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/agents/providerSwap.ts`. An absent `tabProvider` inherits the agent default; a named provider is an explicit pin even when equal to that default. The pure switch reuses `switchTabProvider()` in `translation/session.ts` to park native ID, usage, selected model (including Auto), effort and Run on. It restores and removes the destination's parked slot, retains the authoritative `pendingTranslation` source over stale destinations, and cancels an unsent round trip. It preserves transcript and captured turn fields, changes no agent settings and starts no native import or process. Same-provider pin changes affect inheritance metadata only. `resolveTurnProvider()` uses the captured owner first, then this selection resolver; `updateProviderSlot()` routes late writes using the same live-slot ownership. Agent-wide parking leaves explicitly pinned tabs alone. The Ensemble plugin, composer selector and provider selection commands have been removed. Preserve these pure ownership/parking helpers for native translation, reopening and existing saved tab pins. `useTabStore.setTabSettings(sessionId, tabId, settings?)` validates model, effort and Run on against the existing effective provider without changing that pin, rejects migration collisions and stale replies, and preserves native session state. Older `tab_provider` bridge requests are refused. Native preflight and warning acceptance still gate the next send. Renderer imports are re-exported through `providerTabSessions.ts`. Native readers use `resolveTabNativeProvider(tab, agent)` from the same module, then `projectTabProvider()` to read that owner's ID and usage. A pending translation names the latest source, even when the selected provider has an older native slot.
- **Inspecting tab providers:** `inspectTabProvider(tab, agent)`, `TabProviderInspection` and `TabConfigResult` in `src/shared/desktopTabs.ts` serve desktop tab lists, session history/detail, remote broadcasts, runtime projections and CLI responses. Effective `provider`, explicit `tabProvider`, default `agentProvider` and captured `turnProvider` stay separate; null turn ownership stays unknown. Effective model/effort resolve tab and matching-provider overrides, with null meaning the provider's configured default. Run on preserves explicit cloud mode. Inspection excludes environment values and parked native data, uses the canonical provider projection without walking sibling tabs, and remains truthful after removal of the Ensemble controls. Legacy `toolType` still names the agent default; `model` and `effort` still name tab overrides. Do not recreate this wire projection in each consumer.
- **Projecting one tab for launch:** `projectTabProvider(agent, tab, provider?)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/agents/providerSwap.ts` returns a launch-only agent and native tab slot. The default is the effective tab provider; a captured queue provider can override it. It reuses `providerOverridesFor()` and `switchTabProvider()`, clears outgoing provider-only overrides, gateway/proxy, transport and tier routing, and reads Run on from the chosen provider slot. Only the targeted tab is replaced in the projected agent's tab array. Saved agent identity, pins, sibling tabs and parked maps are unchanged. Never persist the projected agent or tab. Renderer and runtime sends share this projection; new queue captures freeze Run on and routing inputs. Late usage and native IDs use `updateProviderSlot()` and retain the latest pending native source. Auto settlement checks the pending turn identity and captured provider before applying a router answer. `turnSessionId` identifies the native session behind the captured cache hold; a translation replacement makes it cold.
- **Changing an agent's provider:** `switchAgentProvider(agent, newProvider)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/agents/providerSwap.ts` parks state for the headless runtime and for a plain desktop switch, which goes through `switchProviderWithoutConversion()` in `src/renderer/services/providerTranslation.ts` (Edit Agent's default and `update-agent --provider`). Converting memory and session history is opt-in and uses `requestProviderTranslation()` in the same file to migrate native conversations.
