Both paths share `switchTabProvider()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/session.ts` (re-exported by providerSwap), and both walk every AI tab an agent holds (open, orphaned, closed, snoozed) through `mapAgentAiTabs()` in that file; a switch that maps only `aiTabs` leaves a closed tab that resumes the old provider's session ID when reopened. `resolveTurnProvider()` and `updateProviderSlot()` in providerSwap keep late events attributed to the provider that started the turn, and are re-exported from `src/renderer/utils/providerTabSessions.ts`. The parking path retains transcripts and provider-specific overrides in `Session.providerOverrides`, over the keys in `PROVIDER_OVERRIDE_KEYS`; queued model/effort snapshots that cannot carry across providers are cleared and reported in `unparked`. The live fields belong to the active provider, and its parked slot must be absent. `providerOverridesFor(agent, provider)` reads the live or parked overrides. Add a provider-specific field to `ProviderAgentOverrides` and its key list together. Native translation requests sent to a headless host are refused without changing state; they require the desktop. Do not hand-roll another parking implementation.

## Agents (renderer state and navigation)

- **The record a new agent starts as:** `buildNewAgentRecord(input, { generateId, now? })` and `newAgentClaudeInteractive(toolType)` in `src/shared/newAgentRecord.ts`. The New Agent flow (`createNewSession` in `useSessionCrud.ts`), the CLI/web `create_session` handler (`useAppRemoteEventListeners.ts`) and the Cue bundle importer all build agents, and the first two used to carry two hand-copied 50-line literals that had already drifted (the remote copy had no `tabGroups`). The skeleton always holds exactly one idle AI tab that `activeTabId` and `unifiedTabOrder` point at: `restoreSession` (`useSessionRestoration.ts`) treats an agent with no tabs of any kind as corrupted data and replaces its tabs with a recovery tab, so an agent written off-desktop without one opens with a corruption warning. Callers spread their own fields on top (git probe, custom path/args/env/model, SSH, resilience flags). Runtime-agnostic, so the CLI can load it.
- **What a duplicated agent inherits:** `buildDuplicateAgentInput(source, overrides?)`, `defaultDuplicateAgentName(name)` and `duplicateAgentBlocker(source)` in `src/shared/newAgentRecord.ts`. The New Agent modal's Duplicate... prefill (`NewInstanceModal.tsx`) and `maestro-cli duplicate-agent` both build from it, so the copy a script makes is the copy an untouched modal submit makes. Before it existed the rules lived only in the modal's `loadAgents()` and `create-agent` could only approximate them by hand. It returns the RESOLVED create input, not form state: strings trimmed to `undefined`, Adaptive Mode as the source's explicit choice or the fresh-agent default (`isAdaptiveModeDefaultOn() || undefined`, so an explicit `false` survives and over SSH never reverts to the TUI default), `maestroPMode` / `maestroPPath` only when Adaptive Mode is on, both resilience flags through `resilienceEnabled()`, `codexAutoResetOnExhaustion` only when the source opted in (a copy must not start spending reset credits), and `codexTransport` as `'exec'` or `'app-server'`. Traps: the working directory of an SSH source is its `workingDirOverride` (a `create-agent --ssh-cwd` agent keeps a local placeholder in `cwd`), and the copy's override is ALWAYS its own working directory, never the source's stored one (that carry-over pinned the copy's terminals, git calls and file tree to the old remote directory). `syncHistory`, `shareHistoryToProjectDir`, `contextWindowSource`, `autoRunFolderPath`, worktree links, `isPianola`, `bookmarked` and every runtime field are NOT carried. `additionalDirectories` comes back cloned, because the modal edits rows in place. `duplicateAgentBlocker()` refuses only the Pianola manager; a worktree child duplicates as a standalone agent. Feed the result to `createNewSession()` or the `create_session` config, never spread it onto a record by hand.
- **Session lookup:** `selectActiveSession()`, `selectSessionById()` in `src/renderer/stores/sessionStore.ts`; `useActiveSession()` hook in `src/renderer/hooks/session/useActiveSession.ts`
- **Session mutation:** `updateSessionWith(sessionId, updater)` for a whole agent, plus one single-tab updater per tab type in `src/renderer/stores/sessionStore.ts` - `updateAiTab(sessionId, tabId, updater)`, `updateFileTab(sessionId, tabId, updater)`, `updateBrowserTab(sessionId, tabId, updater)`. Do NOT hand-roll `setSessions(prev => prev.map(...))` for a single-agent or single-tab patch; reach for the matching helper instead.
- **Which agent this client is looking at:** `persistActiveSessionId()` / `readPersistedActiveSessionId()` in `src/renderer/utils/activeSessionPersistence.ts`. Do NOT call `window.maestro.sessions.getActiveSessionId()` directly to restore focus: the desktop and every web-desktop browser tab share that one pointer, and a browser reloads on every refocus, so reading it landed the user on whatever the DESKTOP had focused rather than the agent they were working in. Web-desktop answers it from a ladder - `sessionStorage` (this browser TAB's own choice), then `localStorage` (the last choice made in this browser), then the shared value - because two tabs share an origin, so a localStorage-only answer just moves the bleed one level down. Writing is unchanged on both - the shared pointer is what plugin `session.activated` and the CLI's current-agent answer read, so a client that went quiet there would trade one bug for another.
- **Which client this page is, across a reload:** `getClientInstanceId()` / `isReloadedClientInstance()` in `src/renderer/utils/clientInstance.ts`. Renderer-owned work dies with its page while the processes it spawned keep running in main: an Auto Run loop is a live async closure, so a web-desktop tab the browser discarded and reloaded on refocus left its task finishing with nobody to start the next one (#1470). Handing that work back is only safe to the SAME tab, since any other client may still be running the loop and two loops spawn duplicate tasks into one working tree. The id is kept in sessionStorage (survives a reload, dies with the tab) and is reused only when `isReloadedPageLoad()` says this load replaced an earlier page (`PerformanceNavigationTiming.type === 'reload'` or `document.wasDiscarded`); a plain navigation mints a fresh one, because a duplicated tab copies its parent's sessionStorage while the parent is still alive. Main holds the other half: `AutoRunStateTracker` records the owner with the start claim, `takeOrphanedRuns(instanceId)` returns only that owner's runs, and any broadcast from the supposedly dead loop cancels the reclaim so the restart fails its claim instead of starting a second loop. Do NOT key ownership on a WebSocket connection (a phone drops its socket while the page lives on) or on localStorage (shared by every tab of the origin). On the renderer side, Auto Run records the id as the run's owner at `claimAutoRunStart`, and `useAutoRunReclaim` (`src/renderer/hooks/batch/useAutoRunReclaim.ts`) uses it to restart a run its page lost to a reload.
- **Telling the other clients an agent appeared or went away:** `sessions:lifecycleSync`, emitted by `sessions:setMany` / `sessions:setAll` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/persistence.ts` and applied by `useSessionLifecycleSync` (`src/renderer/hooks/session/useSessionLifecycleSync.ts`). Every client - each desktop window, each browser tab - holds its OWN session tree and flushes it into one store, so without the push a close never propagated and the peer's stale copy wrote the agent straight back. Four things keep it honest: main tombstones a closed id so a flush already in flight cannot resurrect it (the newest 1000, bounded by COUNT rather than age - a suspended browser tab can be away for hours, and ids are never reused, so an old tombstone has nothing to block but a stale write; a client that missed a close and outlived its tombstone reloads on reconnect anyway, since the web bridge has no replay), only ADDITIONS travel from `setAll` (a bootstrap snapshot predates anything a peer created, so an absent id there is ignorance, not a close), the hook waits for the startup load before applying (the load ends in one `setSessions` that would overwrite the delta), and deltas apply strictly in ARRIVAL ORDER through one promise chain (an add still restoring when the close for the same agent arrives makes the close look like it names an agent this client never had). It is lifecycle only - tab contents, read-state and queued messages remain last-writer-wins.
- **Moving an agent to another directory:** `withWorkingDirectory(session, dir)`, `workingDirectoryChangeBlocker(session)`, `isSameDirectory(a, b)`, `rebasePathOntoRoot()` in `src/renderer/utils/agentWorkingDirectory.ts`. An agent's location is spread across `cwd`, `fullPath`, `shellCwd`, `projectRoot`, `autoRunFolderPath`, and an SSH agent's `workingDirOverride`. `update-agent --cwd` once moved only the first three, so the Files panel and the Edit dialog stayed on the old folder while the agent ran in the new one (#1565). Every relocation goes through `withWorkingDirectory()`, which moves them together, rebases an Auto Run folder only when it lives under the old root, and clears the file tree and git state so both reload. Its "already there" check compares `cwd`, `fullPath`, and the SSH override as well as `projectRoot`, so moving a split agent onto its own `projectRoot` repairs it; `shellCwd` is left out because a terminal `cd` moves it on purpose. Windows paths compare case-insensitively; compare a user-entered directory with `isSameDirectory()`, never `===`, or a trailing slash reads as a move. It also puts down an in-flight file tree load and forgets an SSH agent's reported `remoteCwd`, both of which otherwise keep describing the old project; `useFileTreeManagement` refuses to write a scan whose root no longer matches the session, so a scan that finishes mid-move cannot land either. Gate the move on `workingDirectoryChangeBlocker()`, not `aiPid` alone: a spawned process keeps the cwd it launched with, and a busy agent can have no recorded pid. `useCueAutoDiscovery` refreshes the Cue engine when `projectRoot` changes, so do not add a second refresh at the call site. `workingDirectoryChangeBlocker` is the single reason a move is refused (a live agent process, since the PTY's cwd is fixed at spawn time, or an unvalidated SSH path); ask it rather than re-deriving the guard. Every surface that relocates an agent rides this module: the Edit Agent dialog, the web bridge's `update_session_cwd`, and `maestro-cli update-agent --cwd`.
- **Moving an agent to another group:** `withAgentInGroup(agents, agentId, groupId)` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/agents/rules.ts`, over `agentsMovingWithParent()`. Six places moved an agent between groups by hand: the Left Bar drag onto a group and onto Ungrouped, the right-click Move to Group menu, the command palette, "create group and move", and the `move_session_to_group` handler behind `maestro-cli update-agent --group`. The five UI copies carried the agent's worktree children along; the CLI copy did not, so a scripted move left the children in the old group and split the agent across two Left Bar sections. Every move goes through this one write: worktree children travel with their parent, Ungrouped (`null`, `undefined` or `''`) stores NO `groupId` key, and a move that changes nothing returns the input list so a caller can tell a no-op by identity. Generic over `{ id, parentSessionId?, groupId? }`, so a renderer `Session` and an `AgentRecord` both fit. The Left Bar has no manual agent or group order (both sort by name, see `leftBarAgentOrder()`), so a group move is the whole of "reordering" an agent.
- **Per-agent git actions:** `useGitAgentActions(session)` in `src/renderer/hooks/git/useGitAgentActions.ts` - the shared View Git Log / View Git Diff / Pull / Push / Change Branch / Create PR / Configure Worktrees set behind ALL THREE surfaces that offer it: the header branch pill dropdown, the Left Bar right-click menu, and the command palette (`buildGitWorktreeCommands`). Also exports `resolveGitCwd()` / `resolveGitSshRemoteId()`. Adding a git action means extending this hook and surfacing it in all three, not re-deriving cwd + SSH id + modal calls. See [REMAINING-SYSTEMS.md](REMAINING-SYSTEMS.md#usegitagentactions-srcrendererhooksgitusegitagentactionsts).
- **Whether a PR is being opened right now:** `usePRCreationActive(worktreePath)` / `startPRCreation(target)` in `src/renderer/stores/prCreationStore.ts`, settled by `usePRCreationNotifier`. `gh pr create` can take a minute, so the request lives in the store rather than in `CreatePRModal`, which the user is free to close while it works. A second `startPRCreation` on the same repo re-attaches instead of opening a duplicate PR, `usePRCreationActive` is what the menus badge with, and the notifier reports the settlement exactly once - toast plus history entry - from a host that stays mounted. Do NOT `await window.maestro.git.createPR` inside a component: a close would throw away the PR's url and its error with it. Same shape as `gitCommandRunStore` for pull/push.
- **Taking the user to an agent:** `jumpToAgent(sessionId, { tabId? })`, `revealAgentInSidebar(session)`, `openAgentSettings(session, { tab? })` in `src/renderer/services/agentNavigation.ts`. Landing on an agent is never just `setActiveSessionId`: the Document Graph is a full-screen overlay that would sit on top of it, an active group chat keeps rendering instead of the transcript, the agent may have been left on a terminal / file / browser tab (all of which outrank the AI tab in the render precedence), and the agent itself may be inside a collapsed group or a collapsed bookmarks section. Every caller that forgot one of those steps produced a jump that looked to the user like the click did nothing, which is why the reveal half was lifted out of `QuickActionsModal` into this module. `jumpToAgent` returns `false` when the agent no longer exists so the caller can SAY so - a silent no-op on a deleted agent is the same failure in a new shape. Everything self-sources from the stores rather than props, so a modal deep in a tree can call it without threading `setActiveSessionId` / `setGroups` / `setBookmarksCollapsed` callbacks down; that also means a test drives it through `useSessionStore.setState`, not prop spies. The reveal is deliberately MINIMAL: a bookmarked agent that is already visible in an expanded group leaves both sections exactly as the user left them, and when neither is open it expands bookmarks only, the lighter of the two reveals. `openAgentSettings` is the Edit Agent modal, kept here so a surface offering "jump" and "configure" side by side gets both from one import. Pass `{ tab: 'peers' }` (any `AgentSettingsTabId`) to send the user to one tab: it rides `EditAgentModalData.initialTab` into `useModalTabs({ requestedTab })`, which beats the tab the form was last left on. A caller that sits UNDER a full-window modal (the Usage Dashboard) must close that modal itself after the jump, or the agent lands behind it.
- **Whether an agent is drawn in the Left Bar:** `sessionMatchesFilter()`, `passesUnreadFilter()`, and `passesHiddenGroupFilter()` in `src/renderer/utils/sidebarMembership.ts`. "Visible" means drawn ANYWHERE in the scrollable list; scroll position is irrelevant. Both the render path (`useSessionCategories`) and `Cmd+[` / `Cmd+]` (`useCycleSession`) call these, because a cycle that disagrees with the list about membership moves the cursor onto an agent that is not on screen, which reads as the shortcut jumping at random. Adding a match rule here fixes both at once. The "does this agent need attention?" half of that answer lives in `sessionNeedsAttention()` / `sessionOrChildrenNeedAttention()` in `src/renderer/utils/sessionAttention.ts`, because the bell badge, the collapsed rail (`SkinnySidebar`), and the jump-badge projection (`useSortedSessions`) ask it WITHOUT a filter context - so a new attention rule (unread, busy, **error**, Auto Run batch, stuck outage) goes there and every surface picks it up at once. Errored agents were invisible in the unread filter for exactly this reason: four rival copies of the predicate, and only one of them knew about `state === 'error'`. The inputs are shared state (`uiStore.sessionFilter`, `uiStore.showArchivedGroupChats`) rather than local `useState` for the same reason - see [STATE-PATTERNS.md → uiStore](STATE-PATTERNS.md#uistore). Note that `orderGroupChatsForDisplay()` drops archived chats ITSELF, so a caller that has already decided archived chats are on screen must pass `{ includeArchived: true }` rather than pre-filtering, or they are removed again on the way through. **Hidden groups are the third membership rule**, and they live here too: `collectHiddenGroupIds(groups)` answers which groups are flagged hidden (propagating the flag down a parent's children, since a child renders as its own indented row and would otherwise sit under a header that is not there), `resolveHiddenGroupIds(groups, ctx)` narrows that to what the Left Bar is actually suppressing right now, and `passesHiddenGroupFilter(session, ids)` is the per-agent predicate. Two rules sit on top of the raw flag: the `showHiddenGroups` setting un-suppresses everything at once (the groups still render, just faded, which is the point of the "Show Hidden" button at the foot of the list), and the group holding the ACTIVE agent is always drawn - the same escape hatch `passesUnreadFilter` keeps, for the same reason. A hidden group's agents must leave `useSessionCategories`'s filtered list ENTIRELY rather than being re-bucketed: anything without a live group is categorized as Ungrouped, so merely skipping the group header dumps the agents the user just parked straight back into the list under it. The Cmd+O switcher reads `collectHiddenGroupIds` rather than the resolved set, because hiding never restricts access - a hidden agent is always listed there, it just sorts into the last tier (`AgentBucket` `'hidden'`).
- **Listing agents in the Left Bar's order:** `leftBarAgentOrder(input)` and `orderLeftBarGroups(sortedGroups, groupById, nested)` in `src/renderer/utils/leftBarOrder.ts`. A surface that lists agents OUTSIDE the Left Bar (the Quick Chat picker) must read in the order the user already knows, or they hunt for an agent in a list that looks nothing like the sidebar - the picker shipped in store order and showed every worktree child as its own row. `leftBarAgentOrder()` returns each top-level agent ONCE: Pianola (only while its Encore Feature is on), then Bookmarks, then each group in render order, then Ungrouped; worktree children and agents in suppressed hidden groups are left out, through the same `resolveHiddenGroupIds()` / `passesHiddenGroupFilter()` the Left Bar uses. A bookmarked agent sits in the Bookmarks position only, because a picker row that duplicates another is noise. `orderLeftBarGroups()` is the group nesting `SessionList` renders with (Groups+: each nested group right after its parent; a child whose parent is gone draws as a root), so the two cannot drift. Names compare through `compareNamesIgnoringEmojis()`. It does NOT replace `computeSortedSessions()`, which owns the jump-slot and arrow-key projections and keeps worktree children.
- **Whether an agent is answering a consult right now:** `useSessionIsBeingConsulted(sessionId)` for one row, `useConsultedSessionIds()` / `selectConsultedSessionIds(requests)` for a surface that draws many rows in one pass, all in `src/renderer/stores/crossAgentInFlightStore.ts`. A cross-agent `@mention` runs under a synthetic `cross-agent-<requestId>` process id and writes into a HIDDEN tab, so by design it never touches the consulted agent's `state`, unread flags, or attention badge - background work must not disturb the conversation the user has open with that agent. The cost was that a consulted agent sat there drawing a green "Ready" dot for the whole time it was working, and a Claude agent the user had never opened drew the dim "No active Claude session" hollow dot, because a fresh consult tab has no provider session until the answer lands. This store is the one signal that leaks back out, and it is deliberately narrow: enough for a busy dot, never enough to mark the agent as needing attention - do NOT add a consult check to `sessionNeedsAttention()`, which is what lights badges the user has no chip to clear. Three surfaces read it and must agree: the Left Bar row dot (`getEnhancedStatusColor`, which also suppresses `isDisconnected`), the collapsed rail (`SkinnySidebar`), and the worktree pill strip (`CollapsedSessionPill`). Ranking is the part that is easy to get wrong: ABOVE the unbound-Claude hollow dot (an unopened Claude agent is the COMMON consult target, so the hollow dot would mask exactly the case this exists to show) and BELOW the agent's own `state === 'busy'` and any stuck outage, which draw the same dot but carry a more specific label. Do NOT hand-roll another loop over `requests` at a new call site: the per-row hook returns a boolean so a row re-renders only when the answer flips, and the set form is memoized on the `requests` identity, which changes only when a consult starts or finishes.
- **Dismissing a drawer after the user picks something in it:** `closeLeftSidebarForNavigation()` and `closeRightPanelForNavigation()` in `src/renderer/stores/uiStore.ts`, over `isNarrowViewportNow()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useViewportBreakpoint.ts`. On a narrow viewport (`xs` / `sm`) the Left Bar is an OVERLAY across the main panel - on a phone it covers the whole screen - so activating anything listed in it is a request to LOOK at that thing and the drawer has to get out of the way. On a wide viewport it is a permanent column beside the panel and closing it would be a surprise, so the width check lives INSIDE the action: a call site states the intent ("the user navigated") and the store decides whether that means anything, which is what keeps five callers from each re-deriving the breakpoint. **Call it AT THE TAP, never from an effect keyed on what became active.** That is the whole reason it exists. `App.tsx` already watches the `activeSessionId` TRANSITION, and three of the four things a user can tap in that list move no id at all: a group chat never touches `activeSessionId` (it has its own `activeGroupChatId`), a starred row routinely names the agent that is already active, and re-tapping the current agent from behind an open group chat changes nothing. The effect read each of those as nothing having happened, so the drawer stayed over the very thing the tap was meant to show - which on a phone is a full-screen panel and reads as the tap having done nothing. The five callers are `SessionList`'s `setActiveSessionId` (agent rows), `useGroupChatHandlers`' `handleOpenGroupChat` and `handleProcessMonitorNavigateToGroupChat` (rooms), `sidebarNavStore.activateStarredItem` (starred rows), and `jumpToAgent()` (every programmatic jump - the palette, toasts, cross-agent links). `handleOpenGroupChat` calls it only AFTER the chat loads, so a room that could not be opened does not dismiss the drawer for nothing. `isNarrowViewportNow()` is the non-hook twin of `useViewportBreakpoint().isNarrow` and shares its `classify()` and `isNarrowBreakpoint()`, so a store action reacting to a tap cannot disagree with the layout about where the drawers stop being columns; it answers `false` with no `window`, which is what keeps it safe in a main-process-adjacent or SSR import. The action returns the same state object when there is nothing to do, so a closed drawer or a wide viewport does not re-render every subscriber. The transition-keyed effects in `App.tsx` remain as the net for anything that changes the active agent WITHOUT a tap. **The RIGHT drawer is the same rule, the same failure, and the same cure.** It is the Files / History / Auto Run panel, full-screen on a phone, and `App.tsx` dismissed it from an effect keyed on the active-tab ids (`activeTabKey`). Two everyday opens move none of those ids, so the effect saw nothing happen and the file opened behind the tree the user tapped it in: re-previewing the file that is ALREADY the active tab (long-press a file you have open, tap Preview - the reported bug), and ANY media file, which is diverted to the floating player and never becomes a tab at all, so that key can never change for it. So `handleOpenFileTab` (`hooks/tabs/internal/useFilePreviewTabHandlers.ts`) calls `closeRightPanelForNavigation()` at the OPEN. It is fired from that one function rather than from each caller - the same reason as `requestFileTreeRefresh` - so every path in is covered at once: a tap, the context menu's Preview, Preview All, and a multi-file open. It sits BEFORE the media early-return, which is what covers audio and video. It is gated on the already-computed `activate`, because `activate: false` is the CLI / web `--background` path and that changes nothing on screen by definition. The `activeTabKey` effect stays as the NET for tab activations that do not go through the file open path (a conversation resumed from History). Do NOT hand-roll another `isNarrowViewport && setLeftSidebarOpen(false)` at a new navigation site, and do NOT move either of these into an effect. Per-surface phone rules: [WEB-MOBILE.md → Phone Layout](WEB-MOBILE.md#phone-layout).
- **Which agents own an Opt+Cmd+# slot:** `buildSessionJumpSlotMap(visibleSessions)`, `sessionJumpShortcut(digit)` in `src/renderer/utils/sessionJumpSlots.ts`. The ten jump slots follow the Left Bar's DRAW order (`visibleSessions` from `useSortedSessions`), not `sessions` order, and the tenth slot is bound to `0`, not `10`. The Left Bar's number badges and the Quick Actions jump rows both read this map, so the digit a row advertises is always the chord that actually lands there; computing it a second time from a differently sorted list is how the two surfaces would disagree.
- **Naming an agent from its folder (vs naming the project):** `projectNameFromPath(directoryPath)` and `defaultAgentNameForPath(directoryPath, takenNames)` in `src/renderer/components/Wizard/shared/projectIdentity.ts`. Two different things that the onboarding wizard used to derive from ONE typed string: the name in "Name your agent" was also handed to the discovery prompt as `{{PROJECT_NAME}}`, so an agent called "Scout" made the assistant greet the project as Scout, and anyone who typed their project name there got it as a Left Bar label (issue #1225). The PROJECT is always the folder - never a typed value, never the agent name. The AGENT NAME is a label the user may type and the wizard fills in from the folder when they do not, deduplicated against existing agent names because `validateNewSession` rejects a duplicate name outright and a wizard-supplied collision would be a dead end the user did not create. `defaultAgentNameForPath` returns `''` (not a name) when the path yields no basename; callers treat that as "no default available".
- **Recording wizard usage:** `beginWizardRun` / `countWizardExchange` / `recordWizardDocuments` / `finishWizardRun` (and `recordCompletedWizardRun` for a run only observed at its end) in `src/renderer/services/wizardStats.ts`, landing in `wizard_runs`. Both wizard surfaces ride it - the inline `/wizard` command keyed by AI TAB id, so parallel wizards stay separate, and the onboarding wizard as one row at launch. The design point is that **every milestone re-flushes the whole row under one stable id** rather than writing once at the end: a wizard's payoff (documents written) and its close are separated by however long the user reads the result, and plenty of runs are never closed at all, so an end-only write drops whole runs including their documents. Time spent is `activeMs`, NOT `endedAt - startedAt`: a wizard tab left open for days made the window count days (49 hours across 9 messages in the field). `activeMs` accrues each gap between milestones, in full while the agent works (bracket every agent call with `setWizardAgentWorking(key, true/false)`, capped at 60 minutes) and at most 5 minutes while the wizard waits on the user. A new agent call left unbracketed is counted as user time and capped, so its real duration is lost. Every write is fire-and-forget and the bridge itself is null-checked: analytics must degrade to no analytics, never to a broken wizard. Do NOT add a second counter in `settingsStore.onboardingStats` - that blob is lifetime-cumulative with no timestamps, so it can never answer a dashboard time range.
- **Leaving inline wizard mode:** `flattenWizardIntoTab(tab, { summary? })` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/index.ts`. The inline wizard (`/wizard`) keeps its conversation in `tab.wizardState.conversationHistory` and renders it through `WizardConversationView`, a store entirely separate from `tab.logs` / `TerminalOutput`. So dropping `wizardState` drops the whole wizard conversation, and the provider session handle with it, leaving a tab that looks empty - which is what shipped for every exit except a successful completion. Do NOT set `wizardState: undefined` by hand anywhere; call this instead. It appends the transcript to `tab.logs` (user turns as `source: 'user'`, agent turns as `'ai'`), promotes `wizardState.agentSessionId` onto `tab.agentSessionId` so the plain tab can keep talking to the same provider context, appends an optional closing `summary` entry, and only then clears the wizard. It is idempotent (entries are matched by their `wizard-`prefixed ids), which matters because the wizard sync effect in `useWizardHandlers.ts` can race a caller that already flattened. Three callers, one per exit: `completeWizardImpl` (with the "Wizard Complete" summary), `handleExitWizard` (the Exit Wizard button and the cancel control on the document generation view), and the restart sweep plus the sync effect's safety net. Deliberately NOT wired to closing the wizard TAB: that already warns the user and passes `skipHistory`, and the tab itself is going away. See [[CLAUDE-WIZARD.md]].
- **What the Thinking pill lists:** `buildThinkingItems(sessions, ownsSession?)` in `src/renderer/utils/thinkingItems.ts` builds the busy (agent, tab) pairs, read through the narrow `useThinkingItems()` hook; `useBackgroundAutoRuns(activeSessionId)` in `src/renderer/hooks/batch/useBackgroundAutoRuns.ts` adds Auto Runs on OTHER agents. Two traps made the old inline loop wrong. A closed tab sits in `orphanedThinkingTabs` IDLE while it still owns queued items (a HELD message parks it there indefinitely), so counting every orphan showed "Thinking..." and a Stop button for a tab with no process; count orphans only while `state === 'busy'`. And Auto Run never marks a tab busy, so a run on another agent is not a thinking item at all: without the hook it had no +N badge and no dropdown row. The hook subscribes narrowly (running ids, their names, their run states) so log streaming does not re-render the pill. The pill filters both by `WindowContext.ownsSession`, so a window never lists work on an agent another window owns.
- **Whether this renderer owns an app-wide side effect:** `useIsLifecycleOwner()` in `src/renderer/contexts/WindowContext.tsx` - the desktop app's primary window, and nothing else. `isMainWindow` alone is not enough: a web-desktop page loads with no `?windowId=` param, so every browser tab reads as the main window too. Gate on it for work that must happen once per event across the whole app and is not tied to one agent (Cue time credit, the leaderboard startup sync and outbox flush, the worktree scans). Per-agent `process:*` effects use `useOwnedSideEffectGate()` instead, which follows agent ownership into secondary windows.

## Tabs

- **Making a tab the visible one:** the four `*FocusFields` helpers in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/focusFields.ts` (`aiTabFocusFields`, `fileTabFocusFields`, `browserTabFocusFields`, `terminalTabFocusFields`), re-exported from `tabHelpers`. The main window renders exactly ONE tab type by precedence (terminal > file > browser > ai), so "focus this tab" is never one assignment - missing a higher-ranked selection leaves the previous view on screen and the focus silently does nothing. They live in their own import-free module because both `tabHelpers` and `terminalTabHelpers` need them and those two already point at each other. `src/renderer/utils/tabFocusFields.ts` is a thin re-export of the same four for older import sites.

  **Focus an AI tab:** `aiTabFocusFields(tabId?)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/focusFields.ts` - spread into a session patch (`{ ...s, ...aiTabFocusFields(tabId) }`) to land on an AI tab. It clears `activeFileTabId`/`activeTerminalTabId`/`activeBrowserTabId` and sets `inputMode: 'ai'`. Do NOT hand-roll the literal: those non-AI tab types outrank the AI tab in the render precedence, so omitting even one leaves the previous view on screen.

  **Focus a file tab:** `fileTabFocusFields(tabId)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/focusFields.ts` - the file-tab counterpart to the above. Clears the terminal and browser selections (both outrank the file tab) and sets `inputMode: 'ai'`.

- **Any CLI verb that can move the Maestro view:** `resolveBackgroundFlag()` (CLI side) / `readBackgroundField()` (handler side) in `src/shared/focusPlacement.ts`. **Focus belongs to the human operator**: an agent may create a surface, it may not decide the human should be looking at it. `background: true` means the active AGENT does not change AND the active TAB inside any agent does not change - the surface is still created and addressable, so "created but invisible" is a different bug. **The flag is ADDITIVE: only one verb's default changed.** `open-file`, `open-terminal`, `open-browser`, `tab new`, `dispatch` (the plain `send_command` path, which selects the target agent "for visual feedback"), `create-agent`, `create-worktree` and `switch-mode` all still focus when the flag is absent; `refresh-auto-run` is background by default (CLI side only, `--focus` to switch), because its focusing default only moved the view when the target was off screen, so every unflagged refresh from a Cue script or agent turn pulled the user away; `dispatch --new-tab` is still background-by-default with `--focus`, which is also its pre-existing behaviour. `focus-agent`, `send --tab`, `open` and `open-graph` exist TO move the view and carry no flag at all - a full-window overlay whose only effect IS being looked at has no background reading. `refresh-files` accepts `--background` and IGNORES it (`ALREADY_QUIET_VERBS` / `isAlreadyQuietVerb`): it renders no notice and moves no selection, so there is nothing to suppress, but commander rejects an unknown option, and one verb that refused the flag would turn "pass `--background` unless the user asked to be taken there" from a rule into a lookup table. A verb belongs on that list only while it moves nothing; the moment it grows a notice or a selection it moves into `CLI_BACKGROUND_DEFAULTS` and the flag starts meaning something, with no call site changing. `readBackgroundField` is an OPT-IN - only a literal `true` counts. **Never write `message.background !== false` in a handler**: that reads an absent field as an opt-in and silently stops the verb from focusing, breaking every existing caller at once. Defaults are keyed by VERB, not message, because `tab new --prompt` and `dispatch --new-tab` share `new_ai_tab_with_prompt` and disagree. `--no-switch` on `open-file` is NOT a spelling of `--background`: it stays on the current agent but still activates the tab there, while `--background` changes nothing rendered anywhere (both may be passed; background wins). On the renderer, background placement is simply not spreading a focus patch - `createTab({ activate: false })`, `addTerminalTab(s, tab, { activate: false })`, `handleOpenFileTab(file, { activate: false })`. **A verb that fans out into a second message must CARRY its resolved bit, not let the second message re-resolve**: `create-worktree --background <msg>` created the agent quietly and was then yanked to it one message later, because the follow-on `send_command` re-resolved as `dispatch`, which focuses. **`--background` never costs the work, only the trip**: `refresh-auto-run --background` still re-reads the documents (for a non-active agent, doing nothing IS the refresh - the loader effect reads the folder fresh on switch), and it drops only the success flash, which confirms an action the USER took and not one an agent took. See [CLI-UI-PARITY.md → Focus belongs to the human](CLI-UI-PARITY.md#focus-belongs-to-the-human---background----focus).
- **Which tab a tab-scoped action targets:** `resolveActiveTabRef(session)` in `src/renderer/utils/panelLayout.ts` returns the `UnifiedTabRef` a per-tab command (rename, etc.) should act on - the active tiled group's FOCUSED PANE when a group owns the panel, else the single-view tab via `resolveSingleViewTabRef()`. Do NOT hand-roll the `terminal -> file -> browser -> AI` precedence chain: focusing a tiled pane does not set `inputMode`/`activeTerminalTabId`, so an open-coded chain silently targets the AI tab hidden behind the group. Pair with `resolveTabRefRenameValue()` (user-assigned name only, `null` when the tab is gone) to seed a rename input, and `resolveTabRefTitle()` for display text.
- **Whether an AI tab is drawn in the tab strip:** `isAiTabHidden(tab)` in `src/renderer/utils/unifiedTabOrderUtils.ts`, and `visibleAiTabs(tabs)` beside it for the list form (returns the input by reference when nothing is hidden, so callers can memoize on identity). Any surface that ENUMERATES or COUNTS an agent's tabs runs the list through `visibleAiTabs` first - the tab switcher's Open Tabs, cross-tab search, the merge target list, the palette's "Close all N tabs", the Usage Dashboard's per-agent tab count. A bulk close does too (`excludePreservedRefs` in `src/renderer/hooks/tabs/internal/unifiedCloseHelpers.ts`), because the repaired order deliberately keeps a hidden ref and closing it would destroy the consult transcript and its resume id without the user ever seeing a chip. An inbound `@mention` spawns a consult tab with `hidden: true` that lives in `session.aiTabs` and keeps its `unifiedTabOrder` ref (that ref is what restores its position on reveal) but has no chip. So `aiTabs.length` is NOT the number of tabs on screen, and any code answering "does a tab survive this close?", "which tab is to the left?", or "what do we render when `activeTabId` is stale?" has to filter first - `buildUnifiedTabs`, `getNavigableTabs`, `getActiveTab`, and `closeTab` all do. Counting a hidden tab as a survivor is what let closing the last visible chip skip the replacement-chat branch and activate a consult instead: empty strip, a conversation the user never opened, and no chip to click back from. **Unread is the other half of the same rule:** a consult is answered in the BACKGROUND, so it must raise no unread anywhere, and `hasUnreadVisibleTab(tabs)` (same module, re-exported from `tabHelpers`) is the one predicate behind every unread MARK - `sessionNeedsAttention`, the Left Bar row dot, the collapsed rail, `SkinnySidebar`, and the tab strip's bell. Do NOT write `aiTabs.some((t) => t.hasUnread)`: a hidden tab has no chip, so a badge lit by one can never be cleared - the user opens the agent, every visible tab is already read, and the dot stays lit forever. The batched updater refuses to SET `hasUnread` on a hidden tab for the same reason (clearing one is still allowed), and `findNextUnreadSession` never stops on one, since the jump path reveals whatever it lands on and would turn the background consult into a foreground tab.
- **What an unnamed tab is called:** `getTabDisplayName(tab)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers`, over `formatSessionId(agentSessionId)` for the id label itself (`8535E0E3`, `SES_4BCD`, `THR_ABC1`). Do NOT hand-roll `agentSessionId.substring(0, 8).toUpperCase()`: it agrees with the strip on a UUID and disagrees on every other provider, so `useAgentExitListener` was stamping history entries `THREAD_A` for a Codex tab the strip was drawing as `THR_ABC1`. Before writing a RECORDED name back onto a tab, ask `isSessionIdLabel(name, agentSessionId)` - surfaces that capture a display name (a history entry's `sessionName`, the session-origins store) capture whatever was on screen, so an unnamed tab records its own fallback, and restoring that as `tab.name` renders identically while permanently opting the tab out of ever being auto-named. It compares against the formatter rather than matching a hex shape, because only one of the four id forms is 8 hex characters and a regex silently lets the other three through.
- **Where a closed tab's name still exists:** `HistoryEntry.sessionName`, and in practice nowhere else. The Claude session-origins store that `handleResumeSession` falls back to is Claude-only AND written only by an exit synopsis, so for every other provider - and any session that never produced one - the history record is the last surviving copy. Every surface that reopens a past session must therefore pass the recorded name into `handleResumeSession`'s THIRD argument (the second is `providedMessages`); the four that do are the History pill, the list's `Cmd+Enter`, the detail modal's Resume, and Director's Notes. Dropping it brings the conversation back labeled with the id octet the user was not looking at.
- **Resolving a History conversation's provider:** `resolveHistorySession()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/session.ts` reads the current mapping and parked native inventory without extracting or translating a transcript. Both History and Quick Chat pass its provider to `loadResumableSession()` and its `sourceProvider`/`providerSessions` to `createTab()`. The latter reuses `switchTabProvider()` to park foreign native state. Deduplicate reopened tabs against the matching live or parked provider slot. Agent-wide plans extract only open tabs and carry the rest in `TranslationPlan.deferredTabs`; `commitTranslationSession()` parks those identities and records `pendingTranslation`, and `prepareTabForSend()` imports them on the next send even when an older destination slot exists. Missing legacy ownership still refuses resume. Never put a foreign native ID in the active provider's live slot.
- **Reading a past conversation back into a tab:** `loadResumableSession(options)` in `src/renderer/services/resumableSession.ts`. Two callers reopen a conversation that has no tab: `handleResumeSession` (History, the sessions browser, starred sessions), which FOCUSES the tab it makes, and Quick Chat's router (`reopenClosedConversation`), which makes one in the BACKGROUND for a message that belongs in a closed conversation. Both need the same transcript read (`agentSessions.read` with `TRANSCRIPT_RESUME_READ_LIMIT`, on the SSH remote when there is one), the same `stripSynopsisTurns` + `transcriptMessagesToLogEntries` conversion, and the same name / starred / context-usage lookup, or a conversation reopened one way differs from the same one reopened the other. It returns `{ messages, name, starred, usageStats }`, or null when nothing displayable is left, and THROWS when the transcript cannot be read: the caller owns how that is reported (the hook flashes, the router falls back to a new tab). It deliberately does NOT build the tab. `createTab(..., { activate })` and the existing-tab dedupe stay with each caller, because the dedupe has to run on fresh state inside the caller's own store update (the read is awaited, and two tabs for one conversation must never exist). A recorded `sessionName` that is only the session id's own label is dropped (`isSessionIdLabel`), so the tab stays genuinely unnamed and can still be auto-named.
- **Storing the closed-tab stack (Cmd+Shift+T):** `projectClosedTabHistory(history, context)`, `closedTabHistoryContext(record)`, `isClosedAiTabReloadable()`, `normalizeStoredClosedTabs(record)`, `MAX_CLOSED_TAB_HISTORY` in `src/shared/closedTabHistory.ts`, and `loadReopenedTabContent(sessionId, ref)` in `src/renderer/services/reopenedTabContent.ts`. `unifiedClosedTabHistory` persists as pointers, not payloads: an AI entry drops its logs when its provider can give the transcript back (a session id, `supportsSessionStorage`, not SSH) and otherwise keeps the newest `MAX_PERSISTED_SESSION_LOGS`; a file entry drops its content; an incognito browser entry is never stored; runtime fields reset. The legacy `closedTabHistory` is never stored. The projection runs in BOTH processes: `prepareSessionForPersistence()` and `restoreSession()` in the renderer, `loadStoredSessions()` and the `sessions:setMany` / `sessions:setAll` write boundary in main (after the merge, never before). That is not belt and braces: the three-way merge in `sessionPersistenceMerge.ts` treats the stack as one value, so once the stored stack differs from what the renderer projects (a raw ack flush wrote a 0.54 MB closed tab, twice), every later close is a conflict and never reaches disk. Keep the function pure, idempotent, tolerant of malformed entries, reference-stable for untouched entries, and `undefined` (key absent) when empty, never `[]`. A web-desktop client keeps its own runtime stack: its persistence omits the field and `projectWebSession()` drops both keys. A reopened stripped tab is filled by `loadReopenedTabContent()` (through `loadResumableSession()` for an AI tab, `readFilePreviewContent()` for a file); a desktop-only store subscriber (`startReopenedTabContentLoader()`, mounted by `useSessionRestoration`) runs it for visible tabs, so no reopen path calls in, and it never overwrites logs or an edit that arrived meanwhile. Do NOT add another cap constant (tabHelpers, terminalTabHelpers and maestro-lib's `MAX_CLOSED_TAB_ARCHIVE` import this one), and do NOT persist the stack through any path that skips the projection.
- **Reading or reopening the closed-tab stack from a script (`maestro-cli tab closed` / `tab reopen`):** `runRemoteClosedTabCommand()`, `listClosedTabs()`, `getClosedTabLabel()` in `src/renderer/services/closedTabCommands.ts`, wire shape in `src/shared/closedTabCommands.ts`. ONE `closed_tab_command` message with an `action` (`list`, `reopen`), answered by the agent's OWNING window from its live `unifiedClosedTabHistory`, never from the stored inventory: the store lags a close by the persistence debounce, so `tab close X; tab closed` would miss X and `--index 0` would name a different entry than Cmd+Shift+T reopens. A reopen runs the shortcut's own `reopenClosedTabWithTiling(session, index)` (the `index` parameter, default 0, is how a deeper entry or a `--tab` id is reopened; there is no second reopen path), honours `background` by putting the agent's focus fields back, then awaits `loadReopenedTabContent()` within `REOPEN_CONTENT_BUDGET_MS` (answering `content: 'pending'` past it) and a tracked flush before replying with the restored tab's NEW id. Labels: `getTabDisplayName()` for AI, `getParkedTabLabel()` (shared with snooze) for the other kinds.
- **Compact, Merge Into, or Send to Agent from a script (`maestro-cli tab compact` / `merge` / `transfer`):** `runRemoteTabContextOp()` in `src/renderer/services/tabContextOps.ts`, wire shape in `src/shared/tabContextOps.ts`. It calls the AI tab overlay menu's OWN handlers, handed in from App's hooks: `summarizeTab()` (`useSummarizeAndContinue`), `mergeTabContext()` and `transferTabContext()` (`useMergeTransferHandlers`). Those are the explicit-source cores the menu wrappers (`handleSummarizeAndContinue`, `handleMerge`, `handleSendToAgent`) now call with the active agent and tab, so a new caller passes an agent and tab instead of focusing them first. The service resolves only what the menu resolves by having the tab on screen: the default source tab (the agent's active AI tab), the merge target's default tab, and the modals' target rules (live agent, no `terminal` target, no merge into itself, no transfer to the same agent). Merge Into and Send to Agent move the transcript VERBATIM (`REMOTE_MERGE_OPTIONS.groomContext` is false); only Compact summarizes. `background` keeps every selection where it was: Compact leaves the source tab selected, Send to Agent adds the target's tab unselected, and Merge Into leaves the context staged on the target tab (`pendingMergedContext` + `autoSendOnActivate`), which App's effect sends when that tab is next active, reported as `staged`. Main forwards the three messages to the source agent's owning window with per-op budgets (`TAB_CONTEXT_OP_REPLY_MS`, 10 min for a compaction), answering `timedOut` rather than a failure.
- **Forcing a tracked sessions flush from outside App's hook tree:** `flushSessionPersistenceNow(latestSessions?)` in `src/renderer/services/sessionPersistenceFlush.ts`, registered by App with `registerSessionPersistenceFlush()` over `useDebouncedPersistence().flushNow`, which now returns the write's promise (a flush asked for mid-write resolves after the rerun, not the older write). Use it whenever a service must answer only once its change is on disk. Never write a raw `sessions.setMany([session], [])` for that: it skips the baseline the three-way merge needs, and the next change to the same field can stop persisting.
- **Which tab a Quick Chat message goes to:** pure decision in `src/shared/quickChatRouting.ts` (`decideRoute()`, `scoreRouteCandidates()`, `buildRoutePrompt()`, `parseRouteAnswer()`, `collectRouteText()`), side effects in `src/renderer/services/quickChatRouter.ts` (`routeQuickChatMessage()`, `reopenClosedConversation()`). Every Quick Chat message is routed on its own: to an open tab of the agent, to a conversation closed in the last `QUICK_CHAT_ROUTE_LOOKBACK_DAYS` (reopened in the background), or to a new tab. Two stages, cheapest first. WORDS: each candidate is scored by IDF-weighted coverage of the message's informative vocabulary; a clear winner (`ROUTE_STRONG_SCORE`, `ROUTE_CLEAR_MARGIN`, at least `ROUTE_MIN_SHARED_WORDS` shared words, because one shared word is a coincidence) routes at once, and a message that resembles nothing with no chat in progress starts a new tab at once. MODEL: anything else is a close call words cannot settle (two tabs on one subject, or a follow-up that names no subject), so a cheap answer-only model reads a shortlist and answers one token. The bias is toward NEW, because a message routed into the wrong conversation becomes part of its context and cannot be taken back. Three rules guard that. The answer parser is strict (a bare in-range number or NEW, nothing else), since the conversations the model reads may hold text written to steer it; for the same reason every title, excerpt, and the message go into the prompt through `promptField`, one line with its angle brackets escaped, inside tags (`<conversation number>`, `<title>`, `<excerpt>`, `<message>`) the text cannot close or imitate. When the model gives no answer the message stays with the chat's CURRENT tab (what Quick Chat did before it could route), or starts a new tab when there is none: it never guesses among other tabs. And closed candidates come from History `USER` entries only (`history.getAllPaginated` with `lookbackHours` and `types`), never `agentSessions.list`: Auto Run and Cue sessions are not conversations the user was having, and on a busy agent they outnumber the rest many times over. A conversation that still has a tab (shown, hidden, or closed mid-reply) is reached through that tab and never reopened beside it. A tab mid-reply is not a candidate unless it is the chat's own (`routableTabs`): routing a message into someone else's running turn made it wait behind work the user was not following, so a close match starts a new tab instead. The engine (`quickChatEngine.ts`) delivers through the queue (see the next group's `sendQueuedItemNow`), so a routed message for a busy tab waits its turn and can be moved to a new tab. Its waiting rule is PER TAB (`isTabFreeFor`, `runFreedTurns`): the agent's queue holds a write-mode item while any other tab is busy, which would leave a Quick Chat message waiting on tabs it has nothing to do with, so the engine runs it the moment its own tab comes free.
- **Naming a tab from the user's message:** `requestTabAutoName({ session, tabId, prompt })` in `src/renderer/services/tabAutoNaming.ts`, with `collectNamingPrompt()` to build the capped prompt out of the user's own messages and `requestWizardTabAutoName()` for the wizard's variant. Naming is four steps - build the prompt, try the free client-side pattern match (`extractQuickTabName`, which turns a PR/issue/ticket reference into a name with no spawn), spawn the ephemeral namer with the SAME provider, cwd, env and SSH remote the chat uses, then write the result back ONLY if the tab has not been renamed in the meantime. Three surfaces wanted all four and each hand-rolled them: the send path (`useInputProcessing`), the rename modal's "Auto" button (`useSessionLifecycle`), and the inline wizard. The copies had already drifted - only the send path re-checked the tab before writing, so a slow naming turn from the rename modal could stomp a name the user typed while it ran. Pass the `session` rather than an id: the namer must inherit the sending agent's provider auth and Claude token source (`getClaudeTokenSourceFields`), and looking it up separately is how those drift apart. `canApply` is checked twice, once before the spawn and once against the LIVE tab before the write, and defaults to "only name a tab with no name"; `force: true` is for the rename modal, where the user asked explicitly and the `automaticTabNamingEnabled` setting and the existing name are both beside the point. **A wizard tab is "unnamed" while it still reads `Wizard`.** It is created before anyone knows the subject, so `WIZARD_TAB_PLACEHOLDER_NAME` is the placeholder and `isWizardTabAutoNameable()` - not `!tab.name` - is the question to ask; the name that lands carries `WIZARD_TAB_NAME_PREFIX` (`wizard: Ingest Pipeline`) so the tab still reads as a wizard at a glance. It is triggered from the `/wizard <input>` argument when the command carried a subject, and otherwise from the first message typed into the wizard. Everything here is fire-and-forget and retries on the next send: a failed name leaves the tab as it was, never blocks a message, and must always clear `isGeneratingName` or the tab spins forever.
- **Closing a tab while the unread filter is on:** `closeTab(session, tabId, showUnreadOnly?, options?)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/index.ts`. The third argument is an OVERRIDE, not the filter state: omit it and `closeTab` reads `uiStore.showUnreadOnly` itself. It used to default to `false`, and five of the six call sites took that default, so closing a tab under the filter ran the plain left-neighbor math and activated a tab the filter was hiding - the user pressed Cmd+W and landed somewhere not on screen. The replacement is now picked from `filterUnifiedTabOrderForUnread(...)`, the exact list `TabBar` renders, computed against the PRE-close session so the closed tab still occupies its slot; the left neighbor of that slot wins, which means a terminal/file/browser tab that survives the filter is a legitimate target and the result is a `UnifiedTabRef`, not an AI tab id. Pass an explicit `false` only where no tab survives to be selected (bulk close in `unifiedCloseHelpers.ts`, the close-agent path in `useAITabHandlers.ts`) or in a test that pins the state; anywhere else, omit it. `snoozeTab()` in `src/renderer/utils/snoozeHelpers.ts` and `tabStore.snoozeTab()` thread the same optional override through for the same reason. When nothing visible survives the filter, the pick falls back to the unfiltered math.
- **Ending a turn with no process exit:** `settleTabThinkingState(session, tabId)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/index.ts` - the inverse of `markTabRunningQueuedItem`. A tab's pulsing dot and its row on the Thinking pill are driven by `tab.state === 'busy'` plus the session's `state`/`busySource`/`thinkingStartTime`, and normally ONLY the process-exit listener clears them. Any path that ends a turn without a process exiting has to settle that state itself, or the tab pulses forever and the pill keeps counting elapsed time for work nobody is doing - `cancelRetry` (`src/renderer/stores/retryStore.ts`) is the live case, where a `scheduled` auto-retry's resend never spawned. Do NOT hand-roll the patch: it also recomputes the SESSION from whatever is still running (another busy tab, or an entry in `orphanedThinkingTabs` - a tab the user closed mid-turn, kept solely so the pill keeps counting it, which settling retires outright), and it deliberately leaves `state === 'error'` alone, since that is the blocking modal's state and settling a tab must not dismiss an error the user still has to act on. Only settle a tab with nothing behind it: an `in-flight` resend IS a live process, and its busy state belongs to the exit listener.
- **Never leaving an agent with an empty tab strip:** `ensureAgentHasVisibleTab(session)`, `agentHasVisibleTab(session)`, `createBlankAiTab()` in `src/renderer/utils/unifiedTabOrderUtils.ts` (re-exported from `tabHelpers`). When the last visible tab of ANY kind closes, the agent lands on a blank AI chat. `closeTab` always did this for the last AI tab, but `closeFileTab`, `closeBrowserTab`, and `closeTerminalTab` did not, so closing a lone file/browser/terminal tab left `activeTabId` blank and `getActiveTab` fell back to a hidden consult tab: a conversation the user never opened, rendered under an empty strip with no chip to leave by. Every non-AI close primitive, `closeTabGroup`, and session restore run their result through it. Hidden consult tabs do NOT count as visible, which is why it checks `visibleAiTabs` rather than `aiTabs.length`. It lives in `unifiedTabOrderUtils` rather than `tabHelpers` so `terminalTabHelpers` can call it without an import cycle. Do NOT hand-roll another blank `AITab` literal or another `tabCount === 0` check at a new close site.
- **Handing the agent back when a shell command or a CLI playbook ends:** `settleAgentStateFromTabs(session)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/index.ts`. A terminal-mode run and a `maestro-cli` playbook both take over the AGENT-level `state` while they run, but an AI tab can be mid-turn underneath. Ending them hands the agent back to that turn (`busy` / `'ai'`) rather than to idle; going straight to idle on a failed command, on `clear`, or when a playbook finished painted a working agent "Ready" and hid its Thinking pill. Like `settleTabThinkingState`, it counts a tab closed mid-turn (`orphanedThinkingTabs`) as still running and leaves the blocking `error` state alone. The command-exit listener, every shell-command failure path, and the CLI activity monitor ride it. Do NOT gate this on `session.aiPid`: per-tab agents leave it 0 (or -1 after a restore) whether or not a turn is running.
- **Agent state from tab states another client reported:** `syncAgentStateToTabs(session)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/index.ts`, applied where a web-desktop browser reconciles the desktop's `tabs_changed` inventory (`useRemoteIntegration`). The snapshot carries each tab's `state` but not the agent's, and the Left Bar dot and the Thinking pill read the agent's, so a phone drew a tab mid-turn under a green "Ready" agent. It leaves alone what the tabs do not decide - `error`, `connecting`, a running shell command (`busySource: 'terminal'`), and a CLI playbook (`cliActivity`) - and returns the session itself when nothing changes, so a steady snapshot writes nothing.
- **A tab's own process produced output:** `markTabBusyFromOutput(session, tabId)` in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/index.ts`, called from the data listener and, through `noteLiveTabOutput()` (`src/renderer/hooks/agent/internal/helpers/liveTabOutput.ts`), from the thinking and tool-execution listeners. Output on `{agentId}-ai-{tabId}` proves that turn is running, so a tab this client believes idle is marked busy. Event-driven rather than polled, and it covers every path that starts a turn without telling this client: a browser draining its own queue (nothing broadcasts that send, so the desktop's inventory painted it idle everywhere), an auto-retry resend, any future dispatch site that forgets the transition. It cannot strand a tab, because main emits a turn's output before its exit and that exit settles it. Callers must check the tab first and write only when it is NOT busy: the store helpers always produce a new sessions array, and one per streamed chunk would re-render the app.
- **Stopping a running turn (Stop pill, REST, `maestro-cli interrupt`):** `interruptTurn(sessionId, { tabId?, confirmForceKill? })` in `src/renderer/services/turnInterrupt.ts`; the result type, `isTurnProcessInScope()` and `parseInterruptTurnResult()` in `src/shared/turnInterrupt.ts`. The desktop pill, the REST route and the CLI each had their own Stop, and the remote one signalled the bare `{id}-ai`, which no per-tab turn uses, so a remote stop ended nothing while writing the agent idle. Now all three run this one function. No `tabId` is the agent-level Stop: every busy tab, every live `{id}-ai-*` process from main's table (forced-parallel siblings and a turn the store already settled), consults, retries, compactions, the pending synopsis and a terminal-mode command. A `tabId` narrows every one of those to that tab, and the agent's other tabs run on. It ignores `inputMode`: Stop ends AI turns, while terminal tabs take `send-terminal --control C`. Nothing in scope running answers `wasBusy: false` and writes nothing, so a repeat is safe. Hook-owned deps (synopsis cancel, the gated queue dispatch) arrive through `registerTurnInterruptDeps()`; never thread them as props. Main waits for the stopped processes to leave its table (`snapshotAiTurnProcesses()` / `waitForAiTurnsToExit()` in `src/main/utils/agent-busy.ts`) and reports `exited`.
- **Stop and a pending auto-retry:** `stopRetriesForAgent(sessionId, tabId?)` in `src/renderer/stores/retryStore.ts`, called first thing by `interruptTurn()`; `tabId` narrows it to one tab's outage and the return value counts what it ended. A countdown is work the agent WILL do, so leaving it alone made Stop resend the stopped prompt half a minute later. It ends every outage the agent has open as `stopped` and HOLDS (pauses) the failed prompt an outage parked in the queue, so Stop's own queue drain cannot send it straight back out. Distinct from `cancelRetry`, the outage card's Stop, which raises the blocking error state on purpose; the agent's Stop raises nothing.
- **Killing every AI process an agent owns:** `killAgentAiProcesses(agentId)` in `src/renderer/services/process.ts`. Turns run per tab (`{agentId}-ai-{tabId}`, plus `-fp-<n>` for forced-parallel), so killing the bare `{agentId}-ai` - what agent delete, group delete, remote delete, and provider switch used to do - matched nothing and left every running turn alive with no tab to stop it from. The ids come from the MAIN process table, which also reaches a closed tab still draining; if that read fails it still kills the legacy id rather than nothing. Restarting ONE failed tab is not this: `restartAgentAfterError` kills `{agentId}-ai-{agentErrorTabId}` only, so a healthy turn on another tab survives.
- **Toggling the unread filters:** `toggleTabUnreadFilter()`, `toggleAllUnreadFilters()`, and `areUnreadFiltersActive()` in `src/renderer/services/unreadFilters.ts`. The agent filter (`uiStore.showUnreadAgentsOnly`) and the tab filter (`uiStore.showUnreadOnly`) are independent state, but the tab one is NOT a plain setter: entering it saves the active AI tab in `preFilterActiveTabId` and leaving it restores that tab, so writing `setShowUnreadOnly()` directly strands the user on whatever tab the filtered view left them on. The combined toggle counts as active only when BOTH are on, so a half-filtered view COMPLETES rather than clears - "either one" would make one press turn everything off, the opposite of what a half-filtered view asks for. Everything reads the stores at call time rather than a render snapshot, because the palette entry, the keyboard shortcut, and the tab bar button fire from different render trees and a stale snapshot is the one way they disagree about direction. Do NOT inline a fourth copy of the save/restore dance.
- **Creating a tab straight into a tile:** `tileNewTab()` / `canTileNewTab()` in `src/renderer/hooks/tabs/tileNewTab.ts` - creates an AI/file/terminal/browser tab and splits the current view around it in one session update (the palette's "Tile New ... Below" family). It reuses the drag path's `tileTabIntoGroup` / `createGroupFromDrop`, so keyboard and drag produce identical layouts. Do NOT hand-roll "create a tab, then tile it": the ordinary new-tab paths all clear `activeGroupId` to claim the panel, which tears down the group you are building - that is why this mints every tab non-activating.
- **Restoring an AI tab's scroll position:** the `initialScrollTop` + `initialIsAtBottom` pair, consumed by `useTerminalOutputScroll()` in `src/renderer/components/TerminalOutput/hooks/useTerminalOutputScroll.ts`. `initialScrollTop` is an ABSOLUTE offset and the transcript keeps growing while the tab is off screen, so restoring it verbatim strands a tail-following tab however far the agent wrote in the meantime - and because the offset is then far above the new bottom, the restore also PAUSES auto-scroll, so the transcript will not even follow the output that stranded them. `initialIsAtBottom !== false` is the real gate (an `undefined` legacy tab counts as at-bottom, matching the unread gate in `useAgentDataListener`); the saved offset is honored ONLY for a tab deliberately parked mid-history, where new entries append below and what the user was reading has not moved. One `requestAnimationFrame` is not enough either - images, web fonts, markdown reflow and code highlighting all grow `scrollHeight` after the first frame, so a deep offset clamps short and the tab opens above where it was left; the restore re-applies under a `ResizeObserver` until it lands or the user takes over.
- **How big a tab's conversation is and how long it ran:** `computeTabConversationStats(logs)` and `formatConversationDuration(ms)` in `src/shared/tabConversationStats.ts`. Three surfaces read the figures from here - the Context Details popover in the main panel header (`MainPanelHeader.tsx`), the HTML export (`generateTabExportHtml` in `src/renderer/utils/tabExport.ts`), and `maestro-cli sessions` (`src/cli/commands/list-sessions.ts`) - so what the app shows live, what a saved transcript prints, and what the CLI reports cannot drift. The CLI's own count used to filter on `stdin`, which is not a `LogEntry['source']` at all, so it silently reported the `stdout` count and nothing else. **The CLI has TWO readers and both report these figures.** Non-Claude agents are counted straight from the stored tab. The Claude reader derives its numbers by scanning `~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl`, because that file is the only record of a session Maestro never rendered (one started in a bare terminal, or predating the install), so it cannot simply be replaced - instead `overlayTabConversationStats()` swaps in the tab's figures for every session a tab exists for, which is exactly the set the popover can show. A tab with an EMPTY log is deliberately left out of that index: overlaying it would report `0 msgs` for a real conversation whose transcript is only on disk. The overlay runs after `listClaudeSessions` has sorted and paginated, which is safe only because it sorts on `modifiedAt` and searches `sessionName` / `firstMessage` - neither figure is a sort or filter key, so a new sort option over `messageCount` would have to move the overlay earlier. It lives in `shared/` rather than `renderer/utils/` for that third caller: the CLI tsconfig has no DOM lib, so importing the renderer type barrel from there fails on modules that reference `window`. Its input type is structural (`ConversationLogEntry`, just `timestamp` and `source`) for the same reason, and the generic hands the caller's own entries back unchanged. **`totalMessages` counts EVERY conversation entry**, tool calls and thinking included, because the export draws one card per entry and its Messages card has always meant "cards in this document" - the user/agent split is returned separately (`userMessages` / `aiMessages`) for surfaces that want to break it down, which is what the popover puts in the row's hover title. The source list is `CONVERSATION_LOG_SOURCES`, so a new transcript log source is admitted once rather than in each surface's own literal array. **The duration is a WALL-CLOCK SPAN** between the first and last entry, not working time: a tab left open overnight reports the night. For ONE tab that is the honest reading of "how long has this been going", but do not present it as effort, and do not copy the pattern to a group chat - a room's span is meaningless, which is why `computeGroupChatActivity` exists instead. Below two entries the span is zero, and `formatConversationDuration` renders that as `0m` rather than the `0s` a bare `formatDurationCompact` would give, matching what the export has always printed. Walking the log array is O(entries), so the popover computes it only while it is actually open.
- **What a snooze opener was handed:** `resolveSnoozeTarget(session, id)` in `src/renderer/utils/snoozeHelpers.ts`, returning `{ tabId, tabLabel, canRunWakePrompt }`. Every entry point gets ONE id and no kind - the tab strip passes whatever chip was right-clicked (any of the four kinds, or a tiled GROUP), while the shortcut and the palette pass the active AI tab - so the kind is resolved here rather than at each opener. It checks `tabGroups` first, then the unified tab order, which is the same lookup `snoozeTab` itself does, so an id this resolves is an id that will park. `MainPanel.handleOpenSnooze` searched `aiTabs` alone and returned early for everything else, which made "Snooze Tab" on file/terminal/browser chips and "Snooze group" on a group chip silently do nothing. `canRunWakePrompt` must be DERIVED, never hard-coded beside it: only a conversation can be prompted on return (`canSnoozeRunWakePrompt`), so a file, terminal, or browser tab hides the field rather than collecting a prompt that could never be sent, and a group qualifies on holding one AI pane. Both ends of a snooze's transcript mirror run `collectSnoozedAiTabs(entry)` - a group holds one transcript per AI pane, and a copy taken on the way in that nothing releases on the way out is a mirror kept forever.
- **Parking, waking, dismissing, or rescheduling ONE snooze:** `snoozeTabWithMirror()`, `wakeSnoozeNow()`, `dismissSnoozeNow()`, `rescheduleSnoozeNow()` in `src/renderer/services/snoozeActions.ts`, with `runRemoteSnoozeCommand()` beside them for the CLI's far end. None of the four verbs is just the store write, and the steps they drag behind them fail INVISIBLY: the transcript mirror must be taken BEFORE the snooze (the write removes the tabs and their `agentSessionId`s from the session, so a mirror taken afterwards has nothing left to resolve a transcript from - and a snooze easily outruns the provider's retention, which is why the mirror exists at all), and a wake or dismiss must RELEASE that mirror and RECORD the resolution (releasing rehydrates a transcript the provider aged out while the tab was parked; the record is the note the user left themselves). Three callers share it - the Snooze dialog, the Snoozed Tabs list, and `maestro-cli snooze` - and the sequence was written out at each of the first two before this module. `sessionId` is optional and defaults to the ACTIVE agent, which is what every click path means; a scripted snooze names its own agent, since "active" is whatever the human happens to be looking at. `announce` suppresses the NOTICE, never the work (`--background` lands here): the parked tab leaving the strip IS the verb rather than placement, so what the flag buys is not flashing "Snoozed until ..." at someone looking at a different agent. The wire shape is `src/shared/snoozeCommands.ts` - ONE `snooze_command` message with an `action`, not six messages, because the six verbs differ only in which id they name. Do NOT hand-roll another mirror-then-write sequence at a new call site. See [RENDERER-SERVICES.md](RENDERER-SERVICES.md#snoozeactionsts---the-four-single-snooze-operations-with-their-side-effects).

## Composer, queue, and transcript

- **Command mode (`!`) is STATE, not a text prefix, and it is a LADDER:** the live value is `composerInputStore.aiCommandMode` (`'off' | 'shell' | 'ai'`), persisted per tab as `AITab.commandMode`. `!` on an EMPTY composer climbs one rung (agent -> `'shell'`, a literal command line -> `'ai'`, a plain-English request the model turns into one); Escape on an empty composer climbs back down; the caret never leaves the textarea. The bang is consumed on entry and never appears in the draft, so NEVER test the text for a leading `!` to detect the mode - a real command can contain bangs, and the draft won't start with one. **Never test the value for truthiness either: `'off'` is a truthy string.** Compare against the literals, or use `isShellCommandMode()` / `isAiCommandMode()`. Persisted values from older builds are booleans (`true` meant `'shell'`), so always read `AITab.commandMode` through `normalizeComposerCommandMode()`. The invariant: the same string is a shell command, a request for one, or an agent message depending only on this value, so any path that persists or restores `inputValue` MUST carry `commandMode` with it (`syncAiInputToSession` reads it from the store itself so a caller can't forget). Helpers all live in `src/renderer/utils/shellCommandInput.ts`. See [RENDERER-SERVICES.md](RENDERER-SERVICES.md#command-mode-is-state-not-a-text-prefix).
- **Run a shell command from the chat:** `dispatchShellCommand()` (records it in recall history, then runs it) / `runShellCommand()` / `cancelShellCommand()` / `resolveCommandCwd()` in `src/renderer/services/shellCommand.ts`. This is command mode (`!command` in the AI composer). Any new surface that runs a one-off command and shows its output in a transcript should reuse this rather than calling `window.maestro.process.runCommand` directly - the synthetic `{sessionId}-shell-{runId}` id is what keeps the output out of the agent listeners. AI command mode routes its accepted suggestion through the SAME `dispatchShellCommand`, so it runs, records, and recalls identically; do NOT add a parallel run path for it. The one difference is the optional `request` field, stamped onto the card only when the caller passes one - its presence means exactly "this was generated, not typed", and it is what the card shows as provenance and what the next AI command sees as the `Asked:` line. `dispatchShellCommand` forwards its whole options object to `runShellCommand` rather than naming fields, so a new field cannot be silently dropped in between. See [RENDERER-SERVICES.md](RENDERER-SERVICES.md#shellcommandts-200-lines).
- **Running a shell code fence from an agent's reply:** `requestCodeFenceCommand({ sessionId, tabId, command, mode })` / `CODE_FENCE_COMMAND_EVENT` in `src/renderer/services/codeFenceCommand.ts`, with `isRunnableShellLanguage()` and `commandFromShellFence()` in `src/renderer/utils/shellCommandInput.ts`, drawn by `<CodeRunButton>` (`src/renderer/components/CodeFence/CodeRunButton.tsx`). Same shape as the Codex followup chip and for the same reason: `LogItem` renders `<MarkdownRenderer>` at several call sites, so the click raises one app-level `CustomEvent` that a single listener in `App.tsx` answers. The command is agent-authored, so the listener checks the request's session and tab through `resolveCodexFollowup()`: a click that lands after the user switched away degrades to a prefill, never a run in a conversation they are not looking at. The run itself goes through `dispatchShellCommand()`, so it records and recalls like a typed `!` command. A transcript-style fence (`$ npm test` plus output) runs only its `$` lines. `fish`, `powershell` and `bat` are deliberately not runnable: command mode runs the user's default shell. Do NOT add a second run path.
- **Ask the model for a shell command (AI command mode):** `requestAiCommand()` / `acceptAiCommand()` / `dismissAiCommand()` in `src/renderer/services/aiCommand.ts`, state in `src/renderer/stores/aiCommandStore.ts`, prompt in `src/prompts/ai-command.md` (id `ai-command`, editable in Settings -> Maestro Prompts). The suggestion runs on the tab's OWN provider at its current model and effort, read-only and with tools disabled. Do NOT hand-roll another one-shot model call for this: the main-process handler (`src/main/ipc/handlers/aiCommand.ts`) goes through the shared `groomContext()` runner.
- **A composer's send column (notification bell + Send):** `<ComposerSendColumn>` in `src/renderer/components/ChatComposer/ComposerSendColumn.tsx`. The AI composer and the Group Chat composer drew two copies of the same bell, popover and Send button. The shared column knows nothing about sessions, tabs or rooms: each surface says what Send means through props - `sendTitle`, `sendDisabled` (drawn in the border color and refusing clicks), `sendTone` (`warning` is the room's "this will queue" tint), and an optional `sendMenu`. `sendMenu.available` is what puts `aria-haspopup` / `aria-expanded` on the button, and `sendMenu.shouldOpen()` is read AT CLICK TIME, because whether the menu is a real choice depends on live store state (is the agent working, is there a draft) that must not become a render dependency of the composer. A click on an open menu closes it without sending. The AI surface binds it as `NotificationSendControls` (`InputArea/components/`), which owns the store reads, the terminal-mode title and the `SendMenuPopover`; a room passes no menu, since a busy room queues through main. The phone toolbar's inline Send in `ToolbarControls` is a separate control and is not this.
- **A composer's Attach Image input:** `<ImageAttachInput ref>` and `stageImageFiles(files, options)` in `src/renderer/components/ChatComposer/ImageAttachInput.tsx`. The hidden file input and the read that stages what was picked. Every picked file is read as a data URL and staged through `addStagedImageIfUnique` (so a duplicate flashes "Duplicate image ignored"), and a read that fails or is ABORTED is reported through `captureException` with `extra.component` set to the surface's `reportComponent` and flashed, rather than vanishing. The Group Chat copy this replaced had no read-error handling at all. The attach button clicks the input through the forwarded REF, never `document.getElementById`: the AI toolbar draws its button in a phone row and a desktop row, and an id lookup resolves to whichever element mounted first once a second composer is on screen. `id` survives only as a stable test hook. `checkFile` refuses a file before it is read and returns the message to flash; Group Chat passes its type allowlist and 10MB cap there, and AI chat deliberately passes none - adding the room's checks to AI chat is a behaviour change, not a cleanup. `readBlobAsDataUrl()` in `utils/imageData.ts` is not a substitute here: its promise cannot tell an error from an abort, and never settles on an abort.
- **Trimming a text paste in a composer:** `applyTrimmedTextPaste(e, currentValue, setValue)` and `clipboardHasImage(data)` in `src/renderer/components/ChatComposer/pasteTrim.ts`.
