One registry serves `maestro-cli open <surface> [--tab] [--group] [--agent]`, the main-process `open_modal` validator, and the renderer dispatcher, and it carries the discovery hints (hotkey id, command-palette text, click target) so an agent can teach the manual path while opening the pane. Adding a modal means adding one entry, not touching four files - and if it has tabs, list them there so `--tab` can deep-link. A surface that can open on ONE agent group says so with `groupClick` (how a person gets that by hand; today only `delegation-graph`), which is also what makes `--group` valid for it: `resolveUiSurfaceTarget(surface, { tab, subTab, group, agent })` is the one validator for all of them, run by the CLI, by the bridge handler and by `openUiSurface(request)` (which takes the same `OpenUiSurfaceRequest` the bridge carries), and it refuses a group on any other surface. The registry knows no groups, so the id is passed through as given: the CLI resolves it first (`resolveGroupId()`, a prefix is fine), and a group that is gone by the time the renderer acts opens the surface unscoped. A surface that is ONE agent's says so with `agentClick` (today only `agent-settings`, the Edit Agent form), which makes `--agent` valid AND required: a hotkey acts on the agent the user is looking at, and a CLI caller cannot see which that is. The CLI resolves the id (`resolveAgentId()`), and the bridge handler refuses an agent that is gone, because the renderer would open nothing without a word.
- **Whether a modal takes the window over:** `DESTINATION_MODALS`, `closeOtherDestinations()`, and `registerExternalDestination()` in `src/renderer/stores/modalStore.ts`. A DESTINATION is a full-window view you GO to (Settings, Usage Dashboard, Director's Notes, Symphony, Cue, Marketplace, Process Monitor, System Logs, Agent Sessions, Memory, plus the Document Graph); only one is ever open, and `openModal`/`toggleModal` evict whichever other one was up. Before this, ordering was decided by each surface's fixed rank in `MODAL_PRIORITIES` instead of by what the user just asked for: the Usage Dashboard (540) opened BEHIND Director's Notes (848), and a main-panel destination opened under any overlay changed nothing on screen - both read as a dead keystroke. A dialog that answers a question ABOUT the surface beneath it is NOT a destination and is meant to layer (confirmations, rename prompts, `cueYamlEditor`, the dashboard's per-agent detail, the Symphony agent picker). Do NOT try to fix a "wrong surface won" bug by re-ranking `MODAL_PRIORITIES` - decide membership here instead. A destination owned by another store (currently only the Document Graph in `fileExplorerStore`) joins via `registerExternalDestination()` and calls `closeOtherDestinations()` on its own way in; `modalStore` must never import that store back. `DESTINATION_SHORTCUT_IDS` in the same file is the second half of that membership question and is DERIVED from it: the window keyboard handler blocks most shortcuts while a modal is up, and consults this set to decide which hotkeys still get through. It used to be a hardcoded chord test (Alt+Cmd plus `l`/`p`/`u`/`s`), which let three destinations through and killed the rest - Director's Notes to Usage Dashboard worked because `Opt+Cmd+U` matched the chord, and the way back did nothing because `Cmd+Shift+O` did not, so switching between two full-window surfaces worked in one direction only and read as a dead key. Deriving the set from `UI_SURFACES` means adding a destination cannot silently drop it back out of the guard, and a user who REBINDS a surface keeps a working hotkey, which a chord test can never promise. Do NOT add a chord back to that guard to fix a "hotkey does nothing over an open surface" bug - give the surface a `shortcutId` in `UI_SURFACES` instead.
- **Opening a modal from inside Settings:** `launchFromSettings(open)` in `src/renderer/utils/launchFromSettings.ts`. Settings renders at `z-[9999]`, so a launcher inside it (Extensions -> "Open Pianola", a plugin's `modal` panel) opens the new modal BEHIND Settings and the click looks like a no-op. This closes Settings first, then runs the launcher. Not for confirmations or pickers that are meant to stack on top of Settings. See [UI-PATTERNS.md](UI-PATTERNS.md#modals-launched-from-inside-settings-launchfromsettings).
- **Modal layer:** `useModalLayer()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useModalLayer.ts` (do NOT use manual `registerLayer()` boilerplate)
- **Whether a surface is the topmost layer:** `useIsTopLayer(priority)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useIsTopLayer.ts`. Matching is by `MODAL_PRIORITIES` value rather than layer id, because `useModalLayer` owns the registration and never hands the id back. Reach for it whenever a surface binds an unmodified key or wants to reclaim focus (`AutoRunExpandedModal`) - do NOT re-derive `getLayers()[length - 1]` inline.
- **Modal / find-bar ESC pill:** `<EscCloseButton>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/EscCloseButton.tsx`. Every modal, palette, and find bar needs a graphical exit - Escape alone strands users on remote desktop and tablets. Pass an `onClose` that does exactly what the Escape handler does (extract it into a shared `useCallback` rather than duplicating the body). Do NOT hand-roll the `px-2 py-0.5 rounded text-xs font-bold` pill; the inert `<div>` version used to be copy-pasted in nine places. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Every Modal Needs a Graphical Exit](UI-PATTERNS.md#every-modal-needs-a-graphical-exit-escclosebutton).
- **A modal default sized to the screen:** `viewportModalSize({ width, height })` in `src/renderer/utils/modalSizing.ts`, passed as `defaultSize` to `useResizableModal`. Only for surfaces the user pans around INSIDE (a graph, a dashboard) - a form or dialog has a natural width and more room buys it nothing. A fixed default that reads as generous on a laptop is a postage stamp on a 5K display, and the user re-drags it on every machine. Memoize it once per mount: the hook re-clamps the live size on `resize`, and a default that moves under it fights that listener.
- **A widget that floats over the whole desktop:** `createFloatingWidgetWindow()` in `src/main/floating-widgets/floating-widget-window.ts`, `publishStoreState()` / `mirrorStoreState()` / `sendWidgetCommand()` / `onWidgetCommand()` in `src/renderer/utils/windowStoreMirror.ts`, `floatingWidgetsAvailable()` in `src/renderer/utils/floatingWidgetMode.ts`; ids, specs and command guards in `src/shared/floatingWidgets.ts`. A surface that must float over OTHER apps (the Throughput Gauge, media player, voice HUD) gets its own frameless always-on-top window instead of an in-app card. The controller is Quick Chat's window recipe (macOS `panel`, every Space, bounds persisted per widget and clamped to a connected display, never saved below `minSize`) except that it shows with `showInactive()` and never takes focus. The voice HUD's `acappella-voice` spec uses the originating app window as authority, `VoiceSessionProjection` in `src/shared/acappella/hud-state.ts` is its shared data shape, and `isVoiceWidgetCommand()` validates controls. `VoiceWidget` reuses `VoiceHud` with protocol subscriptions and persistence disabled. Each widget has ONE authority window that publishes its state and answers commands; every other window mirrors and never publishes back, so state cannot echo. Do not hand-roll IPC per widget, keep a second copy of the state, or persist widget geometry from the renderer. The browser build cannot open windows, so every host branches on `floatingWidgetsAvailable()` and keeps its in-app card for that case. Steps and rules: `UI-PATTERNS.md` -> "Floating Widget Windows".
- **Audio/video playback:** media NEVER becomes a file preview tab. `handleOpenFileTab()` (`src/renderer/hooks/tabs/internal/useFilePreviewTabHandlers.ts`) is the single choke point every open path funnels through, and it diverts playable media to `useMediaPlaybackStore.openMedia()` (or `enqueueMedia()` when the caller passes `mediaMode: 'queue'`) before a tab can be created. Any path that opens SEVERAL files must pass `mediaMode: 'queue'` for every media file after the first - see `openFilesInOrder()` in `useFileContextMenu.ts` - or each open steals the player from the one before and only the last survives. The only surface media appears on is `FloatingMediaPlayer` - there is no docked/in-panel placement, so do NOT add one. Exactly ONE player exists app-wide, owned by `MediaPlaybackHost` (`src/renderer/components/MediaPlayback/`), mounted once in `App.tsx` and never unmounted: anything rendered per-tab or per-agent is torn down on switch, and removing a media element from the document pauses it. Do NOT mount a second player to play two files at once - the single-player invariant is what makes overlapping audio impossible; `stepMediaItem()` walks the queue in open order, a finished file hands off via `advanceAfterEnded()`, and the history menu jumps by recency. **Queue and history are two lists with opposite lifetimes:** the queue persists (settings key `mediaPlayerQueue`, debounced writes, hydrated in `settingsStore`) while history is per-boot, so history holds whole `MediaItem`s rather than IDs into the queue - never "simplify" it back into pointers, or dropping a queue entry would rewrite what the user already heard. **"Open the player" is one store action, `openPlayer()`**, not a sequence a caller reassembles: the command palette entry and its shortcut both call it, and it resolves `selectMediaPlayerTargetId()` at INVOKE time so a queue that advanced while the palette sat open cannot strand it on a finished file. There is exactly ONE palette entry for it - a second, "Show Floating Media Player", used to appear beside it whenever the widget happened to be minimized, and two commands a word apart that both mean "put the player on screen" is a choice the user has to stop and read for a distinction that is internal bookkeeping. **Both deliberate "show me the player" gestures raise `focusRequest`** (`openPlayer` and `restore`, consumed by a layout effect in `FloatingMediaPlayer`), because the widget is never unmounted and so has no mount to hang focus off: without it a keyboard-opened player is keyboard-DEAD until clicked, and Escape-to-minimize does nothing. Opening or queueing a FILE deliberately does not raise it - that would pull the caret out of whatever the user was typing in. It loads PAUSED at the remembered position - opening the widget is a request to SEE it, not to start listening - and it RE-QUEUES a target that only survives in history, because only queue entries are ever drawn, so activating a history-only id resolved a target, rendered nothing, and read as the feature being broken. That is also why `closeItem` keeps an item's `resumeTimes` entry when the item lands in history: reopening from "recently played" has to resume where the user stopped, and only a track leaving BOTH lists loses its bookmark. Classify media with `getOpenedMediaKind()` in `src/renderer/utils/mediaItems.ts`. **A `file://` link clicked in markdown must go through `openFileUrl()`** (`src/renderer/utils/openFileUrl.ts`), never straight to `shell.openPath`: the file-link plugins emit `file://` for any path outside the project root, so an agent mentioning `~/Scratch/x.mp3` was handing it to the OS player and bypassing the choke point entirely. It PERCENT-DECODES the path, which is not optional - a `file://` href is a URL, so both an agent writing one by hand and mdast-util-to-hast's `normalizeUri` encode it, and the literal `%20` handed to `fs.readFile` is an ENOENT that `handleMainPanelFileClick` reports by simply returning. Every media file in a folder with a space in its name was a link that did nothing when clicked. Same rule and same reason as `resolveLocalImagePath` in `Markdown/components/LocalImage.tsx`; do NOT strip the scheme without decoding at a new call site. **The player's height is derived, never stored** (`src/renderer/utils/mediaFloatGeometry.ts`): chrome for audio, chrome + `width / aspect` for video, where the aspect is the file's real `videoWidth / videoHeight` and the chrome is MEASURED (font metrics differ per platform, so a hard-coded transport height letterboxes video somewhere). Only position and a per-kind width persist, so a mixed queue reshapes itself as it advances. **The toast stack steps OVER the widget, never through it:** both open in the bottom-right corner and toasts render at `Z_LAYERS.TOAST` (100000) against the player's 60, so an arriving notification painted straight across the widget and read as the player having closed itself. `FloatingMediaPlayer` publishes its live rect as `floatFootprint` - expressed from the bottom and right edges, so the lane compares the two without needing a viewport measurement of its own - and `ToastContainer` raises itself by `mediaFloatLaneLift()`. Do NOT fix a collision like this by raising the player's z-index instead: toasts have to stay readable over modals, and a video frame would swallow them. **Minimize and close are different actions and must stay that way:** the `-` button sets `dismissed` (widget hidden, audio continues, `NowPlayingIndicator` in the Left Bar header becomes its play/pause via `requestToggle` plus a separate restore button), while `x` calls `closeItem` (player released, sound stops, rest of the queue intact). Minimize is a `hidden` STYLE FLAG on `FloatingMediaPlayer`, never a different render branch in `MediaPlaybackHost` - moving the media element in the React tree unmounts it, and that runs the spec's pause steps. **Escape minimizes, never closes**, and it is scoped to the widget (a `tabIndex={-1}` root plus `onKeyDown`) rather than registered with the layer stack: the player is not modal, it floats over a workspace the user keeps typing in, so Escape is only its to claim while focus is inside it. Wiring Escape to `closeItem` would let a reflex kill playback, which is the one thing the minimize/close split exists to prevent. It defers twice - to an open queue/history list, and to `document.fullscreenElement`, since Escape is already how a user leaves fullscreen - and `MediaViewer` swallows it only when its speed menu is open, so exactly one thing happens per press. **Neither list shows the loaded track:** history records DEPARTURES (a track enters when it stops being active) and `historyForActiveChange` also strips the incoming id, while the queue menu filters via `upcomingMediaItems()` at display time - the active item must stay in `items` or `stepMediaItem` loses prev/next. `clearQueue` keeps the loaded track, since clearing what is queued must not stop playback. **Queueing un-hides the widget** (`enqueueMedia` clears `dismissed` and `dormant`), and that is deliberate rather than a violation of the minimize invariant above: adding files is a request to SEE the queue, so doing it into a hidden player produces no visible response anywhere in the app and reads as the command having done nothing. It is safe because un-hiding is not interrupting - the loaded track is untouched and keeps playing. It fires even when NOTHING was added: queueing files that are all already queued adds zero items, and gating the reveal on `added > 0` made "Add to Play Queue" a silent no-op on exactly the second attempt, which is when a user retries a command that appeared to do nothing. For the same reason the idle branch loads the first file the user POINTED AT rather than the first one newly added, so re-queueing something the queue already holds still puts a widget on screen. A control that hides itself must not silently stop media, and a close button that only hid it leaves audio coming from nowhere. User docs: [media-player.md](../media-player.md).
- **Drafting a pull request title and body on a local model:** `draftPullRequest()` / `draftPullRequestRequest()` in `src/main/local-models/git-drafts.ts`, over the shared contract in `src/shared/gitDrafts.ts` (`GIT_DRAFT_PR_REPLY_SCHEMA`, `parsePullRequestDraftReply()`, `formatPullRequestDraftInput()`, `capDraftLines()`, `PullRequestDraftResult`), prompt `git-draft-pr-local`, drawn by `<PullRequestDraftButton>` (`src/renderer/components/PullRequestDraftButton.tsx`). One function behind both callers: the Create Pull Request form's "Draft with local model" button (`localModels:draftPullRequest`) and `maestro-cli models run git-drafts --cwd <path>` (`local_models_git_draft`). It runs the `git-drafts` Local Models assignment and never falls back to an agent: the task has no agent path, so `off` (the default) hides the button and `unavailable` disables it with the readiness reason. The model reads a SUMMARY (`git log --oneline base..HEAD` plus `git diff --stat base...HEAD`), capped in lines and characters by `capDraftLines()` so it fits a 4,096 token local context; when the log is cut the newest commits are kept and the model is told how many it did not see, and the diff summary's totals line always survives. It only returns text: the form fills its two fields and the person presses Create PR. There is no plain-text fallback for a reply that is not the JSON object, because splitting prose into a title and a body is a guess. Local checkouts only, matching `git:createPR`. Do NOT add a second draft path for a new git surface (a commit dialog, say): add a prompt and a schema beside these and reuse the caps.
- **Suggesting tabs to close, snooze, or wake on a local model:** `suggestTabCleanup()` in `src/main/local-models/tab-cleanup.ts` (with `collectTabCleanupTabs()` and `configureTabCleanup()`), over the shared contract in `src/shared/tabCleanup.ts` (`TAB_CLEANUP_REPLY_SCHEMA`, `parseTabCleanupReply()`, `selectTabCleanupCandidates()`, `formatTabCleanupInput()`, `rebindTabCleanupCandidates()`, `guardTabCleanupSuggestions()`, `tabCleanupProtection()`, `tabCleanupApplyCommand()`, `TabCleanupResult`), prompt `tab-cleanup-local`, checked and applied in the renderer by `resolveTabCleanupLiveState()` / `applyTabCleanupSuggestions()` in `src/renderer/services/tabCleanup.ts`, drawn by `<TabCleanupModal>` (`src/renderer/components/TabCleanupModal.tsx`). One function behind both callers: the Tab Cleanup Suggestions modal (`localModels:suggestTabCleanup`) and `maestro-cli tab suggest-cleanup` (`local_models_tab_cleanup`). **It only ever suggests, and the model is never trusted.** Losing a tab is the worst failure in this app, so four rules hold and each closes a specific hole. (1) A tab that is busy, starred, or holding unsent composer text is never suggested: it is left out of the prompt, and `guardTabCleanupSuggestions()` drops it again after the model, whatever the reply says. `tabCleanupProtection()` is the ONE rule for that; do NOT restate the three conditions at a call site. (2) The guard runs against a SECOND read of the app, taken after the model answered (`rebindTabCleanupCandidates()`): a local model can take a minute, and a guard run against the facts the model was shown would pass a tab that started a turn in the meantime. (3) The model answers with handles (`T1`..`T40`), never ids, and the reply schema is ONE fixed schema with all forty handles in its enum: a schema built per request would compile and cache a grammar per tab count, and real UUIDs cost more tokens than every other fact on the line. (4) The store main reads is a few seconds behind the renderer, and a draft still being typed exists only there, so the renderer checks the LIVE tab when a row is drawn and again the instant before it is applied. Only the modal applies, only rows the user ticked, and no row is pre-ticked. The CLI verb is read-only and has no `--apply` on purpose: it prints `tabCleanupApplyCommand()` for each suggestion, which is the ordinary `tab close` / `snooze tab` / `snooze wake` verb. Hidden consult tabs are never read (`tab.hidden`), so a suggestion cannot name a tab with no chip. `off` is the default and nothing falls back to an agent.
- **Writing a Computer History digest (local model or agent):** the scheduler `DigestScheduler` in `src/main/computer-history/digests.ts` picks ONE writer by `digests.source` in the store's `config.json` (`DigestSource`, `DIGEST_SOURCES` in `src/shared/computer-history/types.ts`; `normalizeDigestSource()`, `DIGEST_SOURCE_LABELS`, `describeDigestWriter()` in `src/shared/computer-history/config.ts`). `local` is `createHistoryDigestWriter()` in `src/main/local-models/history-digests.ts`, which runs the `history-digests` Local Models task (prompt `computer-history-digest-local`); `agent` is the consult in `src/main/computer-history/digest-consult.ts`. The model's input is built by the pure `renderSegmentForDigest()`, `renderDigestsForRollup()` and `buildLocalDigestInstruction()` in `src/shared/computer-history/digestInput.ts`, and fenced with `fenceUntrusted()` from `src/shared/computer-history/status.ts`. The setting is drawn by `<DigestWriterField>` in `src/renderer/components/Settings/tabs/EncoreTab/components/DigestWriterField.tsx`. Before this, an agent was the only writer, so every digest sent screen text to a cloud provider and spent its tokens. Rules to keep. **Which model is the task assignment, never a second setting:** the Digest model picker, the Computer History digests row in Settings > Models > System Two and `maestro-cli models assign history-digests` write the same `localModelAssignments` entry; only the local-or-agent switch lives in `config.json`. **A local model has no tools and a 4096-token context,** so the writer reads the files itself and reads a long input in pieces (`chunkText()`): notes per piece, then one call that merges the notes. One piece that fails abandons the digest. Do NOT hand a local model file paths. **The segment is bounded before it is chunked** (`DIGEST_MAX_INPUT_CHARS`): each event's text is cut to a share of the budget, an unchanged snapshot is skipped, and a window that still does not fit is sampled at an even stride so all 15 minutes are covered. **A source with no writer chosen queues nothing** (`HistoryDigestWriter.hasModel()` for `local`, a null `agentId` for `agent`), so an unassigned model is not a failure every 15 minutes. **There is no fallback between the writers:** a local model that cannot run is reported (`lastError`, plus one `reportLocalTaskUnavailable()` notice) and never swapped for the agent, which would send the history to a provider the user did not choose. **A config written before the source existed keeps its behavior** (`normalizeDigestSource()`: a chosen agent reads as `agent`, everything else as `local`). Every call is `background: true` and waits on `whenNoInteractiveLocalTask()`, because nobody is waiting on a digest.
- **Gating a Cue run on a local model (`gate`):** the pure contract in `src/shared/cue/gate.ts` (`CUE_GATE_REPLY_SCHEMA`, `parseCueGateReply()`, `compactCueGateEvent()`, `formatCueGateInput()`, `resolveCueGateDecision()`, `withCueGateRecord()` / `readCueGateRecord()`, `CUE_GATE_ON_UNAVAILABLE`), the model call `evaluateCueGate()` in `src/main/local-models/cue-gate.ts` (prompt `cue-gate-local`), applied in exactly one place, `dispatchSubscription()` in `src/main/cue/cue-dispatch-service.ts`, and recorded by `recordSkippedRun()` in `src/main/cue/cue-run-manager.ts`. A subscription's `gate` is a condition in plain language; the `cue-gate` task answers yes or no against the event payload before a run is queued, and a "no" records a `gated` event with the reason instead of spawning. It exists for COST (most Cue events do not need a cloud turn), and it is NOT a security control: the payload is untrusted text that can argue with a yes/no model. **The failure policy is fail OPEN and lives in `resolveCueGateDecision()` alone:** a gate whose model cannot answer (task `off`, which is the default; model missing; server down; timeout; a reply that is not a verdict; a throwing evaluator; an engine with no evaluator) starts the run and raises a `gateUnavailable` warning, because a missing gate model must not silently stop the user's automation, and `gate_on_unavailable: skip` is the per-subscription opt-in to fail closed. Do NOT branch on a check's status at another call site, and do NOT read an unparseable reply as a pass or a fail (`parseCueGateReply` returns `null` so a model that answers in prose decides nothing). **The Cue engine never imports the model stack:** the evaluator is injected as `CueEngineDeps.evaluateGate`, the standalone runner (`maestro-cli cue engine`) passes none, and absent means "unavailable", not "no gate". **`gated` is a `CueRunStatus` that is not a run**, so anything that counts runs, failures, or "the last run" asks `isCueRunSkipped()` (`src/shared/cue/contracts.ts`): the History predicate `CUE_EVENT_WORTH_SHOWING_SQL`, the group `failure_count`, the Cue stats aggregation, `derivePipelineHealth()`, and the dashboard's average runtime all leave it out, because gated events are the bulk of what a gate sees and would bury the real runs. Nothing raises a completion for one, so no chain step fires and no Conductor time is credited. The verdict rides the event payload under `gate`, which is what `cue_events` keeps, so the Activity Log can say why after a restart. Manual triggers (`payload.manual`) are never gated, checks for one subscription are chained through `createKeyedWriteQueue()` so runs start in arrival order, and both fields are in `triggerGroupKey()`.
- **Setting a Cue gate from any surface:** the field `<CueGateField>` in `src/renderer/components/CuePipelineEditor/panels/CueGateField.tsx`, the patch helpers `writeCueGateFields()` / `readCueGateSetting()` / `cueGateConditionProblem()` / `parseCueGateOnUnavailable()` in `src/shared/cue/gate.ts`, the engine write `CueEngine.setSubscriptionGate()` in `src/main/cue/cue-engine.ts`, and the membership rule `isSameTriggerGroup()` in `src/shared/cue/trigger-group-key.ts`. Four surfaces author a gate and must agree: the Gate field on a trigger node in the pipeline editor, the same field in the Scheduled Task form, `maestro-cli cue schedule --gate`, and `maestro-cli cue gate <subscription>` (the `set_cue_subscription_gate` bridge message). Three rules ride the helpers so no surface re-derives them. **The stored shape is canonical:** a blank condition is NO keys (never an empty string), the condition is trimmed, `null` removes the policy with the gate, and the default policy (`run`) is never written, because `triggerGroupKey` compares `gate` and `gate_on_unavailable` AS WRITTEN and a spelled-out default would split one trigger from a sibling that left it out. **A gate set by name lands on the whole trigger:** a trigger that fans out is several subscriptions in `cue.yaml`, so `setSubscriptionGate()` patches every subscription `isSameTriggerGroup()` matches - the same predicate Play (`triggerSubscription`) fires by - and reports each name it changed; gating one branch would leave the others firing on every event and would split the trigger node in two on the next editor load. It shares `patchSubscriptionRecords()` and the per-root write chain with `setSubscriptionEnabled()`, so do NOT add another read-mutate-write of `cue.yaml` for a single-field change. **A gate is two halves:** the condition lives on the subscription, the model is the `cue-gate` Local Models task, and that task is OFF by default. The engine fails open, so a condition with no model starts every run unchecked while looking live. Every surface that writes a gate therefore reports the task's readiness and what the policy does until it is ready (`CueGateField` through `useLocalModelTaskReadiness(revision, 'cue-gate')`, `cue gate` through `local_models_task_readiness`); do NOT add a gate input that omits it. `cli.trigger` gets no Gate field: it only fires from a manual run, and a manual run is never gated. On the bridge, an ABSENT `gate` is refused rather than read as a removal, so a caller that dropped the field cannot erase a gate.
- **Screening Cue text for prompt injection (`screen_injection`):** the list of untrusted variables `CUE_UNTRUSTED_TEMPLATE_VARIABLES` with `findCueUntrustedTemplateVariables()` and `cueEventCarriesUntrustedText()` in `src/shared/templateVariables.ts`; the pure contract in `src/shared/cue/injection-screen.ts` (`resolveCueInjectionScreen()`, `cueSubscriptionTemplates()`, `collectCueInjectionTargets()`, `screenCueInjectionTargets()`, `resolveCueInjectionDecision()`, `writeCueInjectionScreenFields()`, `readCueInjectionScreenSetting()`, `withCueInjectionRecord()` / `readCueInjectionRecord()`); the model call `scoreCueInjectionText()` in `src/main/local-models/cue-injection-screen.ts` over `runClassifierTask('injection-screen')`; applied only in `dispatchSubscription()` (`src/main/cue/cue-dispatch-service.ts`), after the gate. A new template variable that carries text someone else wrote is added to the LIST, with the `TemplateContext.cue` key it is substituted from; a test pins that the two name the same field, because a mismatch means the screen scores text other than what reaches the agent. Only what the subscription's templates reference is scored. Absent `screen_injection` is "apply the default" (an `action: command` subscription that substitutes an untrusted variable is screened), so no reader may turn it into `false`. The failure policy lives in `resolveCueInjectionDecision()` and nowhere else: a command fails CLOSED when the screen was asked for (the key is `true`, or a model is assigned and cannot run), any other action fails OPEN with a warning, and a command with the key absent while the task is `off` runs marked `ran-unscreened`, because blocking there would stop every existing command subscription on upgrade. `blocked-injection` is a `CueRunStatus` that was not spawned but IS a failure: `isCueRunSkipped()` (no run time or tokens) and `isCueRunBlocked()` (health, History, failure counts) are both true for it, so a reader picks by question. The screen is a prompt-injection classifier and NOT shell escaping. Authoring is `<CueInjectionScreenField>` in `src/renderer/components/CuePipelineEditor/panels/` and `maestro-cli cue screen`, both through `writeCueInjectionScreenFields()`, where the automatic mode and the default threshold are no keys at all (`triggerGroupKey` compares the keys as written).
- **Closing an AI tab of any agent, the way a click does:** `closeAiTabInSession(sessionId, tabId, endWizard?)` in `src/renderer/services/aiTabClose.ts`. The tab strip only closes a tab of the agent on screen, so this sequence lived inside `useAITabHandlers` bound to the active agent; Tab Cleanup Suggestions closes tabs across every agent and rides the same function, which `performTabClose` now calls. Do NOT re-inline it: the steps fail invisibly. The transcript snapshot (`snapshotClosedTabTranscript`) must be taken BEFORE the close, because afterwards the tab is gone from the session; the tab goes into the unified closed-tab history, which is what makes the close undoable with Reopen Closed Tab; a wizard tab is closed WITHOUT history and its wizard ended; and in the browser build the desktop owns the tab inventory, so the close is `requestDesktopTabClose`, not a local write. It asks no confirmation: a caller that should confirm (an unsent draft, a wizard in progress) does so first. `endWizard` is hook-bound, so a caller outside React that never closes wizard tabs may omit it. Known sibling: the remote close handler in `useRemoteIntegration.ts` still carries its own shorter copy (no snapshot, no draft clear) because it must report whether the store write happened inside the same update.
- **Naming a snoozed tab from either process:** `snoozedEntryLabel(entry)` and `clampSnoozeLabel(text)` in `src/shared/snooze.ts`. The label rules (an AI tab falls back to its opening message, a browser tab to its URL, a terminal to its working directory) are read by the Snoozed Tabs list and `maestro-cli snooze list` in the renderer and by tab cleanup in main, which passes records straight out of the sessions store. The input type is structural with every field optional for that reason. The renderer's `getSnoozedTabLabel()` delegates to it; do NOT write a second label switch over `entry.type`.
- **Opening a clicked link:** `openUrl(url, { ctrlKey })` in `src/renderer/utils/openUrl.ts`, over `parseUrlRoutingHint()` / `isWebUrl()` / `stripUrlRoutingHint()` in `src/shared/urlRoutingHint.ts`. `openUrl` is the ONE place that decides Maestro browser tab vs system browser: the `useSystemBrowser` setting, inverted by Ctrl OR Cmd (pass `e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey` - on macOS Ctrl-click is the secondary click, so `ctrlKey` alone leaves the inversion unreachable), and overridden per link by a routing hint (`maestro+https://...` always a Maestro tab, `system+https://...` always the system browser, prefix stripped before opening). Every link surface gates on `isWebUrl()` rather than `/^https?:\/\//`, or a hinted link silently stops being a link there. A hint in front of anything but http(s) is `invalid` and opens NOTHING: the fallback for an unknown scheme is `shell.openExternal`, which would hand `maestro+file://` or `system+ssh://` to the OS. Do NOT call `window.maestro.shell.openExternal` from a link's click handler (`linkify.tsx` did, and ignored both the setting and the hint).
- **Right-click menu on an image:** already done, app-wide. `<ImageContextMenuHost>` (`src/renderer/components/ImageContextMenuHost.tsx`) is mounted once in `App.tsx` and runs ONE delegated `contextmenu` listener that resolves the image from the click target, so raster `<img>`, inline `<svg>`, Mermaid charts, thumbnails, and the lightbox all get Copy Image / Save to Project / Save As with **zero per-surface wiring**. Do NOT add an `onContextMenu` to a new image surface or hand-roll copy/save buttons - a new surface is covered the moment it renders. To opt a surface out (it owns its own right-click behavior), put `data-no-image-menu` on its container. Export helpers live in `src/renderer/utils/imageExport.ts` (`saveImageToProject()` writes to `.maestro/diagrams/` and works over SSH). See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Right-Click Image Menu](UI-PATTERNS.md#right-click-image-menu-imagecontextmenuhost).
- **Saving generated image bytes to disk:** `saveImageDataUrlToDisk(dataUrl, defaultName?)` in `src/renderer/utils/imageExport.ts`. The sibling `saveImageElementToDisk()` exports an image that is already in the DOM; this one takes bytes produced on the fly (a page screenshot, a canvas render) that have no element to point at. Both go through the native save dialog and then `fs.writeImageFile` - `fs.writeFile` is UTF-8 and would corrupt raster bytes - and both fall back to a browser download when the dialog is unavailable (web renderer). A cancelled dialog reports `{ saved: false }` with no `error`, so a caller must not treat every non-save as a failure toast. Unlike `saveImageToProject()` it does not call `requestFileTreeRefresh`, because the user usually points it outside any workspace.
- **Reading a pasted or dropped image (or any Blob) as a data URL:** `readBlobAsDataUrl(blob)`, `imageFilesIn(dataTransfer)`, `downscaleImageDataUrl(src, maxEdge)` in `src/renderer/utils/imageData.ts`. `imageFilesIn` takes a paste's `clipboardData` or a drop's `dataTransfer` and returns its image files in order (a paste lists them under `items`, a drop under `files` too). `downscaleImageDataUrl` draws a WebP copy no longer than `maxEdge` on its longer side and hands back the original when it is already small or the canvas cannot draw, so a caller never loses the image. The Quick Chat window stages images with the first two and its engine draws message previews with the third; the clipboard read and image export use `readBlobAsDataUrl` instead of their own `FileReader` promise.
- **Screenshotting a surface exactly as it is painted:** `window.maestro.shell.capturePage(rect?)` (preload in `src/main/preload/system.ts`, handler `window:capturePage` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/system.ts`). Reach for it instead of serializing a canvas or rasterizing DOM whenever the thing on screen is composed of several render paths - the Document Graph is a `<canvas>`, a React markdown preview pane, and an HTML legend, and only the compositor knows what the user is actually looking at. Three things the handler already solves, so do not re-solve them: it captures `event.sender` rather than the main window, so a surface opened in a second window shoots itself; it floors and clamps the rect, because `getBoundingClientRect()` returns floats and Chromium answers an out-of-range region with an empty image; and it resolves `null` rather than an empty data URL when there is nothing to capture. Callers must dismiss any modal of their own and wait two animation frames first, or the shot includes the dialog that asked for it. Feature-detect (`!!window.maestro?.shell?.capturePage`) before offering the affordance - the web renderer has no bridge.
- **Loading an image for canvas compositing:** `loadImageElement(src)` in `src/renderer/utils/loadImage.ts`. Wraps `new Image()` + `onload` / `onerror` in a promise and REJECTS on failure, so a caller can never draw a half-decoded element and report success. `compositeAnnotatedImage` and `cropImageDataUrl` both use it, which is what keeps a crop and the save that follows it from disagreeing about whether the source loaded. Accepts anything an `<img src>` accepts, including `maestro-image://` store refs, and the resolved element is decoded so `naturalWidth` / `naturalHeight` are safe to read immediately. Two other loaders in the renderer look like duplicates and are NOT - do not "consolidate" them without reading why: `loadImage()` in `AchievementShareButton.tsx` resolves `null` on purpose (a missing avatar or GitHub logo falls back to a drawn placeholder rather than failing the whole share card), and `svgToPngDataUrl()` in `imageExport.ts` sets `img.decoding = 'async'` before assigning `src`, a hint this helper does not expose. Reach for `loadImageElement` when a failed load must abort the operation.
- **Pan and zoom a diagram or image:** `usePanZoom({ minZoom?, maxZoom?, resetKey? })` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/usePanZoom.ts` owns the canvas math (wheel zoom about the cursor, drag pan, stepped zoom, `fitToView`); `openZoomViewer(element, title?)` in `src/renderer/components/ZoomViewer/zoomViewerStore.ts` opens the one app-wide full-window viewer (`ZoomViewerHost`, mounted in `App.tsx`) on an already-rendered `<svg>` or `<img>`. Before this, `FilePreview/ImageViewer` held the only copy of the math and nothing else could zoom. Invariants: the wheel listener is NATIVE and non-passive, because React's `onWheel` is passive and a trackpad pinch (ctrl-wheel) would fall through to Chromium's page zoom; the drag tracks on `window` so it survives leaving the canvas; view state is one `{zoom,x,y}` object so zoom-about-a-point is one updater, not a `setOffset` nested inside a `setZoom` updater. The viewer CLONES an SVG via `prepareSvgForViewer()` (drops fixed width/height, keeps or derives a viewBox) rather than re-rendering its source, and must not put `will-change: transform` on vector content, which would blur it. Surfaces add a way in with `<ExpandToViewerButton resolveTarget>` inside a `relative group` wrapper; the image right-click menu already offers Expand for everything else, so do not wire per-surface menus.
- **Diagram content clipped at the SVG edge:** `expandSvgViewBoxToContent(svg, padding?)` in `src/renderer/utils/svgViewBox.ts`, called by `MermaidRenderer` immediately after the diagram is appended. Most mermaid types size their viewBox from laid-out content (`setupGraphViewbox` pads a `getBBox()`), but a few do not: `quadrantChart` hard-codes `viewBox="0 0 500 500"` and then draws the title centered at x=250 and point labels wherever the data puts them, so a long title or an edge-of-quadrant label is painted outside the viewport and the browser clips it - the visible damage is a chart title with both ends sliced off. The fix is NOT `overflow: visible`, which would let the diagram paint over the surrounding chat text; the viewBox is widened to the union of its declared box and the real content bounds, and the size attributes are scaled by the same factor so the diagram keeps its on-screen scale instead of shrinking to fit the wider box. Three properties to preserve if you touch it: it must run AFTER the element is in the document, because `getBBox()` needs a layout; it pads only the sides that actually overflow, because padding every edge nudges a correctly-sized diagram into looking like an overflow and rescales it; and it returns false untouched when `getBBox` is missing or throws, which is what keeps it inert in jsdom and on a detached or `display:none` SVG.
- **The message of a rejected IPC call:** `ipcErrorMessage(error)` in `src/renderer/utils/ipcErrorMessage.ts`. A throw from a main-process handler reaches the renderer wrapped by Electron as `Error invoking remote method '<channel>': Error: <what the handler said>`. A surface that prints `error.message` raw shows the user the channel name and the error class, and buries the one sentence written for them. The helper drops the wrapper and the class name behind it (`Error`, `TypeError`, any other) and returns the handler's own message whole, colons included. A message that was never wrapped comes back unchanged, and so does one that only starts with `Error: ` by itself, because nothing says that text was plumbing. A value that is not an `Error` is shown as text. It replaced a private `errorText()` in `WebLoginUsers.tsx`, which split on `': Error: '` and kept the LAST piece: that cut a handler message that itself quoted an inner error down to its tail, and it left a `TypeError` wrapper in place. Two older call sites still strip the wrapper inline with a regex and keep the `Error: ` class prefix in the toast (`src/renderer/hooks/groupChat/useGroupChatHandlers.ts`, `src/renderer/services/remoteGroupChat.ts`). Move them here when you touch them. Do NOT add another local copy.

## Keyboard and shortcuts

- **Modifier-key display text:** `formatKey()`, `formatShortcutKeys()`, `formatMetaKey()` (symbol: `⌘` / `Ctrl`), `formatMetaKeyName()` (prose: `Command` / `Ctrl`), `formatAltKeyName()` (prose: `Option` / `Alt`) in `src/renderer/utils/shortcutFormatter.ts`. Do NOT hard-code `⌘`, `Cmd+`, `Ctrl+`, `Option`, or `Alt` in UI copy, tooltips, or setting descriptions - it renders the wrong key on the other platform. Outside the renderer (CLI, main process) use `formatShortcutKeysFor(keys, isMac)` / `formatKeyFor()` in `src/shared/shortcutKeys.ts`, which owns the key maps; the renderer file is just the platform binding over it.
- **Advertising a shortcut next to the control that fires it:** `ShortcutHint` (`variant="chip"` for an inline key-cap, `variant="row"` for a hint header pinned above a dropdown's option list) and `shortcutSuffix(keys)` (for a `title=` tooltip) in `src/renderer/components/ui/ShortcutHint.tsx`. Maestro is keyboard-first, so every clickable route to an action that ALSO has a chord should show that chord where the user is already looking; this is the one component that draws it. Four byte-identical local `ShortcutHint` definitions had accumulated across the tab-bar components before it was extracted. Two invariants: read the chord from the LIVE shortcut map (`useSettingsStore(s => s.shortcuts)` / `s.tabShortcuts`) rather than spelling a default combo into the string, or a user who rebinds sees our chord instead of theirs; and both helpers render NOTHING for an empty or missing `keys`, because an action can ship unbound and a blank key-cap advertises a combo that does nothing. When a row already has a right-aligned badge, wrap the badge and the key-cap in ONE `ml-auto` container - two auto margins on siblings split the free space and strand the badge mid-row. Both shapes draw the chord with `<ShortcutKeys>` (same file): one accent-tinted cap per key, `whitespace-nowrap shrink-0`, so a chord never stacks vertically in a narrow row or a wrapping tooltip. It is the ONE look a chord has as UI: use it directly wherever a chord is drawn without a ShortcutHint around it (`HoverTooltip`'s `shortcut` renders through it; pass raw keys `['Meta', 'k']`, or alternatives `[['Escape'], ['Meta', 'w']]`), pass `onAccent` on an accent-filled row, and never hand-roll a `<kbd>` or a `font-mono` badge for a chord. `shortcutText()` is the plain-text twin for a native `title=`. Tests find a drawn chord with `getShortcutKeys(keys)` from `src/__tests__/helpers/shortcutKeys.ts` (its text is split across caps, so `getByText(chord)` no longer matches).

  **Tab overlay-menu shortcut badge:** `<ShortcutHint keys={...} theme={theme} />` in `src/renderer/components/ui/ShortcutHint.tsx` - the same component the entry above describes, and the only copy. Every tab item's overlay menu (AI, file, terminal, browser, group) uses it; it was previously re-declared inline, byte-identical, in four components, and then a second time as a TabBar-local module. Do NOT re-declare it locally or hand-roll the `ml-auto` badge span.

- **Keyboard-shortcut hint / kbd pill:** `<Keycap>` and `<KeycapHint>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/Keycap.tsx`. Draws a key as a physical key (face, border, bottom lip) instead of a bare glyph in a caption. `pressed` sinks the cap so a surface can echo the real keypress - drive it from a short timer, never from `keyup`, because a held key repeats without ever sending one. `onClick` makes the hint the control itself, which is how a legend can double as the Apply / Cancel row and still satisfy the graphical-exit rule. Do NOT hand-roll another `<kbd>` pill. See [UI-PATTERNS.md -> Keycaps](UI-PATTERNS.md#keycaps-keycap--keycaphint).
- **A system-wide (OS-level) hotkey:** `setNamedGlobalHotkey(id, keys, onPress)` and `keysToAccelerator(keys)` in `src/main/global-hotkey-manager.ts`. Every hotkey that fires while Maestro is in the background goes through this one registry, keyed by owner (`show` for the summon hotkey via `setGlobalShowHotkey`, `quickChat` for Quick Chat). Each owner holds at most one accelerator and re-binding releases the old one, so a typo never leaves a stale combo grabbed system-wide. Two owners may NOT share a combo: the second registration returns `false` (Electron would otherwise silently route the key to whichever registered last). `keysToAccelerator` maps the recorder's literal space (`' '`, and the non-breaking space macOS reports for Option+Space) to `Space`; without that, recording Option+Space produced an accelerator Electron rejects. Do NOT call `globalShortcut.register` directly from a feature.
- **Whether a chord may be bound at all:** `findReservedShortcutCombo(keys)` over `RESERVED_SHORTCUT_COMBOS` in `src/shared/shortcutKeys.ts`. `Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+Arrow` is the OS's select-to-end inside a text field, and a Maestro binding on one wins in every input in the app, so the user loses the native behavior with no way to tell that apart from a broken text box. It is checked at BOTH ends and the second half is the one that is easy to skip: the recorder (`ShortcutsTab`) refuses a fresh binding, and `migrateShortcuts()` in `settingsStore.ts` strips one already on disk and falls back to the bundled default. A recorder-only guard leaves anyone who already bound the chord broken forever. The table is written in terms of `Meta` and comparison collapses `Ctrl`/`Command` into it, matching `eventMatchesShortcutKeys`, so one entry covers every platform - do NOT add a separate `Ctrl` row.
- **Whether a keystroke belongs to a text field (guard before `preventDefault`):** `isTextInputTarget(target)` and `isTextEntryTarget(target)` in `src/renderer/utils/messageScrollNavigation.ts`; `isEditingTextTarget(target)` / `isEditingTextFocused(doc?)` in `src/renderer/utils/editableTarget.ts`. A surface-level key handler that fires while the caret is in an input steals the keystroke from whatever the user is typing, and the bug reads as the app randomly misbehaving rather than as a shortcut conflict - a bare Enter in the markdown editor also opened the file tree's selected row, because the Files tab held the app's `activeFocus` while the DOM focus was elsewhere. `activeFocus` is APP state and DOM focus is the real thing; when they disagree, real focus wins. Pick by what the key is: **`isTextInputTarget`** counts every `<input>`, `<textarea>`, and contenteditable - use it for arrow keys and Enter, where a range slider legitimately owns Left/Right. **`isTextEntryTarget`** is narrower, counting only inputs that swallow typed characters, so a focused slider or checkbox cannot silently disable `Cmd+Z` or a single-letter tool key. **`isEditingTextTarget`** asks the same question by instance rather than `tagName` (so it sees an `<input>` from another document/realm), and `isEditingTextFocused()` asks it of `document.activeElement` for handlers bound to `window` where the event target is `<body>`. Do NOT hand-roll another `tagName === 'INPUT' || ... || isContentEditable` chain: there are already ~8 inline copies across the renderer (`useKeyboardNavigation`, `DocumentGraphView`, `usePipelineKeyboard`, `MediaViewer`, and others) and they have drifted - some miss contenteditable, `MediaViewer`'s misses `<textarea>` entirely. Import one of these instead; see [DEDUP-TRACKER.md](DEDUP-TRACKER.md).
- **Hiding a switched-off Encore Feature's shortcut, menu item, or setting:** `availableShortcuts(map, encoreFeatures)` / `isShortcutAvailable(id, encoreFeatures)` over `SHORTCUT_ENCORE_FLAGS` in `src/renderer/utils/encoreShortcuts.ts`; `requiresEncore` on a `SearchableSetting` (`src/renderer/components/Settings/searchableSettings.ts`); `encoreFlag` on a `MenuCommand` in `src/main/app-menu.ts`, fed by `setMenuEncoreFeatures()`. A disabled feature must leave no trace, and gating only its hotkey handler, palette entry and hamburger item left three listings behind: Shortcuts Help and Settings -> Shortcuts still showed (and let the user rebind) "Maestro Cue" or "Computer History", the Usage Dashboard listed them as unused shortcuts and counted them in the mastery denominator (a chord that cannot fire holds the user below 100% forever), the macOS View menu kept a dead "Usage Dashboard" item, and Settings search returned a disabled feature's options. A new Encore Feature that adds a shortcut adds its id to `SHORTCUT_ENCORE_FLAGS`; one that adds a native menu item sets `encoreFlag`; one that adds a setting outside its own tile sets `requiresEncore` AND gates the rendered control on the same flag. Do NOT set `requiresEncore` on the feature's tile entry: the tile is how the feature gets switched on, so it has to stay findable. The recorder's conflict check in `ShortcutsTab` deliberately scans the UNFILTERED map, so a hidden chord cannot be handed to another action and collide when the feature comes back.
- **Hiding a switched-off Encore Feature's prompts and template variables:** `isPromptAvailable(promptId, encoreFeatures)` over the `encoreFlag` field of a `PromptDefinition` in `src/shared/promptDefinitions.ts`; `availableTemplateVariables(variables, encoreFeatures)` in `src/shared/templateVariables.ts`, read in a component through `useAvailableTemplateVariables(list?)` (or `getAvailableTemplateVariables()` at the moment a popup opens) in `src/renderer/hooks/input/useAvailableTemplateVariables.ts`. Settings -> Maestro Prompts listed `pianola-system`, `computer-history-system` and `_maestro-cue` for features that were off, and every `{{` picker offered `{{COMPUTER_HISTORY_DIR}}`, `{{MAESTRO_RUN_PATH}}` and the `{{CUE_*}}` family. Both filters are for LISTS only: a hidden prompt still loads for agents and `maestro-cli prompts get`, and a hidden variable still substitutes (to an empty string), so nothing a user already wrote breaks when a feature goes off. A variable declares `encoreFlag`; a `cueOnly` one belongs to `maestroCue` without saying so. Do NOT iterate `TEMPLATE_VARIABLES` in JSX directly. The stronger rule sits one level up: text that tells an agent to USE an Encore Feature never goes in `maestro-system-prompt.md`. It goes in its own prompt, delivered through `systemPromptSection` on the feature's `FirstPartyPluginDefinition` (`systemPromptSectionsFor()` in `src/shared/maestroSystemPrompt.ts`), which is what stopped every agent being told to answer on the Concerto stage while Concerto was off and its payloads were dropped.
- **How much of the keyboard the user has mastered:** `collectBoundShortcuts(...maps)` (denominator) and `countUsedBoundShortcuts(bound, usedIds)` (numerator) in `src/renderer/constants/keyboardMastery.ts`. Every mastery figure - the help modal's bar, the Usage Dashboard ring and its Unused Shortcuts list, the leaderboard payload, and the level-up check in `settingsStore` - runs the LIVE maps (`shortcuts`, `tabShortcuts`, `FIXED_SHORTCUTS`) through these, so they cannot disagree about the total. **A shortcut with no chord bound is excluded from both ends**: it can never be fired, so counting one in the denominator makes Keyboard Maestro unreachable forever, and listing one as "unused" tells the user to go press a chord that does not exist. The numerator is not `usedShortcuts.length` either - that list keeps ids whose binding was later cleared, which reports over 100%. Do NOT hand-roll another `Object.keys(DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS).length + ...` total; there were already two rival copies that disagreed with the surfaces rendering the number.
- **Arrow-key navigation over a list or grid:** `useListNavigation()` in `src/renderer/hooks/keyboard/useListNavigation.ts`. Pass `columns` for a 2-D grid (left/right step one item, up/down jump a row); grid mode keys on the option's PRESENCE, so a grid reflowed to one column still answers all four arrows. Omit it for a plain list, where left/right stay inert. For a responsive grid feed it `useGridColumnCount(el, itemCount)` from `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useGridColumnCount.ts`, which takes the ELEMENT (held in state via a callback ref, since a ref mutating in place is invisible to React and would keep measuring an unmounted grid) and reads the RESOLVED `grid-template-columns` - a hard-coded row width walks to the wrong tile as soon as an `auto-fill` grid reflows. Render the result as a roving tabindex over native `<button>`s. See [UI-PATTERNS.md](UI-PATTERNS.md#arrow-navigation-over-a-list-or-grid-uselistnavigation).
- **Bare `+` / `-` / `0` zoom on a surface:** `useScaleShortcuts(control, { enabled })` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useScaleShortcuts.ts`, over any `useScalePreference` control, with the keys named in the buttons' tooltips via `<ScaleControl shortcutHint>`. It is deliberately MODIFIER-FREE and skips any event carrying Cmd/Ctrl/Alt, because `Cmd+=` / `Cmd+-` is the app-wide font zoom and must keep working while a zoomable surface is open. It listens on `window` at CAPTURE rather than on the surface's node: focus falls to the body whenever a nested overlay closes, so a node-scoped handler goes dead exactly when the user comes back to the surface, and `stopPropagation` is what keeps a bare `0` or `-` out of the global shortcut handler. **Gate it with `enabled`, normally `useIsTopLayer(priority)`** - two stacked surfaces both answering the same unmodified key is the failure this replaces. `FilePreview` keeps its own inline copy of the same branch because that one sits inside a single ordered key chain where position decides which branch wins; every other surface rides the hook.
- **Keyboard navigation in a `<DualPaneFileEditor>` list:** built in. Up/Down walk the VISIBLE rows (collapsed categories are skipped, the ends do not wrap), and Backspace/Delete raise `onDeleteItem(selectedId)` - the list reports the intent, the consumer owns the confirmation. Both keys are ignored unless the event came from a row, so the "+ New" button in the same container cannot delete anything. Pass `autoFocusList` when the surface's whole job is walking the list (the Memory Viewer) so the keys work without clicking a row first; it fires on the FIRST selection rather than on mount (the list loads async) and defers to anything that already holds focus, so a user who went straight for the filter box keeps the caret. After a consumer-driven delete, bump `listFocusToken`: the focused row was just unmounted, focus falls to `<body>`, and the next Backspace silently does nothing, which reads as the keyboard dying halfway through a cleanup pass.

## UI components and widgets

- **A spinning loading icon:** `<Spinner>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/Spinner.tsx` (`icon` for a glyph other than `Loader2`, `decorative` when nearby text already says it). It rotates a wrapping `<span>`, never the `<svg>`: Chromium cannot composite a transform animation on an `<svg>` element, so `animate-spin` on a Lucide icon forces a main-thread style + paint pass on every frame for as long as it is on screen (measured ~120 main frames/s from one spinner, zero when wrapped). A field trace caught one such spinner keeping the renderer at full frame rate for the whole capture. Do NOT put `animate-spin` on an icon directly; a spinner that is on screen only for a short refresh is tolerable, a busy indicator that can stay up for minutes is not.
- **Tiny uppercase state chip:** `<MiniBadge>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/MiniBadge.tsx` - "WT" beside a worktree agent, "Snoozed" beside a tab. The generic text counterpart to `<CountBadge>` (a number and nothing else) and to the fixed-word domain pills beside it (`WorktreePill`, `GitRunningBadge`). Do NOT hand-roll another `text-[9px] px-1 rounded uppercase` span: the same chip was written per surface and drifts on padding and weight. The label is its own accessible name, so pass a real word rather than an abbreviation, unless the abbreviation is the established UI term (then pass `title` with the long form).
- **A labeled action button in a panel header:** `<HeaderActionButton>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/HeaderActionButton.tsx` - the "+ New Memory" / "+ New Session" / "Resume" pill. It was hand-rolled in five files as the same `px-3 py-1.5 rounded-lg text-sm font-medium` string. It sizes ONE STEP BELOW the header title (`text-xs`, icon `w-3.5`), deliberately: the copies used `text-sm`, which is exactly the title's size, so the button read as heavy as the heading it sits beside and dominated a header it is subordinate to. Pass the icon without size classes - the component sizes it. Do NOT hand-roll another accent pill, and do not "fix" it back to `text-sm`.
- **A text box that narrows a list:** `<FilterInput>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/FilterInput.tsx` - search icon, borderless input, optional `resultLabel` count, and a clear button that appears only once there is something to clear. Do NOT hand-roll another `<Search/> + <input>` pill; there are already a dozen near-identical copies that disagree on whether a clear button exists and what Escape does. Distinct from a FIND bar (`AutoRunSearchBar`, `TerminalSearchBar`), which walks matches inside one document and owns next/prev plus a match index - pick by question ("which rows do I see?" vs "take me to the next hit") and do not add match navigation to `<FilterInput>`. **Escape needs the host's help inside a modal:** the layer stack handles the key at capture on `window`, so the input never sees it and the surface closes instead of the filter clearing. Clear the query from the host's own `onEscape` first (see `MemoryViewer`). **Do NOT add a collapsed/magnifier-only mode to it** - that was tried and removed: hiding a pane's primary control to buy space in a crowded toolbar trades a layout problem for a discoverability one. When a toolbar cannot fit on one line, move what is NOT a control off that line (the Memory Viewer's corpus stats went to a footer).
- **Segmented toolbar (sort/filter pill bar):** `<SegmentedControl>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/SegmentedControl.tsx`. The "Sort by: [Name][Created][Queries]" bar above a grid or chart. It owns active-segment coloring, seam borders, `role="radiogroup"` semantics, arrow-key navigation, and a single tab stop; each segment gets `data-testid="${testId}-${value}"`. A `value` that names none of the options is legal, for a state the bar shows but cannot select (the Delegation Graph's `manual` layout beside its three algorithms): no segment is checked, the first one takes the tab stop so the bar stays reachable from the keyboard, and Right enters at the first segment, Left at the last. Do NOT hand-roll another `.map()` over buttons with `borderLeft` seams. A bar wider than its row SCROLLS sideways rather than clipping: the container was `overflow-hidden` (which is what rounds the corners), so a five-segment bar on a phone lost its last two options outright - invisible AND unclickable, with no scrollbar to hint they were there. Keep `overflow-y-hidden` beside the `overflow-x-auto` (alone, `overflow-y` computes to `auto` and adds a vertical bar) and keep the segments `shrink-0`, since a squeezed segment reads as a different label rather than a narrower one - shrinking stays the host's call via `shortLabel`. Distinct from `<RadioGroup>`, which is the stacked list-row form for settings panes - pick by layout, and do not add a `variant` prop to either to cover the other. The two LOOKS of the horizontal form are one component: `appearance="pills"` draws a recessed track of rounded pills with the active one filled in the accent (the voice HUD's Tab / Agent / Conductor switch), for a switch that IS the row rather than one control in a toolbar, and an option's `icon` is drawn before its label in either look. `stretch` fills the row with `minmax(max-content, 1fr)` columns, which is "an equal share, but never less than the label": do NOT stretch with `flex-1` and `truncate`, because equal shares enforced regardless clip the longest label first and which label that is depends on the user's font ("Conductor" beside "Tab" in a monospace UI font). See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Segmented Toolbars](UI-PATTERNS.md#segmented-toolbars-segmentedcontrol).
- **Tabs across the top of a modal:** `<ModalTabStrip>`, `useModalTabs()`, `useModalTabCycling()`, `tabPanelProps()` in `src/renderer/components/ui/ModalTabStrip.tsx`. One row of `role="tab"` buttons in the Usage Dashboard's accent-tinted look, with a roving tab stop (Left / Right wrap, Home / End jump, selection follows focus), a per-tab error dot (`hasError`, with an `sr-only` "(has errors)"), and element ids `${idPrefix}-tab-${id}` / `${idPrefix}-tabpanel-${id}` so two strips on one page do not collide. Spread `tabPanelProps(idPrefix, id)` on the panel root so `aria-controls` resolves. `useModalTabs({ tabs, initialTab, rememberKey?, requestedTab?, enabled })` owns the active tab, remembers it per `rememberKey` for the app session (in memory, like Settings' `lastOpenSettingsTab`), opens on `requestedTab` ahead of the remembered one when the opener named a tab (read once, at mount), falls back to `initialTab` when the active tab leaves the set, and wires Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+[ / ] cycling through `useModalTabCycling`. That hook is the ONE implementation of the shortcut: Settings calls it directly because its tab state is entangled with deep links and search. Render only the active panel; a `display: none` panel still takes Tab-key focus. Four older strips (`UsageDashboardTabs`, `MergeSessionModal`, `SnoozedTabsModal`, `TabSwitcherModal`) still hand-roll the markup and are folding candidates, not models to copy.
- **Compact chat presentation:** `<ChatComposerSurface>`, `<ChatSendButton>`, `<ChatUserMessage>`, `<ChatTooltip>`, `<ChatIconButton>`, `<ChatThinkingButton>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/ChatSurface.tsx`. Shared by Quick Chat and Document Chat for themed composers, user bubbles, send controls, hover hints and the three thinking modes; compose these primitives rather than copying the presentation between chat surfaces.
- **Compact chat image drafts:** `useChatImageDraft()` in `src/renderer/hooks/input/useChatImageDraft.ts`. Shared staging, paste, file drop and thumbnail reorder behavior. Reuses `imageFilesIn()`, `readBlobAsDataUrl()` and `useStagedImageDnd`; deduplicates data URLs and reports unreadable images without dropping readable ones. The host owns clearing the draft after a successful send.
- **The agent settings form (tabs and panels):** `buildAgentSettingsTabs()`, `firstTabWithError()`, `AGENT_SETTINGS_FIELD_TAB` and `<GeneralTab>` / `<ProviderTab>` / `<ModelsTab>` / `<PeersTab>` / `<SshTab>` in `src/renderer/components/AgentSettings/`. The tabbed body shared by New Agent, Edit Agent and the Group Chat moderator Customize dialog (`AGENT_FORM_TAB_IDS` for the first two, `MODERATOR_TAB_IDS` for the moderator, which has no Models or Peers tab). The panels are PRESENTATIONAL: they read no store and take values, setters and slots as props, because the three modals keep three state models (New Agent keys provider settings by provider id, Edit Agent stores one flat set, the moderator uses `useAgentConfiguration`) and unifying them is a separate refactor. Where the modals differ, the panel takes a slot instead of a prop set: the working directory block on General, the provider picker on Provider (the `<select>` + `<AgentConfigPanel hideEnvVars>` in edit, `<AgentPickerGrid hideEnvVars>` in create), and `runOn` / `gateway` on Models. Provider includes an `environment` slot for `<ProviderEnvironmentSection>`, which reuses the existing variable editor and expands existing live or parked variables. The Group Chat create/edit form has no tabs; Customize opens a separate modal with General, Provider and SSH, and Cancel restores its draft. Every tab panel roots in `AgentSettingsPanel`, which carries the `tabPanelProps` wiring and the `space-y-5` spacing. A new validation field gets a row in `AGENT_SETTINGS_FIELD_TAB`, or its tab shows no error dot and a Save attempt cannot jump to it. Group Settings is the fourth mount (`AGENT_FORM_TAB_IDS`, state from `useAgentConfiguration` like the moderator): it names no agent, so it passes `<GeneralTab>` no name props and the name field is not drawn. Two pieces of a form that holds ONE set of provider settings are shared here too, so the next such form does not copy them out of Edit Agent: `<AgentProviderSelect>` (the provider `<select>`; `noneLabel` adds a first option that names no provider) and `<BrowseFolderButton>` with `sshFolderPickerUnavailableTitle()` (the folder-picker `addon` beside a working directory input).
- **The tabs of the agent settings form (ids and labels):** `AGENT_SETTINGS_TAB_IDS`, `AGENT_SETTINGS_TAB_LABELS` and the `AgentSettingsTabId` type in `src/shared/agentSettingsTabs.ts`. The form has two readers that must agree on its tabs: the renderer's strip (`AGENT_SETTINGS_TABS` in `src/renderer/components/AgentSettings/agentSettingsTabs.ts`, which maps this list and adds the icons, and re-exports the type) and the surface registry (`AGENT_SETTINGS_SURFACE_TABS` in `src/shared/uiSurfaces.ts`, the `--tab` values of `maestro-cli open agent-settings --agent <id>`). Before this list existed the ids lived in the renderer only, so the CLI could not name a tab without a hand-copied list that a renamed tab would leave stale. Add, rename or reorder a tab HERE: the strip, the deep link and `open --list` follow, and a new tab also needs its icon in `AGENT_SETTINGS_TAB_ICONS` (a `Record` keyed by the id type, so `tsc` fails until it has one). The order is strip order; `MODERATOR_TAB_IDS` stays in the renderer because which tabs a mount shows is a property of the mount, not of the form.
- **A group's defaults for new agents:** `withGroupDefaults(group, defaults)` in `src/shared/groupAgentDefaults.ts`; `buildGroupDefaults(form, context)`, `groupDefaultsToForm(defaults)`, `PROVIDER_BOUND_DEFAULTS`, `groupDefaultWorkingDirProblem()` in `src/renderer/components/GroupSettingsModal/groupDefaultsForm.ts`. `Group.defaults` (`GroupAgentDefaults`) is COPY-AT-CREATE: it is stamped into the create-agent form and never read again, so no spawn path, prompt builder or `resolveAgentEnvironment()` may read it. A fourth invisible layer that depends on group membership is exactly what `agentEnvironment.ts` exists to prevent. Every write of a group's defaults goes through `withGroupDefaults`, which removes the field for no defaults and never stores `{}`: the create form reads "has defaults" as "show the pre-filled banner". `buildGroupDefaults` is the only way form values become defaults, because of one rule: a blank or default-valued field is OMITTED. An absent key means "no opinion"; a key written with a default value is an opinion the user never had, and a publish would stamp it onto every agent in the group. Three consequences that look like bugs and are not: model, effort and context window are `undefined` in the form until the group has an opinion (the config panel shows the provider's own value meanwhile, and saving must not write it back); an explicit API token source is omitted locally, where it is the default, and KEPT over SSH, where the unset default is the TUI; and a default holds a token proxy mode OR a gateway URL, never both, because an agent on the proxy has the proxy's address as its gateway and that address is worked out when the agent is created. Provider-bound fields are written only when the form names a provider, and each Models value goes through the validator every other writer of it shares (`parseRunOnEdit`, `parseTierTargetsEdit`, `parseGatewayUrlEdit`, `parseBackgroundRequestsEdit`).
- **Publishing a group's defaults to its existing agents:** `diffGroupDefaults(before, after)`, `applyGroupDefaultsToSession(session, diff, context?)`, `publishableChangesFor(toolType, diff)`, `summarizeGroupDefaultsDiff(diff)`, `PUBLISHABLE_KEYS` / `UNPUBLISHABLE_KEYS`, `directGroupMembers(agents, groupId)` in `src/shared/groupAgentDefaults.ts`; `publishGroupDefaults(group, diff)`, `notifyGroupDefaultsPublished()`, `undoGroupDefaultsPublish(publishId)` in `src/renderer/services/groupDefaultsPublish.ts`; the dialog is `<PublishDefaultsDialog>` in `src/renderer/components/GroupSettingsModal/`. Copy-at-create governs how a setting is RESOLVED, not how it is distributed: a publish is a one-time write into each agent's own record, never a layer above it, so nothing reads a group's defaults at spawn. Every rule exists because the plain version is destructive, and all of them are in the pure apply so the desktop and the CLI cannot disagree. (1) Only the DIFF of one save is written, never the whole profile: a profile that has held a model for a week must not stamp it over agents retuned by hand since. (2) `UNPUBLISHABLE_KEYS` (`toolType`, `projectRoot`, `sessionSshRemoteConfig`) are never written: each moves an agent somewhere its conversations are not. (3) A provider-bound value reaches only the agents on the provider it was written for, and a save that MOVES the profile to another provider removes the old provider's values from nobody. (4) Records (`customEnvVars`, `customEnvVarsDisabled`, `tierTargets`) merge by key and leave the agent's own keys alone; lists replace whole, and the dialog says so. (5) A Run on or tier target an agent's SSH remote cannot reach is skipped with a reason. (6) Direct members only: a publish never descends into child groups. (7) `tokenProxyMode` and `backgroundRequests` are NOT written by the pure apply: it returns them in `proxy`, and the caller applies them with `setAgentTokenProxyMode` then `setAgentBackgroundRequests`, because a stored mode does nothing until the gateway points at the proxy. `Group.shortLabel` is not a default, so it is in neither key list and a label-only save is an empty diff. The field table `GROUP_DEFAULT_FIELDS` is a `Record` over `keyof GroupAgentDefaults`, so a new default does not compile until someone decides whether a publish may write it. `applyGroupDefaultsToSession` returns the SAME object when nothing changed and an `undo` holding the prior value of exactly the fields it changed; the service keeps that as an in-memory snapshot and the publish toast restores it through the `undo-group-publish` `ToastClickAction`. The three-outcome dialog is `ConfirmModal`'s `alternative` prop (a third button, focused by default), not a hand-rolled modal.
- **Seeding a new agent from its group's defaults:** `resolveGroupDefaultsSeed(defaults, context)`, `groupDefaultsCreateExtras(seed, agent, context)` in `src/shared/groupAgentDefaults.ts`; `applySeededTokenProxy(agentId, extras)` in `src/renderer/services/groupDefaultsPublish.ts`; `<GroupSeedBanner>` in `src/renderer/components/NewInstanceModal/GroupSeedBanner.tsx`. This is the copy in copy-at-create, and it happens ONCE: `NewInstanceModal` reads the group named by `presetGroupId` from the store as it opens, and never again. Changing the group picker afterwards moves the new agent and does not reseed, because a reseed would replace what the user typed; a duplicate is never seeded, because it is its source's copy. `resolveGroupDefaultsSeed` removes the two things a new agent cannot use as stored: an SSH remote that was deleted since (the seed runs locally and `sshRemoteMissing` makes the banner say so; with `sshRemoteIds` absent no remote is judged gone, since an unknown remote fails the launch by name and a remote wrongly dropped would run the agent on this machine without a word), and a Run on target the seed's own remote cannot reach. The form then fills through `loadAgents(seed, undefined, { seedKind: 'defaults' })`, the path a duplicate uses, with one difference that is the whole point of `seedKind`: a copy is the whole truth, so a provider setting it lacks is cleared, while a default is sparse, so an absent path, args or env var keeps what the form loaded for the provider. Env vars, resilience flags and the SSH remote are not provider-bound, and the form keeps all three per provider, so a seed fills them for EVERY provider (the SSH config under `_pending_` too): a default that names no provider still applies, and one that does survives the user picking another. Five defaults have no control in the create form (`tierTargets`, `backgroundRequests`, `systemOneOptOut`, `tokenProxyMode`, `customEnvVarsDisabled`). `groupDefaultsCreateExtras` decides which of them the new agent takes, judged against the form as it stands at Create and not as it was seeded; the banner lists them from the same value, and `createNewSession` stamps them from its last argument. The token proxy mode is never stamped: `applySeededTokenProxy` applies it through `setAgentTokenProxyMode` and then `setAgentBackgroundRequests`, the writers that work out the proxy's address, and a refusal is a yellow toast, never a silent drop. A seed's own SSH remote makes the form re-detect providers on that host; that one pass runs with `keepForm`, or it would reload the provider settings over the values the seed just filled. Do not write a second seeding path: a CLI create into a group must call the same two pure functions.
- **Seeding a create from outside the dialog (CLI):** `seedRemoteCreate({ groupId, toolType, explicit, seed? })`, `seedableGroup(groupId, groups)` in `src/renderer/services/groupSeededCreate.ts`; `groupDefaultsCreateFields(seed, toolType, explicit?)`, `GroupSeededCreateReport` in `src/shared/groupAgentDefaults.ts`. `maestro-cli create-agent --group` has no form, so the caller's explicit flags stand where the user's edits would, and the merge runs in the APP (the renderer's `maestro:remoteCreateSession` listener), not in the CLI: only the app holds the SSH remotes, the saved sources and the token proxy writers that a seed is judged against. The CLI resolves the group id (and the provider, when `--type` was left at its default, through `getOptionValueSource`) and sends only the flags that were typed; `seedFromGroup: false` is the one thing it adds, for `--no-group-defaults`. The rules are the dialog's, in shared code so the two cannot drift: `resolveGroupDefaultsSeed()` for the seed, `groupDefaultsCreateFields()` for the fields the record takes, `groupDefaultsCreateExtras()` for the five the form has no control for, and `applySeededTokenProxy()` once the agent is in the store. Four rules to know: (1) an explicit value beats the seed, and an `undefined` one is no opinion; (2) a provider-bound default reaches only the provider the seed names, so `--type codex` into a Claude group takes the group's nudge and environment but not its model; (3) environment variables merge key by key with the explicit side winning, and a switched-off variable the caller switched on is not stamped; (4) the gateway and a Run on target exclude each other, so an explicit one drops the seed's other. The reply carries `seededFromGroup` (the group id or `null`) and `skipped` (`Label: reason`), which the CLI prints and `--json` reports. A group with no defaults, a worktree group, no group, or `seedFromGroup: false` leaves the explicit fields exactly as they came, so a create outside a group is unchanged. Do not add a second seeding path: the dialog and the CLI call the same pure functions.
- **Reading and describing a group's Group Settings in a CLI verb:** `readGroupSettingsFlags()`, `groupSettingsBlocker(group)`, `describeGroupSettings(group)` and `verifyPersistedGroupSettings()` in `src/cli/services/group-settings.ts`, shared by `group-settings` and `update-group-settings`. The write goes through the running app (the renderer owns the group list and the validator the dialog saves with), so the verb sends a typed `update_group_settings` patch; this module is the half that needs no app: flags into a patch, a stored group into a printable report, and the read-back after a write. The read-back is not optional: the desktop answers as soon as the renderer accepted the message, and an older desktop would drop a field it does not know, so a verb that reports success without `verifyPersistedGroupSettings()` can print a lie.
- **Writing a group's Group Settings from outside the dialog (CLI):** `applyRemoteGroupSettings(groupId, request)` in `src/renderer/services/groupSettingsRemote.ts`; `applyGroupDefaultsPatch(current, { configPatch, sshPatch }, context)` in `src/renderer/components/GroupSettingsModal/groupDefaultsPatch.ts`; `parseGroupSettingsUpdateRequest()`, `GROUP_SETTINGS_PATCH_KEYS` in `src/shared/groupSettingsUpdate.ts`. `maestro-cli update-group-settings` cannot build a whole profile the way the dialog's form does, so it sends a PATCH in `update-agent`'s vocabulary (`configPatch`, where `null` clears one field, plus `sshPatch`) over the `update_group_settings` WS message, and the app folds it into the form the dialog would hold for the group (`groupDefaultsToForm()`), then builds the profile with the dialog's own `buildGroupDefaults()`. That is the whole point: one validator, so a flag and a click cannot store different things. The builder silently DROPS a provider-bound value when the form names no provider (and a Claude or Codex value on another provider), which a dialog can never send but a typed patch can, so the applier refuses each of those by name BEFORE the builder runs. Changing the provider resets the provider-bound values first (the dialog's `PROVIDER_BOUND_DEFAULTS`), so `--provider codex --model gpt-5` is the model on Codex. A gateway URL and a proxy mode cannot both be set (the mode stands in for the gateway). `applyRemoteGroupSettings` then takes the dialog's Save steps: `diffGroupDefaults()`, one write of the profile and the pill label (`withGroupDefaults()` then `withGroupShortLabel()`), a flush with `flushGroupsToDisk()` so the CLI's readback is deterministic, and, only when asked, `publishGroupDefaults()` of exactly this call's diff. Nothing is written when any step refuses. `--clear` removes the defaults and never touches `shortLabel`; it cannot be combined with defaults or with `--publish`, because a clear has no values to write into agents.
