The publish raises the dialog's Undo toast: the CLI has no undo of its own.
- **Writing the group list to disk and waiting for it (before answering a remote caller):** `flushGroupsToDisk(groups)` in `src/renderer/services/groupPersistence.ts`. The store persists from a React effect, which lands AFTER a remote listener has answered, so a CLI that verifies its write by reading `maestro-groups.json` back would see the old list and report a false mismatch. Await this before sending the response. It logs and swallows a failed write, the way the listener it was lifted out of always did; the CLI's readback is what reports a write that did not land.
- **Agent settings the dialog and `update-agent` both write (resilience, auto reset, extra directories, parked env):** `applyAgentConfigExtras(agent, patch, homeDir?)`, `parseAdditionalDirFlag(entry)`, `AGENT_SWITCH_KEYS` and `AGENT_CONFIG_EXTRA_KEYS` in `src/shared/agentConfigExtras.ts`. The Agent Resilience switches, the Codex auto reset, Additional Directories and parking an environment variable were reachable only from the Edit Agent dialog; this module is the one check and write both the dialog's save and `maestro-cli update-agent` run, built on the dialog's own helpers (`normalizeAdditionalDirectories()`, `setKeysParked()`). It is pure and sits outside the renderer's remote listener so it is tested without one. `updated` holds only the keys the patch named, `undefined` clears a field, a switch set to `null` returns to its default (which is ON for the two retries), and a key this module does not own is ignored so a caller may hand over the whole patch. Do NOT validate these fields a second time in a CLI verb.
- **Editing an agent's env vars:** `<AgentEnvVarsSection>` in `src/renderer/components/shared/AgentEnvVarsSection.tsx`; `hideEnvVars` on `<AgentConfigPanel>` when it is mounted elsewhere; `agentEnvVarHandlers()` in `src/renderer/components/shared/agentEnvVarHandlers.ts` for its callbacks over a live and a parked record. The per-agent rows with the park eye, deferred key rename, Add Variable focus and the built-in `MAESTRO_*` list, extracted from `AgentConfigPanel` so a tabbed form can mount it as a collapsible `<ProviderEnvironmentSection>` under Provider. `AgentConfigPanel` still renders it in place by default, so its six composers did not change. It is NOT `Settings/EnvVarsEditor`: that editor owns its rows, emits whole records, commits keys on change and drops invalid rows, while this section reports per-row callbacks, defers key renames to blur and never drops a stored value. The file's header lists the differences; do not swap one for the other without reading it. A form that keeps the environment as two flat records (live and parked) builds the section's callbacks with `agentEnvVarHandlers({ customEnvVars, customEnvVarsDisabled, setCustomEnvVars, setCustomEnvVarsDisabled })` and spreads the result into BOTH `<AgentConfigPanel hideEnvVars>` and `<ProviderEnvironmentSection>`: Edit Agent and the Group Chat moderator do. Each edit goes to the record its row lives in and a toggle moves the row with its value, so a key is never in both; hand-rolled copies read the wrong record on a parked row. New Agent keeps one environment per provider with no parked rows, so it keeps its own handlers.
- **Editing an agent's peer grants:** `<PeerAgentsSection>` in `src/renderer/components/shared/PeerAgentsSection.tsx`, fed by `usePeerFleet(groupId)` in `src/renderer/hooks/agent/usePeerFleet.ts`; `PeerGrants`, `normalizePeerAgents()`, `PEER_GRANT_LABELS` in `src/shared/peerAgents.ts`. The section edits the three stored sources of a peer roster (the explicit rows, the group grant, the worktree grant) and reads no store, so a form that is not about one live agent (a create form, a group's seed editor) can mount it. The fleet comes from `usePeerFleet`, whose result has the section's own prop names and spreads onto `<PeersTab>`: pass it the group the agent is in now (Edit Agent), the group selected in the form (New Agent), or `undefined` (the wizard's directory step, whose agent starts in no group, so the group grant is drawn disabled). The wizard keeps the three sources in `WizardState` and its resume state, and `handleWizardLaunchSession` writes them onto the new agent. A screen that routes Enter and Escape itself must leave both to the open picker: `useDirectoryKeyboard` returns early for a key that comes from inside a `[role="listbox"]`, because `ThemedSelect` does not stop propagation and the same keystroke would otherwise also continue the wizard or step it back. Do NOT map `sessions` to `{ id, name, groupId }` again in a form. A form hands its save ONE `PeerGrants` argument, never three positionals, with the rows already through `normalizePeerAgents()`. Three traps: (1) an absent field is what the roster reads as off, so a grant is `undefined` when unchecked, never `{ read: false, write: false }`; (2) an explicit row with neither bit lit is an EXCLUSION that overrides a group or worktree grant, so `normalizePeerAgents()` keeps it, and a filter that drops no-access rows hands the access back; (3) a save writes all three fields, so an absent one clears that source (`handleSaveEditAgent`), while a create stores only the sources that are on (`createNewSession`). `PEER_GRANT_LABELS` is what the tab calls the two derived grants (`Peers in my group`, `Worktree family`): the section's checkbox labels, the publish summary in `groupAgentDefaults.ts` and the Delegation Graph's edge tooltip all read it, so copy that names a grant to the user imports it and does not retype the words.
- **Paginating a list already in memory:** `usePagination(items, pageSize, resetKey)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/usePagination.ts`, pure helpers in `src/renderer/utils/pagination.ts`, UI in `<Pager>` (`src/renderer/components/ui/Pager.tsx`). Do NOT confuse it with `useHistoryPagination`, which is the async IPC-backed windowing engine for data that arrives page by page - this one is for a list you already hold. The hook clamps the current page on every read rather than in an effect, which is what stops a filter that shrinks 1236 rows to 18 from rendering an empty grid under "Page 30 of 1"; pass a `resetKey` built from whatever the user can change that reorders the list (sort mode, filter mode, query text) so re-sorting does not strand them on an arbitrary slice. Render `<Pager>` in the TOOLBAR ROW, not under the list: a pager below a long grid inside a scrolling modal makes the user scroll to the bottom, click, then scroll back up.
- **Sortable table header:** `<SortableTh>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/SortableTh.tsx` + `useTableSort()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useTableSort.ts`. The hook owns the flip-vs-switch rule (clicking the ACTIVE column reverses it; clicking a DIFFERENT column jumps to that column's own default direction instead of inheriting the previous one - inheriting is what makes a freshly-picked column show Z-A or least-used-first and read as broken data). The component owns `aria-sort` placement (on the `<th>`, not the control), a stable caret slot so switching columns doesn't reflow the header row, and a real `<button>` for the click target. Do NOT hand-roll `<th role="button" onClick>`: `role` grants the semantics without the tab stop or Enter/Space handling, so it announces as a button and then does nothing from the keyboard.
- **A list of editable `key = value` pairs:** `<KeyValueRows>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/KeyValueRows.tsx`, with `recordToKeyValueRows()` / `keyValueRowsToRecords()` beside it. The plain form of the control: a row per pair, a monospace input on each side, an optional eye that parks the row, a trash button. An SSH remote's environment variables and its extra `ssh -o` options are the same widget with different labels, and the second was about to become a copy of the first. Rows carry a stable `id` rather than being keyed by their key text - a list keyed by the editable field remounts the input on every keystroke and the caret jumps out after one character. `keyValueRowsToRecords` returns BOTH halves together (each `undefined` rather than `{}` when nothing survives, so a section the user emptied stores no key at all): a caller that wrote only the half it remembered would silently drop the other, which for the parked half means losing exactly the values parking exists to keep. The eye is opt-in via `onToggleRow` - without somewhere to keep a parked value there is nothing the button could do, so it is hidden rather than rendered dead. Only LIVE rows are validated by the consumer, because a value that cannot work right now is precisely what gets parked and a parked row that blocked Save would defeat the control. Distinct from `Settings/EnvVarsEditor`, which is the SHELL environment editor and owns secret masking, known-auth path completion, and absolute-path validation - pick by whether those behaviours are wanted, and do not add a mode to either to cover the other.
- **Switching a `key = value` entry off without deleting it:** `splitParkedEntries()` / `setKeysParked()` / `parkedPairKeys()` in `src/shared/parkedRecords.ts`. TWO records, never one record with an `enabled` flag. Being present in the ACTIVE record is exactly the same statement as being live, so every consumer - `resolveSshOptions`, the four SSH spawn sites, `resolveAgentEnvironment` - keeps reading one record and needs no filter; a single flag inside one record would put that filter in every read site, and the first one that forgets it ships the `ProxyCommand` the user thought they had switched off. The rule the module enforces so no caller has to: a key lives in exactly ONE of the two records, and a live row wins the key outright, because a key in both is unreachable state (re-enabling the parked copy after deleting the live one silently restores a value the user cannot see). `setKeysParked` IGNORES a key present in neither record rather than inventing it, so disabling something already deleted is a no-op instead of a resurrection. Three things ride it: the eye in `<KeyValueRows>`, the save handler, and the CLI's `--disable-*` / `--enable-*` flags, where disable runs BEFORE enable so one command can swap which of two keys is live. A `--clear-*` verb must wipe BOTH records - "remove all" that left parked entries behind leaves invisible state a later `--enable-*` could resurrect. Field pairs today: `shellEnvVars`/`shellEnvVarsDisabled`, `SshRemoteConfig.sshOptions`/`sshOptionsDisabled`, `SshRemoteConfig.remoteEnv`/`remoteEnvDisabled`.
- **Record view for one table row:** `<RecordDetailModal>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/RecordDetailModal.tsx` - the field/value flip of a row that every tabular preview opens on double-click beside a mouse and on a SINGLE tap under a coarse pointer (CSV/TSV via `CsvRowDetailModal`, parquet via `ParquetViewer`) - a finger cannot double-tap, iOS reserves the gesture, and a phone has no Enter key to fall back on. Callers pass a flat `fields` list plus their own `priority` / `resizeKey` / `testIdPrefix`; the mapping from a row to fields belongs in the caller, since a CSV row is positional strings and a parquet row is typed values. Do NOT hand-roll a second one - focus starts on the FIELD LIST rather than the filter input, because Left/Right step rows and Up/Down scroll and none of that works while a text input owns the caret (`/` moves focus to the filter).
- **A Usage Dashboard panel that fails on its own:** `<PanelErrorNote theme title message onRetry testId?>` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/PanelErrorNote.tsx`. What a dashboard panel that loads for itself (Cue, Models) shows when its own fetch fails: what failed, the message the call rejected with, and a Retry that runs the panel's fetch again. The modal's "Failed to load usage data" screen belongs to the stats aggregation, and a panel's failure must not blank the tabs that still work. Presentational only: the caller owns the fetch and unwraps the message (see `ipcErrorMessage()`).
- **What the Models tab derives from a `ModelUsage` record:** `summarizeModelUsage()`, `otherModelSources()`, `sortModelSources()`, `sortModelTasks()`, `defaultSortDirection()`, `latencyBarRows()`, `latencyBarPercent()`, `systemOneOutcomeSlices()`, `routedTierSlices()`, `formatShare()`, `formatLatency()` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/ModelUsage/modelUsageView.ts`, drawn by `<LatencyBars>` (p50 over a fainter p95 on a log scale) and the sections in `ModelUsageSections.tsx`. The `ModelUsage` record is built once in the main process (`getModelUsage()`) and is the one `maestro-cli stats models` prints; nothing in the renderer re-counts a row. These are the tab's own shapes over it, kept pure so they are tested without rendering. Do NOT add a second sort or share formatter to a Models section.
- **Usage Dashboard card tile:** `<EntityTile>` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/EntityTile.tsx` - the shared tile behind every dashboard card grid (agent grid, per-tab grid): status dot, title, badges, corner age, subtitle, labeled stat row, sparkline, plus all four border states and the staggered `card-enter` animation. A new grid shapes data into `EntityTileStat[]`; it does not re-derive the chrome. Not the same thing as the widget library's `StatCard` (one headline metric), and it stays under `UsageDashboard/` because widgets may not import from there.
- **Usage Dashboard metric tile:** `<MetricCard>` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/MetricCard.tsx` - icon, uppercase caption, one headline figure, optional sub-line, with the `role="group"` + `aria-label` that lets a test assert on a tile by its label rather than by DOM position. The Auto Run, Resilience, and Wizard sections all render rows of these; the identical component had been pasted into the first two before it lived here. Pass PRE-FORMATTED strings (`formatNumber`, `formatDurationHuman`) - it does no math. Distinct from `<EntityTile>`, which is the richer card for an agent or group in a grid, and from the widget library's `StatCard`, which lives outside `UsageDashboard/` and cannot import from it.
- **Color for the Nth chart series:** `getSeriesColor(index, theme, colorBlindMode?)` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/chartUtils.ts`. Four byte-identical private copies (`getAgentColor` in `SessionStats`, `AgentEfficiencyChart`, `agentComparisonUtils`, `getProviderColor` in `ProviderTrendsChart`) were folded into it, so the same index is the same color when two charts sit side by side. `AgentUsageChart` keeps its own theme-less palette on purpose.
- **A label that must not truncate:** `useOptionalLabelFits(rowRef)`, or `useOptionalContentFits(ref, { axis: 'height', contentKey })` for rows that must not make a column scroll (the Settings sidebar's Models subtree), in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useOptionalLabelFits.ts` - returns whether an optional inline label still fits, so a caller renders it whole or omits it rather than clipping it. Reach for it when a fragment is worse than nothing: "GROUP CHA..." occupies the same row space as the full label and says less than the icon next to it. **Do NOT solve this with a container query**, which is what the Group Chats header tried first and why it clipped: the `fontSize` setting is written to `documentElement.style.fontSize`, so every rem-based size in a header scales with it while the panel's width stays in pixels, making any px threshold correct at exactly one font size; and conditional controls beside the label (a count badge, a sort button) change how much room is left, which a width threshold cannot see. The layout contract is load-bearing - the label needs `shrink-0` and the row needs `overflow-hidden`, so `scrollWidth` is the width the row wants and `clientWidth` is what it has, and overflow IS the "would have truncated" condition. The restore is the half that bites: once the label is gone the row no longer overflows, so re-showing on the same test oscillates; the hook remembers the width measured while the label was shown and returns it only at or above that, which cannot flap because that width demonstrably fit. Returns `true` before the first measurement and in jsdom, so a label renders by default. Distinct from `useElementWidth`, which reports a number for JS-computed layout and makes no decision.
- **Adding a control to the Left Bar header:** the row is three zones - identity (wand + wordmark, `shrink-0`), indicators (`flex-1 justify-center min-w-0`, `data-testid="sidebar-header-indicators"`), menu (`shrink-0`). A status control - the trophy badge, the now-playing pill, the LIVE toggle, whatever comes next - goes in the CENTER band; the Conductor voice-floor mark is the exception, beside the wordmark because its binding is Maestro itself. Its width is reserved while `useConductorHoldsFloor()` is true; `flex-1` is what centers it between the two fixed zones. The row neither wraps nor scrolls and the sidebar goes down to 256px, so it has a declared yield order rather than whatever CSS shrinks first. The MAESTRO wordmark is drawn IN FULL or dropped entirely (`showWordmark` in `SessionList.tsx`); it must never `truncate`, because a brand rendered as "MAE..." reads as a rendering bug. The now-playing pill is the shrink target of last resort - its filename is the one thing here that can be clipped without looking broken, so its label track is `minmax(0, max-content)` while the pill itself keeps its automatic minimum (never `min-w-0 overflow-hidden`, which clipped the restore button in half) - the transport never yields. Rem-built reserves (badge, pill) scale by `useHeaderTextDelta().remScale`. Reserve width for the form a control is CURRENTLY in (the pill sheds its filename when narrow, so its compact and labeled reserves are separate constants), and ask `selectNowPlayingVisible` whether the pill is on screen rather than re-deriving it. A reserve is charged only against what is still DRAWN: the LIVE label adds `headerBadgeWidth` only while `showWordmark` is true, because once the wordmark drops out the ~110px it vacated more than covers the badge, and charging anyway left a 256px sidebar showing a bare radio dot with empty space beside it. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Left Bar Header Width Gates](UI-PATTERNS.md#left-bar-header-width-gates).
- **Color math and contrast:** `readableTextOn()`, `isReadableOn()`, `contrastRatio()`, `relativeLuminance()`, `blendColors()`, `transparentize()`, `adjustBrightness()`, `hexToRgb()` in `src/shared/colorContrast.ts`. Whenever a foreground and its background are BOTH derived from theme colors (diagram fills, chart labels, badges, generated SVG), run the foreground through `readableTextOn(preferred, backgrounds)` so a theme whose accent sits near its text color can't paint near-identical colors on top of each other. It returns the preferred color untouched when it already clears WCAG AA and nudges the theme's own color otherwise, so nothing snaps to hard-coded black/white.
- **Diagram labels on fills the AUTHOR chose:** `repairDiagramLabelContrast(svg)` in `src/renderer/utils/diagramLabelContrast.ts`, over `cssColorToHex()` in `src/shared/colorContrast.ts`. `initMermaid` derives label colors against fills it knows; an agent writing `style S4 fill:#f9e79f` (or a `classDef` fill) with no `color:` left the dark theme's near-white label on a pale yellow box. The repair runs on the LIVE DOM because `classDef` fills only resolve through `getComputedStyle`, measures each `g.node` / `g.cluster` shape fill against each text element in its own label, and rewrites failures with inline `!important` (mermaid's own label rules are `!important`) via `readableTextOn`, so passing pairs are untouched. `cssColorToHex` returns null for translucent, named, `hsl()`, and `url()` colors, which the repair treats as unmeasurable and skips. Any new surface that renders mermaid must call it after the SVG is attached.
- **Naming the OS file manager in copy:** `getFileManagerName(platform)` / `fileManagerName()` in `src/renderer/utils/platformUtils.ts` return the bare noun (`Finder` / `Explorer` / `File Manager`), and `getRevealLabel()` / `getOpenInLabel()` are built from it. Do NOT hard-code `Finder` in a hint, tooltip, or menu label - it reads wrong on Windows, and the failure is invisible on a Mac dev machine. Same class of bug as hard-coding `⌘`.
- **Rendering raw terminal output (ANSI):** `useAnsiConverter(theme)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useAnsiConverter.ts` for the theme-aware `ansi-to-html` converter, then `getCachedAnsiHtml(text, theme.id, converter)` in `src/renderer/utils/textProcessing.ts` to convert, sanitize (DOMPurify), and cache. The 16 ANSI slots map onto the ACTIVE theme rather than the xterm palette, so do NOT hand-roll another `new Convert({...})` - a second palette drifts the first time a theme adds a color. Collapse carriage returns with `processCarriageReturns()` BEFORE converting, or a progress line arrives as a screen of dead rows, and run any regex over the output through `stripAnsiCodes()` first. Both of those live in `src/shared/stringUtils.ts` (the renderer module re-exports `processCarriageReturns` for convenience), so the MAIN process can reach them without pulling DOMPurify into a process with no DOM - `plainGitErrorText()` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/git/streaming.ts` uses them to turn a colored `--progress` failure into text a toast can show. That surface split is the whole point: the streaming runner ASKS for color, because the command console renders ANSI, but the `error` field it returns goes to a failure toast and a modal footer, which are plain `<div>`s - so a pre-push hook's colored output arrived there with a literal `[33m` in front of every word, and the `--progress` counter arrived with every intermediate percentage concatenated into one line. The renderer only owns half of this: **nothing Maestro spawns is a TTY**, so the producing side must force color too (`-c color.ui=always` for git, `FORCE_COLOR=1` / `CLICOLOR_FORCE=1` for whatever its hooks run) or the console renders a perfect wall of gray.
- **Previewing a parquet file:** `<ParquetViewer>` in `src/renderer/components/ParquetViewer/`, over the main-process engine in `src/main/parquet/` and the shared filter language in `src/shared/parquet/`. Parquet is the one previewable format that never crosses IPC: `fs:readFile` short-circuits it to a marker (`shared/parquet/preview.ts`) and the viewer queries windows of rows through `window.maestro.parquet.*`. Three invariants: **filtering and sorting round-trip to the engine** (filtering the loaded page would only ever search the first few hundred rows of a hundred million); **`matchedRows` is a LOWER BOUND until `complete` is true**, rendered `1,204+`, converged by a background `countAll: true` pass that doubles as the prefetch; and **hiding a column changes the projection**, so the engine stops decoding it. The one thing that silently loses data: **filter literals compile into the CONVERTED domain, not the physical one** - hyparquet converts footer statistics while parsing, so a `TIMESTAMP(MICROS)` bound is epoch MILLISECONDS, and compiling microseconds prunes every row group in the file while looking healthy. See [REMAINING-SYSTEMS.md → Parquet Query Engine](REMAINING-SYSTEMS.md#5-parquet-query-engine-srcmainparquet-srcsharedparquet).
- **Graphing a set of documents rather than one:** `BuildOptions.scopeFiles` / `scopeDirectory` in `src/renderer/components/DocumentGraph/graphDataBuilder.ts`, opened via `openGraphScope()` in `fileExplorerStore.ts`. Focus mode ("what does this document reach?") only creates nodes for files its BFS walks INTO, so a file nothing links to can never appear; scope mode ("how do these documents relate?") makes a node for every file in the set. Unreachable documents were being dropped TWICE - the builder's BFS, and then again by `prepareLayoutInput()` in `mindMapLayouts.ts`, which all six layout algorithms funnel through - so fixing only the builder renders nothing. They ride a `showOrphans` flag into the layout and get their own band via `positionOrphanNodesBottom()`, NOT a borrowed depth ring: an unreachable node has no depth, and giving it one draws it as a child of a center it has no link to. Scope mode also skips `startBacklinkScan`, which would pull in files outside the scope - the exact files the user did not select.
- **Sizing a Document Graph node:** `calculateNodeWidth(label, previewCharLimit)` and `calculateNodeHeight(previewText, previewCharLimit)` in `src/renderer/components/DocumentGraph/mindMapLayouts.ts`. Do NOT space a layout off the `NODE_WIDTH` constant: with previews off a document draws as a filename pill about a third of a card wide and a fraction of its height, so reserving the card width claims roughly five times the real footprint. That is what made the radial rings thousands of pixels across, and it reached the force layout too, whose `forceCollide` radius read the same constant. The layout vocabulary itself (`MindMapLayoutType`, `LAYOUT_LABELS`, `MIND_MAP_LAYOUT_TYPES`, `nextMindMapLayout`, `isMindMapLayoutType`) lives in `DocumentGraph/layoutTypes.ts`, a leaf module with no d3 import so `settingsStore` and the `Session` interface can name a layout without pulling in the engine - it had been written out three times, and a layout added to the graph was silently rejected on the way to disk by a copy nobody updated.
- **Filtering the Git Log:** `gitLogSearchTerms(query)` and `matchesGitLogTerms(commit, terms)` in `src/renderer/utils/gitLogSearch.ts`, plus `contractGitGraphNodes(nodes, keep)` in `src/renderer/utils/gitGraphLayout.ts`. The List and Graph views are built from DIFFERENT `git log` invocations (the list is the current branch, the graph is `--all`), so the one thing they must agree on is what counts as a hit - a query that hides a commit from one view and leaves it lit in the other reads as the filter being unreliable. Matching is SUBSTRING, never fuzzy: a commit hash is the motivating query, and a fuzzy matcher scores `a1b2c3d` against nearly every hash in the repository, so the one search where the user knows exactly what they want would return the whole log. Terms are ANDed on whitespace because `fix usage` has to find `fix(usage): ...`, which no single literal substring does; the fields are joined with a NEWLINE before the substring test, since concatenating them lets a term straddle two and match text that is nowhere on screen. Highlighting takes the same term ARRAY (`highlightMatches` accepts `string | string[]`), so what is marked is exactly what was matched on. **A filtered graph must be CONTRACTED, not merely thinned:** `contractGitGraphNodes` reattaches every survivor to the nearest kept ANCESTOR of each of its parents, preserving parent ORDER because `parents[0]` decides which branch a commit stays on and a second parent is what draws a merge. Simply removing nodes dangles every link through a dropped commit, so `assignGitGraphBranches` inherits nothing, hands each survivor a fresh anonymous lane, and the graph degenerates into a column of unconnected dots - strictly less information than the list beside it. Note the whole filter runs over the FETCHED window (`COMMIT_FETCH_LIMIT`, 200 commits), not the repository, so a hash older than that window will not be found; deepening that is a server-side `git log --grep` search, not a change to this predicate.
- **Layout and navigation for the Git Log graph:** `buildGitGraphCore()`, `computeGitGraphGeometry()`, `stepGitGraphVertical()`, `stepGitGraphHorizontal()`, `jumpGitGraphVertical()`, `gitGraphColumnEdge()`, `gitGraphTopCommit()`, `buildGitGraphTemplate()`, `assignGitGraphBranches()`, `pickBranchFromRefs()` in `src/renderer/utils/gitGraphLayout.ts`. `GitGraphView` DRAWS the graph and `GitLogViewer` NAVIGATES it with the arrow keys, and both go through the SAME `buildGitGraphCore()` - navigation here is VISUAL, so it moves by the layout @gitgraph actually produced (`getRenderedData()` gives every commit an `x` and `y`), never by an order re-derived beside the renderer. That re-derivation is the bug this replaced: @gitgraph orders its columns by when each branch first appears in ITS OWN iteration, so a hand-written lane list sent Left/Right to a column somewhere else on screen and the user lost their place. In its coordinates a column IS a branch (each gets a distinct `x`) and `y` GROWS DOWNWARD with age, so the newest commit is at the top of its column. **Each axis answers one question:** Up/Down walk `commitsInColumn` - the anchor's OWN column, newest first - and Left/Right are the only keys that cross to another branch, landing on the commit nearest the CURRENT HEIGHT (ties resolve upward) so the selection moves sideways instead of teleporting to some branch's tip. Do NOT move vertically by the global commit order: that drifts sideways on its own, which both leaves Left/Right with nothing to do and makes Home/End/PageUp/PageDown land on a branch the user never asked for (they are column ends too, via `gitGraphColumnEdge` / `jumpGitGraphVertical`, and a jump CLAMPS to the end where a single step holds). A commit's message carries the same `onClick` as its dot (`onMessageClick`), because the 5px dot is otherwise the only target and clicking the text a user is reading does nothing. `GitGraphView` also scrolls the selection back into view, since a 200-commit graph runs well past the viewport in both axes and an unseen selection is indistinguishable from a dead key. **The @gitgraph core must NOT know which commit is selected:** `<Gitgraph>` reads its `graph` prop only in its constructor, so a core rebuilt per keypress remounts the SVG, and the momentarily empty scroll container has its offsets clamped to zero - the graph snaps back to the newest commit on every arrow key and the whole feature reads as broken. Selection rides a context consumed by the view's own `renderDot`, and the `<Gitgraph>` key is derived from the core's CONTENTS so new commits still remount. That dot renderer is also what makes a commit clickable at all: it draws a transparent hit circle several times the 5px dot, concentric with it at `(dot.size, dot.size)`, which is where @gitgraph offsets the branch paths to. Note the graph is built from `git log --all` while the List view holds only the current branch, so graph navigation must move by COLUMN rather than by the list's index - a commit selected on a side branch has no list index, and the key reads as dead.

## Markdown, text, and fonts

- **Render markdown:** `<Markdown preset="chat | document | release-notes">` from `src/renderer/components/Markdown/` (do NOT hand-roll `<ReactMarkdown>` + a per-surface `components`/plugin map). The chat preset is what `MarkdownRenderer` wraps, and both wizard conversations render agent replies through it via `<WizardMarkdown>` (`Wizard/shared/WizardMarkdown.tsx`) - a wizard is where users ask for mockups, so it must draw everything chat draws. Do NOT bring back a reduced wizard preset. Shared internals: `buildMarkdownPlugins` (`Markdown/plugins.ts`), `preprocessMarkdown` (`Markdown/preprocess.ts`), leaf renderers in `Markdown/components/*`, and the document component map `createMarkdownComponents()` in `src/renderer/utils/markdownConfig.ts`. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → `<Markdown>`](UI-PATTERNS.md).
- **Markdown to plain text (list rows, previews):** `stripMarkdown()`, `flattenMarkdownTables()` in `src/shared/markdown.ts`. `stripMarkdown` drops table grids outright, because raw `| col | col |` pipes render as garbage in a list row. That is right beside prose and wrong when the table IS the whole answer: a Cue run whose stdout was only a table stripped to an empty string, stored a NULL excerpt, and History hid it as a run that printed nothing. `flattenMarkdownTables` keeps each row's cell text joined by a `·` with a space on each side, drops separator rows, and keeps an escaped `\|` as a literal pipe rather than a cell boundary. The two share their row and separator patterns, so they cannot disagree about what a table is. The order is the one `extractCueOutputExcerpt()` in `src/shared/cue/cue-summary.ts` uses: `stripMarkdown(text)` first, and only when that leaves no text, `stripMarkdown(text, { tables: 'flatten' })`. That option flattens at the step where tables are otherwise dropped, after code fences, inline code and emphasis are unwrapped, so a row wrapped in any of them is still found; `flattenMarkdownTables` on raw text misses a row in single backticks. Do NOT write another table regex or a second markdown-to-text pass.
- **Clickable task checkboxes in rendered markdown:** `toggleTaskCheckboxAtLine(content, line)` in `src/renderer/utils/markdownTasks.ts`, plus `rehypeSourceLine` (`src/renderer/components/Markdown/rehypeSourceLine.ts`) and the `onTaskToggle` option on `createMarkdownComponents()`, which swaps in `<TaskCheckbox>`. react-markdown renders every GFM checkbox `disabled`, so those three pieces are what make one clickable - the file preview and the Auto Run panel both ride them. Do NOT count checkboxes in the DOM and map them onto the Nth `- [ ]` line: that drifts the moment a task marker appears inside a code fence. **Every component override in `createMarkdownComponents()` must forward its props** (destructure `node` out, spread the rest): `rehypeSourceLine` tags EVERY block, and `lineSync.domGetTopLineByAttr()` reads those tags to keep the file preview and the editor on the same line across the toggle. `React.createElement('p', null, children)` eats the attribute, which left HEADINGS as the only anchored blocks - so a document scrolled to the very top reported the first heading, and hitting Edit jumped there. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Clickable task checkboxes](UI-PATTERNS.md#clickable-task-checkboxes) and [Preview/edit scroll sync](UI-PATTERNS.md#previewedit-scroll-sync-rides-the-same-data-source-line-tags).
- **A pane that reads and edits a markdown document:** the File Preview stack, never a bare `<textarea>`. `<Markdown preset="document">` for reading (scoped with `generateProseStyles({ theme, scopeSelector })` so its heading and table rules do not leak onto the surrounding chrome), `<MarkdownEditor>` from `src/renderer/components/FilePreview/markdownEditor` for writing (CodeMirror colouring, wrap-aware line-number gutter, imperative `focus` / `scrollToLine` / `setSearchMatches` / `getCaret` / `getSelectionRange` / `setSelection` / `replaceRange` / `getScrollTop` / `setScrollTop` handle), and `Cmd/Ctrl+E` to flip between them - read from the user's LIVE `toggleMarkdownMode` binding via `eventMatchesShortcutKeys`, never from a literal `e`, or the chord that flips a file preview and the chord that flips a memory drift apart after a remap. **Open in Preview**: these panes are opened to read far more often than to write. A pane registered through `useModalLayer` must bind the chord ITSELF (it blocks the app-level handler), must hand the caret to the editor on the way in and back to the list on the way out, and must key the editor on the filename - undo history belongs to one document, and carried across a switch an undo pastes the previous file's text into this one. Search highlights are PUSHED through `setSearchMatches()` from an effect rather than passed as a prop (CM6 owns its document; rebuilding the view discards the undo stack and the caret), built with `searchMatchRanges()` so the two modes cannot disagree about what is a hit. `readOnly` needs BOTH halves of the switch (`EditorState.readOnly` refuses edits, `EditorView.editable.of(false)` also drops the caret and the `contenteditable` attribute) - set only the first and you ship a pane that still looks like a text box and silently swallows typing. `onKeyDown` runs at `Prec.highest`, BEFORE CodeMirror's own keymap, and returning `true` swallows the key: that is how a host-owned popup claims the arrow keys, and returning nothing is the safe default. `onPaste` is the paste-side twin of `onKeyDown` - also `Prec.highest`, also `true` to swallow - which is how a host rewrites a paste (trimming whitespace, saving a clipboard image as an attachment) before CM6 inserts the clipboard text; it must answer SYNCHRONOUSLY, so claim the event first and finish the async work afterwards. `MemoryViewer` is the reference implementation; `MaestroPromptsTab` is the second rider and differs only where a prompt differs from a memory (it opens on `edit`, and Preview resolves `{{TEMPLATE}}` variables against the active agent first); the Auto Run panel is the third, and is the one that drives the handle hardest - tab insert, list continuation, checkbox insert, undo/redo, paste and scroll sync all go through `replaceRange` / `getSelectionRange` / `setScrollTop` rather than touching a DOM node. A host that edits through `replaceRange` comes back out through its own `onChange`, so an explicit edit that already pushed an undo entry has to mark the new content (Auto Run stamps `lastUndoSnapshotRef`) or that `onChange` schedules a second one. **Under test, CodeMirror cannot lay itself out** - jsdom has no `getClientRects` on a text range and CM6 throws - so any suite rendering a surface that contains the editor mocks the module with `markdownEditorModuleMock()` from `src/__tests__/helpers/mockMarkdownEditor.tsx`, a `<textarea>` double that implements the same handle against that textarea. Spell the `vi.mock` path relative to the TEST file. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → A Surface That Reads and Edits Markdown](UI-PATTERNS.md#a-surface-that-reads-and-edits-markdown).
- **Jumping a markdown preview to a heading:** `scrollToHeadingSlug(slug, container, behavior, onSelectHeading?)` plus `headingLevelColor(theme, level)` in `src/renderer/components/FilePreview/shared/headings.ts`. TWO surfaces navigate the same heading list - the Table of Contents overlay (`FilePreviewToc`) and the `#` heading palette (`HeadingPalette`) - and they must not disagree about what a jump does, because the preview has THREE render tiers and only one of them can be scrolled by slug. Rich and Giant mount every heading, so a `querySelector` on the slug is enough; the Fast tier virtualizes its blocks, so most headings are not in the DOM at all and the lookup silently no-ops. The `onSelectHeading` override is how the Fast tier scrolls by block index instead, and it returns a BOOLEAN rather than being tier-gated at the call site: `markdownFastRef` is null under the other two tiers, so "not handled" falls through to the DOM path on its own and no caller has to know which tier is mounted. `CSS.escape` is not optional - slugs come from heading text, so they routinely start with a digit (`#1-intro`), and a bare selector throws. `FilePreview` owns the single jump path and hands it to both surfaces - the palette directly, and the ToC through `FilePreviewToc`'s `onSelectHeading`, which the shared `TocOverlay` calls before falling back to its own `containerRef` lookup; do NOT give a new heading surface its own `markdownContainerRef` and scroll logic. The `#` keycap that advertises the palette is an opt-in `searchShortcutKey` prop on `TocOverlay` rather than a fixture of the panel, because that panel is shared with Director's Notes, which binds no such key. Keyboard navigation passes `'auto'` and clicks pass `'smooth'` on purpose: a held arrow key repeats faster than a smooth scroll animates, so every repeat cancels the one in flight and the list stalls. `FilePreview` owns the single `jumpToHeading` callback and hands it to both surfaces; do NOT give a new heading surface its own `markdownContainerRef` and scroll logic.
- **Which heading the reader is under right now:** `findActiveHeadingSlug(scroller, container, onReadActive?)` plus `ACTIVE_HEADING_FOLD_PX` in `src/renderer/components/FilePreview/shared/headings.ts`. This is the read half of the jump above, and it has the SAME tier problem: Rich and Giant mount every heading so a DOM walk answers it, while the Fast tier virtualizes its blocks and its headings mostly do not exist in the DOM. The `onReadActive` override is how the Fast tier answers from its parsed block array (`getActiveHeadingSlug` on `MarkdownPreviewFastHandle`), and it is tri-state on purpose: a slug means "this one", `null` means "above the first heading" (a real answer, not a miss), and `undefined` means "no opinion" and falls through to the DOM walk, so callers never have to know which tier is mounted. The DOM walk BINARY SEARCHES: headings sit in document order so their tops increase monotonically, and this runs on every scroll frame - do not turn it back into a linear `find`. `FilePreview` owns the measurement because only it holds both the scroller and the container ref, throttles it into the existing scroll listener, and skips the work entirely while the overlay is closed. Do NOT let `FilePreviewToc` measure for itself.
- **Highlight vs selection in a heading list:** `FilePreviewToc` takes `activeIndex` (where the document is scrolled) and keeps its own `selectedIndex` (which row is lit). They are separate for two reasons: a click or arrow press must light the row IMMEDIATELY rather than waiting for the jump's scroll to land, and near the bottom of a document the scroll clamps so `activeIndex` stops changing - a list driven only by the document would freeze and the last few headings would be unreachable by keyboard. `-1` is a real value on both: it means the reader is above the first heading, so no row is lit and the `Top` sash gets the accent rail instead.
- **Reaching the open file preview from a modal:** `requestHeadingPalette()` and `HEADING_PALETTE_EVENT` in `src/renderer/services/headingPalette.ts`. The command palette and the File Preview are siblings that cannot see each other: the preview owns its overlays in local state, and its `filePreviewRef` is created inside `MainPanel`, three levels below the modal that wants to act on it. Worse, the palette is a modal whose whole job is TAKING focus from the surface it is about to operate on. So the request rides one app-level `CustomEvent` (the same shape as `requestFileTreeRefresh`) instead of drilling a callback up through `MainPanel` and `App`; exactly one `FilePreview` is mounted at a time, so there is no ambiguity about who answers. Two rules make it safe. The SENDER decides whether the command exists at all - `buildFilePreviewCommands` only offers "Jump to Heading" for a markdown tab in preview mode that actually has headings, so a palette entry never appears under different conditions than the `#` key it mirrors. The RECEIVER re-checks the same guards anyway, because the user can close or edit the file between opening the palette and picking the command. The action is also deferred by a tick: both are modals that focus their own search box on mount, and the closing one restores focus on its way out, landing on top of the box that just opened. Do NOT reach for this to make an ordinary parent-to-child call - it exists for the modal-to-sibling case where a ref genuinely cannot cross.
- **Highlighting a search hit:** `highlightMatches(text, query, accentColor)` in `src/renderer/utils/highlightMatches.tsx` for ordinary text, `searchMatchRanges(text, query)` in the same file for the `{from, to}` byte ranges a CodeMirror `setSearchMatches()` wants, and `<TextareaHighlightOverlay>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/TextareaHighlightOverlay.tsx` for text inside an editable `<textarea>`. All three split on the shared `splitOnMatches()`, so the regex escaping and the match parity cannot drift between them - which is the whole point in a pane offering BOTH a rendered preview and a source editor, where two matchers would let the two halves disagree about what is a hit. Do NOT re-derive offsets by walking the text again; `splitOnMatches` already carries each segment's `start`. A textarea renders plain text and nothing else, so the overlay is a backdrop UNDER it whose own glyphs are transparent - the visible characters are always the real editable ones and only the mark backgrounds show through, which keeps the caret, selection, undo stack, and IME intact. Its font, padding, and border width are COPIED FROM THE LIVE COMPUTED STYLE rather than restated in CSS: a duplicated font rule works right up until someone edits the textarea's and not the overlay's, and every highlight then lands a few pixels off the word it belongs to. Do NOT swap the textarea for a contenteditable to get markup - that trades a one-off alignment problem for a permanent input-handling one.
- **Highlighting a FUZZY hit:** `renderFuzzyHighlight(text, indices, styles?)` in `src/renderer/utils/search.ts`, fed by that file's `fuzzyMatchWithIndices()`. This is a DIFFERENT problem from `highlightMatches` above, and reaching for the wrong one silently paints the wrong characters. A substring search hit is one contiguous run, so `splitOnMatches` can mark it; a fuzzy hit is a scattered subsequence (`oec` matching **O**PSWAT **E**quity **C**ase), so there is no run to wrap and the only honest highlight is per character. Pass the index set the MATCHER returned rather than re-deriving positions at render time - `fuzzyMatchWithIndices` prefers boundary-anchored positions and backtracks so the rest of the query can still match, and a naive first-occurrence scan disagrees with it on exactly the queries where the emphasis matters most. Riders: the slash-command popup (`highlightSlashCommand`, which shifts indices by one for the leading `/`) and the File Preview heading palette. Returns the plain string when the set is empty, so a no-query list costs no extra DOM.
- **Rendering a surface containing `<MarkdownEditor>` in a test:** `markdownEditorModuleMock()` in `src/__tests__/helpers/mockMarkdownEditor.tsx` (CM6 cannot lay itself out in jsdom)
- **Line numbers beside a `<textarea>`:** `<TextareaLineNumbers>` + `lineNumberGutterMetrics()` in `src/renderer/components/ui/TextareaLineNumbers.tsx`. The gutter is scroll-synced (translated by the textarea's `scrollTop`, written straight to the DOM so a fast scroll can't lag a frame) and wrap-aware (each logical line is measured against a hidden mirror sharing the textarea's font and wrap width, so number N sits on the FIRST visual row of line N). The Cue YAML editor is the one caller left - Auto Run's source editor moved to `<MarkdownEditor>` and rides CodeMirror's own gutter. Do NOT hand-roll another `value.split('\n').map((_, i) => <div>{i + 1}</div>)` gutter - that's what the YAML editor had, and it drifted out of alignment the moment the document scrolled or any line wrapped. Derive the textarea's `paddingLeft` from `lineNumberGutterMetrics(value).textPaddingLeft` rather than guessing a pixel value; the metrics are in `ch` and reserve two digits minimum so the editor doesn't reflow at line 10. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Line Numbers on a `<textarea>`](UI-PATTERNS.md#line-numbers-on-a-textarea-textarealinenumbers).
- **Font-family fallback chain:** `withMonoFallback()` / `resolveSurfaceFont()` over `MONO_FALLBACK_STACK` and `SANS_FALLBACK_STACK` in `src/shared/fontStack.ts`. The font pickers store a BARE family name (`Roboto Mono`) with no generic on the end, so a name that is not installed and not web-loaded resolves to the browser's document default, which is a proportional SERIF - the whole app renders in Times. Apply the fallback where the setting becomes a CSS value, never at the setting source: the picker's `<select>` has to match its options against the raw stored name. Every surface font (terminal, chat, file preview, file editor) stores the empty string to mean "inherit the interface font", and `resolveSurfaceFont` is what that empty string means - re-deriving it and forgetting the `.trim()` renders a whitespace-only family, which resolves to nothing. Do NOT add a surface-local fallback chain; a second chain drifts the first time one of them gains a platform, and that surface then degrades to different faces than the rest of the app. `XTerminal.tsx` shipped exactly that duplicate (`ensureMonospaceFallback`) and it was folded back in. **A fallback chain is not the whole answer for the terminal**, which additionally MEASURES: a configured font can resolve perfectly and simply not be fixed-pitch, so the appended generic is never reached while xterm draws every glyph on the pitch of `W` (`Cl aude`). That half is `isFixedPitchStack()` / `resolveTerminalFontFamily()` in `src/renderer/utils/fixedPitchFont.ts` (see the entry below), and it composes with the shared chain rather than replacing it. The MAESTRO wordmark is deliberately NOT derived from any setting - it uses `WORDMARK_FONT_STACK`, because a brand mark that changes identity with the reading font is a bug.
- **Fixed-pitch font for shell text:** `resolveFixedPitchFontFamily(fontFamily, fontSize?)` in `src/renderer/utils/fixedPitchFont.ts`, or `useFixedPitchFont(fontFamily)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useFixedPitchFont.ts` from a component. Maestro has ONE `fontFamily` setting shared with the app chrome, so a user who picks a proportional UI font would otherwise get proportional terminals, command-mode composers, and shell-output cards - and shell text is a grid, so `ls -l` stops lining up and box drawing stops joining. The check is a MEASUREMENT, not a name test: CSS cannot be asked whether a face is fixed-pitch, so `isFixedPitchStack()` compares the advance of `W` against `i` (in Avenir Next, the font this was found with, they are 1025 and 296). Appending a monospace fallback is not enough on its own - a proportional font that RESOLVES never reaches the fallback - so a stack that measures proportional is replaced outright with the shared `MONO_FALLBACK_STACK`. The FALLBACK half is not this module's: it calls `withMonoFallback` from `src/shared/fontStack.ts`, and a local duplicate of that chain (`ensureMonospaceFallback`, `FIXED_PITCH_FALLBACK_STACK`) did ship here once and was folded back in - do not reintroduce one. Do NOT reach for Tailwind's `font-mono` on a surface that shows shell text: it is a different stack from the terminal's, so the same command renders in two faces between the proposal card and the transcript. It lives in `utils/` rather than `XTerminal.tsx` so the chat-side surfaces can use it without importing xterm. The terminal additionally rides `resolveTerminalFontFamily()` from the same module, which composes the measurement with the shared fallback chain (see **Font-family fallback chain**). When a surface overrides the composer font this way, any decoration layered under it takes the SAME override - the mention-chip overlay is measured against the textarea's font, so a monospace textarea over a proportional overlay lands every chip off its word.
- **Saving the user's own font setup:** `captureTypographySnapshot(values, savedAt?)`, `parseTypographySnapshot(raw)`, `typographySnapshotPatch(snapshot)`, `typographySnapshotMatches(snapshot, values)` in `src/shared/typographySnapshot.ts`; the store half is `saveTypographySnapshot()` / `restoreTypographySnapshot()` in `src/renderer/stores/settingsThemeSlice.ts`, behind the single `typographySnapshot` setting. The Display tab's Factory Reset presets write all six surfaces' families AND sizes in one click, which made them unusable to the only people who would want them: a user who had taken the pickers apart could not so much as LOOK at Default or Hacker without losing work that took a dozen deliberate changes to build. The snapshot turns that from a decision into an experiment. Three invariants, each replacing a way this goes wrong. (1) The keys come from `TYPOGRAPHY_SURFACE_LIST` in `src/shared/typography.ts`, never from a hand-written list - a second list is exactly how a newly registered surface stops being saved without anyone noticing, and the surface registry already exists to prevent that. (2) Restore writes ONLY the keys the save actually holds, so a snapshot taken before a surface existed leaves that surface where the user has it rather than blanking its family to the empty string, which would silently re-point it at the interface font. (3) The persisted value is NARROWED by `parseTypographySnapshot`, not cast: it survives across versions and can be hand-edited or written by `maestro-cli settings set`, and a malformed one would otherwise arm a Restore button that wipes the fonts it touches. Zoom is deliberately outside the snapshot - it is an accessibility accommodation the user moves with `Cmd+=` as they read, not part of the look being saved - which matches `resetTypography` leaving it alone for the same reason. It is ONE slot, overwritten on save. Do NOT grow it into named profiles: the job is "get back to mine", and a list needs naming, renaming, and deleting UI for something most users save once.
- **Font zoom on a reading pane:** `useFontScale(storageKey)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useFontScale.ts` + `<FontScaleControl>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/FontScaleControl.tsx` (`variant="inline"` for a toolbar, `variant="floating"` for a pill over a scrolling pane, `variant="ghost"` for a row of borderless icon buttons; `tooltips="overlay"` swaps the native `title` for a `HoverTooltip` naming the `shortcutHint` key, for a surface where a native title never shows, such as the Quick Chat panel). The hook owns localStorage; the range, two-decimal rounding, and stepping are `clampScale()` / `stepScale()` / `FONT_SCALE_RANGE` in `src/shared/scaleRange.ts`, shared with the main process because a scale it owns (Quick Chat's text zoom, settable by `maestro-cli quick-chat font`) must clamp identically. A surface whose zoom is NOT renderer state builds the `UseFontScaleReturn` object itself and still draws `<FontScaleControl>` (see `QuickChatRoot`); do NOT hand-roll another `AArrowUp`/`AArrowDown` pair or another `clampFontScale`. Render it ONLY where the zoom moves type - Director's Notes hides it in Rich Mode, the file preview gates it on `canScaleFontForView()` in `filePreviewUtils.ts`, and Auto Run only floats it once the folder actually has a document. A floating pill is pinned with a `sticky top-* z-20 h-0` row, not `absolute`, so it needs no positioned ancestor and never displaces the content it sits over. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → `<FontScaleControl>`](UI-PATTERNS.md#fontscalecontrol-srcrenderercomponentsuifontscalecontroltsx).

- **The label above a form field:** `<FormFieldLabel theme htmlFor?>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/FormFieldLabel.tsx` - the small uppercase label `FormInput` draws for its own input, for a field that is NOT a `FormInput` (an autocomplete such as `EnvVarKeyInput`, a picker). The class string `block text-xs font-bold opacity-70 uppercase mb-2` was written out in more than a dozen files, and inside a Settings tab that has moved to `SettingsSectionHeading` the copy is a test failure: `settingsStyleGuide.test.ts` reads a bare `font-bold uppercase` class as a hand-rolled section heading. With `htmlFor` it renders a real `<label>` bound to that control; without one it renders a plain block, because a `<label>` that names no control is wrong markup. Do NOT use it as a section heading (that is `SettingsSectionHeading`, which carries the icon and the description), and do NOT write the class string out again for a new field. The older copies outside `FormInput` and the Local Models endpoint form are known debt, not a pattern to follow.

## Dataview (Obsidian queries and DataviewJS)

The maintainer guide for the whole feature is [DATAVIEW.md](DATAVIEW.md). These are the entry points other code calls.

- **Parsing or running a Dataview query, and finding a note's Dataview blocks:** `parseQuery(source)`, `executeDql(source, thisPath, index)`, `executeInlineDql(expr, thisPath, index)`, and `extractDataviewBlocks(markdown)`, all imported from `src/shared/dataview/index.ts`, the ONE import site for Dataview. `parseQuery` is upstream Dataview 0.5.68's own parser (vendored under `src/shared/dataview/vendor/`), so query semantics match Obsidian (DV-2); an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule rejects any import of `vendor/` from outside `src/shared/dataview/`, and tests follow the same rule by convention. `executeDql` runs one `dataview` fence against a `DataviewIndexReader` (the 9-member `FullIndex` contract, DEV-3) with `this` bound to the vault-relative `thisPath`, and NEVER throws: a parse error, a failed step, or `CALENDAR` comes back as a `DataviewResult` of kind `error` or `unsupported` carrying upstream's `Dataview: ` text, which the preview and the CLI print identically. Results are wire values (`toWireValue()` in `src/shared/dataview/wire.ts`); revive them with `reviveDataviewResult()` after they cross IPC or JSON. `executeInlineDql` is the `` `= expr` `` form with upstream's inline error prefixes. `extractDataviewBlocks` lists every `dataview` and `dataviewjs` fence with its 1-based line from markdown-it tokens (the parser the index uses), so a fence inside a list or a quote counts and a fence-shaped line inside other code or the frontmatter does not; `forEachMarkdownLine()` cannot do this because it skips fence lines themselves (DEV-4). Outside main, do not run `executeDql` yourself: the index lives in main (D2), so the renderer asks `window.maestro.dataview.query` and the CLI asks `maestro-cli dataview query`, both of which reach `DataviewService` below.
- **One markdown file to one Dataview page:** `parsePage(input, resolver, options?)`, `parseRawPage(input, options?)`, and `finishPage(raw, resolver)` in `src/shared/dataview/page.ts`. The replacement for Obsidian's per-file indexing step: `extractMetadata()` (M1, markdown-it tokens) builds the `CachedMetadata` Obsidian would have, the vendored upstream `parsePage` reads inline fields, tags, aliases, lists, and tasks from it unchanged, and `PageMetadata.canonicalize` resolves every link through `createLinkResolver()` (M3, Obsidian's link rules, in `src/shared/dataview/resolve.ts`). Pure and never throws: invalid YAML leaves an empty frontmatter and reports through `onWarning`. The split matters for caching: `parseRawPage` keeps links as written and is what the disk cache stores, because how a link resolves depends on the other files in the vault; `finishPage` resolves them and MUTATES its input, so encode a raw page before finishing it. Any change to what these return (M1, a vendored parser edit, the `Transferable` codec) must bump `DATAVIEW_INDEX_CACHE_VERSION` in `src/main/dataview/vault-cache.ts`, or warm caches keep serving the old shape (DATAVIEW.md, "Cache version rule"). Do NOT reuse `parseMarkdownLinks()` (`src/renderer/utils/markdownLinkParser.ts`) for Dataview: it drops positions, subpaths, and display text, extracts links inside code, and invents paths for unresolved links.
- **The Dataview vault index (query, status, rebuild, change events):** `DataviewService` and the lazy singleton `getDataviewService()` in `src/main/dataview/dataview-service.ts`. One `VaultIndex` per `(vault root, SSH remote)`: the root is the nearest `.obsidian/` ancestor of the note, else the owning agent's cwd (DV-4); it opens on first query from its disk cache when one exists, reconciles in the background, stays fresh from a chokidar watcher (local) or a 30 s poll (SSH), and closes after 10 minutes idle or when a ninth root opens. Its request methods (`query()`, `queryInline()`, `queryFile()`, `indexStatus()`, `rebuildIndex()`, `jsSnapshot()`, `readInRoot()`, and the trust edits) ARE the bodies of the IPC handlers in `src/main/ipc/handlers/dataview.ts` and of the CLI's WS handler in `src/main/web-server/handlers/messageHandlers/dataview.ts`, so a new caller calls the same method instead of opening an index. Each method validates the wire request, resolves the agent in main (`resolveDataviewAgentContext()`, which refuses an unresolvable SSH remote rather than falling back to local), and refuses with `encoreDisabledMessage('dataview')` while the Encore Feature is off, before any root opens; the DataviewJS methods also require `dataviewJsEnabled` and a trusted root. Answers carry `{ root, sshRemoteId, revision }`, and revisions never go back per root, so `(root, revision)` is a safe cache key; `onChange()` is the source of the `dataview:changed` broadcast, which the renderer fans out through `subscribeDataviewChanges()` (`src/renderer/services/dataviewChanges.ts`, one listener per window). `stopAll` on the Encore lifecycle calls `disposeAll()`; quit calls `shutdownDataviewService()`.
- **Whether a DataviewJS block may run:** `dataviewJsRunPolicy({ encoreOn, jsEnabled, trustedRoots, root, isWebDesktop })` and `isTrustedDataviewRoot(root, trustedRoots)` in `src/shared/dataview/trust.ts`, read in the renderer through `useDataviewJsRunPolicy(note)` in `src/renderer/hooks/file/useDataviewJsRunPolicy.ts`. The policy is `disabled` (feature or setting off: ordinary code, checked first so an off feature leaves no pill anywhere), `desktop-only` (the browser build never mounts a guest, DV-14), `run` (trusted root), else `ask` (also while the root is unknown). `isTrustedDataviewRoot` is the one comparison main (which refuses a snapshot or a file read in an untrusted root), the renderer, and the CLI's `dataview trust` verbs share: whitespace and trailing separators dropped, otherwise exact. Edit the list only through `withTrustedRoot()` / `withoutTrustedRoot()` / `cleanTrustedRoots()` in the same file, and from the renderer through `trustDataviewRoot()` / `untrustDataviewRoot()` (`src/renderer/services/dataviewTrust.ts`), which ask main and apply its answer, so the settings store and main never disagree. The hook asks main for the note's root (`dataview:jsRoot`) through `useCachedResource`, so a remounted block in a trusted vault repaints its guest instead of flashing the trust prompt. Never decide trust from a renderer setting alone: main re-checks on every snapshot and read.
- **A message from the DataviewJS sandbox:** `parseSandboxMessage(raw, from)` in `src/shared/dataview/sandboxProtocol.ts`. The validator for the closed message set of the `<webview>` bridge (D4 plus DEV-12's `eval` and `eval.result`). `from` names the SENDER, so the host's `parseSandboxMessage(raw, 'guest')` can never accept a `run` or a host reply forged by a script. A guest message passes only at 1 MB or less (`serializedJsonByteLength()`), as a plain object with exactly its type's fields (an own `__proto__` or `constructor` key fails), ids matching `^[\w.:-]{1,128}$`, and every path through `normalizeRootRelativePath()` (`src/shared/pathContainment.ts`), returned normalized. It answers a fresh object or `{ ok: false, reason }` and never throws; drop and log a refusal. Host messages are shape-checked without the cap, because a large vault's snapshot is several MB (DEV-11). Add a message by extending the union AND this validator together, with a case in `src/__tests__/shared/dataview/sandboxProtocol.test.ts`; never let a host handler read a guest message that has not passed it.
- **What counts as a wiki link, embed, or file path in markdown text:** `WIKI_LINK_PATTERN`, `IMAGE_EMBED_PATTERN`, `PATH_PATTERN`, `ABSOLUTE_PATH_PATTERN`, `TILDE_PATH_PATTERN`, `INLINE_CODE_EXT_PATTERN`, `MAESTRO_DEEP_LINK_PATTERN`, and `LINKABLE_EXTENSIONS` in `src/shared/fileLinkPatterns.ts`. Moved from `src/renderer/utils/fileLinks/patterns.ts` (which re-exports it, so existing renderer imports keep working) so that the Rich-tier remark plugin, the Fast-tier markdown-it adapter, the Dataview index (M1 in `src/shared/dataview/metadata.ts`), and the DataviewJS guest's markdown step all agree on what a `[[wiki link]]` is; drift showed up as "works in small files, broken in big ones". The patterns carry the `g` flag, so reset `lastIndex` before reusing one. They define SYNTAX only: what a link resolves to differs by consumer on purpose (`findClosestMatch()` for the preview, `createLinkResolver()` for Dataview), so do not route Dataview links through the preview's matcher or the reverse.

## Layout, scroll, sizing, and hook utilities

- **Following streaming output in a capped box:** `useStickToBottom(contentKey)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useStickToBottom.ts` - returns a callback ref that keeps a scrolling box pinned to its newest content and releases the moment the user scrolls up. Reach for it whenever a box has BOTH a capped height and content that arrives over time: once it hits its cap it stops growing, so the transcript's own auto-scroll has nothing left to follow and the user is stranded on the FIRST screen of output while the live tail piles up below. Pinning is recomputed from geometry on every scroll event, never remembered as a flag - a flag has to tell a programmatic scroll from a user one, and scrolling to where you already are fires no event to clear the guard. Do NOT collapse it into a boolean. Distinct from `useScrollIntoView` (one element in a list) and `TerminalOutput`'s MutationObserver (the whole pane). See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Following Streaming Output](UI-PATTERNS.md#following-streaming-output-usesticktobottom).
- **Reaching an off-screen item while dragging:** `useDragAutoScroll(ref, { active, axis?, edgeSize?, maxSpeed?, startInset?, endInset? })` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useDragAutoScroll.ts`. Any scrollable strip that also accepts an HTML5 reorder drag needs this, or the drag can only ever reach the items already on screen: the pointer pins the container's edge, the drop target stops moving, and the user has to drop, scroll, and drag again. While `active` is true it records `dragover` positions on the container and scrolls on a rAF loop whenever the pointer sits in an edge band, ramping speed with how deep into the band it is so a nudge creeps and a pinned edge moves fast. Three details are what make it not worth rewriting inline: the insets are FUNCTIONS evaluated per frame, because a bar with sticky overlays (the tab bar's search cluster and its `+`) has its real content edge somewhere inside its own border; a pointer outside the container on the CROSS axis is ignored, so a drag that wandered off the strip does not keep it moving; and a pointer that stops being reported for 600ms is dropped, because a drag that leaves the window delivers no further events and the last known position would otherwise scroll forever. Scroll deltas are time-scaled, never per-frame constants, so the speed does not change with frame rate. The loop starts its clock on the first real animation frame rather than at arm time, because the gap between the two would be charged as elapsed time and make the first frame jump.
- **Keeping a virtualized list on its selection:** the virtualizer's own `scrollToIndex` from an effect keyed on the selected index, never a `ref` on the selected row. An inline arrow ref is a new identity every render, so React re-attaches it (and re-fires it) on every render rather than on every change of selection - and since `@tanstack/react-virtual` re-renders inside `flushSync` on every scroll-offset change, that closes a loop: a wheel tick scrolls the list, the ref re-attaches, and `scrollIntoView` snaps it back inside the same event. `FileSearchModal`'s wheel was never broken; the row ref was undoing it. `scrollIntoView` also cannot reach a row outside the rendered window, and `behavior: 'smooth'` over thousands of rows animates long enough for the next gesture to fight it. In a NON-virtualized list use `useScrollIntoView` (`src/renderer/hooks/ui/useScrollIntoView.ts`), which keys on the value. Its fourth argument picks the scroll behavior: `'smooth'` (the default) for a dropdown stepped one item at a time, `'auto'` for a list the user HOLDS an arrow key on, because key repeat outruns a smooth animation and every repeat cancels the one in flight. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Scrolling a Virtualized List](UI-PATTERNS.md#scrolling-a-virtualized-list-to-the-selection).
- **Sizing a virtualized row the user's font decides:** `virtualizer.measureElement` as the row's `ref`, `data-index={virtualRow.index}` on the same node, and NO inline `height`. `estimateSize` is a guess, not a height: the moment a row's real height depends on something the app does not control - the UI font, a wrapped second line, a badge only some rows carry - the guess is wrong for somebody. `FileSearchModal` stacks a file name over a directory, and under a proportional UI font those two lines did not fit its 44px constant, so the text crammed together; a fixed-pitch font wants the tighter box and must not be padded out to match, so no single number works. All three parts are required and each fails silently on its own: without `data-index` the measurement is filed against `NaN`, and an inline `height: ${virtualRow.size}px` clamps the row to the number the virtualizer already believes so measurement can never disagree with it. Read `measureElement` straight off the virtualizer (a stable instance property); wrapping it in an inline arrow re-attaches the ref every render and remeasures the whole window, the same identity trap as the entry above. `HistoryPanel` and `UnifiedHistoryTab` did this first; keep a plain `estimateSize` for a genuinely uniform one-line list (`TextPreviewFast`, `ParquetGrid`), which is cheaper. jsdom reports zero height for everything, so tests assert the wiring, not the pixels - see [UI-PATTERNS.md → Sizing a Virtualized Row](UI-PATTERNS.md#sizing-a-virtualized-row-the-users-font-decides).
- **Element width for JS-computed layout:** `useElementWidth(ref, enabled?)`, `useElementHeight(ref, enabled?)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useElementWidth.ts`. `useElementHeight` is the same observer on the other axis, for a host that sizes itself to content (the Quick Chat window follows its composer's height); measure an element whose content decides its height, never one stretched to fill its container. Reach for it only when the value must exist in JS - an inline SVG chart needs real pixels for its viewBox, and a breakpoint that switches column counts needs a number to compare. Anything expressible in CSS stays in CSS. It returns 0 until the first measurement, so gate width-dependent children on `width > 0` rather than drawing a zero-width chart, and it no-ops without `ResizeObserver` so jsdom tests need no polyfill. When the element's own size is the thing being decided, use its siblings in the same file instead: `useFreeWidthInFlexRow` and `useFreeHeightInFlexColumn` report the room left from the PARENT (content box minus the other children), so the choice cannot feed back into its own measurement. The wizard's provider tiles use the height one to pick one row, two rows, or the scrolling strip.
- **Keeping a floating menu on screen:** `useContextMenuPosition(menuRef, x, y)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useContextMenuPosition.ts`, which `useAnchoredMenuPosition` also passes through. It returns `maxHeight` beside `left`/`top`, and **a menu whose content is unbounded must apply BOTH `maxHeight` and `overflowY: 'auto'`**. Clamping position alone is not enough and fails silently: for a menu taller than the viewport the clamp computes a NEGATIVE `maxTop`, so `top` pins to the padding and every item past the bottom edge is unreachable - these containers are `overflow-hidden`, and a context menu does not scroll the page behind it. The file tree's menu carries up to ~27 entries, which overflows a phone in portrait and a laptop in a short window. The budget is measured from where the menu LANDS, not from the click, because a short menu near the bottom is pushed up and has more room than `y` suggests. Do NOT substitute a viewport-relative cap like `maxHeight: 'calc(100vh - 1rem)'`: a menu anchored low still runs off the bottom with nothing to scroll it. Do NOT drop the sibling `opacity: ready ? 1 : 0` when adding these - it is what stops the menu flashing at the pre-measurement position.
- **How a floating surface looks (popover, menu, hover card, tooltip):** `elevatedSurfaceStyle(theme)` / `getElevatedSurfaceColors(colors)` in `src/renderer/utils/elevatedSurface.ts`. Spread it into the surface's `style` and drop any `border` / `shadow-*` classes and `backgroundColor` / `borderColor` entries, since it sets the fill, a 1px edge, and the shadow together. Every popover used to paint itself with a base token (`bgSidebar`, `bgActivity`, `bgMain`) and `theme.colors.border`, so one opened over a surface of the same token had the same fill as everything behind it, and the border (1.0-1.6:1 against `bgActivity` on most bundled themes) was too faint to show the edge. The fill steps one rung PAST `bgActivity` toward `textMain`: `bgActivity` is the outermost base surface on every bundled theme, so one more step differs from all three bases in both light and dark modes with no mode branch. The edge blends `border` toward `textMain` (at least 1.66:1 against the fill on every bundled theme, about 2:1 or better on most). A theme's declared `surfaceElevated` wins, and `useThemeStyles` publishes the same fill as `--surface-elevated`, so CSS and inline styles agree. Do NOT reach for a base token on a new popover, and do NOT raise the blend ratio to fix one theme: dim text on the fill pays for every step. Floating PANELS (Thought Stream, Context Timeline, the media player), find bars, and drag ghosts are not popovers and keep their own treatment.
- **Dropdown/tooltip anchored to a header element:** `useAnchoredMenuPosition(menuRef, anchorRef)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useAnchoredMenuPosition.ts`, rendered via `createPortal(..., document.body)`. Anything anchored inside the Main Panel header MUST use this. `absolute top-full` is silently clipped: the header wraps its left cluster in `overflow-hidden` boxes only as tall as the pill, which is how the git pill's hover card and the git status tooltip were both invisible for months. Bare `position: fixed` doesn't help either, because `.header-container` sets `container-type: inline-size` (implying `contain: layout`), making the header a containing block for fixed descendants. Since jsdom has no layout engine, a test asserting `toBeInTheDocument()` passes while the element is invisible - assert it is NOT a descendant of the header subtree instead.
- **A pane width the user sets by dragging:** `useResizablePanel()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useResizablePanel.ts` for the drag itself, then pick the persistence by where the pane lives. Top-level chrome (Left Bar, Right Bar) is a real setting: pass `settingsKey` and back it with a `settingsStore` field. A pane INSIDE another surface (the Document Graph's preview pane, a split inside a panel) is a view preference: pair it with `usePersistedPanelWidth(storageKey, { defaultWidth, minWidth, maxWidth })` and OMIT `settingsKey`, or the hook writes the width a second time under a key nothing reads back. Keep the stored bounds and the live clamp separate - the stored bounds decide what may be written to disk, while the `maxWidth` passed to the drag folds in the container as it is right now, so a width that was legal on a maximized window can't swallow the canvas after the modal is resized down.
- **A dropdown the user resizes by its bottom edge (never off screen):** `useResizableDropdownHeight({ storageKey, open, defaultHeight, minHeight })` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useResizableDropdownHeight.ts`, paired with `<ResizeHandles directions={['s']} contained onResizeStart onResetSize canReset />`. First user: the Auto Run document dropdown (`AutoRunDocumentSelector`). It keeps two numbers apart on purpose. The PREFERRED height is what the user dragged to, persisted through `usePersistedPanelSize` (the axis-agnostic core under `usePersistedPanelWidth`) and never rewritten by the viewport. The RENDERED max height is `fitDropdownHeight(preferred, room)`, where `room` is the space from the dropdown's top edge to the bottom of the window less `DROPDOWN_VIEWPORT_MARGIN`, re-measured in a layout effect on open and on every window `resize`. So a height picked on a big monitor shrinks to fit when Maestro moves to a smaller one and comes back when it returns, and the bottom row is never off screen: when `room` is below `minHeight`, the room wins. It is a MAX height, so a short list stays short. Do NOT clamp the stored value to the viewport (that loses the big-monitor preference), and do NOT size from the anchor button: measure the dropdown itself, since its top does not depend on its own height. The drag writes `style.maxHeight` directly and commits on release, swallows the one click the release would synthesize under the cursor, and commits on window blur, matching `useResizableModal`.
- **Sizing a surface to its longest label:** `widestLabelWidth(labels, { fontSizePx, chromePx?, minPx?, maxPx? })` and `estimateLabelWidth(label, options)` in `src/renderer/utils/labelWidth.ts`, for a dropdown or menu that should open wide enough to read its entries. It is an ESTIMATE from character count on purpose: a real measurement needs a live layout, so it is unavailable while computing an initial size and produces nothing in jsdom, whereas a per-character average is synchronous and testable and wrong by a few pixels - the right trade for a size the user can drag afterwards. Pass the font size rather than a px-per-character constant: three earlier inline copies each hard-coded their own (`7.5`, `7.5`, `7`) without recording the size it was tuned for, which is how a type-scale change turns into a clipped label with no error anywhere (see [DEDUP-TRACKER.md](DEDUP-TRACKER.md) finding #42). Always pass `maxPx` on a surface fed by user-supplied names - one pathological name otherwise opens a menu the width of the screen - and count ALL of the row's chrome in `chromePx`, icons and gaps included, or the estimate is right and the label still truncates.
- **Remembered textarea height:** `useResizableTextarea()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useResizableTextarea.ts` - every `resize-y` textarea should keep the height the user dragged it to, across reopen and app restart. Spread its `style` last and pass `externalRef` if the component already owns a ref. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Resizable Textareas](UI-PATTERNS.md#resizable-textareas).
- **Auto-growing composer textarea:** `useAutosizeTextarea()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useAutosizeTextarea.ts` (helpers in `src/renderer/utils/textareaSizing.ts`). Every composer that grows with its content rides it - AI composer, both wizard composers, group chat, feedback chat. Do NOT hand-roll `height = 'auto'` then `height = scrollHeight` again: the `'auto'` toggle collapses the internal scroll to the top, so a full, scrolling composer clipped the line being typed on every keystroke. Run it on the committed value, never inside `onChange` - an `onChange` resize never fires for dictation, draft restore, or template insertion, which grows the text without growing the box. Distinct from `useResizableTextarea`, which remembers a height the USER dragged.
- **Guarded `localStorage` access:** `safeLocalStorage()` in `src/renderer/utils/safeLocalStorage.ts`, with `safeSessionStorage()` beside it for state that belongs to one browser TAB rather than to the browser, `writeStorageValue(storage, key, value)` for a write to any Storage, and `safeStorageGet(key)` / `safeStorageSet(key, value)` for ordinary localStorage preference reads and writes. Reading the global itself can THROW (storage-blocked renderer, private mode, a jsdom test without Storage), so never touch `localStorage` directly in a hook - three private `storage()` copies existed before this was pulled out. The accessors only cover REACHING the object: `getItem` / `setItem` still throw on a denied or quota-exceeded origin, which is what the get/set pair and `writeStorageValue` swallow. Keep `safeLocalStorage()` for `removeItem` / `key` / enumeration. A hostile Storage must cost the user their persistence, not their pane.
