- **A view preference a user sets by clicking:** `usePersistedToggle(storageKey, defaultValue)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/usePersistedToggle.ts` - one boolean in localStorage, for a collapsed banner or a folded section that must survive the surface unmounting (a Right Bar tab switch, a re-render from new data) but isn't worth a Settings row. Storage failures degrade to in-memory only, so a storage-blocked renderer costs the user persistence, not their pane. Do NOT hand-roll another `useState(() => localStorage.getItem(...) === 'true')` pair - a collapse that resets on every re-render reads as the banner refusing to stay closed. `AutoRunNoticeBanner`'s `collapseKey` is the first caller. For a preference that is one of several words rather than yes/no (a sort mode, a view density), use `usePersistedChoice(storageKey, options, defaultValue)` in the same folder: it validates the stored string against the option list, so a mode from an older build falls back to the default instead of stranding the surface in a state its control cannot express. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Collapsible Advisories](UI-PATTERNS.md#collapsible-advisories-autorunnoticebanner-usepersistedtoggle).
- **Focus after render:** `useFocusAfterRender()` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/useFocusAfterRender.ts` (do NOT use `useEffect + setTimeout(() => ref.focus())`). Same file: `useFocusOnMount()` for a modal that must land with its input focused, and **`useFocusOnClose(ref, isOpen)` for handing the caret BACK when a transient surface closes**. The layer stack restores focus generically now (`useLayerStack` snapshots `document.activeElement` when a focus-capturing layer registers and hands it back when the layer unregisters, but ONLY if focus landed nowhere), so a modal registered through `useModalLayer` needs nothing. Reach for `useFocusOnClose` when the surface is NOT a registered layer (an inline editor, a popover) or when the caret should land somewhere OTHER than where it started - it wins, because the stack defers to anything that already claimed focus. It fires only on the true -> false edge, so it cannot steal focus from something the user clicked afterwards. Do NOT restore focus by hand in a modal's `onClose`: a surface dismissed with Escape never runs it, which is how focus ended up on `document.body` with the pane behind it looking ready while swallowing the next keystroke.
- **Event listeners:** `useEventListener()` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/useEventListener.ts` (do NOT pair raw `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` inside useEffect)
- **Debounce/throttle:** `useDebouncedValue()`, `useDebouncedCallback()`, `useThrottledCallback()` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/useThrottle.ts` (filename is misleading - all three live here)
- **Identity-stable callback:** `useStableCallback()` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/useStableCallback.ts` - one identity forever, always calling the newest implementation. Reach for it when a new callback identity is read as a STRUCTURAL change rather than a fresh value. The canonical case is `createMarkdownComponents()`: it builds a fresh map of component functions, so rebuilding it gives React a new component TYPE for every element and it unmounts and remounts the entire rendered document, discarding the reader's scroll position and restarting images and Mermaid. Any callback that closes over document content (a task-checkbox toggle, for instance) is reborn on every edit and triggers exactly that. Do NOT use it to silence an exhaustive-deps warning - it hides the callback from dependency arrays, so an effect that should re-run when its captured values change no longer will.
- **Data a surface fetches that must survive it unmounting:** `useCachedResource(key, fetcher, { enabled?, onError? })`, `revalidateCachedResource()`, `readCachedResource()`, `clearCachedResources()` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/useCachedResource.ts`; the Usage Dashboard wraps it as `useDashboardResource()` (`src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/useDashboardResource.ts`, adds the `stats:updated` refresh) and `useTokenUsage()` (`src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/useTokenUsage.ts`). The failure it replaced: the dashboard is mounted only while open, every panel held its fetch in `useState`, so every reopen started from a skeleton and fetched the same data again, and a `stats:updated` refresh that set `loading` blanked the panel mid-read. Invariants: results live at MODULE scope per key, so a remount renders the last one on its first frame and revalidates on mount; concurrent consumers of one key share one promise; `loading` is true only while there is NOTHING to show, and a failed refresh keeps the previous data beside the error; a `clearCachedResources()` bumps a generation so a fetch started before it cannot repopulate the cache. Traps: put everything that changes the request (range, filters, a Cue trigger exclusion list) in the KEY, never only in the fetcher, or two requests share an entry; and do not cache per-viewer UI state here - it is for fetched data.
- **Honoring the user's settings in a secondary window:** `useWindowSettingsSync()`, `usePublishTypographyVars()`, `useReloadSettingsOnExternalChange()` in `src/renderer/hooks/settings/useWindowSettingsSync.ts`. A window that boots its own renderer and mounts one view (Quick Chat) does not run `useSettings`, which is where the main window loads settings, follows changes made elsewhere, and publishes the typography CSS variables. Without those side effects the window loads the store defaults and stays there: Quick Chat shipped drawing every glyph in the store-default monospace face whatever typography the user had picked, and a theme or font changed in the main window never reached it. Call `useWindowSettingsSync()` once at the root of any such window. `useSettings` runs the two halves itself, so the main window and every secondary window publish typography through one effect. Peer windows hear about a write through `settings:externalChange` (`notifyPeerWindows` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/persistence.ts`); the writer is deliberately never echoed. Do NOT hand-roll another `applyTypographyVars` effect or `onExternalChange` listener in a new window.

## Phone and touch (web-desktop)

- **Whether a surface is on a phone:** `usePhoneLayout()` / `isPhoneLayout()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useViewportBreakpoint.ts` - the web-desktop bundle at the `xs` breakpoint (below 640px), with the CSS twin `html[data-runtime='web-desktop'][data-bp='xs']` in `src/renderer/index.css`. THE gate for simplifying a surface on a handheld: icon-only toolbars, full-screen drawers, a sheet instead of an anchored popover, a folded composer. It is viewport-driven and never pointer-driven, because the two questions have different answers: a tablet has a finger (coarse pointer) without being short on room, and a desktop browser squeezed to phone width is short on room without a finger. Touch GESTURES (long-press, swipe) therefore gate on `isCoarsePointer()` separately, while every layout decision keys off this. The native Electron app never reports phone layout, however narrow its window - it has a keyboard and a mouse, and its user chose that width. Do NOT hand-roll `window.innerWidth < 640 && isWebDesktop()` at a call site; the moment one side uses a different number, a surface simplified in JS and one simplified in CSS disagree about when a phone is a phone. See [WEB-MOBILE.md → Phone Layout](WEB-MOBILE.md#phone-layout).
- **Touch primitives:** `isCoarsePointer()`, `triggerHaptic()`, `supportsHaptics()`, `HAPTIC_PATTERNS`, `GESTURE_THRESHOLDS`, `MIN_TOUCH_TARGET` in `src/renderer/utils/touch.ts` (do NOT re-derive `navigator.vibrate` calls or `matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)')` queries)
- **Long-press gesture:** `useLongPress()` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/useLongPress.ts` (scroll-aware touch long-press that opens right-click affordances like context menus and tab overlays; do NOT hand-roll `setTimeout` + `touchmove`)
- **A bottom sheet on a phone:** `<PhoneBottomSheet>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/PhoneBottomSheet.tsx`, with `PHONE_SHEET_SCRIM_ARM_MS` beside it. THE shell for any surface that is an anchored popover on desktop and cannot be one at 390px: measured off a 40px control, an anchored menu runs past the bottom of the screen with no way to scroll it and no way to dismiss it. It owns the scrim, the grip-only swipe-down (`useSwipeGestures`, bound to the grip row alone because a swipe that starts inside the scrolling body must scroll that body rather than dismiss the sheet), the `<EscCloseButton>`, the `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` padding, and the rise animation, so a caller supplies content and nothing else. It was written inside `TabOverlayPortal` and lifted out when `ComposerOptionsSheet` needed the same chrome; do NOT `createPortal` another `fixed inset-0 z-[100]` shell. Two props carry the whole difference between callers. `bodyClassName` is the caller's own styling hook - `.maestro-tab-sheet__body` flattens a menu authored for a mouse, and a sheet authored for a phone in the first place wants none of that, which is why that rule stayed scoped to the tab sheet when the shell was shared. `scrimArmMs` MUST match the gesture that opened the sheet: one opened by a LONG-PRESS keeps the 500ms default, because the browser follows the finger lifting with synthesized mouse and click events at a position the sheet now covers and the sheet would close the instant the user let go, while one opened by a plain TAP passes `0`, since arming it there only swallows the user's first real dismissal. `maxHeight` is the third dial: `80dvh` for a menu that should stay out of the way, `50dvh` for a panel the user works inside. `scrimRef` is deliberately the SCRIM's ref, not the panel's - `TabOverlayPortal` hands its hover overlay's ref there so click-outside treats the whole screen as inside the menu and the sheet owns its own dismissal.
- **Rows inside a `PhoneBottomSheet`:** `PhoneSheetSection`, `PhoneSheetOptionRow`, `PhoneSheetActionRow` in `src/renderer/components/ui/PhoneSheetRows.tsx`, with `PHONE_SHEET_ROW_HEIGHT` (52) and `PHONE_SHEET_OPTION_HEIGHT` (44) beside them. `<PhoneBottomSheet>` owns the CHROME; these are the only three shapes that go inside it. A multi-choice control is an ACCORDION rather than a tap-to-cycle pill - cycling one step per tap is a fine control beside a mouse and a poor one under a finger, since the user cannot see the options, cannot go back, and has to tap through a state they did not want - and only ONE section is open at a time, which the CALLER owns, because a sheet is a fraction of a screen and a second open list sits below the fold with no sign it is there. `PhoneSheetActionRow` is the row that DOES something rather than selecting a value (copy, reveal, delete) and is a peer of a section header, so it carries the same height. The two heights are constants rather than inline numbers because they ARE the tap-target floor: Apple's 44pt minimum, applied here by hand. `src/web/index.css` declares a blanket 44px `min-height` on every button under 767px, but nothing imports that file (the web-desktop bundle loads only `src/renderer/index.css`), so no such floor exists at runtime. They were written inside `ComposerOptionsSheet` and lifted out when `FilePreviewActionsSheet` needed the same three shapes - do NOT re-declare a local `OptionSection` / `OptionRow` in a third sheet.
- **A tab chip's action menu shell:** `<TabOverlayPortal>` in `src/renderer/components/TabBar/TabOverlayPortal.tsx`. Every chip (AI, file, terminal, browser, group) draws its action menu in it. On desktop it is the anchored popover that hangs off the chip at the position `useTabHoverOverlay` measured, faded in once clamped to the viewport - the shell that used to be copy-pasted, byte for byte, into all five chip components. On a phone (`usePhoneLayout`) it hands off to `<PhoneBottomSheet>`: an anchored popover positioned off a 40px chip is unusable at 390px, and it ran past the bottom of the screen with no way to scroll it and no way to dismiss it. The sheet chrome - the scrim, the grip swipe, the close button, the safe-area padding, the scrim arm delay - lives in that shared shell now, so the phone branch here is a handful of props; `TAB_SHEET_SCRIM_ARM_MS` survives as a re-export of `PHONE_SHEET_SCRIM_ARM_MS` under the name this menu and its tests already use. The menu CONTENT is the same element in both shapes; the sheet restyles it through `.maestro-tab-sheet__body` (full width, finger-sized rows, and every `<ShortcutHint>` hidden via its `data-shortcut-hint` attribute, since a chord badge on a phone advertises a dead key), so a menu never knows which shell it is in. That CSS rule is scoped to the TAB sheet rather than to every sheet: it exists to flatten a menu authored for a mouse, and it would only damage a sheet whose content was written for a phone to begin with. The chips themselves are `<LongPressable>` - a tap selects on any tab, active or not, and a LONG-PRESS opens the menu; tapping the already-active tab used to open it on touch, which read as the tab refusing to switch. `LongPressable` takes `innerRef` so the chip keeps its own ref, and its post-long-press click suppression is TIME-BOUNDED (iOS sends no click after a long-press, so a flag that waited for "the next click" ate the user's next deliberate tap). `draggable` is off on coarse pointers because a long-press is also how the OS starts an HTML5 drag, and the two cannot share the gesture; reordering stays reachable from the menu.
- **The file preview toolbar on a phone:** `<FilePreviewActionsSheet>` in `src/renderer/components/FilePreview/FilePreviewActionsSheet.tsx`, fed by the `FilePreviewHeaderAction` / `FilePreviewHeaderStat` lists `FilePreviewHeader` builds. The desktop header puts up to fourteen icon buttons beside the file name, then a directory path line, then a stats subbar; at 390px the 44px tap-target floor turns seven visible icons into two rows on its own, and the three bands together ate roughly 400px of an 844px screen before a word of the file was readable. On a phone the header keeps the file name, Save, and a single "..." that opens this sheet. **The actions are not restated in the sheet.** `FilePreviewHeader` builds ONE ordered list that it renders as icon buttons and the sheet renders as labelled rows, so every `toolbarVisibility` gate is applied once for both - a sheet with its own copy of those fifteen conditions would hand back a button the user went to Settings to remove, and a button added later would land in one surface and be missing from the other. The stats are one list for the same reason. Save is deliberately NOT in the list: it is a labelled primary control with a dirty state the user is watching for, and two taps to reach it is worse than the width it costs, so it stays inline on both. The preview tier is the one entry that is not a plain button - `PreviewTierChip`'s menu is an anchored popover, which a scrolling sheet body would clip, so in the sheet it becomes a `PhoneSheetSection` accordion over the same `TIER_META` / `TIER_DESCRIPTION` the chip uses. The sheet's path-and-stats header is NOT gated on `showStatsBar`: that flag hides the subbar while the reader scrolls the document, which says nothing about a sheet the user opened by hand.
- **Every composer setting on a phone:** `<ComposerOptionsSheet>` in `src/renderer/components/InputArea/components/ComposerOptionsSheet.tsx`, opened by the `...` button in `ToolbarControls`' phone branch. The desktop toolbar spreads History / Access / Thinking / Model / Effort across a row of pills that each open their own anchored dropdown, and at 390px that row wrapped, crowded the send button, and opened popovers sized for a mouse. So the phone row keeps only what a thumb reaches for mid-sentence - attach, mic, send in the centre, `...` - and everything else moves into this sheet. Two controls are dropped outright rather than moved: the prompt-composer pencil and the notification bell, both of which open surfaces of their own that do not belong on a 390px composer. Send MOVES here from the column beside the composer (`InputArea` renders `<NotificationSendControls>` only off-phone), so the composer gets the full width it needs to show what is being typed. Everything in the sheet is an ACCORDION, never a tap-to-cycle pill: cycling one step per tap is a fine control beside a mouse and a poor one under a finger, because the user cannot see the options, cannot go back, and has to tap through a state they did not want. That is what put the "set this mode" halves beside the cycle halves in `src/renderer/utils/tabHelpers/focusFields.ts` - `permissionModeFields()` / `nextPermissionMode()`, and `setShowThinkingFields()` which `cycleShowThinkingFields()` now delegates to - so the sheet and the toolbar cannot drift on keeping `readOnlyMode` in lockstep with `permissionMode`, or on dropping thinking logs when the mode goes off. Only ONE section is open at a time, because the panel is `50dvh` and a second open list sits below the fold with no sign it is there. `standard` is omitted for an agent with no working permission relay, so the user can never pick a mode whose tool approvals never arrive, and the model list always carries a leading `(default)` entry even when the caller's array omits the empty string - without it there is no way back to the agent's own configured model.
- **A keyboard-only hint (chord badge, keycap, arrow-key legend):** put `data-shortcut-hint` on it. One rule in the "Phone layout" block of `src/renderer/index.css` hides every element carrying it on a phone. A phone has no keyboard, so a `⌘K` beside a menu row, a `⌘F` keycap inside a search box, or an `↑↓ navigate • Enter select` footer advertises a key the user does not have - and on a 390px screen those badges were the first things to push a modal's close button off the right edge, which is how the cross-tab search modal ended up with no way out. `<ShortcutHint>` (the tab overlay menus) sets it, and so do the hamburger badges, the palette row chords, the Settings search keycap, the Usage Dashboard "Press Esc" footer, and the file-search, process-monitor, branch-switcher, and cross-tab-search legends. Do NOT gate a hint on `usePhoneLayout()` in JSX instead: the attribute is what makes the rule exhaustive, and a hint that forgets it is the only way this drifts.
- **A control whose only pointer path is a double-click:** give it a single-tap path under `isCoarsePointer()`. iOS reserves double-tap and a home-screen web app never dispatches `dblclick`, so a surface that accepts only on `onDoubleClick` opens on a phone and then answers every tap with nothing - the slash-command popover, the model/effort console, the process list, and both table previews all shipped that way. The mouse path stays as it was; the coarse branch is the addition: accept on the first click where a click was otherwise inert (`SlashCommandPopover`, `ParquetGrid`, `CsvTableRenderer`), or on the second tap of the row already picked where the first click has a job (`ModelEffortModal`). Drive it in tests with `setCoarsePointer()` from `src/__tests__/helpers/mockPointer.ts`. There is NO 44px tap-target floor to lean on either: `src/web/index.css` declares one, but nothing imports that file, so a small control grows its hit area with a `::before` (`[data-tab-close]`, `.resize-handle`) rather than a blanket `min-height` that would wreck the dense rows sized by hand.
- **A tab chip on a phone:** the chip is capped at `60vw` and only its label (`data-tab-label`) shrinks; the close button carries `data-tab-close` and a `::before` that grows its 18px hit area by 8px on any coarse pointer. Every chip label is `whitespace-nowrap` with no cap, which is right beside a mouse, but a long name made the chip wider than the strip between the sticky search cluster and the sticky "+", and the keep-active-visible scroll in `TabBar` then chose the label's start and left the close x painted under the "+" cluster. Mark the label and the close button with the attributes on any new chip type; the rules key on them, not on the component.
- **A modal that fills a phone:** nothing to write. Every modal that passes a `resizeKey` stamps `data-modal-resize-key` on its dialog (35 sites: the palette, Settings, Usage Dashboard, Director's Notes, Process Monitor, file search, Symphony, Cue, ...), and the phone stylesheet sizes those to the viewport, hides their `[data-resize-handle]` grips, and pads the overlay for the iOS status bar and home indicator. The overlay rule is keyed on the modal's PARENT (`:has(> [data-modal-resize-key])`), not on `.modal-overlay`, because nine modals draw their own `fixed inset-0` overlay without that class and were sized to the visible viewport but centred in the layout viewport, which put their top edge under the status-bar band that swallows taps. Before that a 390px phone showed a 326px box floating in a padded overlay with the app peeking around it, headers wrapping to two lines, and the resize grips reachable but useless. A small dialog (confirm, rename tab) passes no resize key and keeps its natural size, which is the right shape for a question. Do NOT add a per-modal phone branch for sizing; the phone branches in `TabSwitcherModal` and `CrossTabSearchModal` predate the rule and also change CONTENT, which is why they stay.
- **Swipe down to close a modal on a phone:** `useLayerSwipeDismiss()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useLayerSwipeDismiss.ts`, mounted once in `LayerStackProvider` (`src/renderer/contexts/LayerStackContext.tsx`). Every modal has a keyboard exit and is supposed to have a graphical one, but on a phone the first does not exist and the second has failed in practice: a header that overflows a 390px screen pushes its close pill off the right edge, and the user's only way out was to reload the page. This is the safety net, wired to `closeTopLayer()` - the same path Escape takes, so dirty-state confirmation and nested layers behave identically. It is deliberately conservative so it cannot fire by accident: web-desktop with a coarse pointer only; only while a layer is open; the gesture must START in the top quarter of the viewport (where a full-screen modal's header lives; lower down is scrolling); not from a form control; not inside a region that is already scrolled down (pulling a list back toward its top is a scroll, and iOS sheets follow the same rule); and it must travel at least 80px, mostly vertically, within half a second. A surface that pans on drag (a canvas, a graph) opts out with `data-no-swipe-dismiss` on its root. Do NOT add per-modal swipe handlers - one gesture, one place, one rule.
- **Clearing the iOS status bar in a home-screen web app:** `--maestro-top-inset` (`src/renderer/index.css`, "Home-screen web apps on iOS" block) and `installStandaloneStatusBarInset()` / `measureStandaloneStatusBarInset()` in `src/renderer/utils/standaloneStatusBar.ts`. The failure it replaced: the Main Panel header rendered inside the iOS 26 status bar band, where the system draws a frosted layer and swallows taps, so the hamburger was unreachable; and the shell was clipped at the status-bar line because the roots were `height: 100%` (the small viewport) while the shell was `100dvh`. Invariants: the property is `max(env(safe-area-inset-top), var(--maestro-status-bar-inset))` because WebKit bug 301994 reports `env()` as 0 and shortens the viewport instead, and the measured half is `screen.height - innerHeight` only for `navigator.standalone === true`, portrait, and a difference under 80px (a keyboard is larger; landscape hides the bar). Traps: `display-mode: standalone` also matches Android PWAs, whose page never extends under the bar; a hard-coded 62px is wrong on every other iPhone and on a healthy WebKit; and every fixed full-screen surface (drawers, phone modals) needs the inset too because it sits outside the shell's padding. The same installer publishes a second property, `--maestro-viewport-height`, from `measureVisibleViewportHeight()`: `visualViewport.height` when the browser reports one, `innerHeight` otherwise, rounded DOWN so a fractional height cannot push a modal's footer off the bottom edge. It exists because on iOS the on-screen keyboard slides OVER the layout viewport rather than shrinking it, so `100dvh` is unchanged while the bottom ~45% of the screen is covered - a full-screen phone modal sized to `100dvh` keeps its full height with its lower half behind the keys. The failure that surfaced it: both search surfaces autofocused their input on open, so the keyboard was up from the first frame and the results list was born buried, which reads as "I can filter but I can't scroll the list." The fix is two-sided and a phone surface with a list plus a filter box needs both halves - size the sheet to `var(--maestro-viewport-height, 100dvh)`, and gate the mount focus on `usePhoneLayout()` (`useFocusAfterRender(ref, !phone)` / `useFocusOnMount(ref, undefined, !phone)`). Do NOT drop the `100dvh` fallback: a browser without `visualViewport` would otherwise get a zero-height modal. See [WEB-MOBILE.md → Standalone mode](WEB-MOBILE.md#standalone-mode-ios-home-screen).

- **Tap-vs-hold on a microphone button:** `usePressAndHold({ holdThresholdMs, onHoldStart, onHoldEnd, onTap })` in `src/renderer/hooks/utils/usePressAndHold.ts`. A microphone button has to answer two different habits and a voice UI that picks one has half its users fighting it: someone dictating a paragraph holds the button like a walkie-talkie, and someone having a conversation taps it once and forgets about it. The document chat's push button and the dictation button classify against the SAME `resolveHoldThresholdMs(...)` the global hotkey uses (`src/shared/acappella/voice-controls.ts`), so a button and a key can never decide "hold" at different moments. The voice HUD's talk button rides the hook too, but only in push-to-talk and at a ZERO threshold: hands-free versus push-to-talk is an explicit mode there (see the next entry), so there is no tap to tell apart, and any threshold is that much of the first word spoken into a shut microphone. Three behaviours are the reason it is a hook rather than two copies of a `setTimeout`, and each one is a bug a copy would have: the release listener is on the WINDOW, because an element-scoped `onPointerUp` never fires when the user drags off the button - the commonest way to abort a press - and the floor is then left open with nothing holding it; UNMOUNT releases the hold, since a press interrupted by the surface closing otherwise leaves a hot microphone with no widget attached to it; and the press state lives in refs, because the window listener is attached once and would otherwise read a stale `holding` from the render it was created in. The callbacks are read through a ref for the same reason, so a caller passing inline arrows cannot tear the listener down mid-press. Do NOT hand-roll another hold timer beside a microphone.
- **Opening and shutting the voice microphone without ending the session:** `openVoiceMic({ hold?, scope? })`, `closeVoiceMic({ send?, scope?, endSession? })`, `setVoiceListenMode(mode)`, `toggleVoiceListenMode()`, `notifyVoiceStartFailed(error)` in `src/renderer/services/voiceListening.ts`; `VoiceSessionService.setListening(open, { hold?, send? })` in `src/main/acappella/voice-session-service.ts`; the `listen-gate` event in `src/shared/acappella/protocol.ts`; `VoiceListenMode`, `asVoiceListenMode()`, `nextVoiceListenMode()`, `resolveSendWait()`, `clampSendWaitOverrideMs()`, `formatSendWait()`, `SEND_WAIT_STOPS_MS`, `sendWaitStops()`, `sendWaitOverrideForStop()` in `src/shared/acappella/listen-mode.ts`; `<VoiceWaitPicker>` in `src/renderer/components/ACappella/VoiceWaitPicker.tsx`. The HUD's talk button used to END the session when clicked while listening, and it guessed tap-versus-hold from how long the press lasted. Both are gone. The button is now one of two explicit things, chosen with the hand button and remembered in `ui.listenMode`: hands-free (a click opens the microphone, another shuts it; a send phrase or the send wait sends) or push-to-talk (open exactly while held; the release sends). Neither ends the session, and only Escape closes the HUD (`voiceSessionStore.surfaced` keeps it up after a session ends on its own). Invariants, each one a way this fails quietly. **A paused session is still `listening`**: the gate is the DEVICE, not a session state, so the session can still dispatch, speak and be re-bound. Never derive "the microphone is open" from the state alone; `voiceHudVisualState(state, listenPaused)` in `src/shared/acappella/hud-state.ts` turns the pair into one answer (`paused`), and a surface that skips it draws a live meter over a shut microphone. **The gate rides its own event**, never `listen-start`: the session re-emits `listen-start` at the end of every turn, so the audio bridge ignores it while paused, and a session that STARTS gated emits `listen-gate` first so no consumer opens a device it then has to close. **A pause does not flush playback** (`AudioPipeline.pause()` versus `stop()`): "stop hearing me" must not become "stop talking". **Push-to-talk holds the composer open** (`UtteranceComposer.holdOpen()`): a pause inside the sentence endpoints the recogniser, and with the settle timer running half a request would be dispatched while the button is still down. `holdOpen()` also disarms a release left armed by the last hold. **Every renderer call goes through one module-scope queue**: a press may have to start a session first, and unordered, a quick tap's release reaches main before that session exists, finds nothing to shut, and the microphone the press then opens stays open with no button holding it. **Opening resumes or starts**: a session ends on its own (idle timeout, stop word) while the HUD stays up, so `setListening` returning false means "start one", in the scope the last session ended in. **`beginVoiceSession()` opens gated in push-to-talk** unless the caller passes `open: true` (the press itself), so the composer microphone or the palette cannot open a hot microphone in the mode that exists to prevent one. **The send wait is read live**: `resolveSendWait()` joins the stored default (`turnSettleMs`, or `sendHoldMs` under hold-until-send) with the HUD's in-memory override (`acappella:set-send-wait`, cleared when the HUD closes), and `UtteranceComposer` reads it per fragment through `getConfig`. It used to be copied at construction, and the composer outlives every session of the service that owns it, so a changed wait never took effect until the provider trio was rebuilt. Do NOT call `window.maestro.voice.setListening` from a component.
- **Writing the A Cappella settings blob from the renderer:** `patchVoiceSettingsSection(section, patch)`, `readVoiceSettingsBlob()`, `ACAPPELLA_SETTINGS_KEY` in `src/renderer/services/voiceSettings.ts`. Everything A Cappella persists is ONE settings object, so changing a field is a read of the whole blob, a merge, and a write of the whole blob. Two rules ride that sequence and both lose a setting without an error. **Write the patch, not the form:** a writer that saves every field it holds turns the DEFAULTS it was showing into stored values. `useVoiceControls` did that, so a later change to a default (the wake phrase, then the send phrases) reached nobody who had ever moved a slider and needed a migration step each time (`migrateLegacyVoiceProviders`, steps 3 and 5). **One write at a time:** Settings and the HUD both write this blob, and two interleaved read-modify-writes drop whichever landed first, so every patch goes through one module-scope `createKeyedWriteQueue`. `voiceUiStore` (the `ui` section) and `useVoiceControls` (the `controls` section) both ride it; `useVoiceProviderSelection` and `usePairedDevices` still hand-roll the sequence and should move here rather than gain a fifth copy. Main reads the blob per turn, so a patch applies to the next thing said with no further signal.
- **VoiceStudio source configuration:** `readVoiceStudioConfig()`, `voiceStudioConfigProblem()`, `normalizeVoiceStudioConfig()` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-studio.ts`; `VoiceStudioClient` in `src/main/acappella/providers/local/voice-studio-tts.ts`. Accepts loopback URLs only. Resolve profiles by unique name, and fail explicitly when the service or profile is unavailable; never substitute another speech provider.
- **The voice send phrases (the spoken "send it"):** `DEFAULT_SEND_PHRASES`, `parseSendPhrases()`, `formatSendPhrases()`, `isLegacyDefaultSendPhrases()` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-controls.ts`; matched by `matchSendPhrase()` in `src/main/acappella/speech/send-phrase.ts`; edited in the HUD's send panel (`VoiceWaitPicker`, through `voiceUiStore.setSendPhrases`) and in Settings -> Voice Controls. Stored comma separated under `controls.sendPhrases`, and ABSENT is not EMPTY: absent takes the shipped list, an empty string turns the spoken send off. The shipped list is ONE phrase, `go maestro`: the five everyday phrases it replaced ("good to go", "that's it", "send it", "go ahead", "make it so") all end ordinary sentences, so a request that closed with one lost its last words and went early. Do NOT add an everyday phrase back to the default; a user who wants one types it. The FIRST phrase is the one every surface quotes. A phrase ends a request in BOTH timing modes, so its field is not gated on "Wait for me to say send". The HUD's wait slider is a per-conversation override held in main; the phrase it sits beside is the stored setting, because a phrase that reverted when the HUD closed would be one that stops working without saying so.
- **Which agent a voice session is bound to:** `voiceScopeAgentId(scope)` in `src/shared/acappella/document-scope.ts`, alongside `isDocumentScope()`, `documentScopeName()` and `applyDocumentScope()`. `VoiceScope` has FOUR kinds and two of them carry an agent (`agent` and `document`; the Conductor and Quick Chat name none), so the hand-written `scope.kind === 'agent' ? scope.sessionId : null` that used to be copied into six files reports a document conversation as an unbound Conductor session: the HUD relabels itself "Conductor", the Left Bar's talking indicator goes dark on the agent that is actually being talked to, and the router loses its bias. A document scope is an agent scope that also knows what the conversation is ABOUT, and `applyDocumentScope` is the one place its three rules live (it stays with its agent, the opening turn hands the document over, every later turn recalls the tab that conversation opened in). Apply it AFTER routing, never before - the Brain still has to read what was said. **Starting one goes through `talkWithDocument()` in `src/renderer/services/documentVoice.ts`**, the same arrangement as `requestFileDeletion`: the File Preview toolbar microphone, the Files panel menu entry, and the palette's `File: Talk with Document` all route there so the Encore gate, the agent resolution, the HUD un-hiding, and the refusal toast cannot drift. Which files it is OFFERED on is `isTalkableDocumentPath()` in `src/shared/fileKinds.ts` (images including SVG, playable media, and compiled binaries have no text to read), asked once so a file cannot show the microphone in one surface and hide it in another.
- **Binding a voice session to Quick Chat (locked):** `QUICK_CHAT_VOICE_SCOPE`, `isQuickChatScope()`, `quickChatScopeDecision()`, `scopeRebindBlocker()`, `QUICK_CHAT_SCOPE_ROUTER_ID` in `src/shared/acappella/quick-chat-scope.ts`; `scopeRouterId()`, `isScopeRouterId()`, `isSameVoiceScope()` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-mode.ts`; `dispatchToQuickChat()`, `QuickChatVoiceHost` in `src/main/acappella/dispatch/quick-chat-dispatch.ts`; `QuickChatController.showForVoice()` in `src/main/quick-chat/quick-chat-controller.ts`; `stopVoiceSessionForWindow(windowId, cause)` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/acappella.ts`; renderer `useQuickChatVoice()` in `src/renderer/hooks/voice/useQuickChatVoice.ts` and `<QuickChatVoiceButton>` in `src/renderer/quickChat/`; `openVoiceMic({ scope, onError })` in `src/renderer/services/voiceListening.ts`. The Quick Chat window's microphone opens a voice session bound to the Quick Chat conversation and to nothing else. The scope is `{ kind: 'quick-chat' }` and carries NO agent or tab id on purpose: Quick Chat's tab does not exist until the first message, a new chat replaces it, and the picker moves the chat to another agent, so a scope with ids would be stale the moment the user did any of those. Three rules, one place each. **Every sentence is the message:** no Brain runs (`scopeRouterId()` names the two scopes that work this way, Tab mode and Quick Chat, and `isScopeRouterId()` keeps their turns out of the routing log). **Quick Chat delivers it:** the executor branches on the SCOPE and calls `dispatchToQuickChat()`, which runs the same `send` command the window's Send button and `maestro-cli quick-chat send` use. Do NOT route a Quick Chat turn through the `remote:*` channels: `remote:focusAiTab` un-hides an ephemeral chat's hidden tab and raises the main window, and the roster filters hidden tabs out, so a recall of it fails. It also skips the dispatch replay cache, which would drop the second of two identical short answers. **It cannot be re-bound, in either direction:** `VoiceSessionService.setScope()` asks `scopeRebindBlocker()`, so the HUD's mode switch, the focus follow and `maestro-cli voice scope` all get the same refusal, and the CLI prints its sentence instead of "no session". The session is OWNED by the Quick Chat window whoever asked for it (`ownerWindowFor()` in the IPC layer claims it through `showForVoice()`, which also puts the window on screen for `maestro-cli voice start --quick-chat`), so no app window draws a HUD for it; a start with Quick Chat turned off is refused by name. Putting the window away ends the session (`QuickChatWindowDeps.onHidden` to `stopVoiceSessionForWindow(id, 'hidden')`), because Quick Chat hides on Escape and stays alive, and a session bound to it would otherwise listen behind a window nobody can see. In the renderer the Quick Chat window boots its own bundle, so `useQuickChatVoice()` mounts that window's ONE `useVoiceSession()` subscription. Every window mirrors the one session, so "live" there means `isQuickChatScope(scope)` AND an active state, never an active state alone, and `openVoiceMic({ scope })` resumes only a session already on that scope and replaces any other (read inside the queue, so a hold queued behind a tap sees the session the tap opened). That window has no toast surface: pass `onError` and show the sentence inline.
- **Switching the voice mode (Tab / Agent / Conductor):** `useVoiceModeSwitch()` returning `{ mode, switchTo, cycle }` in `src/renderer/components/ACappella/useVoiceModeSwitch.ts`; `SWITCHABLE_VOICE_MODES`, `stepSwitchableVoiceMode()`, `switchableVoiceMode()`, `scopeForMode()` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-mode.ts`; `voiceHudClaimsTab()` in `src/renderer/stores/voiceUiStore.ts`. Two controls switch the mode and must agree: the segmented switch (`<VoiceModeSwitch>`) and the `Tab` key while the HUD is up (`Shift+Tab` goes back). Both call the hook, so the key walks the modes in the order the switch draws them, and `cycle()` steps OVER a mode with nothing to bind to (Tab or Agent with no agent open) rather than stopping dead on it. The key is a window listener in the BUBBLE phase, enabled only while the HUD is visible, not minimized, on top (`useIsTopLayer`), and has a live session; it returns on `defaultPrevented`, which is how a completion popup, a shell line and an editor's indent keep their Tab without the HUD knowing any of them exist. The one wrinkle is a surface that calls `preventDefault()` on Tab only to keep focus from wandering: the AI composer does, and focus is usually there. It asks `voiceHudClaimsTab()` (the exact condition the listener is enabled under, published by the HUD) and lets the key through when the HUD wants it. Do NOT move the listener to the capture phase to fix a surface that eats the key: that takes Tab from every completion popup in the app. **Tab and Agent mode FOLLOW the user**: `scopeFollowingFocus(scope, focus)` in the same shared module is the rule, `useVoiceScopeFollow()` (mounted in `VoiceHud`, the one voice surface that is always mounted) applies it, and `resolveFocusedAiTabId(session)` in `src/renderer/utils/panelLayout.ts` is "the tab in front" for both the follow and the mode switch. **Picking a mode binds to what is ON SCREEN** (`scopeForMode` puts the focused agent and its front tab ahead of the bound agent and the last dispatch, which are fallbacks only), and re-picking the mode that is already on runs the follow rule once, so the pill always means "this agent" or "this tab". Both read the screen through `readVoiceFocus()` in `useVoiceScopeFollow.ts`. The follow acts on a CHANGE of focus, never on a mismatch: a session an agent's own wake phrase opened is bound to that agent whatever is on screen, and enforcing "scope equals focus" would re-bind it the moment it started. An identical scope is never re-sent, because `setScope` clears the Conductor's pending question; that is also what makes a dispatch that focuses its own tab a no-op. A session that ended while the HUD stayed up is re-bound in the store (`voiceSessionStore.rememberScope`), since the talk button restarts on that scope and main has no session to re-bind.
- **Marking what holds the voice floor (agent, tab, Conductor):** `<VoiceFloorMark>` in `src/renderer/components/ACappella/VoiceFloorMark.tsx`; `useConductorHoldsFloor()` in `src/renderer/components/ACappella/ConductorVoiceIndicator.tsx`. One filled microphone in the readable theme accent for every place the floor can sit: the agent row (`AgentVoiceIndicator`, Tab and Agent mode), the pinned tab (`TabVoiceIndicator`, Tab mode), and beside the Left Bar wordmark (`ConductorVoiceIndicator`, Conductor mode). Callers decide WHETHER they hold the floor; the mark only draws it. Do not hand-roll another `<Mic fill>` span for a floor glyph. The Conductor glyph is the one status glyph allowed in the header's identity zone, because in Conductor mode the floor belongs to Maestro itself; `SessionList` reserves its width through `useConductorHoldsFloor()` before deciding to draw the wordmark.
- **Teaching the wake word a phrase from the user's own recordings:** `runWakeEnrollmentRequest(raw)`, `registerWakeEnrollmentService()`, `WakeEnrollmentService` in `src/main/acappella/wake/wake-enrollment-service.ts`; `trainEnrolledWakeModel()`, `findSpeechBounds()`, `wakeFrameWeights()` in `src/main/acappella/wake/wake-trainer.ts`; `scoreEnrolledWakeWindow()`, `prepareWakeWindow()`, `nearestWakeSimilarity()` in `src/main/acappella/wake/enrolled-wake-model.ts`; `loadEnrolledWakeModel()`, `enrolledWakeRevision()`, `hasEnrolledWakeModel()`, `addWakeClip()`, `deleteWakeEnrollment()` in `src/main/acappella/wake/wake-enrollment-store.ts`; `wakePhraseKey()`, `parseWakeEnrollmentRequest()`, `canTrainWakePhrase()`, `wakeEnrollmentPassage()`, `describeWakeEnrollmentReport()` in `src/shared/acappella/wake-enrollment.ts`; renderer `useWakeEnrollment(phrase, enabled)` and `<WakePhraseTrainingRow>` in `src/renderer/components/Settings/ACappella/`. openWakeWord ships one classifier per phrase, each trained on thousands of synthetic clips, so a phrase of the user's own had nothing that could fire on it. Enrollment records the phrase a couple of dozen times plus half a minute of other speech and builds a matcher from them. **It is a matcher, not a classifier, on purpose:** a logistic or MLP head fitted to thirty clips learns "is not the thirty seconds of other speech I was shown" and fires on the first sentence it has not heard (measured on four synthetic voices: three to six times the false alarms of the matcher at the same recall). So every recording is KEPT as an example, a few times over with pace, level, room noise and preceding speech varied, and live audio scores by cosine similarity to the nearest one in the space openWakeWord's own front end produces. Do NOT replace it with a trained head without a much larger negative set than a user can record. Invariants, each one a measured failure. **Frames are weighted by how much of them the phrase accounts for** (`wakeFrameWeights`): a speech embedding spans 760 ms, so at the moment a phrase ends only the newest few are all phrase; compared one for one, a phrase said straight after other speech matched a recording made in silence half the time. **Embeddings are centred before comparing** (`prepareWakeWindow`): raw ones all point roughly the same way, so unrelated sounds already score about 0.9. **The threshold comes from leave-one-recording-out**, never from a recording scored against itself (always a perfect match), and sits past the middle of the gap toward the recordings, because thirty seconds understates what other speech will sound like. **Examples must be computed exactly as live audio is**: the trainer runs recordings through the same `WakeFrontEnd` stream the scorer uses, with sub-hop jitter, because the live stream is cut into hops wherever it happens to be cut. **An enrolled phrase outranks a pretrained one** in both the scorer (`createOnnxWakeScorer`) and the gate (`resolveWakeClassifier`); they must agree, or the gate reports a phrase ready that the detector scores with a different model. **Retraining has to replace the armed detector**: `HandsFreeSettings.modelRevision` (from `enrolledWakeRevision()`) is part of the always-on listener's arm key. **One entry point for every transport**: the `acappella:wake-enrollment` IPC channel and `maestro-cli voice wake` (the `voice_wake_enrollment` bridge message) both call `runWakeEnrollmentRequest()`, which owns the rule that record, import and train need voice switched on while status, stop and remove do not (switching voice off must still close a microphone and delete a recording of the user's voice). The service owns no microphone: who may borrow it is the IPC layer's call (`microphoneBusyReason` / `openMicrophone` / `closeMicrophone`), and the always-on listener stands down through `isMicrophoneBorrowed`. The recordings are user data under `userData/acappella/wake-phrases/<key>/`, NOT the Local Models store: no catalog entry describes them and no download restores them. Do NOT hand-build that path, and do NOT add a second writer of `model.json`.
- **A voice action from outside the app (`maestro-cli voice`):** `runVoiceControlRequest(raw)`, `registerVoiceControlService(handlers, isVoiceEnabled)`, `VoiceControlRefusal`, `VoiceControlHandlers` in `src/main/acappella/voice-control-service.ts`; the wire contract (`VoiceControlRequest`, `VoiceControlResults`, `parseVoiceControlRequest()`, `parseVoiceControlScope()`, `voiceControlActionNeedsVoice()`, `VOICE_CONTROL_MESSAGE`, `VOICE_PREVIEW_LINE`) in `src/shared/acappella/voice-control.ts`; the CLI round trip `sendVoiceControl()` / `tryVoiceControl()` in `src/cli/services/voice-control.ts`; the bridge hop `handleVoiceControl()` in `src/main/web-server/handlers/messageHandlers/voiceControl.ts`. Every voice action a person takes in the HUD or in Settings > Voice is a function the A Cappella IPC layer owns, because that layer holds the session service, the audio bridge, the pipeline and the microphone. So the handlers are BUILT there (`controlHandlers` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/acappella.ts`), from the same named functions the `acappella:*` channels call, and registered here, the arrangement wake phrase enrollment has. A new voice verb adds one action to the request union, one result shape, one handler in that table, and never a second implementation. Three rules the entry point enforces for every transport: the Encore gate matches the IPC channels exactly (ending a session, managing a stored key and revoking a device work with voice off), a refusal the app gives on purpose is `ok: false` with a sentence (a handler throws `VoiceControlRefusal`) while any other throw stays an error, and a scope with an unknown kind is refused instead of read as the Conductor. The bridge hop answers only `maestro-cli` (`client.cli`): a voice credential is not a browser's to write. A session a verb starts belongs to the focused window (`resolveVoiceWindowId()` with no sender), as it does for a global hotkey. Pairing has NO action here on purpose: Approve is a decision for the person at the desktop.
- **The A Cappella settings blob as a table (what a writer outside the panels checks against):** `VOICE_SETTING_FIELDS`, `voiceSettingProblem(path, value)`, `voiceSettingDefault(path)`, `isVoiceSettingPath()`, `redactVoiceSettings()`, `storableVoiceProviderIds(slot)`, `withAgentWakePhrase()`, `readAgentWakePhrases()` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-settings-schema.ts`; reached generically through `nestedSettingProblem(key, value)`, `nestedSettingPathProblem(key)`, `getSettingDefaultAtPath(key)`, `isNestedTextSetting(key)`, `redactNestedSettingSecrets(key, value)` in `src/shared/settingsMetadata.ts`. A Cappella keeps everything it persists in ONE object and every reader of it sanitises, which is right for a reader and wrong for a writer that is not a form: `settings set acappella.controls.turnSettleMs fast` stored the word, reported success, and left the user on the default. The table has one row per stored field (its path under `acappella`, its kind, its bounds, its default), and the bounds and defaults are the constants the panels and `readVoiceProviderSettings()` already use, never a second copy. `settings set|get|reset` and the `voice providers|voices|wake-phrase` verbs all go through it, so none can store what a panel would refuse, an unset field reads as its default, and a path nothing reads is refused with the paths there are. `ice.turn.credential` is the one field marked `sensitive`: an object read masks it, a read of its own path does not. A NEW A Cappella setting gets a row here in the change that adds it, or `settings set` will refuse it as unknown. A second object setting that wants the same treatment adds an entry to `NESTED_SETTING_SCHEMAS`, not a special case in a `settings` command. After a write to the FILE the main process is brought up to date by `syncACappellaAfterExternalSettingsChange()` (`src/main/ipc/handlers/acappella.ts`), called from the settings watcher, because the store's change event fires only for a write made inside the app.
- **The wake word's front end (audio to classifier windows):** `WakeFrontEnd`, `loadWakeFrontEndModels()`, `featurizeRecording()`, `createWakeFeaturizer()` in `src/main/acappella/wake/wake-front-end.ts`; the stream's shape (`WAKE_HOP_SAMPLES`, `WAKE_CLASSIFIER_WINDOW`, `WAKE_WARMUP_HOPS`, `WakeFeatureWindow`, `WakeFeaturizer`) in `src/main/acappella/wake/wake-features.ts`. The mel spectrogram and speech embedding graphs every wake phrase shares, as one stream class. Two callers need exactly the same numbers: the always-on scorer and the wake phrase trainer, whose examples are worthless if they are computed any other way than live audio is. It must match openWakeWord's reference exactly: samples at int16 scale, the previous 480 samples in front of each hop, and the mel output rescaled `x / 10 + 2` (three departures together scored a clear "hey jarvis" at 0.009 where the reference scores 0.999). The shape constants live in `wake-features.ts`, which loads nothing, so the matcher and trainer are testable without a native runtime (`fakeWakeFeaturizer()` in `src/__tests__/helpers/wakeTrainingFixtures.ts`). Do NOT open the two graphs with `InferenceSession.create` at a new call site.
- **Cutting a microphone stream into bursts of speech:** `SpeechBurstSegmenter` in `src/main/acappella/audio/speech-burst.ts`, over `VoiceActivityDetector`. One burst is one stretch of talking with silence either side, handed over once: at the cap when the speaker keeps going (`truncated: true`), otherwise when the speech ends (with `speechMs`). It keeps a little audio from BEFORE the onset, because the detector needs a few frames to be sure and those frames are the first syllable. The transcription wake engine decodes each burst and wake phrase enrollment keeps each one as a recording; the bookkeeping was inline in the first and about to be copied into the second. It copies every frame it is given, since capture paths reuse one buffer. Do NOT hand-roll another lead-in ring and burst buffer beside a `VoiceActivityDetector`.
- **Reading a WAV file a caller named by path:** `readPcmWavFile(filePath, { maxBytes, purpose, reader })` in `src/main/utils/wav-file.ts`. For features whose audio normally comes from the microphone and whose CLI path brings a file instead (`maestro-cli models run dictation --audio`, `maestro-cli voice wake add`): an agent has no microphone. The path arrives from a bridge, so it is validated (absolute, exists, is a file, under the size bound) before it is read, and each failure is a sentence that names the problem. Only 16-bit PCM is decoded; the caller resamples with `resampleLinear` / `resampleFloat32` if it needs another rate. Do NOT inline another `fs.stat` + `decodeWavPcm16` at a new call site.
- **Chatting with a document:** `services/documentChat.ts` (the conversation) plus `hooks/ui/useDocumentChat.ts` (live state) and `components/DocumentChat/` (the bubble). The chat is an ORDINARY AI TAB tagged with `AITab.documentOrigin`, not a second chat system, and everything the feature promises falls out of that one decision: it persists across restarts because sessions are already written to disk; popping out is `focusAiTabInSession`; and a spoken turn lands in the same conversation because `RosterTab.documentPath` carries the binding to the voice router (`documentTabId` in `voice-session-service.ts` prefers it over the per-session `lastDispatch`, which is the only source that survives a relaunch). The tab is created HIDDEN, exactly like a cross-agent consult tab and for the same reason - chatting about a file from a preview pane is not the user asking for a chip in the strip - which is why `buildRoutingTabs` carves document chats out of its hidden-tab filter: dropping them made a spoken turn open a SECOND tab about a file the user had already been typing to. Three rules that are easy to get wrong: the opening prompt goes through the shared `buildDocumentOpeningPrompt(scope, request, modality)`, and `'typed'` exists so a chat panel never tells the agent it is a spoken conversation; `readDocumentChatView` draws QUEUED messages as pending bubbles, because a message behind a working agent is not in the transcript yet and a chat that swallows what you typed reads as broken; and New chat archives the old binding (`documentOrigin.archived`) without changing visibility or deleting it. `restoreDocumentChat()` selects a prior thread for that file; `keepDocumentChat()` reveals it without leaving the document; `stopDocumentChat()` removes its queued messages and interrupts only its process. Document transcripts, including archived threads, bypass the ordinary persistence log cap. Messages share Quick Chat thinking projection, with no display-history cap. The panel anchors above the preview's bottom-left chat button. `talkWithDocument()` opens the preview, then `requestDocumentChatPanel()` / `consumeDocumentChatPanelRequest()` match the path and agent before `startDocumentVoice()` opens the embedded microphone. Document sessions suppress the HUD. Hold-release sends through the shared microphone queue; panel close or unmount ends only the matching document session, including a start still in flight. **Open in tab** lives in the header; the compact composer has no Keep or Thinking controls.

If your use case does NOT match an existing utility, prefer extending the canonical file over creating a new one. If you genuinely need something new, add the full entry HERE and a one-line index entry in CLAUDE.md so the next person can find it.

The tracker at [DEDUP-TRACKER.md](DEDUP-TRACKER.md) lists all known duplication findings.

- **The Local Models catalog and store (which models exist, where their files are):** `LOCAL_MODEL_CATALOG`, `getLocalModel(id)`, `isLocalModelId()`, `localModelsForRuntime()`, `allLocalModels()` and the model id constants in `src/shared/local-models/model-catalog.ts` (types `LocalModelEntry`, `LocalModelKind` in `src/shared/local-models/types.ts`); the install layout in `src/main/local-models/model-store.ts` (`getStatus()`, `isInstalled()`, `listStatuses()`, `verify()`, `installPathFor(entry, file)`, `totalFootprint()`, `removeAll(include?)`); downloads through `getModelDownloader()` in `src/main/local-models/model-downloader.ts`; and the one implementation both IPC surfaces call, `src/main/local-models/service.ts` (`listLocalModels()`, `downloadLocalModel()`, `verifyLocalModel()`, `removeLocalModel()`, `installLocalRuntime()`, ...). Local Models is a CORE service, not an Encore Feature, and A Cappella is one consumer of it. Four rules. **The core never grows a field per consumer:** a consumer that needs its own words for a model (a voice slot, a setup bundle, a wake phrase) keeps a table keyed by model id in its own module (`src/shared/acappella/voice-models.ts` is the reference), and a model records only the `runtime` it needs. **A model file path comes from `installPathFor()`, never from a hand-built `path.join(userData, 'models', ...)`:** both roots resolve in `src/main/local-models/paths.ts` (`userData/models/<model-id>/`, `userData/runtimes/<runtime-id>/`), and the legacy `acappella/` roots are moved once at startup by `migrateLegacyLocalModelStorage()` in `src/main/local-models/storage-migration.ts`, so a second copy of the path rule points at a directory that no longer exists. **Bytes reach the final path only after their SHA-256 matches the pinned catalog entry** (resumable `.part` files, streaming hash), so do NOT fetch a model with a bare `fetch()` beside the downloader: that is how an unverified file ends up loaded as a model. **`removeAll()` with no filter deletes the WHOLE models root:** a consumer reclaiming its own disk passes a `ModelDirFilter` (voice passes `isVoiceReclaimableModelDir`), or it deletes the embedding model and the injection classifier another consumer runs. A predicted failure is a `LocalModelError` (`src/main/local-models/errors.ts`) with a stable `code` and a `recovery` sentence written for a person; an unknown id handed to the store is a bug and stays a plain `Error` for Sentry. A new transport (IPC channel, WS bridge message, CLI verb under `maestro-cli models`) calls `service.ts`; it never re-implements a download or an install. Architecture: [docs/architecture/local-models.md](../architecture/local-models.md).
- **Making a local model usable, and which tasks may offer it:** `installLocalModel(id)`, `ensureLocalRuntimeForModel(id)` in `src/main/local-models/service.ts`; `isLocalModelOfferedForTask(taskId, modelId)`, `localModelAssignmentCaution(taskId, assignment)` over `LocalModelTaskDefinition.unofferedLocalModels` in `src/shared/local-models/tasks.ts`; the Engines card `<LocalModelsEnginesCard>` in `src/renderer/components/Settings/tabs/LocalModelsTab/LocalModelsEnginesCard.tsx`. Two rules, each from a shipped gap. **A download is the model AND its engine.** Runtimes are downloaded, not bundled, and the only Install button for one used to live in Voice Setup, so a text model fetched from the Local Models tab verified clean and then reported `runtime-unavailable` with nothing in the tab or the CLI that could fix it. `installLocalModel()` runs the download and `ensureLocalRuntimeForModel()` side by side and reports both (`LocalModelInstallResult.runtime`: `ready`, `installed`, `unavailable`, `failed`); it is what the core `localModels:download` channel and `maestro-cli models install` call, and the button that calls it states both sizes. `ensureLocalRuntimeForModel()` never throws: weights that arrived are a real result even when the engine did not, and the caller has to be able to say both. Voice's own `models:download` channel still calls `downloadLocalModel()`, because Voice Setup lists and installs its runtime as a row of its own. **A model that is the right KIND for a task is not thereby good at it.** `pending` means "nothing in this build can run this model" and hides it from every task; it was the wrong tool for Qwen3 1.7B, which runs fine and measured well on tab naming, the Cue gate, pull request drafts and digests, and badly on voice routing (never reaches a named agent) and shell commands (turned "find the process" into "kill the process"). So the failed check is written on the TASK (`unofferedLocalModels`, model id to reason). A picker asks `isLocalModelOfferedForTask()` before listing a model; an assignment made anyway is HONORED, shown as `(not recommended)`, and answered by `localModelAssignmentCaution()` on every surface that saves one (`maestro-cli models assign` prints it). Add an entry only with a measurement behind it (`llama-housekeeping-e2e.test.ts` is where the two existing ones came from), and never remove a model from the catalog to keep it out of one task.
- **Loading a native runtime for a local model:** `loadLocalRuntime<T>(runtimeId, consumerId)` in `src/main/local-models/runtime/load-runtime.ts`, with `registerNativeRuntimeConsumer()` / `runtimeSetupLocation()` in `src/main/local-models/runtime/runtime-consumers.ts` and the descriptors in `src/shared/local-models/native-runtimes.ts` (`NATIVE_RUNTIMES`, `NativeRuntimeId`: `llama`, `onnx`). The native runtimes are DOWNLOADED, not bundled, so a bare `require('onnxruntime-node')` or `import('node-llama-cpp')` resolves in a dev checkout and throws `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` in an install. The loader resolves the installed payload and throws a `LocalModelError` (`runtime-unavailable`) whose recovery names where THIS consumer's user downloads the runtime and runs its self-test, which is why a consumer registers itself: without a registration the refusal can only point at another feature's settings page. A question about a runtime that must not load it (a readiness check, a settings row) asks `knownNativeRuntimeUnavailability()` in `src/main/local-models/runtime/native-loader.ts`, never the loader: loading maps a native library into the main process, which is too much to pay for a yes or no. The loader reserves its cache slot synchronously, so concurrent callers share ONE dlopen, and it drops a failed load so the attempt after the user installs the missing piece is a real retry. Do NOT call `getLlama()` outside `src/main/local-models/text-gen.ts` and the self-test, and pass `LLAMA_GET_OPTIONS` (`runtime/llama-options.ts`) when you do, or node-llama-cpp tries to compile llama.cpp from source on a machine with no toolchain.
- **Calling a user-run model server (Ollama, LM Studio, OpenAI-compatible):** the pure record rules in `src/shared/local-models/endpoints.ts` (`LocalModelEndpoint`, `validateLocalModelEndpoint()`, `parseLocalModelEndpoints()`, `normalizeEndpointBaseUrl()`, `resolveEndpointApiKey()`, `findLocalModelEndpoint()`, `isSameEndpointServer()`, `addLocalModelEndpoint()` / `updateLocalModelEndpoint()` / `removeLocalModelEndpoint()`), the transport in `src/main/local-models/endpoint-client.ts` (`listEndpointModels()`, `endpointChat()`, `endpointEmbed()`, `endpointTranscribe()`, `detectLocalEndpoints()`, `testEndpoint()`), and the layer that brings in persisted state, `src/main/local-models/endpoint-service.ts` (`getConfiguredLocalModelEndpoints()`, `findUsableLocalModelEndpoint()`, `chatWithLocalModelEndpoint()`, `embedWithLocalModelEndpoint()`, `transcribeWithLocalModelEndpoint()`, `testLocalModelEndpoint()`, `detectNewLocalModelEndpoints()`). A feature does not call any of these to do its job: it names a TASK and calls the task router, which reaches an endpoint only when the user assigned one. They are listed so nobody writes a fourth `fetch('http://localhost:11434/...')`. Three rules. **An API key is never stored on the endpoint record:** the record holds the NAME of a variable (`apiKeyEnvVar`) and `resolveEndpointApiKey()` reads the value from Settings -> Environment at call time, so the saved list, the debug package and the CLI's `models endpoints list` carry no credential. **Every request goes through `fetchWithTimeout`** with a budget per call kind (`ENDPOINT_LIST_TIMEOUT_MS`, `ENDPOINT_CHAT_TIMEOUT_MS`, ...): a model server that accepts the socket and never answers otherwise hangs the IPC handler for the life of the window. **A call names a SAVED, ENABLED endpoint by its exact id** (`findUsableLocalModelEndpoint()`), stricter than Test, which accepts an unsaved record: an assignment whose endpoint was deleted or disabled is `unavailable` by name, never retried on a different server. A failure is a `LocalModelEndpointError` with a `kind` (`missing-key`, `auth`, `not-found`, `timeout`, `network`, `malformed`, ...), which the router maps to an `unavailable` verdict. The Settings section, `useLocalModelEndpoints()` (`src/renderer/hooks/localModels/useLocalModelEndpoints.ts`) and `maestro-cli models endpoints {list,add,remove,test,detect}` all end in the service functions.
- **Whether a Local Models task would run right now:** `resolveTaskReadiness(taskId)`, `resolveAllTaskReadiness()`, `resolveTaskReadinessRequest(value)` in `src/main/local-models/task-readiness.ts`, over the shapes in `src/shared/local-models/task-readiness.ts` (`LocalModelTaskReadiness`, `LOCAL_MODEL_TASK_UNREADY_REASONS`, `isTaskRunnable()`); renderer `useLocalModelTaskReadiness()` in `src/renderer/hooks/localModels/useLocalModelTaskReadiness.ts`; CLI `maestro-cli models status [task-id]`. The router says `unavailable` only AFTER a call failed; this answers BEFORE, which is what a button needs to decide between hidden (`off`), enabled, and disabled with the reason. The verdict is about the source that was ASSIGNED and never another, and every unready verdict carries a closed `reason`, a `detail` sentence and a `suggestedAction`, so a surface never writes its own "model missing" copy. Local checks never re-hash a model and never dlopen a runtime; only an endpoint assignment reaches the network, with one model listing shared by every task on that endpoint when all tasks are resolved together. Do NOT derive readiness in a component from `useLocalModels()` plus the assignment: that copy forgets the runtime check, which comes FIRST because a runtime that will not load is not fixed by a download. Distinct from A Cappella's capability gate (`src/main/acappella/models/capability-gate.ts`), which answers for voice SLOTS and stays voice-specific.
- **Generating text on a local model or an endpoint:** `generateText()`, `getLocalTextEngine()` in `src/main/local-models/text-gen.ts`. One call, two sources (`{ type: 'local', modelId }` or `{ type: 'endpoint', endpointId, model }`), one result shape. Do NOT call `getLlama()` or open a GGUF anywhere else: a second llama instance is a second gigabyte of RAM, and the voice Brain and housekeeping tasks share ONE resident model through the engine, which also serializes turns (the context has one sequence; a parallel turn fails with `No sequences left`) and frees the model after an idle window. Asking for a different model unloads the resident one first. The engine also owns two things a hand-rolled `LlamaChatSession` gets wrong, both found on real hardware with Qwen3.5: it disposes the sequence itself and WAITS (a session's `autoDisposeSequence` only starts the dispose, so the next turn fails with `No sequences left`), and it resolves the chat wrapper with `LLAMA_CHAT_WRAPPER_OPTIONS` (`runtime/llama-options.ts`) so a Qwen reply does not open inside a thought and lose the first token of a grammar reply. Do NOT call `endpointChat()` directly for real work either: `chatWithLocalModelEndpoint()` in `endpoint-service.ts` resolves the endpoint by EXACT id, refuses a disabled one, and supplies the key from `shellEnvVars`. A source that cannot run throws a classified error naming it; nothing here falls back to another model or a cloud agent.
- **Which source a Local Models task runs on:** `LOCAL_MODEL_TASKS`, `resolveLocalModelAssignment()`, `parseLocalModelAssignments()`, `parseUtilitySource()`, `resolveUtilityProvider()`, `localModelAssignmentProblem()`, `withLocalModelAssignment()` in `src/shared/local-models/tasks.ts`. Every consumer of a local model names a TASK (`tab-naming`, `git-drafts`, ...) and reads that task's assignment; do NOT add a per-feature "which model" setting. The `localModelAssignments` setting is SPARSE: a task with no entry uses its registry default, so read it through `resolveLocalModelAssignment()`, never `assignments[taskId]` directly, and write it through `withLocalModelAssignment()`, which drops an entry equal to the default. `utility-agent` inherits `utilitySource`, parsed by `parseUtilitySource()` with the provider path as the legacy default. `readLocalModelAssignments()` resolves a local or endpoint source for both execution and readiness. For a provider source the router returns control to the caller; `resolveUtilityProvider()` applies `utilityAgentId` / `utilityModelId` only while that path is selected. Only tasks with an existing provider path accept inheritance. The reader keeps a model id the catalog no longer knows rather than reverting the task to its default, so the router can name the missing model; the stricter catalog and kind check is `localModelAssignmentProblem()`, for save time. A choice travels as ONE string, `formatLocalModelAssignmentSpec()` / `parseLocalModelAssignmentSpec()` (`off`, `utility-agent`, `local:<model-id>`, `endpoint:<endpoint-id>:<model>`, everything after the second colon being the model so an Ollama tag survives): the Settings -> Local Models picker uses it as its option value and the CLI takes it as an argument, so do NOT invent a second spelling. The shared Utility source controls live on System Two, beside task overrides. Tool-based grooming and transfer stay on the session provider while the shared source is local or an endpoint.
- **Naming a Local Models source for a person:** `localModelAssignmentSourceLabel(assignment, endpoints)` in `src/shared/local-models/tasks.ts` (catalog display name, or `<model> on <endpoint name>`; `null` for `off` and `utility-agent`; a task router `source` has the same shape and is labeled through it too)
- **The Maestro token proxy (address, routing, lifecycle):** `TOKEN_PROXY_ROUTES`, `resolveTokenProxyTarget(method, url, upstreams?)`, `classifyTokenProxyPath()`, `tokenProxyGatewayUrl(port, route)`, `gatewayPointsAtTokenProxy(gatewayUrl, port)`, `tokenProxyErrorBody()` in `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts`; the server is `createTokenProxyServer()` / `forwardableHeaders()` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/server.ts`; the lifecycle is `configureTokenProxy()`, `ensureTokenProxyPort()`, `syncTokenProxy()`, `ensureTokenProxyForGateway(url)`, `getTokenProxyStatus()`, `stopTokenProxy()` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`. Maestro's own gateway target: an HTTP server on `127.0.0.1` whose first path segment names the upstream (`/anthropic`, `/openai/v1`, `/chatgpt`), so one port serves every provider. Today it is an EXACT PASSTHROUGH, and four things keep it one. **It is built on Node `http`, not `fetch` or `fetchWithTimeout`:** `fetch` decodes a compressed response and re-frames the body, and a total timeout kills a model stream that runs for minutes. **Headers go out from `rawHeaders`** (casing, order, duplicates), minus hop-by-hop headers and with `Host` rewritten; credentials pass through and the proxy adds none (the one exception is a request routed to an agent's background target, see "Routing a token proxy request to a background target"). **Bodies are never decoded:** a `content-encoding: zstd` request is forwarded as bytes, and the path and query are carried as text. **An upstream error is a response** and is forwarded whole; only the proxy's own failures (`unknown-route`, `upstream-unreachable`) are answered by the proxy, in a body that names it, and a stream the upstream drops is cut, never ended cleanly. The lifecycle has three rules. **The port is chosen once and persisted** (`userData/token-proxy.json`) because stored gateway URLs name it; a taken port is a start FAILURE, never a move to another port. **It listens only while a local agent's gateway points at it:** `syncTokenProxy()` runs at startup and from the persistence handlers' `onSessionsPersisted` hook, because the sessions store raises no change event (do NOT wire `sessionsStore.onDidChange('sessions')`, which never fires under `deferStoreWrites`). **A launch never trusts that it is up:** every spawn path in the main process that applies a gateway calls `ensureTokenProxyForGateway(plan.gateway.url)` after the launch plan and refuses the launch on `{ ok: false }`; without it a port held by another program receives the agent's request, credentials included. Do NOT add a main-process spawn site that applies a gateway and skips that call. `ensureTokenProxyPort()` is the only function that creates the state file; do not call it at startup. **It carries HTTP only and says so**: a request with an `Upgrade` header (Codex opens a WebSocket to `/responses` first) is answered `426 Upgrade Required` by the proxy and never forwarded. Forwarded, the handshake loses its hop-by-hop `Upgrade` header, comes back 405, and Codex retries it nine times (9.3 of a 12.3 second turn) before it uses HTTP; do NOT tunnel the socket instead, since the proxy could then neither count nor shorten what crosses it. **The terminal event is read from an untyped stream too**: the ChatGPT backend answers a Codex turn with an event stream and no `Content-Type`, so a message request answered with no type is scanned by `streamAnnouncedItsEnd()` like a typed one. Real-traffic check for all of it, modes included: `token-proxy-real-e2e.test.ts` (`MAESTRO_TOKEN_PROXY_E2E=1`, spends provider tokens).
- **Compressing tool results in a model request (token proxy):** `compressRequestBody(kind, body, options)` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/compress.ts`, with `findToolResultSpans()`, `summarizeToolResult()`, `formatCompressionMarker()` / `parseCompressionMarker()` beside it; the heuristics are `compressWithHeuristics()`, `compressJson()`, `compressLogs()`, `truncateLongLines()` in `proxy/compressors.ts`; the store is `createCompressionCache()` / `getCompressionCache()`, `contentHash()`, `isContentHash()` in `proxy/compression-cache.ts`. It shortens the large tool results of one request (Anthropic `tool_result`, a Chat Completions `tool` message, a Responses `function_call_output`) and changes nothing else. Five rules, each a cost or a corruption if broken. **The body is TEXT, never a parsed value:** only the string literal of a compressed block is rewritten (`json-spans.ts`), and an unchanged request returns the same string with `changed: false`, so the caller forwards the bytes it received. **The first verdict for a content hash is the only one:** `compressed` AND `kept` are stored under `userData/proxy-cache/`, because the model tier is not deterministic (it can be unassigned, time out, or word a summary differently) and a block whose bytes change between turns breaks the provider's cached prefix, which costs MORE than no proxy. Do NOT add a path that re-decides a block that has a verdict, and do NOT prune the directory. **A verdict is stored before it is used:** a cache that cannot be written leaves the block as sent (`stats.storeFailures`), since an unrecorded compression cannot be reproduced or audited. **Protected regions are decided in ONE place** (`findToolResultSpans`): never the system prompt, tool definitions, the most recent user message, a tool result the model has not answered yet, or a block marked with `cache_control`. **A compressor is a pure function that names what it left out** in a `[maestro: ...]` note; JSON is compressed as JSON (numbers and strings copied as text) and is never handed to the line compressors whole, because minified JSON is one line. The model tier goes through `runLocalTask('token-proxy')` like every housekeeping task and never falls back to another source. Token counts are `estimateTokenCount()` everywhere, the marker included. Known cost, not measured yet: a block is sent in full once and compressed after, so its bytes change once (see [local-models.md](../architecture/local-models.md), "Known cost").
- **An agent's Maestro token proxy mode (`off | passthrough | compress | compress-early`):** `resolveTokenProxyMode({ gatewayUrl, storedMode, port })`, `asTokenProxyMode()`, `asStoredTokenProxyMode()`, `tokenProxyRouteForAgent(toolType, env)`, `tokenProxyAgentGatewayUrl(url, port, agentId)`, `TOKEN_PROXY_MODES`, `TOKEN_PROXY_MODE_LABELS` in `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts`; written by `setAgentTokenProxyMode(sessionId, mode)` in `src/renderer/services/agentGateway.ts`; `tokenProxyLaunchGatewayUrl(agentId, gatewayUrl)` and `modeFor` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`. The mode is THREE states over TWO stored fields, and the split is the thing a copy gets wrong: `off` is not a stored value, it is `gatewayUrl` not reaching the proxy's port, so a surface that reads `session.tokenProxyMode` alone reports `passthrough` for an agent whose traffic never touches the proxy. Always go through `resolveTokenProxyMode()`. **Choosing a mode is choosing the gateway**: `passthrough` and `compress` point the agent's gateway at the proxy (on the route `tokenProxyRouteForAgent` picks from the agent's effective environment: Codex with an API key and Codex with a ChatGPT login talk to different hosts), `off` clears it, and both fields are written and flushed in one `setAgentTokenProxyMode()` call. Do NOT write `tokenProxyMode` from a new call site: the surfaces that ride the service are `<TokenProxyModeField>` with `useTokenProxyModeChoice()` (`src/renderer/components/shared/TokenProxyModeField.tsx`, `src/renderer/hooks/localModels/useTokenProxyModeChoice.ts`), drawn in the Gateway table and on Edit Agent's Models tab, and `maestro-cli update-agent --proxy-mode` through the `update_session_config` bridge patch. A mode stored without its gateway is a setting that silently does nothing. A form that saves on Save (Edit Agent) still applies the mode at once and then sets its own gateway draft to the result, so its Save writes the same URL back and keeps the mode. **A mode belongs to the gateway it was chosen for**, so every path that changes `gatewayUrl` (`setAgentGatewayUrl`, the Edit Agent save, a bridge patch, a provider switch) drops the stored mode: compression must not be carried onto a URL typed afterwards. **The server asks per request.** `modeFor(agentId)` reads the main-process sessions store each time, which is why the write must flush and why a change applies mid-turn with no restart; a request with no agent id, an agent that is gone and any stored value but `compress` are passthrough. **The agent id rides the LAUNCH URL, never the stored one.** The stored gateway is `http://127.0.0.1:PORT/anthropic`; desktop `process:spawn` and Cue's `buildSpawnSpec` hand the process `.../agent/<id>/anthropic` through `tokenProxyLaunchGatewayUrl()`, so a worktree agent that inherited its parent's gateway is still counted as itself.
