Only a gateway on the proxy's own PORT is rewritten: LiteLLM serves `/anthropic` on its own port, so do not recognize the proxy by path shape. `maestro-cli send` launches with the stored URL, so its requests are passthrough and unattributed. **`compress` and `compress-early` are two modes, not one mode and an option**, because they differ in what the MODEL reads. `compress` holds a tool result back until the model has answered it, so the model sees everything once and the provider caches the result twice (2% to 8% more than passthrough over 4 to 5 requests, measured). `compress-early` shortens it before the first read: `compressRequestBody(kind, body, { early: true })` lifts the two rules that cause the double write (results not answered yet, and the block carrying the `cache_control` breakpoint) and nothing else. Ask `isCompressingTokenProxyMode(mode)` whether a mode reads bodies; a `=== 'compress'` test silently sends an early agent's requests unread. `proxy_requests.mode` stores both as `compress` (the column says whether the body was shortened, and its CHECK constraint predates the second mode); the agent's own mode is read from its record. Every surface that offers the choice shows `TOKEN_PROXY_MODE_DESCRIPTIONS[mode]` beside it, since a one-word label cannot carry the trade.
- **What the token proxy carried, saved and shortened:** `recordTokenProxyRequest(entry)`, `proxyRequestFromLog(entry)`, `readTokenProxyUsage()` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/request-stats.ts`; `getTokenProxyReport()`, `readTokenProxyBlock(hash)` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`; the `proxy_requests` table (stats migration v16) through `recordProxyRequest()`, `getProxyRequestUsage()`, `getRecentProxyCompressions()` in `src/main/stats/proxy-requests.ts`; renderer `useTokenProxyReport()` and `<LocalModelsProxyUsage>`; CLI `maestro-cli models proxy status` and `models proxy block <hash>`. One report (`TokenProxyReport`) feeds Settings -> Local Models -> Gateway and the CLI, so they cannot disagree about what was saved. Four rules the mapping holds so no caller re-derives them: only requests that hold a message list are recorded (token counting and model lists would dilute the averages); tokens and added latency are stored ONLY where the proxy read the body (`compress` mode), because bytes / 4 for passthrough and characters / 4 for compress are two different units and summing them makes "tokens saved" meaningless, so a passthrough row counts as a request and renders a dash, never a zero; tokens saved is the whole body before and after, cache hits included; and a block is listed ONCE, in `new_blocks` of the request that first shortened it (`cached: false`), because every later request resends the conversation and would repeat it. Recording honors `statsCollectionEnabled` and swallows a failed write: a request that was forwarded correctly must never be failed by its own bookkeeping. `readTokenProxyBlock` is the only read of the content-addressed cache a surface may use, and it runs `isContentHash()` before the hash becomes a path; do NOT read `userData/proxy-cache/` from a renderer or a CLI command. A saving is printed through `describeTokensSaved(totals)` and the hold through `describeAddedLatency(totals)` in `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts` (`10,000 (25%)`, `14 ms`, and a dash when the proxy read no request body), by Settings and by the Usage Dashboard's Models tab alike, always beside `TOKEN_PROXY_ESTIMATE_NOTE`.
- **Routing a token proxy request to a background target:** `planBackgroundRoute(body, toolType)` and `withRequestModel()` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/background-route.ts`; `modelIdInTierFamily(harness, tier, modelId)` in `src/shared/modelTiers.ts`; `endpointServesAnthropicMessages()` in `src/shared/local-models/endpoints.ts`; `resolveSourceCallTarget()` in `src/main/local-models/run-on.ts`; the server asks `backgroundRouteFor(agentId)` once per Anthropic Messages request (`src/main/local-models/proxy/server.ts`), answered in `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`. A provider CLI sends its own turns and, beside them, self-contained side requests (a session title, the summary of a fetched page) on the provider's SMALL model. An agent with a `backgroundRequests` target has the second kind sent to a model on a Local Models source. **A request is routed only when it names the small model AND shows no sign of a turn,** and the decision errs one way, because routing a turn changes the model in the middle of a session. The skip reasons are `unreadable`, `not-small-model`, `probe`, `thinking` (a `thinking` or `redacted_thinking` block), `cache-control` (a breakpoint anywhere but the system prompt, a tool definition and the request-level marker included), `tool-result` (also `*_tool_result`) and `tools` (any declared tool). The `tools` rule is what keeps the FIRST request of a loop on the provider when `DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING` leaves it with no marker, tool result or thinking block. The `probe` rule (`max_tokens` of 1 or less) keeps Claude Code's quota probe on the provider: it exists to read the ACCOUNT's state from the provider's answer, which the provider answered 429 and a local model answered 200, reading as no limit. It reads `max_tokens`, not the word `quota`. **The small model is matched by NAME only** (the built-in `low` id, or the family as one whole word of the id): do NOT read `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL` or `ANTHROPIC_SMALL_FAST_MODEL` as aliases, because a value equal to the agent's main model would make every turn look like a background request. **The body is never parsed to a value and printed again:** `withRequestModel()` writes one string literal over each top-level `model` span (`json-spans.ts`), and a request kept on the provider is sent byte for byte. **A failure is the answer, never a retry on the provider.** A target that answers with an error is forwarded whole; a target that cannot be used at all (stored value malformed, source gone or disabled, a source not known to serve the route, the model server unable to serve the model, an address that is not a URL, a key no header can hold) is answered by the proxy with 502 `background-target-unavailable` naming it, and the request is never sent to both places. The target is looked up and the model server started only for a request already decided to be a background request, so a turn starts nothing. **The proxy adds one credential of its own here:** the client's `Authorization`, `x-api-key` and `cookie` are removed and the target's key goes as a bearer token (none when the target has none), so the "adds no credential" rule of the exact passthrough has this one exception. **A source is "known to serve the Anthropic route" by its `kind`** (the Maestro model server, Ollama, LM Studio); an `openai-compatible` source is refused by name, and a new field on the endpoint record needs a writer in the Sources form and in `models endpoints`. Compress mode is unchanged: a compressing agent's turn is still compressed, and a background request is not. It is logged as `background { routedTo, skipped, error, addedLatencyMs }` and recorded in `proxy_requests.routed_to` (stats migration v17); count routed requests with `routed_to IS NOT NULL` AND read `outcome`, since a title the TUI left before it was answered is aborted. Real-traffic check: the background case in `token-proxy-real-e2e.test.ts` (`MAESTRO_TOKEN_PROXY_E2E_BACKGROUND_MODEL`), whose results are in [local-models.md](../architecture/local-models.md), "Real run: 2026-10-12, background requests". Known and unfixed: the request states no thinking setting, so a model whose template thinks takes a minute to summarize a page (Qwen3.5 4B 49 to 84 s; Qwen3 Coder 30B 0.56 s).
- **Setting an agent's background requests target:** `parseBackgroundRequests()`, `parseBackgroundRequestsEdit()`, `backgroundRequestsSupport()`, `parseBackgroundRequestsSpec()` / `formatBackgroundRequestsSpec()`, `buildBackgroundRequestsOptionGroups()`, `BACKGROUND_REQUESTS_DESCRIPTION` and `BACKGROUND_REQUESTS_NEEDS_PROXY` in `src/shared/local-models/background-requests.ts`; `setAgentBackgroundRequests()` and `OFF_TOKEN_PROXY_FIELDS` in `src/renderer/services/agentGateway.ts`; `<BackgroundRequestsField>` in `src/renderer/components/shared/BackgroundRequestsField.tsx`, drawn by `AgentTokenProxyModeControl`. `Session.backgroundRequests` is `{ target }`, a model on a Local Models source; absent means every request goes to the provider, and `provider` and a blank value read as absent. `parseBackgroundRequests()` is the one reading, shared by the proxy and every writer, so no writer stores a value the proxy would refuse as malformed. Every writer (Edit Agent's picker, `update-agent --background-requests`, the `update_session_config` bridge) checks with `parseBackgroundRequestsEdit()`: Claude Code only (the proxy routes the Anthropic Messages route alone), a source id that names something and serves that route, and an agent that is on the proxy (`BACKGROUND_REQUESTS_NEEDS_PROXY`; turning it off is always allowed). **The target leaves with the proxy.** The picker is hidden while the proxy is off, and the proxy can be switched on again from the Gateway table or the CLI, where the picker is not on screen, so a stored target would come back into force with nobody seeing it. Every writer that takes an agent off the proxy (mode off, another gateway URL, a Save that changes the gateway, a provider switch) spreads `OFF_TOKEN_PROXY_FIELDS`; do NOT add one that does not, and do NOT write `backgroundRequests` from a new call site. **It applies at once, like the mode,** through `setAgentBackgroundRequests()` (write and flush, because the proxy reads the stored agent per request) and not with the dialog's Save. One CLI call can turn the proxy on and name the target (`--proxy-mode passthrough --background-requests <id:model>`: the bridge applies the mode first); a target beside `--proxy-mode off` or `--gateway-url` is refused before either is acted on. `contextTokens` is not stored: a request states its own limits. The picker's sentence is the single `BACKGROUND_REQUESTS_DESCRIPTION`; it names the two requests a real session routed and says to pick a model that answers without thinking, so change it when what is routed changes.
- **A unified diff of two texts already in memory:** `<TextDiff theme before after name? emptyMessage?>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/TextDiff.tsx`. It builds a synthetic git-style patch with `buildSyntheticGitDiff()` (`src/renderer/utils/gitDiffParser.ts`) and draws it with the same `react-diff-view` renderer and `generateDiffViewStyles()` theme (colorblind mode included) the Git Diff viewer uses, so a comparison of two strings reads like every other diff in the app. The token proxy's "Recent compressions" diff rides it. `MaestroPromptsTab` still inlines the same three steps for its bundled-default comparison and can move onto this; do NOT write a third copy.
- **Rewriting part of a JSON text without re-printing the rest:** `parseJsonSpans(text)`, `memberOf(span, key)`, `stringValue(text, span)`, `isNullSpan()`, `spliceSpans(text, edits)` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/json-spans.ts`. `JSON.parse` followed by `JSON.stringify` is not the identity on bytes: it drops whitespace, re-spells escapes, moves integer-like keys to the front of an object, and rounds an integer past 2^53. When a document must keep every byte you did not mean to change (a request body in transit, a number that is an id), locate the values, decode only the strings you read, and splice new text over exact spans. The scanner checks structure and finds where each string ends; it does NOT validate the inside of a string (`stringValue` does, on decode), so call `JSON.parse` first when you must know the whole text is valid. It refuses nesting past `MAX_JSON_SPAN_DEPTH` rather than overflowing. Proxy-local today; move it to `src/shared/` when a second consumer appears.
- **The agent a spawned process belongs to:** `agentIdFromProcessId(processId)` in `src/main/utils/process-ids.ts`. A process is keyed by its agent's id plus a suffix that says what it is for: `{agentId}-ai-{tabId}` (a turn in a tab), `{agentId}-batch-{ts}` (an Auto Run task), `{agentId}-synopsis-{ts}` (the History synopsis), `{agentId}-cue-...` (a Cue run). Anything that looks an agent's STORED RECORD up from a process id has to strip all of them. `REGEX_AI_SUFFIX` strips one, and a lookup built on it silently finds nothing for the other three: that is how an agent's Local Models gateway applied to its typed turns and not to its Auto Run turns. The branch-switch guard already had the right regex as a private function; it lives here now so the next lookup does not write a third. An id with no known suffix comes back unchanged.
- **An agent's Local Models gateway (per-agent proxy URL):** `agentGatewaySupport(toolType)`, `parseGatewayUrl(raw)`, `isLoopbackGatewayUrl(url)`, `resolveAgentGateway(input)`, `gatewayEnvVarsFor(toolType, url)`, `parseGatewayUrlEdit(toolType, raw)` in `src/shared/local-models/gateway.ts`; applied by `buildAgentLaunchPlan({ gateway })` through the `gatewayEnvVars` layer of `resolveAgentEnvVars()`; written from the renderer by `setAgentGatewayUrl()` / `flushAgentToStore()` in `src/renderer/services/agentGateway.ts`; drawn by `<GatewayUrlField>` (`src/renderer/components/shared/GatewayUrlField.tsx`), which the Edit Agent dialog and `<LocalModelsGatewaySection>` share. `Session.gatewayUrl` points one agent at an HTTP proxy (Headroom, LiteLLM, later Maestro's own) by setting the provider's base-URL variable at launch. Four rules, each a bug if broken. **An explicit user variable wins:** when the agent's effective custom vars (its own, or the provider-level set when it has none) set the variable to a non-blank value, the gateway is not applied; it does beat provider defaults, Settings -> Environment and a shell-exported value. **Only verified variables are mapped** (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` for Claude Code, `OPENAI_BASE_URL` for Codex): the table is keyed by `AgentId` so a new provider needs a variable or a reason, and a guessed name fails SILENTLY - the CLI ignores it and the agent runs direct while the UI says it is proxied. Do NOT add a variable you have not confirmed the provider reads. **Never silent:** a loopback URL for an SSH-remote agent, a stored URL on a provider that cannot use one, or a value that is not a URL fails the LAUNCH with a message naming the gateway (`{ ok: false, reason: 'gateway-...' }` from the plan); nothing drops the gateway to let the turn run somewhere else. **The gateway is never written into `customEnvVars`:** it is injected at launch, so `classifyCredentialKind()` and `resolveAgentBillingCredential()` still see the agent's login - a proxy that passes auth headers through does not change whose plan a turn bills, and folding it into the user vars would report every proxied subscription agent as a gateway credential that no login can repair. The trap on the write side: desktop `process:spawn` reads the gateway from the MAIN-process sessions store (by agent id, beside `isPianola`), not from the renderer's spawn request, because about twenty renderer call sites build that request and one that forgot the field would bypass the proxy with nothing to show for it. The renderer's persistence is debounced by two seconds, so a write that only touches the renderer store is not in force for a message sent straight after it. Every write path flushes (`setAgentGatewayUrl`, the Edit Agent save, the `update_session_config` handler's `setMany`); do NOT add one that does not. Scope is the launch plan's callers (chat, Auto Run, Cue, `maestro-cli send` and playbooks); housekeeping spawns that bypass the plan run direct. Provenance is the `gateway` `EnvVarSource` ("Local Models gateway") in `resolveAgentEnvironment()`. **Two things the launch has to get right beyond the variable, each a shipped failure.** A mapping can carry `configArgs`: Codex CLI 0.162.1 on a ChatGPT login ignores `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, so the variable alone left a Codex agent running direct while every surface said it was proxied; its gateway is also `-c openai_base_url="<url>"`, which goes at the ROOT of the command line (`resolveAgentGateway().args`, `plan.gateway.args`, already at the front of `plan.args`). A caller that spawns from its own argument list instead of `plan.args` (desktop's `handle-spawn.ts`) must put them at the front of that list. And the desktop looks the gateway up from the stored agent by `agentIdFromProcessId(config.sessionId)`, not by an id with only `-ai-` stripped: an Auto Run task (`{agentId}-batch-{ts}`) and the History synopsis (`{agentId}-synopsis-{ts}`) matched no agent and ran direct. **A spawn that builds no launch plan calls `applyAgentGatewayToSpawn({ agentId, toolType, args, customEnvVars, remote })`** (`src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`): one call for the three steps a plan-based path takes (the agent's id in a proxy URL, `resolveAgentGateway()`, the proxy up before the turn), so a new site cannot do two of them. `spawnGroupChatAgent()` calls it when given `maestroAgentId`, which covers a Group Chat participant and a cross-agent consult; a refusal throws, the same way an unresolved SSH remote does. Do NOT read `gatewayUrl` off a session object at a spawn site and set the variable by hand: that skips the user-override rule, the loopback-on-remote refusal, and the proxy start. Still direct, because they hold no agent id: tab naming, `groomContext()`, and the Group Chat moderator.
- **An agent's Run on target (the model behind its harness):** `resolveRunOnLaunch(input)`, `agentRunOnSupport(toolType)`, `parseRunOnTarget(value)`, `runOnHarnessMode(target, chosen, measured)` and `runOnOutputTokens(contextTokens)` in `src/shared/local-models/run-on.ts`; applied by `buildAgentLaunchPlan({ runOn })` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/launch/launch-plan.ts`, over `replaceModelArg()` and `placeSubcommandArgs()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/launch/agent-args.ts` and `applyEnvVarMerges()` in `src/shared/agentEnvironment.ts`; looked up and asked by `prepareLaunchRunOn()`, `prepareAgentRunOn()`, `applyAgentRunOnToSpawn()` and `configureAgentRunOn()` in `src/main/local-models/run-on.ts`; the Maestro model server comes from `modelServerForHarness(model)` in `src/main/local-models/server/server-endpoint.ts`. `Session.runOn` keeps an agent's provider CLI (tool loop, permissions, transcripts, parsers) and changes only the model behind it: the provider's own (`{ type: 'provider' }`, or absent), or a model on a Local Models source (`{ type: 'source', endpointId, model, contextTokens? }`, where `maestro-server` is the Maestro model server). Six rules, each a bug if broken. **A target that cannot run refuses the launch by name, and never falls back.** A fallback would spend the user's money and send the conversation where they chose not to send it. The refusal codes are `run-on-unsupported-provider`, `run-on-invalid-target`, `run-on-source-missing`, `run-on-source-disabled`, `run-on-loopback-on-remote` (decided from data in `resolveRunOnLaunch()`), and `run-on-source-unreachable`, `run-on-model-not-served`, `run-on-server-failed` (decided in main, after asking the source or starting the server, and worded by `runOnUnavailable()`). Every refusal that needs no network is decided BEFORE the model server is started or a source is asked, so a launch that will be refused loads nothing. **Only recipes run against the real CLI are mapped.** `RUN_ON_SUPPORT` is keyed by `AgentId`, so a new provider fails to compile until it has a recipe or a reason, and a guessed variable fails SILENTLY: the CLI ignores it and the agent runs on the provider while every surface says it runs elsewhere. Measured recipes: Claude Code (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, the three tier model variables, `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`), Codex (a `maestro` provider through `-c`, the key in `MAESTRO_RUN_ON_KEY`, `-c model_context_window`), OpenCode 2 (a provider merged into `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` and `--standalone`) and Copilot CLI (`COPILOT_PROVIDER_*`). Do NOT add a provider you have not launched against a server that logs what it is sent, and do NOT read a documented flag as proof. **The model replaces the agent's model; it is not added beside it.** `replaceModelArg()` rewrites the value of every model flag already in the arguments (a tier model and the synopsis's cheap model included) or appends the flag, inside the plan and inside `applyAgentRunOnToSpawn()`, so no surface can forget it. `inject` carries `model` (the source's name, which the pill shows) and `harnessModel` (what the harness is launched with: OpenCode wants `maestro/<model>`). Codex reads `-m` after `exec`, so the model never rides in the root `args`. A recipe can also return `subcommandArgs` (OpenCode's `--standalone` is rejected ahead of `run`) and `envVarMerges` (a variable that holds a whole document, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, is ADDED TO, over the read-only override and the user's own value, because replacing it drops the permission block and yielding to the user's value drops the provider). **The provider's own credential never goes to the source.** Claude Code sends `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as `x-api-key` to its base URL beside the source's token (measured with 2.1.296), so the recipe sets it blank, whoever set it; Copilot CLI's bearer token, key command and headers for another provider are blanked likewise. Over SSH a blank variable is not exported, so a key the remote host's profile exports is outside what a launch controls. The user's own variable wins one variable at a time (`userOverrides`), with one guard: when the agent sets the base URL itself, the source's key is withheld and its source is not asked. **Run on beats the gateway.** The token proxy routes only to cloud providers, so a target other than Provider skips the gateway and records `skippedGatewayUrl`; both gateway controls are disabled with `RUN_ON_GATEWAY_SKIPPED_REASON`. **An agent on a source keeps to the mode its recipe was measured in** (`runOnHarnessMode()`): `claude --print`, never the maestro-p TUI, and `codex exec`, never the app-server. Every spawn surface goes through the same two functions. A surface that builds a launch plan (desktop `handle-spawn.ts`, Cue `cue-spawn-builder.ts`, CLI `agent-spawner.ts`) calls `prepareLaunchRunOn()` and hands the result to `buildAgentLaunchPlan({ runOn })`; a spawn that builds none calls `applyAgentRunOnToSpawn({ agentId, toolType, args, customEnvVars, remote })`, which does the lookup, the network step and the application in one call, beside `applyAgentGatewayToSpawn()`. `spawnGroupChatAgent()` calls it when given `maestroAgentId`, which drives Run on first and the gateway only when the agent has no target. The desktop looks the target up from the stored agent by `agentIdFromProcessId(config.sessionId)`, so Auto Run, the History synopsis (which resumes the same session) and Cue runs follow it. Do NOT read `runOn` off a session object at a spawn site and set the variables by hand: that skips the refusals, the key rule and the model replacement. Two processes run this code, so what it reaches is injected: `configureAgentRunOn()` gives the app its own stores and server, and `maestro-cli` reads saved sources from the settings file and asks the RUNNING app for the model server over the bridge message `local_models_server_ensure` (12 minute wait). An unconfigured process refuses a source target and never guesses. A saved source is asked for its model list on every launch (`/v1/models` or `/api/tags`, 5 second budget, no cache). **Still direct, because they hold no agent id:** tab naming, `groomContext()`, the voice Conductor and the Group Chat moderator. For an agent on a source they send conversation text to the provider's cloud model; the docs say so and name the System Two assignment that keeps it local. Traps measured by the end-to-end run (`src/__tests__/main/local-models/run-on-e2e.test.ts`, opt-in with `MAESTRO_LOCAL_MODEL_E2E=1`): Copilot CLI picks its tools from the model's name, a name it takes for a GPT model gets a freeform `apply_patch` tool and `llama-server` answers 500, so the recipe sets `COPILOT_PROVIDER_MODEL_ID` to a name no family matches; OpenCode 2 works in the directory `PWD` names, not the one it is started in, so a Maestro process that has `PWD` starts an OpenCode agent in the caller's directory (a launch fix that belongs on `rc`, not here). A context above what the server runs a model at is not checked.
- **Setting and testing an agent's Run on target:** `parseRunOnEdit(toolType, value, endpoints?)`, `runOnContextProblem(contextTokens)`, `runOnDefaultContextTokens(target)`, `runOnGatewayEditProblem(runOn, edit)`, `formatRunOnSpec()` / `parseRunOnSpec()` and `runOnPillLabel()` in `src/shared/local-models/run-on.ts`; the picker's options from `buildRunOnOptionGroups()` / `runOnModelFacts()` in `src/shared/local-models/run-on-picker.ts`, drawn by `<RunOnField>` in `src/renderer/components/shared/RunOnField.tsx` over `useRunOnSources()` in `src/renderer/hooks/localModels/useRunOnSources.ts`; the pill's text from `turnRunOnLabel()` in `src/renderer/utils/providerTabSessions.ts`; the CLI flags from `readRunOnFlags()` in `src/cli/services/run-on.ts`; the Test button and `maestro-cli agent test-run-on` from `testAgentRunOn()` / `configureRunOnTest()` in `src/main/local-models/run-on-test.ts`. Every writer of `Session.runOn` (the Edit Agent and New Agent dialogs, `create-agent` / `update-agent --run-on`, and the bridge listener for `create_session` and `update_session_config`) validates through `parseRunOnEdit()` and nothing else, so none can store a target every launch would then refuse. It runs `parseRunOnTarget()`, then the provider's recipe (a source for a provider with none is refused with that provider's own reason), then, when `endpoints` is passed, the source id. A writer that takes the id as TYPED text passes `endpoints`; the dialog does not, because its picker cannot produce an unknown id and an agent whose source was removed must still be able to save its other settings. A disabled source and a model the source may not serve are accepted at the write: the first is refused by name at launch, the second needs the network and is what Test asks. **The context floor lives in `parseRunOnTarget()`** (`runOnContextProblem()`, 40,960 tokens: an agent harness cannot hold its own first prompt in less), so the picker, the CLI and a launch cannot disagree; a target starts at `runOnDefaultContextTokens()` (the catalog context on the Maestro model server, 40,960 elsewhere). The spelling is `provider` or `<source id>:<model>`, split at the FIRST colon because a model name may hold one (`qwen3:latest`) and an endpoint id never does; the context is never part of it. **Flag semantics:** `--run-on provider` clears (`runOn: null` on the wire, no key on the record); `--run-on-context` alone changes the context of the target the agent has and is refused for an agent on its provider; naming the SAME target again keeps its context; a gateway URL or a proxy mode other than `off` is refused while the target is a source (`runOnGatewayEditProblem()`, checked again in the listener BEFORE the token proxy is started), and clearing stays allowed; a provider switch keeps the target when the new harness has a recipe and drops it when it has none. A worktree agent inherits its parent's target. **The write path is the gateway's.** The launch reads MAIN's copy of the agent, and the renderer's persistence is debounced, so every writer flushes (`flushAgentToStore`, the Edit Agent save, the `update_session_config` handler's `setMany`); do NOT add one that does not. `turnModel` is untouched and still what a queued item is spawned with; the label is a separate field (`turnRunOn`), so a queued item never spawns with a label for a model name. **Test is the agent's own launch, read-only.** `testAgentRunOn()` goes through `prepareLaunchRunOn()` and `buildAgentLaunchPlan()` (surface `desktop`, query source `user`), then `runToCompletion()` under a 5 minute budget, with the SSH wrapper for a remote agent. The permission mode is read-only, because the model may be one nobody ran as an agent in the user's directory. It refuses a Provider target (`run-on-test-provider-target`) and throws if a plan comes back without the target applied, so it can never spend a turn on the provider's model. The UI tests the FORM (its unsaved values as `subject`, read as a set); the bridge handler sends the agent's id and nothing else, so a bridge caller cannot hand the test a command or a directory. Do NOT build a second "try this model" path.
- **Running a housekeeping task on a local model or endpoint:** `runLocalTask(taskId, input)` in `src/main/local-models/task-router.ts`, with `runLocalTaskRequest()` as the validated entry for IPC (`localModels:runTask`) and the CLI bridge (`maestro-cli models run`). A consumer calls the ROUTER, never `generateText()` with a source it picked itself: the router is what reads the user's assignment, records the `local_model_runs` stats row, and refuses to substitute. It answers `off`, `use-utility-agent`, `ok`, `unavailable` (with a `reason` naming the model or endpoint and the classified `code`), or `cancelled`. `use-utility-agent` is an instruction to the CALLER to keep its existing spawn path; do NOT add agent spawning to the router. `unavailable` is final: never retry it on another source or a cloud agent, because the user did not choose to spend that. Embedding tasks go through `runEmbeddingTask(taskId, { texts, mode })` beside it, which shares the same assignment, classification, and stats path (in-process only: no transport carries raw vectors). Classifier tasks go through `runClassifierTask(taskId, { text })` the same way, and speech-to-text tasks through `runSpeechToTextTask(taskId, { audio, sampleRate })`; each twin refuses a task whose `needs` is another kind. **This is THE choke point for any feature that wants a local model.** A feature never calls `getLlama()`, opens an ONNX session, or sends a request to an Ollama or LM Studio port for itself: that code runs on a source the user did not assign, records nothing, and has no refusal path. **Adding a task is one registry entry**, not a new call path: add the id to `LOCAL_MODEL_TASK_IDS` and its definition (`label`, `description`, `needs`, `allowsUtilityAgent`, `defaultAssignment`) to `LOCAL_MODEL_TASKS` in `src/shared/local-models/tasks.ts`. The Settings -> Local Models Assignments table, `maestro-cli models assign` / `run` / `status`, task readiness and the skipped-task toast all read that record, so the task is assignable from every surface with no further wiring. A task new with Local Models defaults to `off`; only a task that already ran through an agent may default to `utility-agent`, and `defineTask()` throws at load if the two disagree. The consumer then decides what `off` and `unavailable` mean for its OWN surface (hide the button, skip with a notice, fail open with a warning) and records that row in the Task consumers table of [docs/architecture/local-models.md](../architecture/local-models.md). To ask whether a task WOULD run (a settings row, a consumer deciding whether to show its affordance), call `resolveTaskReadiness(taskId)` in `src/main/local-models/task-readiness.ts`, not a hand-rolled store or endpoint probe: it reports the assigned source only, with a closed reason and a recovery (`localModels:taskReadiness`, `maestro-cli models status`). Every task input extends `LocalTaskAttribution`: a caller that knows which agent the run is for passes `agentId`, which is written to `local_model_runs.agent_id` and never sent to a model. The row's source type comes from `localModelRunSourceType(source, onModelServer)`: a run the Maestro model server answered is `server` whichever way the assignment named it (its own endpoint, or a local model the running server already holds), so its work is never counted as in-process.
- **Telling the user a housekeeping task was skipped (`unavailable`):** `reportLocalTaskUnavailable({ safeSend, taskId, verdict, sessionId })` and `clearLocalTaskNotice(taskId, sessionId)` in `src/main/local-models/task-notices.ts`. An `unavailable` verdict from the router means the operation was SKIPPED (no fallback), and a consumer must say so: silent skipping reads as a broken feature. But tab naming fires on every new tab, so a toast per attempt would bury the user. The helper logs every occurrence and raises ONE yellow `remote:notifyToast` per task and agent (`sessionId` also makes the toast jump to that agent; omit it for an app-wide notice). Call `clearLocalTaskNotice` on an `ok` verdict so a model that recovers and breaks again is announced again. Do NOT raise your own toast for an `unavailable` verdict at a new consumer.
- **A group chat's recent messages for a prompt:** `formatChatHistoryForPrompt(messages, { last, maxContentChars? })` in `src/main/group-chat/group-chat-log.ts`. Every prompt that hands an agent or a model the conversation so far reads it as `[from]: content` lines, oldest first, over the last `last` messages, with each body optionally cut at `maxContentChars` and marked `...`. The router had four hand-rolled copies of that `slice().map().join()` chain, two with the cut and two without; the participant summary on a local model was about to be a fifth. Do NOT hand-roll another, and size `last` / `maxContentChars` to the context the reader has (a 4k-token local model gets far less than an agent).
- **Tokenizing text for an ONNX model:** `loadTokenizer(dir)` in `src/main/local-models/tokenizer.ts`, over `@huggingface/tokenizers`. It runs the `tokenizer.json` the model shipped (WordPiece, Unigram, BPE) and returns `int64` tensors-ready `inputIds` / `attentionMask` / `tokenTypeIds`, cached per install directory. Do NOT re-implement a tokenizer per model, and do NOT truncate by slicing the encoded ids: that drops the trailing `[SEP]` / `</s>` the model expects and the output degrades silently. `encode(text, { maxLength })` shortens the CONTENT and keeps whatever the post-processor wrapped around it, defaulting to the config's `model_max_length` (the transformers `1e30` sentinel means no window). Call `clearTokenizerCache(dir)` when a model is reinstalled or removed. `encodeBatch(texts)` pads to the longest row on the RIGHT with the tokenizer's own `padTokenId`, masked out, flattened into the `[batch, sequence]` layout a tensor takes. `encodeWindows(text, { maxLength, overlap })` cuts a text longer than the window into overlapping windows, each wrapped in the model's specials, and `padTokenizedRows(rows, padTokenId)` batches rows a caller already holds; do NOT hand-roll a sliding window over encoded ids for the same reason.
- **Running an ONNX encoder model:** `getOnnxEncoderPool().run(modelId, kind, consumerId, work)` and `encoderFeeds()` in `src/main/local-models/onnx-encoder.ts`. The pool resolves the model as installed and of the right kind, loads ONNX Runtime through `loadLocalRuntime`, opens the graph from its install path (so an external `.onnx_data` file resolves), loads its tokenizer, and frees the session after 5 idle minutes; work on one model is serialized. Do NOT call `InferenceSession.create` for an encoder model anywhere else, and do NOT hard-code the input list: `encoderFeeds` builds exactly the inputs the graph declares (BERT wants `token_type_ids`, Gemma does not) and refuses any other by name. Production sessions are proxies from `loadOnnxWorkerRuntime()` in `src/main/local-models/runtime/onnx-worker-session.ts`; graph loading and synchronous native inference run in `runtime/onnx-worker.ts`, with at most two compute threads per encoder. Releasing an encoder terminates its worker. Keep the native import in `native-loader.ts` through `importNativeRuntimeEntry()`.
- **Embedding text for search by meaning:** `embedTexts(source, texts, { mode })` in `src/main/local-models/embeddings.ts`, normally reached through `runEmbeddingTask('semantic-search', ...)`. Every vector is L2-normalized from both sources, so a dot product is cosine similarity. `mode` (`query` / `document`) selects the prefix a model was trained with; leaving it off degrades retrieval with no error. Endpoint models get no prefix. Pooling (`cls`, `mean`, `graph`) comes from the catalog run spec, never from the caller.
- **Routing a prompt to a tier and effort by its nearest labeled examples:** `getEmbeddingRouter().route(source, text, { signal })` in `src/main/local-models/model-router.ts` embeds the prompt (`embedTexts`, `query` mode on BOTH sides, because the vote compares a prompt with other prompts) and lets its 5 nearest labeled prompts vote. The labeled prompts are `MODEL_ROUTING_EXAMPLES` in `src/shared/local-models/model-routing-examples.ts` (the TUNE half of the routing set; a prompt added there must never also be in the eval's report fixture) merged with the user's corrections from `getModelRoutingCorrectionStore()` in `src/main/local-models/model-routing-corrections.ts` (`list`, `record`, `clear`, over `userData/model-routing-corrections.json`, capped at 500). The vote is `voteModelRoute()` in `src/shared/local-models/model-routing.ts`: inverse-distance weights, each axis voted on its own, the margin as the confidence, a tie to the HIGHER level. An exact match weighs 100, so a corrected prompt outvotes its neighbours with no special case; that only holds because the router and the store both pass text through `normalizeModelRoutingText()` (secrets replaced BEFORE the cut), so never embed or store a routing text any other way. Example vectors live in memory under `localModelSourceKey(source)`, one source at a time, and a new correction embeds only itself. The trap: the first build embeds every example and is deliberately NOT tied to the caller's signal. An aborted caller stops waiting and the build runs on; tie it to the caller and a slow machine abandons the build on every send and never routes. Call `warm(source)` when routing is turned on, never at a start with routing off (it loads the embedding model). The router has no assignment, timeout or ledger of its own, and a source that cannot run fails as itself.
- **Deciding how much model one turn needs (`routeTurn`):** `routeTurn({ agent?: { id, tabId? }, prompt, recent? }, { timeoutMs?, signal? })` in `src/main/local-models/route-turn.ts` is the one call that asks the router the `model-routing` task is assigned. It answers `{ tier, effort, engine, latencyMs, tierConfidence?, effortConfidence? }` or `{ skipped, detail, engine?, latencyMs }` (`isModelRouteSkipped()`; reasons `off`, `no-prompt`, `timeout`, `unavailable`, `cancelled` in `MODEL_ROUTING_SKIP_REASONS`) and it NEVER rejects, because a routing failure must not fail a send: a classified failure is a skip, an unclassified one goes to Sentry and is answered `unavailable`. The assignment names the router and `modelRouterKind()` in `src/shared/local-models/model-routing-task.ts` reads it: `local:<embedding model>` is the embedding router (`runEmbeddingRouteTask()`, the prompt ALONE), `local:<text model>` or any `endpoint:<id>:<model>` is a local judge (`askLocalJudge(..., { taskId: 'model-routing' })`, on the task's OWN model, not the `local-judge` task's), and `system-one` is the engine in force (`runSystemOneTask('model-routing', ...)`). No router stands in for another: an embedding model that is not downloaded is not answered by asking Jev. The two questions are `modelRoutingSystemOneRequest(input)` (`tier` and `effort` as `score` questions over three described levels), asked of a judge and an engine alike, and `modelRouteFromSystemOne()` reads them back (a position between two levels takes the nearer, a tie the HIGHER, a missing axis is no route). The text is `normalizeModelRoutingText()` (secrets replaced BEFORE the cut) plus the last user and assistant message, each cut to 600 characters, never the transcript. The timeout is a RACE against a timer `routeTurn` owns (`MODEL_ROUTING_TIMEOUT_MS`, 400 ms for a typed send; `MODEL_ROUTING_UNATTENDED_TIMEOUT_MS`, 5 s for Auto Run, Cue and `maestro-cli send`): a model that is still loading cannot be stopped and a router's own budget returns late, so never trust the router's budget alone. Every routing that asked a router is in the ledger once (`modelRouteAnswers()` for the two local routers, `runSystemOneTask` writes its own), after the answer is returned. Another process reaches it through `routeTurnRequest()` (IPC `localModels:routeTurn`, WS `model_route_turn`), and `parseRouteTurnRequest()` never cuts a text because texts are redacted before they are cut. A caller that spawns a user turn does NOT call `routeTurn` and resolve a tier itself: it uses `resolveAutoTurn()` through one of the three entry points under "A turn sent on the Auto model" below. `refreshModelRouting()` / `startModelRouting()` keep the embedding router's vectors in step with the assignment (warm on a change TO an embedding model, never at a start).
- **A turn sent on the Auto model (`auto`):** `auto` (`AUTO_MODEL`, `isAutoModel()` in `src/shared/autoModel.ts`) is a value stored where a model name goes: an agent's or a tab's `customModel`, a queued item's `turnSettings.model`, an Auto Run's run model, a Cue subscription's `model`. It is Maestro's word and no harness is ever handed it: `applyAgentConfigOverrides()` drops it, so a surface with no Auto path runs the agent on its default. Never compare a model to the string yourself, and never pass an agent's `customModel` to a resolver as "the agent's own model" without `ownModel()`, which is `undefined` for Auto. The resolution is `resolveAutoTurn({ toolType, route, hold, agentEffort, routing, marker })` in `src/shared/autoTurn.ts`, pure and shared by every surface, over `resolveTurnSettings()` so a routed tier launches where the same tier launches in a playbook. Three rules live there and nowhere else. (1) `autoModelHold()` keeps the model and place of the provider session a turn RESUMES while its cache is warm (`promptCacheLifetimeMs()`); the router's tier is then recorded and not applied, its effort is. (2) No decision is never a failure: the agent's default model and its own effort apply. (3) The router's answer is frozen in an `AutoRouteStamp` (`autoRouteStamp()`), and what the turn did in the `AutoTurnStamp` on `AITab.turnAuto`; `autoTurnLabel()` is how it reads. A marker (`MAESTRO:MODEL`, a Cue subscription's `effort`) wins per axis, and `autoTurnNeedsRoute()` says whether the router is asked at all. **Renderer:** a send does not wait, so it is two steps. `codifyTurnSettings()` / `codifyQueuedTurnSettings()` take the tab with a `pending` stamp that already holds rule 1's verdict (decided THEN, from `tabSessionLaunch()`, because the busy-write makes a cold session look warm), and `settleAutoTurn({ sessionId, tabId, prompt, itemId? })` in `src/renderer/services/autoTurn.ts` asks the router and makes the stamp final. Every spawn of a user turn calls it inside its own `try` (a tier target that cannot be used throws, by name) and spreads `turnSpawnSettings(launch, own)` into `process.spawn`. A message that is queued calls `routeQueuedAutoItem()` right after it is in the queue: a queued message is routed WHEN IT IS QUEUED, the answer rides on `turnSettings.route`, and the drain asks nothing. `captureQueuedTurnSettings()` keeps the word, never a resolution. **No tab:** `resolveAgentAutoTurn()` in `src/main/local-models/auto-turn.ts` (a Cue run whose subscription says `model: auto`, a headless send; `noteAutoSessionLaunch()` is the one record of what such a session ran) and `resolveCliAutoTurn()` in `src/cli/services/auto-turn.ts` (asks the desktop over `model_route_turn`; no desktop is no decision). A new surface that spawns a user turn calls one of these three; it does not call `routeTurn` and resolve a tier itself. **Offering it and reading it back:** `autoModelChoice(agent, { harnessDefaults, assignments, endpoints })` is the choice a picker offers, or `null` when Auto would change nothing (`autoModelSupport()`: a tier that resolves to a target, an effort ladder); the renderer reads it through `useAutoModelChoice(agent)` in `src/renderer/hooks/agent/useAutoModelChoice.ts`, lists the word with `withAutoModelOption()`, and draws it with `modelChoiceLabel()`. Never add `'auto'` to a model list by hand, and never offer it on a surface whose turns are not routed (`AgentConfigPanel` takes it only through the opt-in `autoModelFor`). A surface that is HANDED the word (`update-agent`, `create-agent`, `send --model`, the `update_session_config` bridge) refuses with `autoModelProblem()` and stores `AUTO_MODEL`. A finished turn's Auto is one pill, `<TurnSettingPills auto={log.turnAuto}>`, labeled by `autoTurnLabel()` with `autoTurnDetail()` as its tooltip; a queued item's pills are `queuedTurnPills(item.turnSettings)`; a caller with no pill gets `autoTurnReport()` (`send`'s `auto` field). A dry run is `maestro-cli s1 route "<prompt>" [--agent <id>]`.
- **What a turn's stats row says about its model:** `turnModelStatsFields({ runOn, auto })` in `src/shared/autoTurn.ts` shapes the three `query_events` columns added in stats migration v20: `runOnEndpoint` and `runOnModel` (the Run on source the turn launched on, written together) and `routedTier` (the tier Auto's router chose). It returns `{}` for a turn on a named model on the provider, so a caller spreads it unconditionally and the columns stay NULL. BOTH writers of the row call it, the interactive exit listener (`useAgentExitListener`) and the Auto Run spawn (`useAgentExecution`), and a third writer must too: two shapers would file the same turn two ways and the Usage Dashboard's Models tab groups on these columns as written. Hand it what the launch USED, never the live agent record. An interactive turn passes the tab's send-time stamps, `AITab.turnRunOnSource` (the source frozen beside the `turnRunOn` label by `codifiedRunOn()` in `src/renderer/utils/providerTabSessions.ts`, read with `turnRunOnSource(session)`) and `AITab.turnAuto`; an Auto Run task passes `turnRunOnTarget({ runOn: options.runOnOverride }, session.runOn)` and `SpawnAgentOptions.autoStamp`. Settings are codified at send, so `session.runOn` read at exit refiles a turn whose agent was retargeted while it ran. `routedTier` is the router's answer and not always the launch: a session kept on its model (Auto rule 1) records the tier and the place it stayed. `bindQueryEvent()` in `src/main/stats/query-event-insert.ts` is the last guard: it writes the source whole or not at all and binds a tier only when it is one of the three levels.
- **What every model other than a provider's own did in a range (the Models tab's read):** `readModelUsage(range)` in `src/main/local-models/model-usage.ts` is the one read behind the Usage Dashboard's Models tab (`stats:get-model-usage`) and `maestro-cli stats models` (the WS message `get_model_usage`). It checks the range against `STATS_TIME_RANGES` (`isStatsTimeRange()` in `src/shared/stats-types.ts`; no range reads the week), gathers what only the running app knows (the saved endpoints, the user's OpenJev URL, agent names through `storedAgentRecord()`) as a `ModelUsageNames`, and calls `getModelUsage(db, range, names)` in `src/main/stats/model-usage.ts`. That function is the ONLY aggregation over the four tables: `local_model_runs` (the task router's and System One's runs), `query_events` (agent turns, filed under their Run on source or under the provider's own cloud for comparison), `proxy_requests` (the token proxy's totals through `getProxyRequestUsage()`, and the background requests it routed, read from `routed_to` with `parseRunOnSpec()`) and `s1_decisions` (outcomes per task and engine). It returns one `ModelUsage`: `totals`, `sources`, `byTask`, `byAgent`, `systemOne` and `proxy`. Do not write a second query for a chart on the tab; add the field to `ModelUsage` so the CLI prints it too. Rules that are easy to break. (1) A source counts work in three units and keeps them apart: `runs` (one model call), `turns` (one agent turn, however many calls it made) and `routed` (one proxied background request); `requests` is their sum, so never divide tokens by it. (2) SQLite has no percentile function: each scan is `ORDER BY` its duration column and every distribution is sliced from that one pass with `percentilesFromSorted()`, so a new grouping must push into its own array inside the same loop, never re-sort. (3) `latency` covers ANSWERED runs only (`outcome = 'ok'`): a failed run's duration is its timeout and a cancelled one's is the caller's patience. A provider's own cloud has no run latency at all; its `turnDuration` is whole turns. (4) Which source a stored row belongs to is `modelUsageRunSource()` / `modelUsageEndpointSource()` in `src/shared/local-models/model-usage.ts`, and both read `maestro-server` as the model server: a run the server answered before stats migration v20 was filed as `endpoint` with that id, and a Run on turn names the server by it. (5) `onDevice` is three-valued: an endpoint is on this machine only when its URL is loopback (`isLoopbackEndpointUrl()`), a LAN server is not, and an endpoint that is no longer saved (or OpenJev with no URL) is `null`, which `totals.onDeviceRequests` does not count. Do not coerce `null` to `false` in a label. (6) The System One block reads the ledger, which is pruned at 30 days: `coversRange` is false for a longer range and the surface must say so. `routedTiers` comes from `query_events` and covers the whole range. (7) `errors` is 0 for a provider's own cloud because `query_events` records no outcome, not because nothing failed. Known gap: `query_events` has one writer, the renderer, so a Cue run and a headless `maestro-cli send` on a Run on source are in no table and are not counted. The two surfaces also share how a record is READ, in `src/shared/local-models/model-usage.ts`: `describeModelUsageRequestSplit(source)` prints the three units (`36 runs, 4 turns, 1 routed`), and `modelUsageSourceLatency(source)` picks the distribution a source's p50 and p95 come from and names its unit: `request` when the source answered model calls, `turn` only when it has no request on record (a provider's own cloud, a Run on target with no housekeeping work). A surface that prints a `turn` figure must mark it, because a turn is many requests and the tools between them. The tab is `<ModelUsageStats>` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/ModelUsage/`: it loads through `useModelUsage(range)` (`src/renderer/hooks/stats/useModelUsage.ts`: a refresh keeps the record on screen, an answer for a range the user left is dropped, `stats:updated` is debounced), and everything it derives (headline numbers, sort orders, the log-scale latency bars, the outcome slices) is a pure function in `ModelUsage/modelUsageView.ts`. Tests of either surface use the two records in `src/__tests__/helpers/modelUsageFixtures.ts`.
- **Scanning content for secrets before it is published:** `scanForSecrets(content, { env, source })` in `src/shared/secretScan.ts` (the detectors; pure, model-free, usable in every process), `screenForSecrets(parts)` in `src/main/local-models/secret-screen.ts` (the one entry point for a publishing path), and `withSecretScreenConfirm(attempt, prompt)` in `src/renderer/services/secretScreen.ts` (the one confirmation flow, drawn by `<SecretScreenConfirmHost>`, mounted once in `AppShell`). Three paths publish for the user and each one screens at the point its routes meet, in the MAIN process, so the UI and the CLI cannot differ: the `git:createGist` handler, the three feedback service functions in `src/main/feedback/index.ts`, and `generateDebugPackage`. A new publishing path calls `screenForSecrets()` there, returns `secretScreenRefusal(findings, what, note)` on a finding, and takes an `allowSecrets` flag. Do NOT screen in the renderer or in the CLI command: a check on the caller's side is a check the other caller skips. **The pattern scan always runs.** It needs no model, so no setting turns it off; the `secret-screen` Local Models assignment adds a model pass that is extra coverage only. A model that is off, unavailable, timed out, or throwing never blocks a publish and never hides a pattern finding: its reason comes back as `secretScreenNote`. That is the reverse of the injection screen's fail-closed rule on purpose, because there the model is the only check and here it is the second one. **Consent is a literal `true`.** Every transport tests `allowSecrets === true`; a string or an absent field is a refusal, the same rule `readBackgroundField` follows. The UI sets it from "Publish anyway", the CLI from `--allow-secrets` (`ALLOW_SECRETS_FLAG`), and an agent must not add the flag on its own to get past a refusal. **Nothing here returns a secret.** A finding's `preview` is masked by `maskEnvValue()`, so the list is safe in a dialog, on stdout, and in a bridge frame; the main-process log records kinds and counts only. Describe a finding with `describeSecretFinding()` (the dialog and the CLI both print it) rather than formatting one by hand. **Refuse before the first upload.** `submitFeedbackConversation` screens the text and builds the support package before it uploads a screenshot, and `subscribeFeedbackIssue` screens the comment before the +1, so a refusal leaves nothing on GitHub; `generateDebugPackage` takes its destination as a function (`DebugPackageDestination`) and calls it only after the screen passes, so a save dialog is never raised for a package about to be refused. Configured values come from `knownSecretEnv()` as ENTRIES, not a record, because two agents can each hold their own `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and a record would keep one. A value is searched for only when `isSecretEnvKey()` flags its name and it is eight characters or more, not a path, and not a number: `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` names a location, and a three-letter value matches ordinary text everywhere. Distinct from `redactSecrets()` in `src/main/acappella/providers/credentials.ts`, which REPLACES anything key-shaped inside the support package and is eager on purpose (a false positive there costs one unreadable token); this module REPORTS and stops a publish, so its patterns are narrower. Pick by question ("scrub this text" vs "may this leave the machine?") and do not merge them. Test fixtures assemble tokens at runtime (`['ghp', body].join('_')`): a literal token in a test file is what a repository secret scanner blocks a push for.
- **Screening untrusted text for prompt injection:** `classifyText(source, text)` in `src/main/local-models/classifier.ts`, normally reached through `runClassifierTask('injection-screen', { text })`. A text longer than the model's window is read in overlapping windows and the score is the MAXIMUM positive probability across them: truncating screens only the start, and averaging lets pages of benign text dilute a short injection. Do NOT score only the first window or average windows. Endpoint sources are refused (`model-unsupported`) rather than approximated with a chat model. Session work rides the shared `OnnxEncoderPool`, and a failed run goes through `encoderRunFailure()` in `onnx-encoder.ts`, the same as embeddings.
- **The Kokoro voice's front end (text to model token ids):** `createKokoroFrontEnd({ tokenizerPath })` in `src/main/acappella/providers/local/kokoro/front-end.ts`, over `kokoroPhonemes()` / `normalizeKokoroText()` in `kokoro/text.ts`, `loadEspeak()` / `withoutAddedErrorHandlers()` in `kokoro/espeak.ts`, and `loadKokoroVocab()` / `phonemesToIds()` / `splitTokenRuns()` / `KOKORO_MAX_TOKENS` in `kokoro/tokens.ts`. Kokoro takes PHONEME ids, so text is normalized (years, clock times, money, decimals), phonemized by espeak-ng between the punctuation, mapped onto Kokoro's phoneme set, and looked up in the model's own `tokenizer.json`. Three traps, each found while building it. **`text.ts` is a rule-for-rule port of kokoro-js `phonemize.js` and must not be tidied**: the whitespace collapse that replaces only its first match and the parentheses that become guillemets (which the vocabulary then drops) are what the voice was trained on, and a golden test pins the 30 ids for one sentence against the reference on every run. **`phonemizer` (espeak-ng as plain JavaScript) registers `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection` handlers on import that rethrow**, which in a process with its own handlers turns any later uncaught error anywhere in the app into an exit with code 7; `loadEspeak()` is the only place it may be required, because it removes what the load added. **A sentence is not a safe unit**: the model takes at most 510 ids between its pad tokens (511 fails the inference in ONNX Runtime), and 240 characters of digits phonemize to about 1500, so the provider cuts with `splitTokenRuns()` (clause punctuation, then a space, then mid-word), picks the style row by each RUN's length, and joins the audio. An empty id list (nothing speakable) is skipped, never sent as two pad tokens. The espeak-ng inside the Apache-2.0 package is GPL-3.0-or-later: `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`. Real-hardware check: `kokoro-e2e.test.ts`.
- **Running a local speech-to-text model:** `getSpeechToTextPool().acquire(modelId, consumerId)` in `src/main/local-models/speech-to-text.ts`, over the engines in `src/main/local-models/stt/` (`parakeet/engine.ts`, `whisper/`) and the one table that binds a catalog model to its engine, files, and pass limit (`stt/engines.ts`). A consumer takes a LEASE (`transcribe(audio)`, `maxSamples`, `release()`) instead of building an engine. Three consumers open these models (a voice session's recogniser in `chunked-stt.ts`, the transcription wake word, a dictated clip), and before the pool each built its own, so an armed wake word plus a session held the same 650 MB encoder twice. The pool also holds the rule that used to live in one provider only: **one decode at a time per model**, because two greedy loops on one decoder session hand each other's recurrent state back and ONNX Runtime reports it as a reshape failure. A decode asked for before `release()` still runs (the released check is made when the decode is ASKED for, and the release queues behind it); a model stays resident while leased and is freed 5 minutes after the last lease. Do NOT construct a `ParakeetEngine` or `WhisperEngine` anywhere else, and do NOT add a speech model to the catalog without a binding in `stt/engines.ts` (a test fails if you do: it would download, show as installed, and never transcribe).
- **Transcribing a whole clip of speech:** `transcribeClip(audio, sampleRate)` in `src/main/local-models/dictation.ts`, which is `runSpeechToTextTask('dictation', ...)` in `task-router.ts` over `transcribeSpeech(source, audio, sampleRate)` in `speech-to-text.ts`. The clip runs on the Dictation task's assignment (a local model through the pool, or an endpoint's `/v1/audio/transcriptions` via `endpointTranscribe`), is recorded in `local_model_runs`, and never falls back. Three rules the service holds so no caller re-derives them: the clip is resampled to 16 kHz once with `resampleFloat32` (which AVERAGES when downsampling, so 48 kHz content above 8 kHz is not folded into the speech band); a clip under 200 ms is answered empty WITHOUT loading a model; and a clip longer than one pass (60 s Parakeet, 30 s Whisper) is cut by `splitSpeechWindows()` at the quietest 20 ms near each limit and EVERY window is transcribed. That last one is the opposite of a live voice session, which keeps the tail: dictation must not lose the start of what was said. Do NOT call an engine or the pool directly for a clip, and do NOT truncate a long one.
- **Recording a dictation without a voice session:** `startDictation(ownerId)` / `stopDictation(ownerId)` / `cancelDictation(ownerId)` and `registerDictationMicrophone()` in `src/main/local-models/dictation.ts`, reached from a renderer as `window.maestro.localModels.dictation.{start,stop,cancel,onInterrupted}`. The app window has NO microphone (the default session denies every media request; only the audio host's webContents is granted), so do not call `getUserMedia` in the app renderer: the recording is made in main through a `DictationMicrophone`. The core imports no capture device; A Cappella registers `ClipCapture` (`src/main/acappella/audio/clip-capture.ts`), the audio host's capture path with no session behind it. It takes no session service, so it cannot open the floor, and it is registered whether or not the Encore flag is on. Rules that each guard a real failure: readiness is checked BEFORE the microphone opens (a missing model is refused before the user speaks, not after); `start()` resolves only once the host announced `capture-start` (a "recording" indicator over a microphone that never opened is the worst outcome); a dictation is OWNED by the window that started it and its interruption is sent to that window alone, never broadcast (voice events are broadcast, and a surface that forgets to gate renders in every window); it is refused while a voice session is live, since that speech is already being dispatched; the always-on wake word disarms while a clip records (`HandsFreeListener.isMicrophoneBorrowed`), so a dictated wake phrase is text and not a wake; and a web-desktop client (no sender) is refused as `no-microphone`, because the device is on the desktop and not where that person is speaking.
- **Dictating into a text box (renderer):** `startDictation(target)` / `stopDictation()` / `cancelDictation()` / `toggleDictation(target)` in `src/renderer/services/dictation.ts`, with `registerDictationTarget()`, `resolveDictationTarget()`, `focusedDictationTarget()` and `requestDictationShortcutPress()` beside them; state in `src/renderer/stores/dictationStore.ts` (`phase`, `target`, `availability`); drawn by `<DictationButton>` (`src/renderer/components/ui/DictationButton.tsx`) and `<DictationHost>` (`src/renderer/components/DictationHost.tsx`, mounted once in `AppShell`); the press is `useDictationPress()` in `src/renderer/hooks/dictation/useDictationPress.ts`. Dictation records one clip through the `dictation` Local Models assignment and types the transcript at the caret. It is NOT a voice session: nothing is routed, nothing is spoken back, nothing is submitted. Two ways in share one path: the button beside a text box, and the `dictate` shortcut (registered with NO default chord, because it has to work inside every text box and any default would take a key from typing somewhere). Both classify a press with `usePressAndHold` against the voice controls' `holdThresholdMs`: a tap toggles, a hold records while held. Five rules. **One dictation per window**, owned by the service: a component never calls `window.maestro.localModels.dictation` itself, or two surfaces disagree about who holds the microphone. **The target is fixed at start**: the transcript goes to the text box the dictation began in even when focus moved while the clip decoded, and a target whose surface is gone gets the words in a toast instead of losing them. **The shortcut's release is ANY keyup, or the window losing focus**: macOS does not deliver a letter's keyup while Command is held, so waiting for the exact chord leaves the microphone open after the user let go. **Readiness is mirrored once** by `<DictationHost>` (`off`, the default, hides every button; `unavailable` draws it dimmed with the reason; a web-desktop client is always hidden, since the microphone is on the desktop), and main re-checks it at every start, so a stale mirror cannot record into a model that is not there. **The recording pill is not decoration**: a dictation the shortcut started in a text box with no button of its own has nothing else on screen saying a microphone is live, so the pill sits above modals (`Z_LAYERS.DICTATION_INDICATOR`) with Stop and Discard. A new text box that needs its own write path passes a memoized `DictationTarget` to `<DictationButton>` (the target's object identity is what decides which button shows "recording"); every editable `MarkdownEditor` already has one.
- **Putting a transcript in a text box:** `DictationTarget` and its builders in `src/renderer/utils/dictationTargets.ts`: `controlledTextDictationTarget({ getElement, setValue })`, `nativeTextDictationTarget(element)`, `editorViewDictationTarget({ getElement, getView })`, the generic `dictationTargetForElement(element)`, and `padDictatedText(before, transcript, after)`. "Insert at the caret" is three different writes. A STORE-BACKED composer (the AI composer, the group chat composer) is written through its own setter, the one a restored draft or an accepted mention uses, and NEVER through the textarea's `onChange`: that handler holds the composer's typing rules, so a transcript that began with `!` would climb the AI composer's command mode ladder and one containing `@` would open the mention picker for words nobody typed. A CodeMirror editor is written by one transaction (one undo step, reported to the host by the same update listener as a keystroke). Any other textarea or text input is written the way a keystroke writes it: `document.execCommand('insertText')` while it holds focus, which keeps the browser's undo stack, otherwise the native value setter plus an `input` event, which is what a React `onChange` listens for. `padDictatedText` adds a space only against a word character, never against whitespace, an opening bracket, or closing punctuation; a recogniser returns bare words, and without it they fuse with their neighbours. `dictationTargetForElement` returns null for a password field, a read-only or disabled control, and anything inside `.xterm` (the terminal's hidden textarea would send the words straight to a shell). Do NOT write a fourth splice at a call site.
- **Replacing a selection in a string:** `insertTextAtSelection(content, start, end, text)` in `src/renderer/utils/textSelectionEdit.ts`, returning `{ content, cursorPosition }`. Hoisted out of the Wizard document editor (which re-exports it) when dictation needed the same edit.
- **Saying a line aloud outside a voice session (spoken notices):** `announceAgentFinished(notice, { appFocused, safeSend })`, `speakNotice(text)`, `cancelSpokenNotices()`, `registerNoticeSpeaker()` and the transport entries `announceAgentFinishedRequest()` / `speakNoticeRequest()` in `src/main/local-models/spoken-notices.ts`; the pure half (`SpokenNoticeMode`, `asSpokenNoticeMode()`, `shouldSpeakNotice()`, `fallbackNoticeLine()`, `composeNoticeLine()`, `cleanSpokenText()`, `boundAgentFinishedNotice()`, `SpokenNoticeResult`) in `src/shared/spokenNotices.ts`; the speaker `NoticeSpeech` in `src/main/acappella/audio/notice-speech.ts`; the voice rule `noticeTtsProviderId()` / `createNoticeTtsProvider()` in `src/main/acappella/providers/provider-registry.ts`; renderer `announceAgentFinished()` / `speakTestNotice()` in `src/renderer/services/spokenNotices.ts` and `<SpokenNoticesSetting>`; reached from a renderer as `window.maestro.localModels.spokenNotices.{agentFinished,speak}` and from the CLI as `maestro-cli notify speak` (`notify_speak`). Anything that wants Maestro to SPEAK without a voice session goes through this one queue; do NOT send `play` to the audio host from a new call site, and do NOT shell out to `say`. What the queue holds so no caller re-derives it: one line at a time, with a quiet window measured from when the last one ENDED (so lines are queued, never overlapping); a short queue whose oldest waiter gives way; a line dropped as stale after a minute; a hold while a dictation records (the microphone would record the notice into the text). **Never while a voice session is live** is checked three times on purpose: before the model is called (no inference for a line nobody hears), again at the moment of speaking (a session can start while a line waits), and by `cancelSpokenNotices()` on the session's `listen-start`, which cuts off the line being spoken. The `spoken-notices` task decides how much a notice SAYS, never whether one is spoken: `off`, `unavailable`, a timeout, and an unusable reply all speak `<agent name> finished`, and the agent's name is put in front by `composeNoticeLine()`, never by the model. Whether one is spoken is the `spokenNotices` setting alone, and `asSpokenNoticeMode()` reads anything it does not know as `off`, because speaking aloud is consent. The voice is ALWAYS local: the Text-to-Speech choice when it is local-tier, the system voice otherwise. A notice fires unasked, so it must not send text to a hosted voice; that is this feature's rule and not a substitution, and a local voice that cannot speak is still reported as itself (`speech-failed`). The renderer reports a turn only from the client that owns its side effects, because the line is spoken on the host and two reports say it twice. Every outcome is a `SpokenNoticeResult`, never a rejection, so the CLI can print why a line was skipped.
- **Which Brain routes a spoken request (the `voice-routing` assignment):** `resolveVoiceBrainSource(assignments, endpoints)`, `effectiveBrainProviderId(source, storedBrainId)`, `assignedBrainProviderId()`, `isAssignedBrainProviderId()`, `parseVoiceBrainChoice()` / `voiceBrainChoiceValue()`, `VOICE_ROUTING_TASK_ID` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-routing.ts`; `ASSIGNED_BRAIN_PROVIDERS` and `summariseVoiceEgress(ids, { endpointName })` in `src/shared/acappella/provider-catalog.ts`; `AssignedBrainProvider` in `src/main/acappella/providers/local/assigned-brain.ts`; `buildBrainOptionGroups()` in `src/renderer/components/Settings/ACappella/brainOptions.ts`. A local model or an endpoint is the Conductor Brain because it is ASSIGNED to the Local Models `voice-routing` task, never because a provider id was stored: the id a session reports (`qwen3-local`, `endpoint-brain`) is derived, so Settings > Local Models, `maestro-cli models assign voice-routing ...`, and the voice panels cannot show different Brains. The built-in router and the hosted Brains are not Local Models sources, so they stay in `acappella.providers.brain` and apply while the assignment is `off`; picking one in a voice panel switches the assignment off FIRST (`setProvider('brain', ...)` in `useVoiceProviderSelection`), because an assignment left on keeps deciding the slot. Read the Brain in force through `readVoiceProviderSettings()` in main and `useVoiceProviderSelection().providerIds.brain` in the renderer; do NOT read `acappella.providers.brain` on its own, which is only the choice waiting behind the assignment. The provider runs every turn through `runLocalTask('voice-routing')` and restates the router's verdict as its own classified failure; do NOT call `generateText()` or the endpoint client from a Brain, and do NOT try another Brain when the assigned source cannot answer. Two traps the code encodes: a llama.cpp grammar emits EVERY listed property, so a local model is held to `ROUTE_DECISION_GRAMMAR_SCHEMA` (optional fields may be `null`) while an endpoint gets `ROUTE_DECISION_JSON_SCHEMA`, and without that split every local decision carried a clarifying question and nothing reached an agent; and the privacy sentence names the endpoint from the assignment (`endpointName`) rather than inspecting its URL, because the transcript leaves this process wherever the server runs. The picker offers a local model exactly when the `voice-routing` task does (`unofferedLocalModels`): Qwen3.5 4B is offered, and Qwen3 1.7B is not (it sends a named agent's request to the Conductor; both measured in `docs/architecture/local-models.md`). A model already assigned is always listed, so the picker never reads "Built-in" while a model routes.
- **Transcribing an audio file from the CLI:** `transcribeAudioFileRequest(path)` in `src/main/local-models/dictation.ts`, reached as `maestro-cli models run dictation --audio <file.wav>` (`local_models_transcribe`). It is the CLI's route to the same `transcribeClip()` a clip recorded in a text box ends in. An agent has no microphone, so it brings the speech as a file. Only 16-bit PCM WAV is read (`decodeWavPcm16`); the path arrives untrusted from the bridge and is validated there (absolute, a file, under the size cap).
- **What a recogniser's raw text may become:** `sanitizeTranscript(raw)` in `src/main/local-models/stt/transcript.ts` (re-exported by `chunked-stt.ts` and `whisper-stt.ts`). Whisper narrates silence as `[BLANK_AUDIO]`, `(keyboard clacking)`, "you", "Thank you."; a session that dispatches one sends an agent a prompt nobody said, and a dictation that inserts one types it into the user's text box. One rule for both.
- **PCM arithmetic (WAV, int16/float, resampling):** `encodeWav()`, `decodeWavPcm16()`, `int16ToFloat32()`, `float32ToInt16()`, `resampleLinear()`, `resampleFloat32()` and `SPEECH_SAMPLE_RATE` in `src/shared/audio/pcm.ts`. Pure and import-free, so the core speech-to-text service and A Cappella's providers read the same functions; `src/main/acappella/providers/pcm.ts` re-exports them and keeps only the accumulating `PcmBuffer`. `resampleLinear` (int16, interpolation) is for the 16 kHz capture path feeding a service that wants 24 kHz; use `resampleFloat32` to bring a foreign clip DOWN to 16 kHz, since plain decimation aliases.
- **Storing and searching embedding vectors:** `getEmbeddingStore()` in `src/main/local-models/embedding-store.ts`, over `userData/embeddings.db` (better-sqlite3, WAL). `upsertChunks(rows)` writes one transaction and validates every row first; `search(queryVector, { modelId, limit, sourceTypes?, agentId? })` is brute-force cosine in JavaScript over the ONE model named, served from memory. Three traps the store already handles, so do not re-solve them: vectors from two models are never comparable, so every read names a `modelId` and old rows are left on disk unread rather than mixed in; the in-memory copy is loaded on the first SEARCH and never by a write, so a background indexer does not pin the whole index in memory; and a `Float32Array` view over a driver's blob throws when the blob sits at an offset that is not a multiple of four, so the bytes are copied on the way out. `upsertChunks` does NOT trim a source that shrank: call `deleteBySource` before re-indexing changed text. Hash chunk text with `embeddingTextHash()`. `knownTextHashes(modelId, hashes)` answers "has this text ever been embedded", which is NOT enough to skip a chunk (two sources with the same text each need a row to be found), so the indexer compares per source with `sourceChunkHashes(modelId, sourceType)`; `sourceIds(sourceType)` is what a prune walks and `hasSource()` is the one-source lookup. Vector math is `l2Normalize()` / `dotProduct()` in `src/main/local-models/vectors.ts`, an import-free leaf so the store does not load the ONNX stack. Do NOT add sqlite-vec or any other native vector extension: at tens of thousands of 384-wide rows the scan is a few milliseconds, and a second native module to rebuild per platform buys nothing.
- **Cutting text into embedding windows:** `chunkText(text, { countTokens, maxTokens?, overlapTokens?, maxChunks? })` in `src/main/local-models/text-chunker.ts`, with `CHUNK_TARGET_TOKENS` (320), `CHUNK_OVERLAP_TOKENS` (40) and `MAX_CHUNKS_PER_SOURCE` (24). An embedding model silently drops whatever is past its window, so a long text embedded whole is an embedding of its first page. Pure and deterministic (the same text and counter always give the same chunks, which is what lets a hash mean "unchanged"). Windows end at sentence and line ends; a unit longer than a whole window is halved at whitespace, never inside a surrogate pair. The caller supplies the counter: `LocalTokenizer.countTokens()` (content tokens, no specials, no truncation) for a local model, `estimateTokenCount()` for an endpoint. **The text is bounded BEFORE it is tokenized**, because counting runs on the Electron main thread: do NOT remove the bound to index "all of" a 2 MB response. Do NOT hand-roll another `slice(0, n)` chunker beside an embedding call.
- **Keeping the semantic index current:** `startSemanticIndex()` in `src/main/local-models/semantic-index-startup.ts` builds the app's `SemanticIndexer` (`src/main/local-models/semantic-indexer.ts`); `refreshSemanticIndexer()` re-reads its two settings, `semanticIndexProgress()` / `onSemanticIndexProgress()` are the read side, `stopSemanticIndexer()` runs at quit. It is driven ONLY by the `semantic-search` assignment: off holds no timer, reads no file, loads no model. Sources come from readers in `src/main/local-models/semantic-sources.ts` (`createHistoryReader`, `createDirectorNotesReader`, `createTranscriptReader`), each listing GROUPS that load on demand so a pass holds one file's text at a time. Rules a new source must keep: mark a source `mutable` only when its text can still change (an immutable one is skipped the moment the store holds it, without being chunked); never emit blank text; and a reader that throws suppresses the PRUNE for its type that pass, so an unreadable file cannot look like deleted entries. A new entry skips the wait for the next pass through `noteItem()` (History via `HistoryManager.onEntryAdded()`, synopses via `onDirectorNotesArchived()`). Rows are keyed by `localModelSourceKey(source)` in `src/main/local-models/sources.ts`, never a bare model name. Embedding goes through `runEmbeddingTask(..., { background: true })`; do NOT call `embedTexts` from a second indexer.
- **Inspecting and clearing the semantic index:** `semanticIndexStorage()` and `clearSemanticIndex()` in `src/main/local-models/semantic-indexer.ts`, plus `browseSemanticIndexRequest()` and `hydrateSemanticIndexPage()` in `src/main/local-models/work-search.ts`. Settings uses `window.maestro.localModels.semanticIndex.storage()`, `.browse(request)`, and `.clear()`. Disk bytes include the database, WAL, and shared-memory files; chunk counts are distinct from indexed-source counts. Browse pages default to 20 sources and cap at 50, read no vector BLOBs, and reuse the search corpus for previews without loading a model. Clear through the indexer, never directly from a renderer handler: it aborts and drains active work before `EmbeddingStore.clearAndCompact()` resets every model and search memory and runs SQLite compaction. Original History, notes, and transcripts remain intact. The next full pass waits 15 minutes; new records can still arrive incrementally while the task is assigned.
- **Searching past work by meaning or by text:** `searchPastWork(request)` / `searchPastWorkRequest(value)` in `src/main/local-models/work-search.ts`, over the wire types and pure helpers in `src/shared/local-models/work-search.ts` (`WorkSearchRequest`, `WorkSearchHit`, `WorkSearchResult`, `parseWorkSearchRequest()`, `parseWorkSearchSource()`, `workSearchSnippet()`, `formatWorkSearchScore()`, `describeWorkSearchIndex()`). ONE search serves the History panel's Semantic toggle, cross-tab search's Semantic toggle, and `maestro-cli search` (`localModels:search` and `local_models_search` both call it). The embedding store holds vectors and NO text, so a semantic hit is turned back into words by re-reading its source and re-cutting it with the indexer's own chunker and budget (`resolveSemanticChunkBudget`); the hit's chunk index then names the window that matched. That only works because the search reads the corpus through the SAME functions the indexer does (`historyGroupEntries()`, `transcriptItems()`, `synopsisItem()` in `semantic-sources.ts`), wired from the same stores in `semantic-index-startup.ts`: a second derivation of a source's text returns the wrong snippet for a chunk index, with no error.
