Three rules: one hit per source (its best chunk); a source that can no longer be read (a deleted entry, a closed tab, a Cue run while Cue indexing is off) is DROPPED, never returned as a bare id; and nothing falls back - a semantic search that cannot run answers `off` or `unavailable` with the reason, never text matches under the same name. Do NOT call `getEmbeddingStore().search()` from a new surface: it returns ids and scores a person cannot read.
- **A Semantic mode on a renderer search surface:** `useSemanticSearchReady()` and `useSemanticSearch(options)` in `src/renderer/hooks/localModels/useSemanticSearch.ts`, drawn with `<SemanticSearchToggle>` and `<SemanticMatchLine>` in `src/renderer/components/SemanticSearch/`. The toggle is rendered ONLY while `useSemanticSearchReady()` is true (the task's main-process readiness is `ready`), because a toggle for a model that is not downloaded switches the user into a mode that can only refuse. `useSemanticSearch` debounces the query (each search embeds it), drops an answer a newer query has overtaken, never searches a debounced value the user has typed past, and reports a refusal in `notice` rather than as an empty list. `SemanticMatchLine` shows the score and the matched passage, which is the only evidence a semantic hit has for being in the list, since it often shares no word with the query. `HistoryPanel` and `CrossTabSearchModal` are the two callers.
- **Whether someone is waiting on a local model:** `isInteractiveLocalTaskRunning()`, `whenNoInteractiveLocalTask()` in `src/main/local-models/task-router.ts`. Every router call is interactive unless its caller passes `background: true`. Background work awaits the second between batches, so a search or a tab name never queues behind it for longer than the batch already in flight. A background caller that forgets the flag counts itself and waits forever on its own call. Voice does not run through the router and is not counted.
- **Hearing about a new History entry in main:** `HistoryManager.onEntryAdded(listener)` in `src/main/history-manager.ts`. `addEntry` is the one in-process choke point for a new entry (the `history:add` handler and the plugin transcript bridge both go through it), so a consumer that must stay current hooks here, not at each writer. It fires after the append succeeded, a listener that throws is logged and the write stands, and entries appended by another process (`maestro-cli`) do not pass through: those are the history directory watcher's.
- **A transcript turn's index id:** `transcriptSourceId(tabId, turnId)` / `parseTranscriptSourceId()` in `src/shared/local-models/embedding-index.ts`. The semantic index files a transcript by TURN (the tab plus the id of the log entry that opens the turn), not by tab: a tab persists a sliding window of `MAX_PERSISTED_SESSION_LOGS` entries, so per-tab chunks would renumber and re-embed every time the oldest entry fell off. A search hit carries this id; parse it rather than splitting on the separator.
- **The semantic index's copy of Director's Notes:** `archiveDirectorNotesSynopsisInBackground()`, `readDirectorNotesArchive()`, `onDirectorNotesArchived()` in `src/main/local-models/directors-notes-archive.ts`. A synopsis is stored nowhere else (the renderer keeps the last one in a module variable), so both generation paths (the IPC handler and the web / CLI callback) hand theirs here. Written ONLY while `semantic-search` is assigned a source, bounded to the newest `MAX_ARCHIVED_SYNOPSES`, stored as the prose a person reads (`synopsisIndexText`) rather than the JSON the agent emitted. It never fails a synopsis. Not a general Director's Notes history: do not build a notes browser on it without lifting the gate deliberately.
- **Reusing a System One answer (the seven-day cache):** `systemOneCacheKey(scope, state, questions)`, `readCachedSystemOneAnswer()`, `storeCachedSystemOneAnswer()`, `sweepSystemOneAnswerCache()`, `SYSTEM_ONE_CACHE_TTL_DAYS` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/answer-cache.ts`. It is the `jev` CLI's cache, kept by Maestro, under `<data dir>/system-one-cache/<xx>/<key>.json`. Four rules: it holds answers, never text (the state and questions are in the key only as a hash, taken AFTER redaction); an entry answers only the questions it was stored for (a read checks every answer's id, type, options and range, and a file that does not fit is a miss, never a guess); entries live seven days, because `jev-latest` moves when TypeSafe releases a model; and it can never fail a request (an unreadable file is a miss, an unwritable one is a log warning). The key is the SHA-256 of `canonicalJson()` of the engine, where it is called, the model, the state and the questions, so key order does not matter and another engine or model is another entry. Only the agent service uses it: a task's gate (`runSystemOneTask()`) always asks, because its answer is recorded as a decision and a decision is not replayed.
- **Recording and reading a System One decision (the ledger):** `keepSystemOneDecision(decision, kept)`, `recordSystemOneDecision(decision)`, `readSystemOneAgentSpend(since)` and `readSystemOneLedger(rawQuery)` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/decisions.ts`, over the table `s1_decisions` (`insertSystemOneDecision()`, `getSystemOneDecisions()`, `getSystemOneAgentSpend()`, `pruneSystemOneDecisions()` in `src/main/stats/s1-decisions.ts`, migrations v18 and v19), with the vocabulary in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-ledger.ts`: `SystemOneDecision`, `SystemOneLedgerEntry`, `SystemOneLedgerPage`, `SystemOneLedgerTaskId`, `systemOneLedgerTaskLabel()`, `parseSystemOneLedgerQuery()` (the one validator the view and `maestro-cli s1 ledger` both send a query through), `formatSystemOneLedgerInput(request)`, `summarizeSystemOneDecision()`, `systemOneLedgerContext()`, `describeSystemOneAnswer()`, `SYSTEM_ONE_LEDGER_OUTCOME_LABELS` and `SYSTEM_ONE_LEDGER_RETENTION_DAYS` (30). `systemOneLedgerContext()` derives a topic and question preview from the task and saved question instructions, including structured rank questions. It never infers a topic from private state; missing or malformed questions return a null preview. There are TWO producers, and both write through `keepSystemOneDecision()`: `runSystemOneTask` records every task run that reached the System One path, answered or not, and `runAgentSystemOneRequest` (`src/main/local-models/system-one/agent-service.ts`) records every request an agent's `maestro-cli s1` verb made, filed under `agent:<verb>` with the `agentId` and `tabId` that asked and `cached` for an answer that came from the answer cache. Any other caller that asks an engine directly (`askSystemOne`, `askLocalJudge`) leaves no record and must go through one of the two instead. `readSystemOneAgentSpend()` sums the `agent:` rows only (a task's run is Maestro's spend, whatever agent it names); it is what the agents' daily budget is seeded from, so do NOT sum `cost_usd` for a budget anywhere else. Four rules. The outcome says what the fail policy did (`ok`, `failed-open`, `failed-closed`, `unavailable`, `cancelled`) and the code says why, so a timeout is NOT an outcome: it is a failed run with the code `timeout`, and the `timeout` filter matches the code. A record carries question ids and typed answers, never text: the state and the questions are kept only while `SystemOneSettings.keepInputs` is on, and `keepSystemOneDecision()` builds that text with `formatSystemOneLedgerInput()`, which redacts it and cuts a long state, so do NOT put a state or a question on a record by hand. It is a stats writer: it honors `statsCollectionEnabled` (a read then answers with a `notice`), and a write that fails is reported by `keepSystemOneDecision()` and never fails the run, so a producer calls that and never `recordSystemOneDecision()` itself. Records older than the retention window are never returned and are deleted at most once a day on the next write or read; there is no separate timer. Do NOT read `s1_decisions` from a second place: the renderer goes through `useSystemOneLedger()` and `window.maestro.localModels.systemOne.ledger()`. A request sent to a remote engine also leaves a usage record: `recordSystemOneDecision()` writes `systemOneDecisionRun(decision)` (`src/shared/local-models/system-one-ledger.ts`) to `local_model_runs` with the source type `system-one`, because the ledger is pruned at thirty days and the Usage Dashboard counts longer ranges. It returns `null`, and no run is written, for a cached answer, for a decision with no engine chosen, and for one a local router decided (the local judge, the embedding router): that model ran through the task router, which already filed the run. Do not write a second `system-one` row from a caller.
- **Asking a System One engine a typed question:** `askSystemOne(engine, state, questions, { timeoutMs, signal, model? })`, `listSystemOneModels()` and `parseSystemOneAnswers()` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/jev-client.ts`, with the builders `noul()`, `choice()`, `score()` and every type in `src/shared/local-models/system-one.ts`. It is the ONLY code that speaks to Jev or an OpenJev server (`POST /v1/systemone`, `GET /v1/models`); a second `fetch` to either, or a second parser for their answers, is the failure. It was written as one client so four rules would hold for every caller. The state and the questions are redacted INSIDE it (`redactSystemOneRequest()` over every string value, never object keys, because a choice's option ids are keys), so no caller can skip it and `SystemOneResult.redacted` says a span was removed. It makes ONE attempt, with no retry and no backoff: a task has about 1.5 seconds and its own fail policy, and the `jev` command's retries belong to a batch tool. Its budget covers the whole answer, body included, because `fetchWithTimeout` stops its clock at the headers and a server that sends headers and stalls is a `timeout`. And every failure is a `SystemOneError` (`src/main/local-models/errors.ts`) that names the engine asked, with a code (`auth`, `rate-limited`, `too-large`, `unavailable`, `timeout`, `invalid`, `cancelled`): nothing falls back to another engine. The builders live in `src/shared` because a task definition declares `systemOneQuestions(input)` there and cannot import from main. Build a question with an id and describe every choice option distinctly; never index into a long array, since an answer is read back by id. A caller never calls `askSystemOne` or `askLocalJudge` for a task: go through `runSystemOneTask` (the next entry), which applies the fail policy and writes the ledger. Two callers ask an engine outside the router, both through `askResolvedSystemOne()`: `testSystemOne()`, for the card's Test button and `s1 test`, which is recorded nowhere on purpose, and `runAgentSystemOneRequest()`, an agent's own `maestro-cli s1` request, which states its own request budget (`timeoutMs`) and writes the ledger itself. The cost it reports is an estimate and a FLOOR on a small request (Jev bills about 256 tokens of overhead per request that `estimateSystemOneRequest()` does not include); the ledger keeps the tokens the engine reports. Do NOT estimate tokens with the generic `estimateTokenCount()` for a Jev request (Jev measures 3.32 characters per token, `JEV_CHARS_PER_TOKEN`).
- **Running a task on System One:** `runSystemOneTask(taskId, input, { signal, timeoutMs, failPolicy, agentId })` in `src/main/local-models/task-router.ts`, over `taskAcceptsSystemOne()`, `buildSystemOneTaskRequest()` and `SystemOneTaskInputs` in `src/shared/local-models/tasks.ts`, and `resolveSystemOneForTask()` and `askResolvedSystemOne()` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/service.ts`. It is the twin of `runLocalTask()` for the `system-one` assignment (`{ type: 'system-one' }`, spelled `system-one`), which names no engine: the engine in force is one choice for every task. A task takes it only when it is a System One task that declares `systemOneQuestions(input)`; every other task drops a stored one and `localModelAssignmentProblem()` refuses it at save. A caller calls it FIRST and reads the status: `not-assigned` means nothing was asked and nothing was recorded, so the caller runs its model twin as before; `ok` carries the typed answers and `engineLabel`; `failed-open`, `failed-closed` and `unavailable` are no answer, with the task's fail policy applied; `cancelled` is a caller abort. Four rules. There is no fallback: no other engine and no model is asked when the engine in force gives no answer, and a task assigned to System One with no engine chosen is no answer too (code `off`, nothing sent). The fail policy (`resolveSystemOneFailPolicy()`, default on the task definition, changed with `s1 set fail-policy`) is applied here and nowhere else, and a caller that holds a more specific rule passes `failPolicy`, so the recorded outcome is the one that happened (the Cue gate passes the subscription's `gate_on_unavailable`; precedence is subscription, then task, then open). Every run that reached System One is recorded through `keepSystemOneDecision()`, so a second place that asks an engine for a task leaves no ledger entry. And an unclassified engine failure still throws, because it is a bug for Sentry. The Cue gate is the first caller (`evaluateCueGate()` in `src/main/local-models/cue-gate.ts`, `cueGateSystemOneRequest()` and `cueGateCheckFromSystemOne()` in `src/shared/cue/gate.ts`). A model twin REFUSES a task assigned to System One by name, so do NOT call `runLocalTask()` for one, and do NOT read an unknown assignment as the Utility Agent. Six tasks run on it (`cue-gate`, `injection-screen`, `secret-screen`, `tab-cleanup`, `voice-routing`, `model-routing`). To add a seventh: write the builder and the function that reads the answers back in the task's OWN shared module, beside its model reply contract (`cueGateSystemOneRequest` in `src/shared/cue/gate.ts`, `cueInjectionSystemOneRequest` in `src/shared/cue/injection-screen.ts`, `secretScreenSystemOneRequest` in `src/shared/secretScan.ts`, `tabCleanupSystemOneRequest` in `src/shared/tabCleanup.ts`, `voiceRoutingSystemOneRequest` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-routing-system-one.ts`, `modelRoutingSystemOneRequest` in `src/shared/local-models/model-routing-task.ts`), declare it as `systemOneQuestions` in the registry, add the input type to `SystemOneTaskInputs`, and map the answers back to the SAME result shape and threshold the model path returns, so a caller cannot tell which engine answered. Key every question and every choice option by a real id, never by a place in a list, and put a per-item fact inside that item's own question. Never cut a value to fit: a request too large for the engine is no answer. `timeoutMs` replaces the engine's stored budget for one request and is for a task that asks many questions while a person waits (tab cleanup). Voice routing is mounted as a Brain decorator, `withSystemOneRouting()` in `src/main/acappella/providers/system-one-brain.ts`: an engine writes nothing, so the Brain in force still speaks, and the assignment fills no Brain slot (`isVoiceRoutingOnSystemOne()` in `src/shared/acappella/voice-routing.ts`).
- **Which key a System One engine is called with:** `resolveSystemOneKey(engine)` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/service.ts`, returning `ResolvedSystemOneKey` (`key?`, `info: SystemOneKeyInfo`); the sealed store is `readSystemOneStoredKey()` and `system-one.json` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/config-store.ts`, and the words every surface uses are `describeSystemOneKey()` and `SystemOneKeyInfo` in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-settings.ts`. The order is the stored key, then `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` (Settings > Environment before Maestro's own environment, the way `resolveEndpointApiKey` reads a variable), then `~/.config/typesafe/api_key`, the `jev` command's file. The last two are read-only fallbacks that belong to the HOSTED engine alone: an OpenJev server is only ever sent a key stored for it, so the TypeSafe key never goes to somebody else's machine. A stored key that does not open (`store-unavailable` or `unreadable`) answers with no key and the reason, and nothing falls through to another source: the engine reports `auth` and sends nothing, and the unreadable bytes stay in the file until the person chooses Replace or Clear. A key is WRITE-ONLY: no IPC channel, bridge message or CLI verb returns one, and a surface learns only where it comes from (`stored` with `os` or `obscured`, `env` with the variable and which environment, `file` with the path, or `none`). `s1 key set` reads the key from standard input so it is never in shell history; do NOT add a flag that takes one. The key is sealed by `createPrivateStateFile()` and `sealSecret()` (see the entry for a per-device state file that holds a secret), so do NOT write another reader of `system-one.json` or another `{ sealed, storage }` record. A call that needs the engine and its key together goes through `resolveSystemOneEngine()` or `resolveSystemOneForTask()`, which read the settings once; do NOT call `resolveSystemOneKey()` and the settings separately and then act on a mixed read.
- **What agents' System One requests cost, and what a job would:** `readSystemOneAgentUsage(rawQuery, callerAgentId?)` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/agent-service.ts` (bridge message `system_one_usage`, `maestro-cli s1 usage`), with the vocabulary in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-spend.ts`: `SystemOneUsageQuery`, `parseSystemOneUsageQuery()` (the one validator), `SystemOneUsageReport`, `SystemOneAgentSpend`, `sumSystemOneUsage()`, `formatSystemOneCost()`, and for a job that has not run `estimateSystemOneJob(shape)`, `systemOneJobShapeProblem()` and `JEV_REQUEST_OVERHEAD_TOKENS` (`maestro-cli s1 cost`). Usage is the `agent:<verb>` rows of the decision ledger per agent since a time (never past the ledger's 30 days), each with the agent's current name, plus TODAY's spend against the budgets from the count the agent service keeps in the process. The two are reported together because they answer different questions and fail differently: the rows stop growing while usage statistics are off or the database is not open (`systemOneLedgerNotice()` says which), and the budget still counts. A Settings view of per-agent spend reads this function; do NOT write a second query over `s1_decisions` for it. `estimateSystemOneJob()` packs a job the way the agent service does (`systemOneAgentRequestLimits()`): a `rank` shares the state across the candidates of a request, a `batch` repeats it per row, and the local judge takes one question a request and has no Jev limit to fit (`fits: null`). It ADDS the 256 tokens Jev bills on top of every request (measured 2026-10-12: about 375 input tokens billed where the text came to about 119), because on a job of many small requests the overhead is most of the bill and an estimate without it is a third of the real one. Only `jev-cloud` has a price. `formatSystemOneCost()` exists because one request costs millionths of a dollar and `formatCost()` prints that as `<$0.01`; use it for a System One figure a person sizes a job by, and `formatCost()` for a budget.
- **The command line of a System One verb (the `jev` flag grammar):** `readState(flags)`, `hasStateFlags()`, `collectQuestionFlags(argv)`, `questionFlagsProblem()`, `buildQuestions(flags, sources)`, `readRankCandidates(sources)`, `readBatchRows(text, flags)`, `parseCliValue()`, `parseCliText()`, `parseKeyedOptions()` and `SystemOneInputError` in `src/cli/services/system-one-input.ts`, registered with their handlers by `registerSystemOneAgentCommands(systemOne)` in `src/cli/commands/system-one-ask.ts` (`s1 yes | pick | rate | ask | rank | batch`, and `usage` and `cost` from `src/cli/commands/system-one-spend.ts`). The grammar is the `jev` CLI's on purpose, so an agent that knows one knows the other: the state is `-s/--state TEXT` (`@path` reads a file, `-` reads standard input), `--state-file`, `--state-json`, `--field key=value` (with `--state` the text becomes the field `text`) or piped text; a question is `--noul ID INSTRUCTIONS [true=...] [false=...]`, `--choice ID INSTRUCTIONS OPTION[=description] ...` or `--score ID INSTRUCTIONS LEVEL LEVEL ...`, and `ID=INSTRUCTIONS` in one word reads the same way. Four rules. Question flags are read from the RAW command line (`collectQuestionFlags`), because the option parser hands every `--noul` back as one flat list and `--noul a "Q1" --noul b "Q2"` loses where one question ends; `questionFlagsProblem()` then checks that reading against the option parser's and refuses when they differ, so nothing is guessed. A candidate and a row are KEYED by where they came from, counted from 1 (a line of a file by its line number with blank lines counted, a JSON item by its own `--id-key` field when every item has one), and never by position in what is sent. Everything typed wrong is a `SystemOneInputError`, which a verb turns into `ExitCode.InvalidUsage`. And the request a verb builds goes through `parseSystemOneAgentRequest()` BEFORE it is sent, so a bad flag costs no round trip and the CLI cannot send a field the desktop does not read. The exit codes are part of the contract: `yes` exits 0 for yes and 1 for no (`NEGATIVE_ANSWER_EXIT_CODE`, also an answer under `--min-confidence` on `pick` and `rate`), and NO failure exits 1 on these verbs, so `if maestro-cli s1 yes ...` never reads an error as a no: a wrong command line is 2 (the option parser's own errors included, through `exitOverride`), a refusal by a switch or the budget is `ExitCode.Refused` (6), and an engine failure or an INCOMPLETE `rank` or `batch` is `ExitCode.NoAnswer` (7). The answer goes to standard output and the line that names the engine and the spend (`formatSystemOneAnswerMeta()` in `src/cli/output/system-one-answers.ts`) to standard error, so `$(maestro-cli s1 yes ...)` holds the answer alone. A new verb that asks System One is added to `registerSystemOneAgentCommands`, not to `src/cli/index.ts`.
- **What agents may ask System One, and switching one agent off:** the global switch and the budgets are `SystemOneSettings.agents` (`enabled`, `dailyBudgetUsd`, `perAgentBudgetUsd`), changed ONLY through `applySystemOneSettingsPatch()` as `{ agents: { ... } }`; `parseSystemOneBudgetText()`, `SYSTEM_ONE_AGENT_USE_DESCRIPTIONS` and `systemOneAgentUseBlocker(report)` in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-settings.ts`. The per-agent switch is `Session.systemOneOptOut`: `parseSystemOneOptOutEdit()`, `isSystemOneOptedOut()`, `SYSTEM_ONE_AGENT_SWITCH_DESCRIPTIONS` and `systemOneAgentGateReason()` in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-agent.ts`; `setAgentSystemOneOptOut()` in `src/renderer/services/agentSystemOne.ts`. Surfaces: `<SystemOneAgentsSection>` in `src/renderer/components/Settings/tabs/LocalModelsTab/` (the switch, the two budgets, today's spend, the per-agent table over `useSystemOneUsage()`), `<AgentSystemOneControl>` in `src/renderer/components/NewInstanceModal/` (Edit Agent's Models tab, over `useSystemOneAgentAccess()`), and `maestro-cli s1 set agents on|off`, `s1 set budget [usd] [--per-agent <usd|none>]`, `update-agent --system-one on|off`. **A budget a person typed is read by `parseSystemOneBudgetText()`,** never by `Number()`: `Number('')` is 0, and a daily budget of 0 refuses every agent request, so an emptied field would switch System One off for every agent with no word said. The per-agent field is the one place where empty is a value (no cap, `null` on the wire). **On is the ABSENCE of `systemOneOptOut`.** `parseSystemOneOptOutEdit()` stores `true` or removes the field; `false` and `null` both remove it. An agent is never recorded as opted in, so a default that moves later still reaches it, and `isSystemOneOptedOut()` reads only an exact `true`. Every writer goes through that validator (the switch through `setAgentSystemOneOptOut()`, the CLI through the `update_session_config` allowlist in `useAppRemoteEventListeners`); do NOT write the field from a new call site. **The opt-out must be flushed to main,** which is why the switch applies at once instead of waiting for Save: the main process reads it from its own sessions store when it answers a `maestro-cli s1` request and when it decides whether the agent's system prompt carries the System One section, and the renderer's ordinary persistence is debounced by two seconds. **The switch being on is not "agents may ask now".** An engine must be chosen, set up and have passed its Test; `systemOneAgentUseBlocker()` is the one sentence for what in the setup still stands in the way (Settings and `s1 set agents on` both print it), and for ONE agent the answer is `getSystemOneAgentAccess()`, whose `reason` is the gate's sentence for a person and whose `message` is the same sentence with the instruction to the agent after it. Do not show `message` in a settings pane. **A worktree agent inherits the opt-out** (`buildWorktreeSession`): an agent the user switched off must not start asking, and spending, from a worktree of the same project. **Changing the agents block keeps the last Test on screen** (`TEST_KEEPING_FIELDS` in `useSystemOne`): who may ask is not part of the engine the Test asked. New Agent has no switch: it applies at once to a stored agent, and there is none yet.
- **The System One guide an agent reads (topics and recipes):** `SYSTEM_ONE_GUIDE_TOPICS`, `SYSTEM_ONE_EXAMPLES`, `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_ONE_GUIDE_TOPIC`, `findSystemOneGuideTopic()`, `findSystemOneExample()`, `systemOneGuidePromptId()`, `systemOneExamplePromptId()` and `systemOneGuidePromptDefinitions()` in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-guide.ts`, printed by `systemOneGuide()` and `systemOneExamples()` in `src/cli/commands/system-one-guide.ts` (`maestro-cli s1 guide [topic] [--list]`, `maestro-cli s1 examples [name|all]`, registered in `registerSystemOneAgentCommands`). This is the second of three levels an agent learns System One through: a short section of the Maestro system prompt says it exists and points here, the guide teaches it on demand, and the full reference is an include. Each topic and each recipe is a core prompt, one file under `src/prompts/` (`system-one-guide-<topic>.md`, `system-one-example-<name>.md`), so a user can edit what their agents are taught in Settings, Maestro Prompts, and the CLI reads it through `getCliPrompt()`, which honors that edit. The registry is the ONE declaration: `CORE_PROMPTS` spreads `systemOneGuidePromptDefinitions()`, so a topic cannot exist without its prompt, and a test refuses a guide file on disk that the registry does not name. To add a topic, add a row and a file; do NOT add a literal entry to `CORE_PROMPTS` or a second list of names to the CLI. Three rules for the text. The CLI prints a guide prompt AS WRITTEN, so it must hold no `{{TEMPLATE_VARIABLE}}`: nothing fills one in on this path. Every shell example sits in a `bash` code fence, and `src/__tests__/cli/commands/system-one-guide.test.ts` parses those fences and fails when a `maestro-cli s1` command names a verb or a flag the option parser does not have, or double-quotes a question that holds backticks: nothing type-checks a prompt, and a wrong example is pasted straight into a shell. And the measured rules are asserted by name (never index into a long list, with its numbers; a local judge's number is not a calibrated probability; an unjudged item is unknown; a screen is not a permission; a refusal is not routed around), so an edit that drops one fails. The verbs talk to neither the desktop nor an engine, so the guide can be read while System One is off; the verbs it describes then refuse by name. The playbook (`when`) is read once per session by every agent that asks: keep it near 1,500 tokens and move depth into a topic.
- **Answering an agent's System One request (the service):** `runAgentSystemOneRequest(raw, caller, { signal })` in `src/main/local-models/system-one/agent-service.ts`, reached by the bridge message `system_one_agent_request` (`handleSystemOneAgentRequest()` in `src/main/web-server/handlers/messageHandlers/localModels.ts`) from `maestro-cli s1 yes|pick|rate|ask|rank|batch`. It is the ONLY code that answers an agent's System One request. It validates with `parseSystemOneAgentRequest()` in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-agent.ts`, which returns only the fields it checked, so no caller can name a model, a key or a budget; applies the gates; splits a job with `chunkSystemOneQuestions()` and `systemOneAgentRequestLimits()`; asks through `askResolvedSystemOne()`; and returns a `SystemOneAgentResponse`: a result (`ok: true`, with `engine`, `engineLabel`, `calibrated`, `model`, `cached`, `latencyMs`, `tokens`, `costUsd`, `redacted`, `budget`, and for `rank` and `batch` a `completeness` with its one-line marker) or a `SystemOneAgentRefusal` (`ok: false`, `code`, `gate?`, `message`). A refusal is a successful round trip; only an unclassified engine failure rejects, because that is a bug for Sentry. Eight rules. **Gate order is what the user switched off, then readiness, then the budget** (`engine-off`, `agents-off`, `agent-opted-out`, `engine-not-ready`, `engine-untested`, `budget-day`, `budget-agent`), and a refusal says so by name and ends "Continue without System One." **"Passed its Test" is a stored fact** (`testPassedAt`, with a signature of the engine's address and model), so a changed URL, pin or judge model reads as untested with nothing to clear. **The budget is a count in the process, seeded once a day from the ledger**, checked before every request that is SENT; a day can pass its budget by the requests in flight and no more. Only `agent:` ledger rows count (a Cue gate's run is Maestro's spend), and a budget in dollars never binds an engine that has no price. **Items are keyed, never indexed:** `rank` embeds each candidate in its own question (`systemOneRankQuestion()`, ids `c<ordinal>`), the id is never sent to the model and maps back to the caller's key by lookup; a list position as a question id is the 27% failure. **An item with no answer is unknown,** reported in the completeness marker (`buildSystemOneCompleteness()`), never a low score; there is no retry, and a second run asks only what failed because the rest is cached. **The cache is this service's only** (`answer-cache.ts`, 7 days, keyed on `canonicalJson` AFTER redaction); a task's gate is never cached. **The caller identity is a claim** from the CLI's environment (`readCallerIdentity()`): an agent that hides it escapes its own cap and opt-out, never the global switches or the daily budget, and a launch that does not stamp it (Cue, group chat, a consult) names no agent. **Every request that reached the engine or the cache is one ledger row** (`agent:<verb>`, with the tab and `cached`) through `keepSystemOneDecision()`; a refusal by a switch or the budget is not recorded, because nothing was asked. The limits are 250 questions and 30,000 estimated tokens per request, 5,000 candidates or rows per call, 4 requests in flight started 60 ms apart; the local judge gets ONE question per request. Do NOT call `askSystemOne()` for an agent, and do NOT add a second reader of `maestro-cli s1` requests.
- **Which core sections an agent's system prompt carries (System One):** `coreSystemPromptSectionsFor({ isSsh, systemOne })` and `coreSystemPromptContext()` in `src/shared/maestroSystemPrompt.ts`, over `isSystemOneAdvertised()` and `systemOnePromptVariables()` in `src/shared/local-models/system-one-prompt.ts`. System One is core, not an Encore Feature, so its section has its own path beside `systemPromptSectionsFor()` (which is for plugins): the assembler takes `coreSections` after the role sections and before the plugin sections. The text is `src/prompts/system-one-system.md` (L0, about 18 lines; its template variables are `{{SYSTEM_ONE_ENGINE}}`, `{{SYSTEM_ONE_CALIBRATED}}` and `{{SYSTEM_ONE_BUDGET}}`) and points at `maestro-cli s1 guide` (L1) and `{{REF:_system-one}}` (L2, `src/prompts/_system-one.md`). Five rules. **The gate is ONE rule and it takes the access, not booleans:** the section is there exactly while `getSystemOneAgentAccess(agentId)` says a request from the agent being spawned would be answered, never for an SSH remote agent, and never when the builder could not ask. The section and its variables come from the same answer, so they cannot disagree. **Every builder asks in its own process on every spawn,** so a switch turned off is gone from the next one: main calls `localSystemOneAccess()` in `src/main/utils/maestro-system-prompt.ts` (never throws), the renderer calls `window.maestro.localModels.systemOne.agentAccess()` in `src/renderer/utils/spawnHelpers.ts`, and the CLI asks the desktop over the bridge (`readSystemOneAccessFromDesktop()` in `src/cli/services/system-one-access.ts`, message `system_one_access`, 3 s) from `prepareMaestroSystemPromptCli()`. A closed or older desktop gives no answer and so no section, which is also right on the merits: the `s1` verbs could not reach it either. The plugin headless runner runs the CLI builder inside the main process and is handed the in-process reader instead of a socket to itself. **The budget is worded as a band** (more than half, less than half, less than a tenth is left), never an exact remainder: a provider caches the system prompt by its exact text, and a figure that changed after every request would cost every agent its cached prompt on its next turn. **A local judge is always said to be NOT calibrated.** **The moderator variant carries no core section** (`buildPluginSystemPromptSections()`): it has no agent to count a request against, cap or switch off, and it is synchronous by contract. To add another core section, add a row to `coreSystemPromptSectionsFor()` and its context to `coreSystemPromptContext()`; do NOT route it through the Encore flags, and do NOT add a fourth builder that computes its own gate.
- **Versioned schema migrations for a SQLite file:** `runSqliteMigrations(db, migrations, logContext)` and `sqliteUserVersion(db)` in `src/main/utils/sqlite-migrations.ts`, with the `SqliteMigration` shape (`version`, `description`, `up`, optional `isApplied`). One runner for every better-sqlite3 database that evolves its schema (`stats.db` via `src/main/stats/migrations.ts`, `embeddings.db`): it creates `_migrations`, reads `user_version`, runs each pending migration in its own transaction, and records a FAILURE outside the rolled-back transaction so the reason survives. `isApplied` exists because `user_version` is a bare number that two branches can number differently; a migration that declares it is re-applied when its schema is missing, so its `up` must be idempotent. A database supplies only its own migration list. Do NOT copy the runner beside a new schema.
